# Lichborne — Bug & Feature Request Tracker

> Reported by testers during early access. Updated as issues are resolved.

---

## Open Bugs

> B285–B305 were opened by the 2026-08-23 internal full-code sweep (three-agent audit + recurring-class greps + harness runs). Seventeen bugs plus B303 were fixed the same day in v0.19.2, and B305 was confirmed deliberate + documented (see Resolved). The two below were **deferred by Sekmeht (2026-08-23)** — conscious calls, not oversights.

> B367–B410 were opened by the 2026-09-15 UI/UX and consistency audit (five read-only audits: dialog chrome, controls and wording, panels and floating windows, interaction and quality of life, and visual tokens). The high-severity claims were checked against the code before logging; estimates and inferences are marked. **Status (v0.19.7):** B367–B409 are fixed (see Resolved); B410 waits on a Mac screenshot (pitfall #125).

| # | Summary | Reporter | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| B454 | **A dialog opened from the NATIVE MENU while a higher-tier dialog is open mounts invisibly** | UI/UX sweep | Every dialog toggle is an independent `setShowX(v => !v)` -- nothing is mutually exclusive -- and they sit on different tiers (Layout Manager / Settings / Session Log 100, Experiences 120, Theme Picker / Contacts / Automations 200, Maps 250, Lich Dashboard 400). Each backdrop covers the app bar so the in-app buttons are correctly blocked, but the View and Tools menu items carry no `enabled:` gating. With Automations open, **Tools > Settings** mounts Settings under Automations' scrim and nothing appears to happen; with the Lich Dashboard open, every other dialog is invisible. Second-order: the Esc stack is mount-ordered, so Esc then closes the dialog you cannot see and leaves the visible one -- the "Esc is broken" signature. **Compounded by a tie-break:** the Layout Manager is the one dialog rendered INLINE rather than portaled, and both its z-100 tier-mates are portaled to `document.body` (appended after `#root`), so at an equal tier it loses on DOM order regardless of what the user just clicked. **Fix shape:** gate the menu items on whether a dialog is open (`anyDialogOpen()` now exists), or give the dialogs one tier and let mount order decide. Not attempted here -- it is a behaviour decision across about ten surfaces. |
| B455 | **Portaled popovers that take no focus, and a search with no keyboard exit** | UI/UX sweep | `ColorField` and the Macros $ menu move focus into their portaled menu and return it on close; **GroupPicker, ModeSwitcher and ContextMenu never got that pattern**. Tabbing to "+ Group" and pressing Enter opens a menu focus never enters, and because it is portaled to the end of `<body>` Tab continues through the form behind it. The context menu's submenu parent is a `<button>` with **no onClick** (it opens on hover only), so it takes a tab stop and does nothing on Enter -- that is the whole `Modify Text` / `Trigger` branch of the right-click menu. Separately the **Lich Map's Search rooms** input has no `onKeyDown` at all and its results are bare `<div onClick>`, so a flow that starts on the keyboard has no keyboard exit. `TableauExperience`'s creature figures also carry `role="button"` with no `tabIndex` and no key handler -- a role without a tab stop. None loses capability (each has a mouse or slash-command path), which is why they are grouped rather than blocking. |
| B456 | **Shared-primitive drift: about 25 private section labels, three button families, four preview treatments** | UI/UX sweep | The v0.19.7 unification held for dialog SURFACES and field fills -- those are clean -- but the furniture inside them has drifted. **Section labels:** `.ui-section-label` exists, and there are about 25 private definitions against 18 uses, spanning 8 sizes, 5 colours and 8 letter-spacings, including two verbatim restatements; label tracking visibly shifts between Automations, Settings, the Theme Editor and Transfer, one app-bar click apart. **Tabs — DONE in v0.19.8 (F119):** Automations was the only one of ten dialogs not using `ui-tabs`/`ui-tab`; it restated the recipe plus a hover transition the others lack, so its tabs animated where every other dialog's snapped. `.at-tab` turned out to be byte-for-byte `.ui-tab`, so the header restructure deleted it outright rather than reconciling it, and the tabs picked up the `role="tablist"` / `role="tab"` / `aria-selected` they had never had. **Menu rows — also DONE in v0.19.8 (F119):** four dialog menus (GroupPicker, the Macros "$" picker, ColorField, the Automations ⋯) were four copies of one recipe differing only by drift — including two hover tokens and two surface colours with no comment defending either. Unified as `ui-menu` / `ui-menu-item` in ui.css. The audit that drove it also established the membership rule now in DESIGN §43.7: rem-vs-em, so the five GAME-tier menus stay out. The rest of this row — the section labels, the button families and the preview treatments — is untouched. **Buttons:** the connect family (`launcher-connecting-cancel`, used by five dialogs) is a near-clone of `ui-btn` with a different border and hover, its `--primary` is byte-for-byte `ui-btn--primary`, and the Quit confirm is a third neutral recipe -- on the last thing a user sees each session. **Previews:** four "how it will look" surfaces, two backgrounds, four type treatments; `.cp-tpl-preview` pins 0.85rem so it does NOT show the game's type, which matters because pitfall #127/B281 was itself a Contacts preview not matching the game. Also two clear-search close buttons with different glyphs, a close glyph used as decoration on a non-close action, and `.hp-empty`/`.trg-empty` where `ui-empty` exists. Each is small; together they are what makes a client read as assembled rather than designed (UX #10/#12). |
| B457 | **The Lich Map's tooltip and location-unknown prose are still fixed px** | UI/UX sweep | `.map-panel` is font-anchored and B338 converted the room detail and search results, but the pass stopped short of about 25 rules: the tooltip (9-12px), the location-unknown explanatory panel (9-11px), and the status/error/loading prose (11-12px). A user on Font Size 20 -- or on **Large Print, which forces 18px** -- reads those at 9-12px. The file's own comment names only "toolbar / subbar / bottom-bar chrome" as the deliberate-px set, and these are not it. The legend is the borderline case: its grid is a fixed `minmax(210px, 1fr)`, so its text can only scale if the column does. |
| B458 | **Profile Transfer offers categories with a literal 0 count** | UI/UX sweep | `presentCategories` filters on the category BAG existing rather than on `count > 0`, and the export's default branch gates on `Object.keys(bag).length > 0`, which an empty array satisfies. Deleting your last mute writes `[]`, so the bundle carries the key and the import screen shows **Mutes 0** as a tickable choice, rendered in `.pt-cat-count` -- an accent-tinted pill, the loudest treatment available, on a count of nothing (UX #1). The `colors` branch's own comment asserts it is left out "rather than offered on the import screen as a category with no contents (every other category below does the same)"; the default branch does not do the same. Comment and code disagree; the code wins. |
| B442 | **Reconnect Last / Team Login silently SKIPS a character who shares a name with one already on, in a different game** | Bug check | Found by the rules harness during the F118 bug check (pre-existing, unreported by a tester). `planReconnect` ([reconnectPlan.ts:42](src/renderer/reconnectPlan.ts#L42)) builds `connectedChars` as `` `${account}:${character}` `` and `ReconnectLive` ([:27](src/renderer/reconnectPlan.ts#L27)) carries **no `game`** — so with DR *Agan* connected, a saved GemStone *Agan* on the same account matches the already-on set and is dropped with nothing to do. It should be a CONFLICT (the account slot is held by a different session), which is the Keep/Switch chooser. One Simutronics account spans DR, the DR test/plat shards and GS, and a name can exist on more than one, so this is reachable. The rules harness has pinned the wanted behaviour for some time (*"DR Agan connected → saved GS Agan is a CONFLICT, not 'already on'"*) and is the single failing case in that suite — 416 passed, 1 failed. **Fix shape:** add `game` to `ReconnectLive`, key on `account:game:character`, and thread the field from the roster (`RosterEntry` already carries `game`). NOT done in v0.19.8: it changes the login path, and the F118 work that surfaced it was unrelated. |
| B425 | **The Experience ⚙ popover is only a few pixels tall in a strip-height window** | Final bug check (2026-09-15) | Found by the final bug check (2026-09-15). B372 made both A−/A+/⚙ variants a horizontal row and opens the popover above it, bounded so it scrolls instead of clipping (pitfall #109). In a 48px-tall Spell Monitor strip that bound leaves about 9px for 11 options, and below roughly 34px it clamps to zero. Better than the old silent clip, but unusable at that height. The real fix is to portal the popover so it can escape .fl-body overflow, the way ContextMenu does, or to give it a floor and let it open beside or below when there is no room above. |
| B410 | **About 60 emoji-classified glyphs lack U+FE0E and may render in colour on macOS** | UI/UX audit (2026-09-15) | ⚙ ▶ ⚠ ♥ ↗ ⚡ and others. Per pitfall #125 a Mac screenshot comes first, so this waits on a Mac tester. |
| B358 | **Linux: the AppImage may not start on Ubuntu 22.04+ without FUSE 2** | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15) | Reported by the packaging review from electron-builder's sources: with no `toolsets.appimage` set, it builds the legacy FUSE2-runtime AppImage. Inference, needs an Ubuntu tester: that 22.04/24.04 don't install libfuse2 by default, so a double-click shows nothing and the error only appears in a terminal. Mitigated in the docs this pass (README and User Guide name the package). Open for the runtime decision: electron-builder's static runtime (`toolsets.appimage`, marked beta) removes the dependency but also changes compression and the sandbox default, so it needs a test release first. Pre-existing (v0.18.0). **Status (v0.19.7):** the docs mitigation is in (README and the User Guide name the package) and CI now builds on Ubuntu; the static-runtime switch stays open as a decision pending a test release. |
| B310 | **A dropped attach that auto-re-attaches reuses the same `StormFrontParser`, so stale stream/mono state can carry into the new connection** — worst case a mid-block drop leaves `activeStream`/`streamStack` pointing at a sub-stream, and subsequent game text is filed there (wrong panel, or buffered invisibly — the pitfall #133 failure mode) with nothing to self-correct it. | Bug check (internal, v0.19.4) | NOT introduced by PR #2 and NOT patched — raise with Kahlen first, it may be considered. `reset()` ([StormFrontParser.ts:255](src/main/parser/StormFrontParser.ts#L255)) exists, its header says "call reset() on every new login (pitfall #4)", and it has **zero call sites** — pre-existing and harmless while every new connection meant a new `Session` (fresh parser, [main.ts:225](src/main/main.ts#L225)); even tab Reconnect destroys + recreates. Auto re-attach ([main.ts:437](src/main/main.ts#L437)) is the FIRST path to reuse a `Session` across a connection boundary, which makes the dormant gap reachable. The prompt boundary ([StormFrontParser.ts:1119](src/main/parser/StormFrontParser.ts#L1119)) already scrubs `boldDepth`/`currentPreset`/`colorStack`/link state each turn, so an IDLE-time drop (the common keepalive case) is harmless; NOT covered are `activeStream`/`streamStack`/`monoMode`, plus `lastRoomTitle` (suppresses the B121 sub-stream clear) and `lastClockOffsetMs` (stale server-clock anchor, the B192 lane). Same class as the splice the author DID guard one layer down (`AttachConnection.connect` clears its byte buffer); the parser holds equivalent state above it. Fix if accepted: `s.parser.reset()` immediately before `connectAttach()` in `scheduleReattach`, and consider `s.sceneParser` (cast survives too, but self-corrects on the post-attach `look`). |
| B302 | NEEDS DESIGN CALL — DEFERRED (Sekmeht, 2026-08-23): alias `passThrough` sends skip the `>` echo and the `[cmd]` log record | Sweep (internal) | Inside the canonical funnel ([GameWindow.tsx:4723-4726](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx#L4723)) the raw passthrough text is sent un-echoed/un-logged while the alias's resolved commands echo normally — the B199 signature, breaking the documented log↔screen parity invariant. Possibly intended ("the user just typed it"); nothing documents it. Decide + either fix or document. |
| B304 | NEEDS DESIGN CALL — DEFERRED (Sekmeht, 2026-08-23): Overview `idle` is blind to stream-routed text — the better the panel layout, the more wrongly "idle" reads | Sweep (internal) | `noteInbound` fires only for main-window lines ([GameWindow.tsx:3194-3205](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx#L3194)); a watched stream never spills to main, so steady lnet/thoughts traffic into an open panel reads idle with `lines 0` — the same watched-stream inversion Catch Me Up had. Softened by prompts landing in main. Fix would be one `+=` for stream totals; decide whether stream chatter should count as "activity". |

## Known Limitations

| Limitation | Notes |
|---|---|
| **One character per SimuCo account at a time** | DragonRealms only allows one active character per account. Attempting to log in a second character on an account already in use returns an "Invalid login key" error from the SGE server (and may also kick the first session). Lichborne now guards against this at the Add Character form — refuses the login with a clear message *before* hitting the server, and also translates the server's "Invalid login key" error into the same hint as a defense-in-depth measure. To run multiple characters concurrently, use multiple SimuCo accounts. |
| **Two characters with the same name on different accounts share one `{Character}.yaml`** | Profile files are keyed by character name only, not account. If two of your accounts each have a character named "Sekmeht", they'll share one profile file. Pre-existing limitation from v0.5.x; multi-character makes it more reachable. Workaround: rename one of the characters in-game. |
| **Sessions are not persisted across app launches — SOFTENED (v0.15.2, F62)** | Closing Lichborne still ends every active session (by design — the game server has no reattach). But the launcher now remembers the last-live character set and offers **"⟲ Reconnect Last (N)"**, which re-runs the bulk-connect flow for the whole set in one click. Window arrangement is not yet restored (deferred). |
| **Map view state is per-character and doesn't follow you between tabs** | Each character's current map zone, selected room, and viewport position are independent. Switching tabs shows whatever zone that character is in (or last opened), not a unified map. Intentional — characters explore independently and a shared map across tabs would be confusing in boxing scenarios. |
| **The Variable Inspector dropdown lists all character scopes from your Lich install** | The Lich Dashboard → Variables tab populates its scope dropdown from every `uservars` row in `lich.db3`, which includes any character you've ever connected via this Lich install (across all your accounts). Useful (you can inspect any character's vars from any tab) but worth knowing — your alt's vars are visible from your main's dashboard. **As of v0.9.0 the Variables tab is editable** (add / edit / delete) — but only for the *connected character's own scope*; viewing another character's scope stays read-only, because Lich variable writes can only safely target the session Lichborne is attached to. |
| **A character with a Lich Scripts panel open polls `;listall` every 5 seconds** | Per-character script tracking injects `;listall` every 5s — but **only while that character has a Lich Scripts panel open** (v0.13.1, B163); with the panel closed it injects nothing. Cheap server-side, and now scoped to characters actually watching scripts. |
| **Trigger commands waiting for roundtime have three edges (v0.20.0)** | (1) Within ONE trigger, a command set not to wait can send before an earlier command that is waiting. (2) Commands still queued are lost if the character is moved to another window mid-queue (the GameWindow remounts). (3) A turn that ends with no roundtime can release the next chained command one prompt early, because "the turn has settled" is inferred from the next prompt, with a 1s cap. All three are rare and none sends a wrong command; each was left alone rather than complicate the queue. |
| **A trigger Toast only appears while Lichborne is the focused app** | By design: a toast is an in-app notice routed to the window you're using. For an alert while Lichborne is in the background, use **Notify**. |
| **Ruby GTK script windows — RESOLVED (v0.9.1 spawn rework, verified 2026-07-03)** | Lich scripts that build their own OS-level window via `Gtk.queue { ... }` + `Gtk::Window.new` (`;vars setup`, `kill-counter`) were flaky before v0.9.1 (windows failing to paint / painting intermittently / crashing Lich on widget interaction — B138). **v0.9.1 reworked the Lich spawn** to the community-standard GUI-subsystem shape (`rubyw.exe`, no `windowsHide`, stdout/stderr → `{userData}/Logs/lich-launch/{Character}.log`), and **GTK was empirically verified working on 2026-07-03** — no longer a limitation. If a GTK script misbehaves on v0.9.1+, it's a real bug worth investigating (capture the launch log). The `<streamWindow>` / `<pushStream>` XML approach remains the more portable option for script authors, and the Lich Dashboard → Variables editor remains the recommended `;vars setup` replacement. Full history: Tracker.md v0.9.1, CLAUDE.md GTK architecture note + pitfall #52. |

| F124 | **Character colours** (Sekmeht: *"the color for the avatar… configurable in the logon screen, when you edit a character… propagate into living tableau and the toast notifications"*; *"anything with a focus border would be really cool to show this"*) | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.20.0. | F121 | **The trigger editor wasn't intuitive** (Sekmeht: *"the dropdown with $var listed is a selector, what are some things we can add to give tooltips"*) | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.20.0. DESIGN §14.y.** Tooltips and section hints throughout; the `$` insert menu (VarMenu, shared with Macros/Aliases); example values for conditions; suggestions for the watched variable. |
| F120 | **Triggers wait for roundtime, on by default** | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.20.0. DESIGN §14.10c.** See B467. Per-command `waitForRt`; `;` commands never wait; `/trigger add … rt=wait\|now`. |
| F119 | **The Automations title bar was doing five jobs at once** (Sekmeht: *"the options in the title bar of Automations is kind of hard to understand… it's just not intuitive or it's not very well separated"*) | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.19.8. DESIGN §43.8.** One flex row at a uniform 8px gap held navigation (8 tab chips), context (the scope switch), a persistent mode (Analytics), a once-ever action (Import) and dismiss — five kinds of control at one visual weight, with the accent painting three different meanings in the same row. Split into an identity band (title · ⋯ · ✕) and a quiet sub-bar (tabs, then **"Applies to"** + the scope switch). Analytics and Import moved into the ⋯, which wears an accent dot while Analytics is on. **The Lich Dashboard got the same split** (it was already carrying B373's wrapper as a workaround for the same crowding). Both now use the shared `ui-modal-head`/`ui-modal-title`, deleting four byte-equivalent private copies, and Automations' tabs use `ui-tabs` (closing B456's tab clause). The **Theme Editor already had this shape** and is the reference; Contacts and the Theme Picker were deliberately left alone at two and three tabs. Rule recorded in DESIGN §43.8 and CLAUDE UX #10 so the next header control lands in the right row. |
| F117 | **The contact template card was one long ladder of fields** (Sekmeht: *"can you add some dividers so the area to configure the template doesn't look so cluttered and confusing… when you select an effect new color boxes pop up, its just very confusing and your eyes get overloaded… it's not really obvious where the text options are, then the tag options, or which bg color matches for what"*). | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.19.8.** The editor is now labelled ZONES under hairline dividers (UX #5) — **Contact name**, **Tag**, **Preview**, **Applies to** — each its own grid, so an effect's colour box appears INSIDE its zone and reflows only there instead of shuffling every field below it, which is what made one new box feel like the whole form moved. The tag's styling appears only once there is a tag to style (UX #1), and the values are kept, so clearing the text and typing it again brings the styling back. Paired with B434 (the card is a responsive `auto-fit` grid rather than a 270px ribbon in an 88vw dialog) and B436 (bold reaching the tag and the previews). |
| F118 | **A trigger's echo can be styled like everything else Lichborne paints** (Sekmeht: *"the echo and giving a highlight/effect: this should appear here too"*). The Echo action offered a colour and nothing else, while highlights and contact templates had grown background, bold, a text effect and an effect colour — so the one line a trigger writes for you was the hardest thing on screen to make stand out. | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.19.8.** Four fields on the action (`echoBgColor` / `echoBold` / `echoEffect` / `echoGlowColor`), and **no new painter**: they are applied as the echoed line's LINE LAYER via `lineLayerFromHint`, the sibling of `resolveLineLayer` that B428 already built — so the background, colour and bold go on the line container, the effect rides an inner span (pitfall #148), and a word-scope highlight still composites on top exactly as it does over a line rule. The style travels on `TextLine.fx` (a new optional `LineStyleHint`; `effect` is typed as a plain string there because the shared module must not import the renderer's union, and it is narrowed in one place — an unknown value degrades to no effect rather than throwing). **The compatibility rule is the careful part:** the hint is emitted ONLY when one of the new fields is set, so every pre-F118 echo is byte-identical — its colour still travels as the segment's own `fg`, and a line-scope highlight that matches the echoed line keeps winning the layer as it always has. Once an echo carries its own styling it takes that slot instead, being authored for that exact message where a line rule is generic. `echoLineStyle` in triggers.ts is the ONE builder the engine (resolving `$vars` at fire time) and the editor's new preview both call, and the preview renders through `renderHighlightedLine` — the same top-level function the game window's rows use, not a lookalike (pitfall #127). Also updated: the action summary now names every style it applies, and `colorUsage` registers both new colour fields so "Used by" stays accurate. `loadTriggers` returns parsed JSON verbatim with no rebuild, so the optional fields persist for free (pitfall #121 does not bite here). No profile-shape change; no slash surface by design — the echo action is editor-only, like gates and multi-action. 14 new harness cases (tmp-hl-harness 21 → 35), including two regression guards that pin the untouched colour-only markup byte-for-byte. |
| F116 | **Pick your named colors in the Theme Editor too — copied, not linked** (Sekmeht: *"what's the effort like if we were to do this for themed colors too?"*) | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.19.8. DESIGN §49.6b.** The ~150 theme rows now use the shared **ColorField** (swatch, typable box with type-ahead, popover offering your colors and the built-ins) instead of a native picker plus a private text input — so the Theme Editor speaks the same control vocabulary as every rule editor (UX #12). **Picking one of YOUR colors stores the HEX, never a link.** A theme is handed to other people through `exportTheme`'s `.lichborne-theme.json`, which carries no palette, so a theme must not depend on an entry only its author has — the palette is a swatch book here, not a live reference (the §37.2 rule, now enforced by `allowLink={false}` rather than by the editor calling a different resolver). The trade is stated in the field's own tooltip: names become a fixed #hex when you leave the field. Two new ColorField props carry it: `allowLink` (copy instead of follow, also used for the type-ahead list and "Save as a color…", so the three pick paths cannot disagree) and `commitTyped` (hold half-typed text in a draft — the Theme Editor's onChange repaints the app live, so `#3f` must not be applied mid-word; B387). That absorbed the editor's private `ColorText`, which is deleted. Also fixes a LATENT hole the same pass found: `GradientRow` read its two values raw, so a gradient stored as `var()` / `color-mix()` would have shown a black swatch — the gap B314 closed for the other two row types. Harness: 7 cases pinning copy-vs-link in tmp-rules-harness §N. |
| F115 | **Named colors you make once and pick anywhere — change one and everything using it changes** (Elore, then "Aubrey": *"if I want to change that color then it'll change everyone who was in that group with one click… I just pick a random orange color and then they're a little bit off"*; Sekmeht: colors as a setup area of their own, applied into contacts and highlights) | Elore | **SHIPPED v0.19.8 (Phase 1). DESIGN §49.** Your colors are LINKED, not copied: a field that picks one stores `var(--lb-color-<id>, #fallback)` and each window defines the variable from the app-wide palette, so an edit repaints every highlight, trigger echo, contact template and group using it — no render-path change, no per-line cost (measured in Electron: a rendered highlight recolored live). New **Automations → Colors** tab (make, rename, recolor, remove/restore, "Used by"), one shared **ColorField** replacing eleven hand-rolled pickers (with "Save as a color…" and "Manage colors…"), `/colors rename` and `/colors manage`, and Transfer carries the colors a bundle's rules link to (same-name colors remap, never duplicate). Removing a color retires it, so nothing using it changes. Built-ins, web names and themes still store hex (§37.2). Harness: 65 cases in tmp-rules-harness §N (12 of them pin failures the bug check reproduced — B430). **Deferred:** Phase 2 "find similar colors in use" (link the slightly-off oranges in bulk) and Phase 3 named highlight styles. |

| F109 | **Windows remember their size, position and maximized state** | Claude (QOL review, approved by Sekmeht) | **SHIPPED v0.19.7.** Every window used to open at 1400×900 on every launch ([main.ts](src/main/main.ts) `createWindow`), resetting anyone who tiles or uses a second monitor. Now `{userData}/window-state.json` — machine-local on purpose, never a profile, since it describes this machine's monitors — holds the primary window as `main` and each decoupled window as `char:<characterId>`. Restores are checked against the monitors attached at launch ([windowBounds.ts](src/shared/windowBounds.ts)): a window whose title bar would land off every display reopens centred with its size kept, one bigger than its display is clamped, and a window straddling two monitors is left alone. Bounds are captured on every move/resize and flushed on quit, because shutdown DESTROYS windows without a `close` event. Rules harness §M. |

| F54 | **Moons Experience [Beta]** (requested as "Weather & Moons"; renamed "Moons" pre-release — the [e] badge distinguishes it from moonwatch's "Moons" stream) — a graphical sky built on moonwatch.lic's output (`[k]-(152) [y]-(59) [x]+(88)`: K/Y/X = Katamba/Yavash/Xibar, `-(N)` rises in N, `+(N)` up and sets in N), as the official Experience #2; part of a larger analytics vision (sunrise/sunset, noon, weather). | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.15.1** (+ same-day live iteration with Sekmeht). A golden **Sun rides both arcs too**, anchored from community-observed rise/set epochs (the dr-scripts Firebase moonwatch itself polls) + DR's own sunrise/sunset prose (the same 11 patterns the script watches — works direct-SGE), persisted per-character. The sky is CONTINUOUS: elevation-blended gradients (bright noon → warm horizon glows → starry night), rise/set horizon-ring effects (epilepsy-safe gated), live "sets/rises in Nm" countdowns, an always-visible "moonwatch: Nm ago" data-age footer. Requires `;moonwatch window` on a Lich character for the moon feed (the empty state teaches this) — **moons stay stream-driven by design; a Firebase moon fallback was proposed and declined** (only the sun reads the community feed). Fantasy polish from Sekmeht's UX review: a two-ridge **horizon silhouette**, **hover lore-cards** on every body (the in-game moon descriptions + local-clock rise/set times), a footer **"next"** chip, and countdown-chip nudge-apart. ⚙ options are one per visual layer — The Sun / Living sky / Countdown labels / Name labels / Horizon silhouette / Rise & set effects (the original combined "Sun & sky" conflated hiding the sun with flattening the backdrop — split at Sekmeht's call); all persist per-instance like the Tableau's (YAML + Transfer). Beta badge like the Tableau. **Genie research: Genie has NO weather tracking** (only `$gametime` from the prompt, already parsed) — weather itself is the recorded Phase 2 (ambient-prose classification, DESIGN §34.9). | F47 | **Main-window right-click "Enable/Disable Timestamps"** — the main game stream should have a right-click toggle to prefix each line with a timestamp, like the side-stream panels already do. Off by default on a new character; persisted in the profile like the other stream settings. | Morress | **SHIPPED v0.14.4.** Reused the existing per-character `streamTimestamps` map + `toggleStreamTimestamp(id)` (scopedKey + `saveProfile`) that the side-stream panels use — keyed `'main'`. The main-window context menu ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) gained a "Enable Timestamps" / "Disable Timestamps" item (in the tail group, before Clear, mirroring StreamPanel's order), and the main `TextLineRow` now gets `showTimestamp={!!streamTimestamps['main']}` — `TextLineRow` already renders the `.ts-prefix` `[HH:MM]` from each line's `timestamp`, so no new rendering. **Off by default** (the map is empty on a new character → `'main'` is falsy) and **persisted per-character** to YAML via the existing toggle, and it rides Profile Transfer's Panel View Preferences category (`streamTimestamps` is already listed) — so it behaves exactly "like the other streams settings." No profile-shape change (just another key in an existing `Record`). |
| F46 | **Drag-to-reorder stream tabs** — in Windowed Panels (when unlocked) the tab strip's order should be draggable, not just rearrangeable via the Panel Manager's buttons. | Sekmeht (tester request) | **SHIPPED v0.13.5.** PanelFrame tabs are now `draggable` behind a `reorderTabs` prop: HTML5 drag with LIVE reorder — crossing the midpoint of a sibling commits the new order immediately (standard sortable midpoint rule with the from<target vacancy adjustment + a same-target bail so the strip doesn't jitter), and the dragged tab ghosts at 0.35 opacity (`.panel-tab--dragging`, opacity-only = theme-safe). **The reordered array flows through the SAME `onTabsChange` path that add/close already use, so persistence came free in BOTH modes** — zone scopedKeys in Static Panels, the floating window's `tabs` (→ `freeWindows` → YAML) in Windowed Panels; no new profile plumbing. Gating: windowed mode passes `!freeLayoutLocked` (Lock freezes layout, and tab order is layout — consistent with F44); static zones pass `true` (no lock concept; the Panel Manager's arrow buttons remain as the click alternative). **Snappy-feedback polish (same pass, Sekmeht):** the instant live-swap made the landing spot hard to track, so neighbor tabs now **FLIP-slide** into their new slots (~120ms ease-out — a `useLayoutEffect` remembers each tab's left edge by id, and on a mid-drag position change starts the tab at its old offset and releases it; gated on an active drag so add/close/tab-switch renders never animate; the dragged tab is excluded so its in-strip ghost snaps), and the ghost carries an accent dashed outline marking the landing slot. **Bug-check pass fixes (same day):** the FLIP measurement is now FULLY gated on a live drag — ungated, its `getBoundingClientRect` calls ran on every PanelFrame render (PanelFrame isn't memoized → every game batch × every zone), a forced-layout tax on the B172-cleaned hot path; the dragstart render seeds the baselines so the first swap still animates, and the position map clears when idle. Also `preventDefault` now covers the dragged tab itself AND the strip's empty space (list-level dragover), so the cursor reads "move" throughout instead of flashing no-drop. Scope note: this is reorder WITHIN one tab strip — dragging a tab BETWEEN floating windows is a separate future feature. No profile-shape change. |
| F45 | **Debug panel: "Export CSV"** — save the active Debug tab's data to a CSV file for offline analysis. | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.13.2.** A toolbar button next to Copy All exports the ACTIVE tab with a per-tab schema: Fires → `timestamp,kind,stream,rule,matched,detail` (ms-precision local timestamps); Events → `index,type,timestamp,data` (the event union is heterogeneous, so `data` is the event JSON minus `type` — every field preserved, json-parseable per row); Raw XML → `index,line`. RFC-4180 quoting, CRLF rows, default filename `lichborne-debug-{tab}-{character}-{stamp}.csv`. **Includes the OWASP formula-injection guard** (Sekmeht hit `=--- Lich: …` → `#NAME?` in Excel): any field starting with `=` `+` `-` `@` or tab gets a leading `'` so Excel renders it as text — also a real security measure, since game text is other-player-authored and a crafted `=cmd\|…` cell is an injection vector; strip the leading `'` when post-processing programmatically. Saving goes through a new **generic `save-text-file` IPC** ([main.ts](src/main/main.ts): `showSaveDialog` parented to the calling window + `writeFileSync`; preload `saveTextFile`) — reusable by any future "save this text" feature. Button shows "Saved ✓" for 2s on success; works in the docked strip, zone tabs, and floating Debug windows (same toolbar). |

Resize handles were already unrendered when locked; this closes the remaining visual affordance. |

Halve them. | TheTargonian (Rakkor) | **SHIPPED v0.13.1.** The reclaimable bulk was the tab's vertical padding — halved `5px → 2px` + a tight `line-height: 1.2`, keeping the label font (`0.93em`) legible. Reclaims ~6px per tab bar; applies to **both** Static Panels and Windowed Panels (windows host the same `PanelFrame`). | F42 | **Text Modification (Mutes & Substitutes)** — let players hide noise (ignore/gag) and rewrite text (substitute) natively, the way Wrayth/Genie/Frostbite all do. The single most-requested thing from people coming over. | Sekmeht (Wrayth/Genie/Frostbite testers) | **Phase 1 SHIPPED v0.12.0 — Mutes.** "Gag/Ignore" renamed **Mute** (friendlier) under a new **"Text Modification"** Automations tab. A mute suppresses any main-window line matching its pattern (text/phrase/regex), group-gated; **display-only — the Session Log keeps the raw line**. **Native, not delegated to Lich `textsubs.lic`** (decision 2026-06-08, Sekmeht; Binu concurred on placement): it's a display concern, must work without Lich (Principle #2), is portable via Profile Transfer, and gives the importer a clean target — and textsubs can't gag anyway. `MuteRule` ([mutes.ts](src/renderer/mutes.ts), `scopedKey('mutes')`, no profile-shape change); compiled+group-gated `activeMutesRef` filters `newMain` in GameWindow before commit; **transfers** via the new `textMod` category. **Import wired for all three clients** (the count-only "belongs in Lich" notices removed): Genie `#gag` (reuses `stripGenieSlashWrap`), Frostbite `ignores.ini` (new `[ignore]` parser + slot), Wrayth `<ignores>` → a Mutes preview tab. Verified end-to-end (all three import + functionally mute "has arrived!"). **Phase 2 SHIPPED v0.12.0 — Substitutes.** Rewrite matching text → a replacement with `$1`/`$&` capture refs (`SubstituteRule` [substitutes.ts](src/renderer/substitutes.ts), `applySubstitutesToSegments` per-segment after mutes/before highlights, display-only, native JS `$N`). Imported from Genie `#subs` (regex, `$N` kept) and Frostbite `substitutes.ini` (88 rules from Rakkor's storm gray; `\N`/`\0`→`$N`/`$&` with a regex-safe Qt unescape). **Restructured to two SEPARATE Automations tabs** — "Mutes" + "Substitutes" — instead of one nested "Text Modification" tab (Sekmeht, cleaner/flat like the other rule types); `MutePanel`/`SubstitutesPanel`, separate Profile-Transfer categories. Also added **right-click entries** (`Mute "word"`/`Mute this line`, `Substitute "word"`/`Substitute this line`, like Highlight/Trigger), the Mute **`scope`** (line vs match, like highlights) with **whitespace-gap tidy** on match-removal, and the CSS rebuilt to mirror HighlightsPanel exactly. **Phase 3 SHIPPED — stream scope:** mutes/subs apply **GLOBALLY by default** (the pass runs over `newMain` AND every `newStream[key]` buffer — Sekmeht's call, = Genie's global model) with an **optional per-rule "Apply to" stream restrict** (Frostbite's `target` model; `STREAM_OPTIONS`); Thoughts is NOT auto-skipped. **Feature complete** — a once-floated "push to textsubs.lic" export was dropped (Sekmeht, 2026-06-08): the native feature suffices and delegating re-imports the no-Lich/portability problems. DESIGN.md §31, CLAUDE.md Automations section. |
| F41 | **Richer character-tab right-click menu** — surface per-character actions (Reconnect, Disconnect, Open in New Window, Move to Main Window) on a tab right-click, showing only what's actually available. | Sekmeht | **SHIPPED v0.11.6.** The tab menu was decouple-only; now a context-aware action menu that lists **only actionable options** (no greyed rows): Reconnect (disconnected) XOR Disconnect (connected); Open in New Window (when >1 char shares this window); Move to Main Window (only in a decoupled/secondary window — `useRoster().isPrimary === false`). Close omitted (the tab's × covers it). Disconnect goes through `window.api.disconnect(sessionId)` directly so it works on ANY tab (the session-action bridge only reaches the active GameWindow). **Reconnect** ([App.tsx](src/renderer/App.tsx) `handleReconnectTab`) tears down the dead session then reuses the connect flow; it reconnects IN PLACE (GameWindow keyed by characterId → no remount → scrollback preserved). **Two fixes found in testing:** the reconnected tab stayed greyed + Lich bridge off (the `dropped` flag wasn't cleared — the 'Connected' event races ahead of the `sessionIdRef` update; fixed by resetting on the `sessionId` prop change — CLAUDE.md pitfall #69); and no connecting feedback (added a per-tab spinning ⟳, `prefers-reduced-motion`-aware, driven by an App `reconnectingIds` set). [CharacterTabBar.tsx](src/renderer/components/CharacterTabBar.tsx) + [AppBar.tsx](src/renderer/components/AppBar.tsx) + [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) + [character-tabs.css](src/renderer/styles/character-tabs.css). No profile-shape change. |

Session roster moved to main + broadcast (`session-roster`); decouple reassigns `ownerWindowId` (socket untouched). Three entry points: right-click tab, Window menu, Bulk Connect "separate windows" (persisted `_shared.yaml`, default off). **Cross-window Quick Send** targets the full roster. **Seamless takeover**: a moved window inherits scrollback + room/map + ALL vital bars via a render-only **replay** (main keeps a per-session snapshot of the latest sticky state + a scrollback buffer). **Close decoupled window = logout**; re-home via Window → "Move Character to Main Window". Last-character decouple guarded (greyed) at 3 levels. **Bugs found+fixed in testing:** vitals-at-100% restored blank (ring buffer dropped unchanged vitals → snapshot fix); fresh-connect login lines doubled (replay overlapped live → `replayTarget` gate + `holdingForReplay` hold-during-handoff); re-home-on-close vitals remount glitch (replaced with logout + explicit re-home). **Multiple exe instances left ungated on purpose** (isolated character sets). CLAUDE.md "Multi-window & decouple" + pitfalls #59/#60. **Pre-release sweep (3 more fixes):** Profile Transfer cross-window (a target open in another window was treated as inactive → silently overwritten by its owner window's save → now roster-based active detection + `session:reload` cross-window remount); cross-window theme sync (storage-event `initTheme`); native-menu Next/Prev/Close/Move enabled-state scoped to the focused window (was global). **Lich Scripts parser hardened:** the `;listall` parser required EVERY script name to match `[a-zA-Z0-9_-]`, so one unusually-named running script (e.g. with a dot) made the whole line fail to parse → the panel went stale and a running script (visible in `;listall`) never appeared (Sekmeht's `beakon`). Widened the name class to `[^\s,()]+` so one odd name can't blank the list; free-form Lich messages (which contain spaces) still don't match. If it recurs, capture the raw `--- Lich: …` line. **Open follow-up:** the residual symptom on a NON-moved character — suspected pre-existing `;listall` parser limitation (that character's routing is unchanged by multi-window), awaiting a repro `;listall` line. |
| F39 | **UI space optimization** — Lichborne shows ~3 fewer lines of main-window text than Frostbite at the same window size; reclaim the wasted vertical space in the top toolbar/status bar and the vitals strip. | TheTargonian | Side-by-side vs Frostbite: ~half an inch of main-window height lost. Two targets from the review — (1) **smaller vitals** with a numeric readout (gated behind a "Compact Vitals" option), and (2) the full-width **"Lichborne Connected" toolbar/status bar** could collapse into a menu or share a row. **Phase 1 SHIPPED v0.10.0**: opt-in **Compact Vitals** (Settings → Layout) — ~half-height bars, acronym labels (`H:`/`M:`/`C:`/`F:`/`S:`, and `IF:` for Barbarian "Inner Fire" — derived from the live label so any guild rename is covered). Per-character; rides the Display Transfer category. **Phase 2 SHIPPED v0.10.0** (2a/2b/2c): unified top app-bar ([AppBar.tsx](src/renderer/components/AppBar.tsx)) — brand + connection dot + character tabs + action buttons + Disconnect/Login in ONE app-level row; the per-session `game-toolbar` was removed (the reclaimed row). Built-out **native** menu (File · Edit · View · Tools · Lich · Window · Help, modeled on Genie/Frostbite — both put Connect/Disconnect in File); Lichborne menu items click-only (no new hotkeys); app-level buttons drive the active session via the `menu-action`/`lichborne:session-action` bridge. ModeSwitcher moved to the Icon Bar (per-session GroupsContext — CLAUDE.md pitfall #57). **Phase 3 (logo) DEFERRED.** **Deferred-polish follow-up DONE (v0.10.0):** per-button active-state glow (active session's open panels surfaced via `SessionStatus.panel*` → AppBar `btn-*--active`); a static **"More ⋯"** dropdown for the less-used buttons (Debug/Logs/Contacts/Theme) that handles narrow windows; vestigial `status`/`scriptPalette` cleanup. Menu tree + app-bar design recorded in CLAUDE.md (top-chrome section + pitfall #57). |
| F38 | **Platform-wide profile Export/Import ("Transfer")** — capture a character's FULL setup (display/accessibility settings, panel layout & sizes & added streams, view prefs, theme, and all automations) into a portable file and fan the import out to many already-added characters at once. | JadedSoul | **SHIPPED v0.10.0.** JadedSoul has a couple dozen characters and wanted to configure one nicely then propagate the whole setup. The Automations export (F29) only covered rules + a layout snapshot and imported into the current character only. New separate feature: Launcher → **Transfer** button (one modal, Export / Import tabs). Export picks ANY source character (reads its YAML, works disconnected); Import lists `.lb.yaml` files from the new `Exports/` folder (sibling of `profiles/`), picks categories, and applies to multiple targets via checkboxes. Driven by an explicit allowlist registry (`TRANSFER_CATEGORIES` in [profileTransfer.ts](src/renderer/profileTransfer.ts)). **Non-destructive guarantee**: import writes ONLY into the target's `state` map (+ `theme`), so identity/launcher fields (guild, name, account, game, favorite, …) are structurally untouched. **Active-target apply**: writes the localStorage working copy + remounts the session (focused OR backgrounded, via a per-session reload nonce in App) so a live import is committed and survives logout. See CLAUDE.md pitfall #56. **Consolidation**: the redundant Automations **Export** button and the ImportWizard **"Lichborne"** card were removed — Transfer is now the single Lichborne↔Lichborne path; the Automations Import button is relabeled "Import from another client…" (Wrayth/Genie/Frostbite only). Theme-share and Session-Log export are untouched. |

Edited regardless of which character is logged in; runtime merges global + per-character before passing to engines. | Binu | Requested by Binu (has come up multiple times across recent versions). Cross-character data already has a precedent in `_shared.yaml` (account, lich settings, custom themes), so the storage model is established — extend `SharedProfile` with `sharedHighlights`, `sharedTriggers`, `sharedMacros`, `sharedAliases`, and `sharedContacts` arrays. **Planned design (Sekmeht, 2026-05-31)**: option (A) from the design discussion — new "Global" tab/section in the Automations panel that's always editable regardless of active character; rules stored separately in `_shared.yaml` rather than per-rule `scope: 'global' \| 'character'` toggle, because the mental model is cleaner ("these rules apply to everyone, those are this character's") and prevents the upgrade-a-rule-to-global UI footgun where a user accidentally promotes a character-specific rule to all characters. Runtime merge: per-character `useHighlights` / `useTriggers` / etc. hooks load both global + character lists and concatenate before the engine compile step — no engine changes needed. **Scope decisions**: groups / modes stay per-character (they're per-character workflow concepts; global rules are always-active, no group gating). Global rules NOT included in F29 Lichborne→Lichborne exports by default (they're intentionally not character-bound, so they shouldn't ride along with a character-specific export). Same dedup rules between globals and character rules — both fire; priority and line-order rules already handle in-list duplicates. **SHIPPED v0.15.2 for highlights / triggers / macros / aliases** exactly per the planned design (option A: an Automations scope switch "This Character \| All Characters"; separate store; always-active; runtime concat character-first; `_shared.yaml` via new `sharedHighlights/Triggers/Macros/Aliases` fields; excluded from character exports). Implementation detail: globals live under a virtual `_global` character scope so the existing panels/stores work unchanged (DESIGN §39). **Global CONTACTS deliberately deferred** — contacts are runtime-mutated by presence tracking (lastSeen/timeSpent buffers), so a shared store would race across windows/sessions; needs its own design. ~~Global mutes/substitutes: recorded follow-up.~~ **Mutes & Substitutes joined the global system same release** (Sekmeht's ask): full parity — stores, merges, "Applies to" moves, Transfer; and the scope switch now renders on every tab (greyed on Groups with a why-tooltip) so the controls never shift position (the switch moved from the header band onto the sub-bar in v0.19.8, F119, under an "Applies to" label — it is disabled on two tabs, which makes it per-tab by definition). Known v1 quirk: analytics badges in the Global-scope editor show no counts (fires are recorded per character). |

### Death XML Reference (captured 2026-05-08)

Key XML sequences from a live death event, for use when implementing F10/F11:

**Death state indicators (on kill):**
```xml
<indicator id="IconKNEELING" visible="n"/>
<indicator id="IconPRONE" visible="y"/>
<indicator id="IconSTANDING" visible="n"/>
<indicator id='IconBLEEDING' visible='n'/>
<indicator id='IconDEAD' visible='y'/>
```

**Rank gain in exp component (F10):**
```xml
<component id='exp Crossbow'><b> Crossbow: 991 00% dabbling </b></component>
You've gained a new rank in using crossbows.
```

**Death's Sting rexp sequence (F11) — fires on EVERY exp update batch while active, not just once:**
```xml
<component id='exp rexp'>Rested EXP Stored: 3:56 hours Usable This Cycle: none Cycle Refreshes: 3:13 hours</component>
<component id='exp rexp'>[Because of Death's Sting, your rested exp is currently not being used.]</component>
```
The cycle refreshes timer counts down live (3:15 → 3:14 → 3:13 ...) in the first component.
When Death's Sting is no longer active, the second `exp rexp` component stops appearing.
Detection: if `skill === 'rexp'` and text starts with `[Because of Death's Sting` → set `deathsStingActive = true`; if `skill === 'rexp'` and text does NOT contain Death's Sting → clear it.

Partial reduction (alyssum tea, BOOST DEATH) produces plain text in main stream but does NOT change the rexp component — the Death's Sting message continues firing on every exp batch as long as any affliction remains:
```
You feel the weakness imparted by your recent death weaken, though some affliction still remains.
That was the last dose. You discard the remains of the alyssum tea.
```
**Full clearance confirmed.** The second `exp rexp` component simply stops appearing — no special clearance tag or message in the component stream. The plain text clear message appears in the main stream immediately before the next exp batch without it:
```
You feel the weakness imparted by your recent death slowly fade away.
```
Then the very next exp batch:
```xml
<component id='exp rexp'>Rested EXP Stored: 3:56 hours Usable This Cycle: none Cycle Refreshes: 3:06 hours</component>
<component id='exp tdp'> TDPs: 59864</component>
```
No second `exp rexp`. The `<pushBold/><popBold/>` pair that always trailed active batches is also absent after clearance.

**Final detection logic:**
- Active: `skill === 'rexp'` and text starts with `[Because of Death's Sting` → `deathsStingActive = true`
- Cleared: `skill === 'rexp'` and text does NOT start with `[Because` (i.e. normal rested exp summary) → `deathsStingActive = false`

**Death's Sting also appears in bold in the EXP command output (plain text, main stream, mono mode):**
```xml
<pushBold/>[Because of Death's Sting, your rested exp is currently not being used.]<popBold/>
```

**Body decay timer (plain text in main stream, sent once on death):**
```
Your body will decay beyond its ability to hold your soul in 250 minutes.
```

**Resurrection — indicators clear, spell/bleed reset, then room nav fires:**
```xml
<spell>None</spell>
<indicator id='IconBLEEDING' visible='n'/>
<indicator id='IconDEAD' visible='n'/>
... resurrection narrative text ...
<nav/>
<streamWindow id='main' title='Story' subtitle=" - [Temple of Light, Alcove of Eylhaar] (2012119)" .../>
```

**Vitals on resurrection (all bars reset to ~10%, recover over time):**
```xml
<progressBar id='health' value='10' text='health 10%' .../>
<progressBar id='mana' value='10' text='mana 10%' .../>
<progressBar id='spirit' value='10' text='spirit 10%' .../>
<progressBar id='stamina' value='10' text='fatigue 10%' .../>
<progressBar id='concentration' value='10' text='concentration 10%' .../>
```

## Resolved

| # | Summary | Resolved In | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| B475 | **Contacts → Templates: with many templates the list squashed every card flat, including the one being edited — Save unreachable** | v0.20.0 | Reported by a tester through Sekmeht (screenshot: ~30 templates, each a sliver, the open editor cut off mid-form). `.cp-tpl-list` was a flex COLUMN and each `.cp-tpl-row` carried `overflow: hidden`. A flex item's automatic minimum height applies only when its overflow is VISIBLE, so every row was allowed to shrink to fit the list instead of the list scrolling (pitfall #163). **Fixed by a rework rather than a patch** (Sekmeht: make Templates behave like Contacts): the tab now uses the Contacts tab's own two-pane layout and classes — a sidebar with + New, search, and a block list that scrolls, beside a detail pane whose `.cp-form` scrolls the editor. Row ✕ delete (not on the built-in Friends/Enemies), ↑/↓ list navigation, and the search box's Esc/↓ behave exactly as they do for contacts. |
| B474 | **An unknown trigger action type crashed the whole Triggers list** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.20.0 | The rule list's action badges did `ACTION_LABELS[a.type].split(' ')` with no fallback, so a trigger carrying an action type this build doesn't know — a hand-edited profile, or one saved by a newer build — threw during render and took the list down. Falls back to `📋 ?` now. |
| B473 | **A trigger Echo to the Game window never displayed — nor did an echo to a stream with no open panel** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.20.0 | `echoToStream` wrote every echo into `streamLines[key]`. Nothing renders `streamLines.main` (the main window renders `lines`), and a stream with no watching panel is buffered where no one looks — pitfall #133's shape. So "Echo → Game window" silently did nothing. **Fix:** `main` and any unwatched stream go to `lines`, trimmed through `appendTrimmed` only while pinned (pitfall #81) and otherwise counted into the new-lines badge; `lastMainLineRef` is nulled so the next prompt shows (#88). **Deferred one tick**, because a trigger fires inside the event loop before the batch appends its own lines, so an immediate append landed ABOVE the line that fired it. Known gap: these echoes are not written to the Session Log. |
| B472 | **A team login could hang forever on one character — no tab, no error, and the launcher showing it as logged in** | v0.20.0 | Sekmeht's second team character never appeared; the + window showed him struck through. `SGEConnection`'s reads checked their deadline only when DATA ARRIVED (`readUntilNewline`/`readBytes` re-tested elapsed time inside the data handler), so a login server that went silent mid-handshake left `login()` pending indefinitely — holding the account slot, which is why the launcher read him as on. **Fix:** one `waitFor(take, what)` primitive that tries `take()` first, then runs a REAL timer and settles on `close`/`error`; `readBytes` and `readRaw` are built on it and `readUntilNewline` is gone. A mutation run of the harness against the old code reproduced the hang (pitfall #162). |
| B471 | **Profile Transfer silently skipped incoming rules that clashed with a different rule** | v0.20.0 | `dropCollisions` dropped any incoming rule whose identity key matched an existing one, whether or not the content matched, and said nothing. Through the same `trKey` bug as B470, every variable-change trigger after the first was skipped. Keeping the destination's rule is still correct (Transfer never overwrites), but the loss is now REPORTED: each skip becomes a `RuleClash` in `TargetResult.clashes`, listed in the modal (de-duplicated, amber, bounded list). Triggers compare in `triggerCompareForm` so a rule saved before a field existed isn't reported as different. |
| B470 | **"Applies to" → All Characters made a trigger appear to vanish, and deleting the "blank" one deleted a different trigger** | v0.20.0 | Sekmeht. Two causes. **(1) Identity:** `trKey` was pattern-only, and a Variable-change trigger's pattern is `''`, so every variable trigger was "the same rule" as every other one and as any blank text trigger. The move removes the source when the target "already has it", so moving one could silently delete another. Variable triggers now key on `var:<watched variable>`. **(2) Collision is not duplication:** even with the right key, a matching key only proves two rules collide. The move now asks `sameRuleContent` (canonical whole-record compare, defaults filled first); an exact twin merges, a different rule sharing the key REFUSES the move with a toast. **UX:** the panel now follows the rule to its new scope and reopens it, instead of leaving you on a list it just left. All six editors move the FINALIZED draft and disable "Applies to" while it can't be saved. |
| B469 | **Genie trigger import lost Genie's send timing — `#send 2 look` imported as the command "2 look", and `#send clear` became a game command** | v0.20.0 | Verified against Genie4's Command.cs. The send family differs in TIMING: `#put` and a bare command send at once, `#send` waits for roundtime, `#do` waits out roundtime, stun and webbing, and the queue forms take a leading pause in SECONDS. The importer treated all of them as plain commands, so the pause stayed in the text. Queue CONTROL (`#send/#do/#queue clear`, and `#queue` with one word, which LISTS the queue) imported as commands that would have sent "clear" or "look" to DragonRealms. Now: the pause becomes `delayMs`, `waitForRt` follows the verb, control forms are dropped as unsupported, and `#do` imports with a note that its stun/web wait isn't modelled. |
| B468 | **A trigger watching `rt` or `ct` never fired when roundtime ended** *(audit-found)* | v0.20.0 | The variables were reported only when a roundtime STARTED (the `<roundTime>` tag), and nothing reported the end, so "act when roundtime is over" could not be written. `noteTimer` now arms a timer that reports `0` at expiry. That end event reaches ONLY rules with a condition on the variable (`onlyGated`): sent to every watcher, an unconditioned `rt` trigger fired twice a round. `$rt`/`$ct` are now live seconds derived from the expiry. |
| B467 | **Triggers ignored roundtime — their commands were sent the instant the line appeared** | v0.20.0 | Sekmeht: *"triggers do not wait for roundtime, instead they fire immediately."* In combat the game discards a command sent during roundtime, so a trigger's command usually did nothing. Resolved by F120: command actions wait for roundtime by default (DESIGN §14.10c). |
| B466 | **`;listall` polled every 5s for a panel that was not on screen** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.19.9 | `lichScriptsOpen` tested tab EXISTENCE (`w.tabs.some(t => t.id === 'lichScripts')`), not whether the tab was SHOWING. Only a surface's ACTIVE tab is rendered, so a `lichScripts` tab sitting inactive in any window meant the character kept injecting `;listall` every 5 seconds, forever, to feed a `ScriptListPanel` that was never mounted. Both of the reporter's connected characters were in exactly that state. **This is the same mistake B307 fixed for the AI stream** (pitfall #133b: existence is not visibility). **Two costs, and the second is the one that matters:** `useLichBridge` lives at GameWindow scope, so the poll's `setPending` and the reply's `setScripts` + `setLastUpdated` each re-rendered the WHOLE GameWindow - about 4 full renders per 5s per character, through unmemoized `WindowLayer`/`FloatingWindow`/`PanelFrame` before the memoized leaves could bail. And anything Lichborne injects is byte-identical to the player typing it, so a script with an upstream hook sees it (pitfall #76 - the reason this gate exists at all), meaning an invisible-panel poll is pure cost on both axes. **Fix:** ONE shared `visibleTabIds` memo now answers 'which tabs are on screen', consumed by both this gate and `activeIdsRef` - which already implemented the rule correctly and cited B307, so the two were answering the same question differently (pitfall #127). Also: an unchanged `;listall` reply no longer mints a fresh array (`lastSigRef` signature compare), while `lastUpdated` still advances on EVERY reply, because the panel's 'updated Ns ago' footer reports when the feed last ANSWERED, not when it last differed - gating that would make a live poll read '5m ago', the mistake the Spell Monitor's feed readout documents. |
| B465 | **Every Spell Monitor bar was in a permanent, never-completing transform transition** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.19.9 | `.sm-bar-fill` carries `transition: transform 1s linear`, and the component re-renders on a **1 Hz** clock writing `scaleX(<unrounded float>)`. The transition duration exactly equalled the tick period, so each bar's transition was re-armed at the precise instant the previous one would have completed - **every bar animated continuously for as long as the window was shown**, including a 29-minute buff whose bar moves imperceptibly. DESIGN §45.10 had explicitly logged this as the unmeasured axis: *'the compositing of N permanently-transitioning bars - that is the axis to measure if a big buff stack is ever reported as heavy.'* **Fix:** round the scale to 3dp, so a bar only re-animates when it moves by 0.1% of its width (sub-pixel on any real panel). An identical value now produces an identical style string and no transition fires at all. Note `Math.max`-style clamping would not have helped; the fix is that the VALUE stops changing, not that the duration changes. |
| B464 | **The Moons sky sat in a permanent opacity transition, day and night, regardless of every setting** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.19.9 | `.moons-layer` (four `position:absolute; inset:0` full-panel gradient layers - night/day/zenith/twilight) carried `transition: opacity 2.5s linear`, while the scene ticks every **2s**. A 2.5s transition re-armed every 2s **never completes**, so four panel-sized layers were permanently transitioning for the entire session - unconditional, unaffected by weather, time of day, or any ⚙ layer, and not covered by the minimize-pause because [global.css](src/renderer/styles/global.css) pauses `animation-play-state` only and states that *'transitions are one-shot and already over'* (untrue here and for B465). **Root cause is pitfall #126 exactly:** the CSS comment justified the 2.5s duration as smoothing *'the 30s tick steps'*, which was true when written and silently invalidated when v0.17.0 dropped the tick 30s→2s for smoother body motion. The tick's OWN comment also still said 30s. Both corrected. **Fix, in two layers:** the driving weights (`wDay`/`wZen`/`wTwi`, continuous floats off sun elevation, so different on every tick) are quantised to 2dp, which means the value only changes when it changes visibly - rarely, for a sun crossing the sky over real hours - so the transition fires, completes and stops; and the duration is cut to 1.5s so it stays below the tick period even when a value does change on consecutive ticks. |
| B463 | **The Lich map database and Genie zones were parsed once PER CHARACTER, not once per app** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.19.9 | Found while investigating a report that the client felt heavier than remembered. `MapPanel` held `lichDb` + four indexes and `genieZones` in per-COMPONENT state, and MapPanel is per-character with TWO mount sites (the panel tab via PanelFrame, and the Maps overlay). Measured on the reporter's install: **14.7 MB of Lich map JSON (~52k rooms) + a 12.3 MB Genie cache**, so a four-character session showing maps could hold eight copies of ~27 MB of source before the 2-4x JSON-to-heap expansion. Worse in Windowed Panels mode, where a map window never unmounts, so nothing is ever reclaimed. **Fix:** module-level caches keyed by SOURCE PATH, with an in-flight promise so two panels mounting in the same frame (connecting a second character, or opening the overlay over a panel) parse once rather than twice. A rejection is never memoized. **What is deliberately NOT shared:** every per-character value stays component state - `currentRoom`, the Genie breadcrumb `geniePersistRef` (whose own comment forbids hoisting), zoom, view mode. The rule the fix encodes: share the WORLD, never the SESSION (pitfall #6 stays intact). A new map file misses naturally because repository.lic writes a timestamped path; the version-change reload and the manual reload button both pass `force` to bust the cache explicitly. **tsc caught a real defect mid-fix:** the manual reload button was wired `onClick={loadLichDb}`, so React would have passed its MouseEvent as the new `force` parameter - and without an explicit force the button would have served the cache and silently done nothing. |
| B462 | **The Lich map tile cache never evicted anything** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.19.9 | `imageCache` in [MapImageView.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/MapImageView.tsx) was a `Map` with only `.has`/`.get`/`.set` - no delete, no clear, no cap - holding one base64 data URL per map tile ever viewed. Measured: **273 tiles, 26.9 MB on disk, ~35.7 MB once base64'd** (base64 is 4/3 of binary). Dates to **v0.3.0**, so it is not a recent regression. Two things made it bite harder than it reads: in Windowed Panels mode a map window never unmounts, so the Static-Panels mitigation (PanelFrame renders only the active tab, so switching away drops the cache) does not apply; and each entry costs a DECODED bitmap in compositor memory on top of the JS string, which is why the renderer AND the GPU process were both observed climbing while travelling (~1.8 and ~0.8 MB/min on a live two-character session). **Fix:** a real LRU bounded at 30 tiles, using the fact that JS `Map` preserves insertion order - a hit re-inserts to refresh recency, a write evicts from the front past the cap. 30 is far more than a travel path revisits, so the hit rate is unchanged; a miss costs one IPC read that already has a loading state. |
| B461 | **The per-stream line cap inverted into UNBOUNDED growth at exactly the cap** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.19.9 | `next[key] = [...base.slice(-(MAX_STREAM_LINES - lines.length)), ...lines]`. When one coalesced batch carries **exactly 500** lines for a stream, `MAX_STREAM_LINES - lines.length` is `0`, the negation makes it **`-0`**, and `-0 === 0` - so `slice(-0)` is `slice(0)`, which returns the WHOLE array instead of none of it, and then appends 500 more. **It compounds:** verified by running it, 500 -> 1000 -> 1500 -> 2000 on repeated full batches. A batch larger than 500 overshot the other way (the keep-count went negative, dropping from the front and still growing). Needs a 16ms batch carrying exactly 500 lines for one stream, so it is rare in practice - but it is unbounded growth in the one structure the docs cite as bounded, which is exactly the kind of claim an audit rests on. **Fix:** trim the JOINED result (`[...base, ...lines].slice(-MAX_STREAM_LINES)`) rather than the base by a computed keep-count. Correct for every batch size and structurally immune to the same mistake. New pitfall #158. |
| B460 | **The Lich Dashboard's session pill rendered as a rounded rectangle instead of a capsule** | v0.19.8 | Found by the alignment audit during the F119 header work; pre-existing, never reported. `.ld-session-pill` pinned no `line-height`, so it inherited body's 1.5 and stood **23.28px tall around 11.52px of text** — 33% taller than its own content (UX #7 / pitfall #77). The height was the boring half. The visible half is that **`border-radius` clamps to HALF the box height**, so `border-radius: 10px` stops clamping once the box passes ~20px: the capsule quietly became a rounded rectangle sitting beside genuinely-capsule tab chips. Fixed with `line-height: 1` (17.52px tall, radius clamps at 8.76px, capsule restored). **The text was always correctly centred** — an auto-height pill grows rather than overflowing, so this is NOT pitfall #75b's "text sits high" case, which needs a fixed-height container. That is precisely why it survived review for so long: nothing looked misaligned, one chip in a row was just slightly boxier than its neighbours. |
| B459 | **A printable key typed while an app-level dialog was open focused the command bar underneath it** | v0.19.8 | Found auditing B449's own fix while writing it up. F60's type-anywhere handler ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) carries the same guard as the macro site and had the same blind spot: `anyModalOpenRef` sees only the eleven overlays GameWindow renders itself, so with About, Attach, Quick Send, Transfer, Team Login, a wizard or any confirm open, a printable key with focus on a plain `<button>` (the normal state for a confirm, which focuses Cancel) focused `.command-input` behind the dialog. The `inEditable` check covers a dialog's text fields, which is why it never showed while typing INTO one. The handler's own comment states the intent — "typing must not land in a bar hidden behind Settings" — so this was the rule failing on the dialogs it could not enumerate, not a missing rule. Fixed the same way as B449: ask `anyDialogOpen()` too. Same class as B375/B448 — an invisible input that still takes keystrokes. |
| B446 | **The selected rule was invisible in five editors, and hovering a row was LOUDER than selecting it** | v0.19.8 | v0.19.8 UI/UX sweep. Highlights, Triggers, Macros/Aliases, Groups/Modes and Lich Scripts all used `background: var(--bg-sunken)` as the ONLY selection cue. `--bg-sunken` is a NEIGHBOUR of the dialog surface, not a contrast to it: `--modal-bg` is `var(--bg-base)`, so on Classic Light the selected row was #fafafa on #ffffff (effectively unpainted) while `--bg-hover` is #e8e8e8, making a merely-hovered row more prominent than the selected one. The hierarchy was inverted on ALL 20 themes (worst on Classic, Terminal, Classic Light, Ivory), in the rule lists that ARE the navigation of those dialogs. Contacts had already been fixed with an accent tint and even carried a comment about this exact trap -- the pitfall #113 shape, where one prefix gets the fix and its siblings do not. ONE rule in global.css now names all six selectors. |
| B447 | **The Launcher's Connect button washed out on hover, on every dark-accent theme** | v0.19.8 | `.launcher-card-connect` is `background: var(--accent-dim); color: #fff` hovering to the brighter `var(--accent)` -- the identical recipe to `.btn-primary` minus the text halo whose own comment says it is what makes white read on BOTH. Measured white-on-accent: **1.37 on Terminal**, 1.93 Darker, 2.30 Dark/Cleric, 2.45 Empath, 2.53 Slate, 2.77 Classic. Light themes are safe (12.4), which is why a light-theme eyeball never caught it -- and this is the most-clicked control in the app. Halo added with the cross-reference. |
| B448 | **Switching character while the Overview was open put the caret in an invisible command bar** | v0.19.8 | Clicking a card, clicking a tab, or Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+1-9 focused `.session-shell:not(--hidden) .command-input`, and the Overview deliberately leaves the active shell laid out so that selector still finds it. Typing showed nothing and **Enter still reached the game**. The effect was a VERBATIM copy of `refocusActiveCommandBar` minus its Overview guard, comment and all -- a third copy, with the guarded helper a few hundred lines above (pitfall #131; the B375 failure through a path that fix missed). Fixed by deleting the copy and calling the helper (pitfall #127). |
| B449 | **Macros and mode hotkeys fired straight through every app-level dialog** | v0.19.8 | `anyModalOpenRef` is a hand-written OR of ELEVEN GameWindow-owned overlays, so About, Quick Send, Profile Transfer, Team Login, Attach, both wizards, the + tab's Connect modal and every confirm were structurally invisible to it (pitfall #57's converse). Bare keys DO match a macro (`comboMods` returns [] with no modifiers) and the F56 numpad seed binds exactly those -- so typing a port number into Attach walked your character, and `preventDefault` meant the digit never reached the field. The ref's own comment already recorded this list rotting once. Fixed by asking the Esc stack instead: a new `anyDialogOpen()` reuses the same liveness test Esc uses, so a future dialog is covered with no list to maintain (pitfall #130). Applied to the macro guard and to Ctrl+F. |
| B450 | **Two popovers could open off the edge of the screen** | v0.19.8 | **The panel + menu** always opened UPWARD with `maxHeight: Math.max(120, Math.min(240, rect.top - 12))`, and that floor defeats the clamp it wraps: a tab strip near the top of the game area (the Main-Top zone, or a floating window snapped up) has `rect.top` about 34, forcing a 120px box whose top edge sits near **-82px**, so clicking + gave a sliver showing one row. It now flips downward when there is more room below and caps to the side it uses. **The colour suggestion list** (new F115 work) estimated its height (`8 * 28 + 10`) and on its flip-above branch set only `bottom` with no cap, so in a short window a field at mid-height anchored the list past the top of the viewport where no scrolling reaches the first rows -- the exact failure `placeMenu` was rebuilt for thirty lines below it (B429). It now measures the rendered list and shifts it onto the screen. |
| B451 | **Line height Compact did nothing -- the shipped DEFAULT was silently clamped** | v0.19.8 | `.text-line` floored every row at `min-height: 1.4em` while `DEFAULT_SETTINGS.lineHeight` is **1.2**, so the default setting rendered identically to 1.4 and the real ladder was 1.4 / 1.5 / 1.8 / 2.0. On a 40-row window that is about 96px -- roughly six lines of game text, the same order as the whole v0.10.0 chrome reclaim -- on the surface people spend all their time looking at. The floor exists so a BLANK line still holds one row, which is right; expressing it as a literal was not. The Overview's copy of the same rule already had the correct `calc(1em * var(--game-line-height, 1.4))` with a comment saying it must be in the row's own metric (B277), so two surfaces rendering the same component disagreed. |
| B452 | **Settings to Line height never reached the Exp, Injuries or Lich Scripts panels** | v0.19.8 | Those three (plus the compact Exp view) anchored their font-size but not their line-height, so they inherited `body { line-height: 1.5 }`. At the default 1.2 their rows sat about 25% taller than the stream panel beside them, Compact and Double did nothing in them, and **Large Print (which forces 1.8) never reached them either** -- an accessibility miss rather than a cosmetic one. DESIGN explicitly claimed these panels DID follow the setting; the code won (Iron rule), and the code now matches what the doc said. |
| B453 | **A wording and polish batch from the same sweep** | v0.19.8 | **The verb on the one action that destroys hundreds of rules was the gentle one.** Automation Analytics' bulk actions said *Remove duplicate copies* / *Remove obsolete rules* while deleting ONE of those same rules two inches away says *Delete* -- and the bulk confirm's own detail reads "This can't be undone here". Per DESIGN 43.7 Remove is for something that can be put back; both are now Delete. **Team Login's result footer said Done** (43.7: a plain dismiss is Close or Cancel, never Done) -- now Close. **The Debug panel's Close hovered destructive-RED** where every other close hovers neutral, teaching that closing is dangerous on a panel people open and close constantly -- now neutral. **The Lich Map's search results painted hover and selected with one shared declaration**, so which result was selected was not communicated at all and hovering any row made it look selected (pitfall #107's second clause) -- split, selection on an accent tint. **`.vital-text` had no `tabular-nums`**, so on the sans-serif and serif font options the most constantly-ticking number in the client jittered its own label on every update. |
| B444 | **A painted effect went patchy wherever a line was split into runs — highlights as well as echoes** | v0.19.8 | The real fix behind B443. The six clip-text effects paint a gradient, and **a background belongs to one element**, so a line split by a word highlight or a contact name gave every run its own copy scaled to its own width: a short word squeezed the whole ramp into a few characters while a long run spread it out. **Fix:** every run of a split line is given the SAME gradient geometry and offset into it by its own position, so the pieces reassemble into one continuous effect — `--fx-bg-size` (twice the LINE width), `--fx-bg-x` (this run's start, negated) and `--fx-sweep-to`, all in `ch`, which is exact because the game font is monospace by design. The sweep keyframe moves each run BY its own gradient width from wherever it starts (`calc(var(--fx-bg-x) + var(--fx-sweep-to))`) rather than to a fixed 200%, so the runs stay in lockstep instead of converging. **Containment is the point:** the vars are set ONLY on runs of a split line, and the CSS falls back to the original per-element values when they are unset — so a whole line, a contact, and the app-bar wordmark (which is NOT the monospace game font, and would have been broken by a global `ch` change) render byte-identically to before. **Fire is excluded** — its gradient is vertical (`0deg`, `auto 220%`), so a horizontal split never affected it. A word highlight carrying its OWN effect is also excluded: that is a deliberate island and its own gradient is correct; only a run that INHERITED the line's effect shares the geometry (`fxFromLine`). Verifiable without Electron: `tmp-hl-harness/gradient/page.cjs` writes a before/after `index.html` openable in any browser (pitfall #154 made the usual screenshot route impossible). |
| B445 | **Gold, Rainbow, Fire and Frost ignore the colour you pick, and nothing said so** | v0.19.8 | Their gradients are hardcoded; only Shimmer and Gradient read `--fx-c1`/`--fx-c2`, and Glow uses the colour for its shadow. So a colour set beside one of those four did nothing, which reads as a broken colour field — Sekmeht set a `#FFFF00` echo and got gold. **Deliberately NOT fixed by teaching them to tint:** the palette IS the effect, and a blue Gold or a green Fire is not the thing you asked for. Fixed by making the UI honest instead (UX #8): `effectColorNote` in highlights.ts returns one sentence for the four, rendered under the effect select in all three editors that offer effects — Highlights, contact templates and the trigger echo — so they cannot word it differently or be fixed in only one place (pitfall #127). The colour field stays visible and editable, because it goes live again the moment the effect is switched back to None. |
| B443 | **A trigger echo's Shimmer/Gold/Rainbow rendered patchy, and bold looked like it switched the effect off** | v0.19.8 | Sekmeht: *"I've seen shimmer apply to random places. Bold working or not working… adding bold disabled the shimmer effect, but now shimmer applies spotty through the text."* Three symptoms, ONE cause. The six `colorReplacing` effects (Shimmer, Rainbow, Gold, Gradient, Fire, Frost) clip a gradient to the glyphs, and **a background belongs to one element** — so every run a match highlight or a contact creates gets its OWN copy of that gradient, scaled to its own width, with its own animation. A seven-character run squeezes the whole dark-bright-dark ramp into seven characters while a forty-character run spreads it out. Hence "patchy", hence "random places" — and hence bold seeming to disable it, because bold only changed the glyph widths, so a short run landed on a flat stretch of its own gradient. Confirmed by dumping the real markup: with no other rules the line is ONE `.hl-fx-gold` span; add a highlight on one word and it becomes three, each with its own gradient. **Fix:** a line the user AUTHORED and gave a painted effect renders as a single run (`splitRuns` in `renderHighlightedLine`).
