Scoped tightly — `o.echo` means the style was written for this exact line, `colorReplacing` means it is one of the six that actually break — so colour/background/bold echoes and the non-painted effects (glow, pulse, neon, wave, bounce) keep full highlight and contact compositing. Cost, accepted: a word highlight or contact name inside a painted echo is no longer separately coloured, which is exactly the overlap that was breaking it. **SUPERSEDED within the same release by B444**, which fixed the cause properly (one line-wide gradient), so the no-split rule was dropped and painted echoes get word highlights and contacts back — strictly better than the stopgap. The diagnosis below is what B444 was built from. **Originally not fixed here: the same bug on a line-scope HIGHLIGHT over game text** (B444) — same cause, but the fix is a shared-CSS rework and this environment cannot render to verify it. Pre-existing from B428; F118 made it easy to reach. 5 harness cases; one earlier case that had encoded the old splitting for a painted effect was updated, which is the harness doing its job. |
| B439 | **"Remove duplicate copies" could delete genuinely different triggers** | v0.19.8 | Found by the F118 bug check; PRE-EXISTING and unrelated to the feature that surfaced it. `actionSig` ([automationHealth.ts](src/renderer/automationHealth.ts)) built a trigger's action signature from a hand-written list of SIX fields — type, command, echoMessage, varName/varValue and the two sound fields. Every other field was invisible to it, so two triggers differing only in an echo's **stream** or colour, a **notify title**, a **webhook URL**, a **log file** or a **delay** hashed identically. That key feeds `duplicateGroups`, and Analytics' **Remove duplicate copies** does `duplicateGroups.flatMap(g => g.slice(1))` — so it deleted all but the first of a set of triggers that were not duplicates at all. Echoing the same message to two different panels was enough to trigger it. **Fix:** derive the signature from the action's own fields (all but `id`, falsy values dropped so "unset" and "explicitly off" agree, keys sorted so property order can't matter) instead of naming them. F118's four new echo fields would have been four more blind spots; deriving means the next field added is covered for free. Erring toward MORE distinct signatures is the safe direction — a missed duplicate costs a stale report row, a false one costs the user a rule. Same family as B430's analytics blindness (v0.19.8), which was the HIGHLIGHT-side twin of this. |
| B440 | **A trigger echo's colour reached CSS un-normalized, so a colour NAME painted the wrong colour — or none** | v0.19.8 | F118 bug check; introduced by F118 and fixed before release. The echoed line's style hint carried `echoColor` RAW while the segment's `fg` carried it through `colorHex`, and the line layer OVERRIDES the segment (`renderSegment`: `overrideColor ?? seg.fg`) — so the un-normalized value won and the good one was unreachable. Three manifestations, all needing only that the echo ALSO set bold/background/effect (which is what emits a hint): a **curated name** painted the CSS colour instead of Lichborne's (`green` → `#008000`, not the curated `#4caf50`; `lime`, `purple`, `teal`, `yellow` and `red` all diverge), one of **your own palette names** is not valid CSS so the echo lost its colour entirely, and a stored **link** stayed live in the layer while `fg` snapshotted — so ticking Bold silently changed whether an already-printed echo re-colours when you later edit that palette entry. Reachable without hand-editing, because the colour box documents that it takes a `$variable` and a variable can hold a name. **Fix:** `echoLineStyle` resolves every colour through the same `colorHex` the segment uses, so both pipelines agree on the value AND on an echo being a snapshot (DESIGN §49). It also closed a preview-vs-game divergence for free: both callers now normalize to hex-or-empty, so the editor preview and the fired echo take the same branch. Found independently by both reviewers. |
| B441 | **A trigger echo's Glow effect rendered nothing** | v0.19.8 | F118 bug check; introduced by F118 and fixed before release. `resolveEffect` resolves glow's accent as the effect colour falling back to the text colour, and an echo is the one surface where BOTH can legitimately be empty — its colour field means "leave it the default colour", and it had no glow-colour default. So picking **Glow** with no colours set produced no text-shadow at all: the effect was selectable and silently did nothing (it also emitted a pointless empty wrapper span). A highlight can't hit this (`newHighlight` sets both) and neither can a contact template (`normalizeTemplate` defaults `textColor`). The editor field had been ADVERTISING `#ffd060` as its default swatch and placeholder the whole time — in three places — while nothing made it real. **Fix:** one exported `DEFAULT_FX_COLOR`, used by those three fields AND applied by `echoLineStyle` when an accent-using effect has neither an effect colour nor an echo colour, so the field's promise and the resolver cannot drift. Precedence is explicit effect colour > echo colour > default, pinned by four harness cases. |
| B438 | **Bold did nothing anywhere in the client at any Text weight below Default** | v0.19.8 | Sekmeht: *"Bold in Contact Templates isn't doing anything, is it the preview that's bugged?"* It was not the preview, and it was not contacts — **every bold in the client was dead**, and had been since B113. `--ui-bold-weight` was `calc(var(--game-text-weight) + 200)`, which assumes the font has a face at BOTH weights. That holds for a variable font and fails for the two-weight fonts most people run. Consolas ships exactly 400 and 700, and CSS font matching resolves any request below 400 UP to 400 — so at Settings → Text weight **Thinnest** (`textWeight: -0.6`, the value in every one of the reporter's profiles) body asked for **100** and bold asked for **300**, and both landed on Consolas Regular. Identical glyphs, nothing on screen to explain it. The same collapse hit **-0.4** (200/400 → 400/400) and **-0.2** (300/500 → 400/400, because a request of 500 also falls back to 400 rather than up to 700). Only Text weight 0 worked, which is why it had never been reported. Scope was the whole app: contact templates, highlights, monsterbold, room names, presets and every panel label on `--ui-bold-weight`. **Fix:** `max(600, calc(var(--game-text-weight) + 200))`. 600 is the lowest request a two-weight font resolves UPWARD to its bold face, so it is the lowest floor that guarantees bold lands on a heavier face than body whatever font is picked; the `calc` is kept for headroom (`--game-text-weight` never exceeds 400 today, since a POSITIVE text weight adds a stroke and leaves the weight at 400). **How it was found:** the React side was cleared first — the real `paintContactText` was bundled and rendered to static markup, confirming `<strong>` with no competing class or inline weight, and a grep of every stylesheet confirmed nothing overrides `strong` on that path. With the markup proven correct the cause had to be the weight VALUE, and the reporter's own profile supplied the two inputs (`fontFamily: Consolas`, `textWeight: -0.6`) that made the arithmetic collapse. Two Electron rendering probes were attempted first and both hung (pitfall #154); the font file listing (`consola*.ttf` — four faces) settled it without one. **Also fixed in the same pass:** `ContactPopover` was a fourth hand-rolled painter — it ignored `tagBold` entirely, applied no effects, and pinned bold at a hard `fontWeight: 'bold'` (700), bypassing the very variable this bug is about. It and both Contacts previews now render through one shared `ContactRun` element in contactStyle.tsx, so there is no longer a second place to assemble a painted run. CLAUDE.md pitfall #157. |
| B436 | **A template's Bold did nothing you could see, and the tag had no bold at all** | v0.19.8 | Sekmeht: *"selecting bold for a template doesn't bold the preview text"*, then *"bold is missing from the tag, the bold button 'Bold name text' doesn't work"*. **Two separate faults.** **(a) The name's bold was real but invisible where you check it.** The markup was correct all along — the probe showed `<strong>` emitted for every bold case — but `<strong>` routes through `--ui-bold-weight` (B113, so bold scales with the text-weight setting), which resolves to **600** at the default, and `.cp-tpl-name-label` hardcoded `font-weight: 600`. The collapsed template row therefore painted a bolded template and an unbolded one identically. A preview must show the TEMPLATE, never the panel's own styling, so the forced weight is gone. **(b) The tag genuinely had no bold** — it had colour, background and an effect, but not the one thing the name had; new optional `tagBold`, with its `normalizeTemplate` copy and round-trip guard in the same change (pitfall #121, the trap that ate `effect`/`glowColor` in B279). **The root cause of both is one missing parameter:** `paintContactText` — the shared builder that exists precisely so previews and game text cannot drift (B281) — had no `bold`, so the game renderer decided it outside the builder with its own `<strong>` wrapper and the tag path simply never did. It now takes `bold` and returns the element as `Tag`, so one input decides it for the name, the tag, both previews and the game. Slash surface updated in lockstep: `/template add … tagbold`, and both summaries report it (Principle #11). Verified by rendering the real builder to markup across nine bold/effect combinations, plus 4 new harness cases. |
| B435 | **The F116 bug check: a dead type-ahead, focus landing on the wrong control, and "No highlight" never reading as chosen** | v0.19.8 | Bug check on F116 (self-found, never shipped — one reviewer plus my own pass; every finding re-verified against the file before it was touched). **(a) The type-ahead was DEAD in the Theme Editor.** `suggestions` derived from `value`, but under `commitTyped` the typed text lives in `draft` — so `suggestColors` was handed the stored `#hex`, which it rejects outright (it bails on a leading `#`). Typing a colour name offered nothing at all, which is precisely the feature the release notes describe. One `typed = draft ?? value` now feeds the box, the suggestions and the combobox ARIA, while `link`/`hex`/the swatch stay on `value` so the swatch keeps showing the colour still in effect. **(b) `focusField` always focused the SWATCH.** `querySelector('.ui-color-chip, .ui-color-text, .ui-color-swatch')` returns the first match in DOCUMENT order, not the first selector that matches, and the swatch is rendered first — so picking from the type-ahead moved the caret off the box, exactly the promise its own comment makes. Now three ordered lookups. (Pre-existing from B433, unreachable until (a) made the list appear.) **(c) "None" has two spellings and only one was compared.** A rule editor stores `'transparent'`; the Theme Editor's preset highlight hands the field `''`. So `value === none.value` was never true there: "No highlight" never rendered as the current choice on any of the nine preset rows, and picking it wrote a redundant change that repainted the whole app. One `isNone` helper now answers it everywhere. **(d) Pick paths compared case-sensitively** while `commitDraft` did not, so a theme carrying an imported `#3FB950` went unsaved-dirty when you picked the lowercase built-in already in effect; `wouldChange` unifies both. **(e) Esc needed TWO presses** to drop a half-typed draft once the list was showing — the list only appears while typing, so that is the common case, and the old `ColorText` dropped it on the first. **(f) My `GradientRow` memo keyed on the `vars` object**, whose identity changes every keystroke, defeating the memo that exists because `resolveDisplayHex` probes the DOM (latent — every gradient value is literal hex today, so the probe early-returns). **(g)** Both gradient ends shared one accessible name. **Verified:** both type-checks, a renderer build, four harnesses. **Deliberately not changed:** the old box's `maxLength={24}` is NOT restored — a linked value (`var(--lb-color-…, #hex)`) is longer than that, so adding it to the shared control would truncate every rule field. CLAUDE.md pitfall #155. |
| B434 | **A template's edit form ran off the bottom of the card, hiding Save and Cancel** | v0.19.8 | Sekmeht, Contacts → Templates: "items clipped off when clicking a new item… it expanding when clicked should show the full form and buttons". Distinct from B429, which was the colour POPOVER; this is the template card itself. `.cp-tpl-edit` was a single flex COLUMN of eleven label+field rows, each about 270px wide — so in an 88vw dialog it used roughly a sixth of the card's width and about 490px of its height. The list scrolls, so nothing was truly unreachable, but the buttons sat below the fold with nothing on screen saying so, and the Name field's own `autoFocus` had already scrolled the TOP of the form into view — the half you could see anyway. **Fix, two parts.** The form is now a **responsive grid** (`repeat(auto-fit, minmax(240px, 1fr))`, capped at 900px so it does not spread into a spreadsheet on a wide monitor): three columns at full width, fewer as the window narrows, one when it is genuinely narrow — nine field rows collapse to three, roughly halving the card. Preview, Groups and the actions row span every column, so the buttons still right-align and the spanned rows read as the break between the fields and the actions. Each control now fills its cell (`flex: 1 1 auto`), which also lines the fields up down a column; `.ui-field` and `.ui-color` already carry `min-width: 0`, so nothing overflows. Second, opening a card **scrolls it into the list** — by adjusting the list's own `scrollTop` rather than `scrollIntoView`, which also scrolls ancestors and can drag the whole app off screen (pitfall #109); the card's TOP wins if it is taller than the list, since scrolling the buttons in would push the field being typed in out. **Verified by type-check and a renderer build only — NOT run.** Every Electron page load in this environment failed with ERR_FAILED (file:// and data:, with the GPU process erroring), so the measuring probe was abandoned rather than trusted. The column arithmetic is stated, not measured: 3×240 + 2×20 = 760 ≤ 900, while a fourth column would need 1020. CLAUDE.md pitfall #154. |
| B433 | **The color popover could strand focus, leak Tab to the page, and close its host dialog** | v0.19.8 | F115 bug check (self-found, never shipped). Three keyboard/pointer faults in `ColorField`, all invisible with a mouse. **(a)** `close(true)` refocused `openerRef`, but a pick can REPLACE its own opener — picking one of your colors from the TEXT BOX mints a link, so the box unmounts and the chip takes its place (Unlink does the reverse). Focusing the detached node drops focus to `<body>`, stranding a keyboard user outside the dialog. Now deferred a frame and re-queried, the shape `pickSuggestion` already used; the "Save as a color…" row's Cancel and Esc do the same for the name box they unmount. **(b)** The popover is `role="dialog"` but nothing trapped Tab, and it is portaled to the END of `<body>`, so one Tab left for nothing. **(c)** The outside-mousedown that dismisses the menu ALSO reached the host dialog's backdrop handler, so one click outside closed the menu and the dialog under it; `cancelBackdropPress()` (new, in backdropClose.ts) forgets the press so the following click can't close a backdrop. Also: `parseColorLink` memoized (a fresh object every render re-ran `suggestColors` on every keystroke), the suggestion list re-places on scroll/resize like the menu, and `aria-controls` only points at a list that exists. |
| B432 | **A linked color printed as `var(--lb-color-…)` in two summaries, and a big palette pushed the built-ins out of the slash chips** | v0.19.8 | F115 bug check (self-found, never shipped). Pitfall #149 in the wild twice: `/template list` and the Triggers editor's echo action summary printed the stored value, so a linked color rendered as `Watchlist (var(--lb-color-c7k2p9, #ff9040))` instead of its name — every other summary had been converted to `colorLabel()`, these two were missed. Separately, the palette's color chips now list YOUR colors before the built-ins and the row is capped, so ≥24 of your own colors meant `red`/`gold`/`blue` were never offered; the cap is now one shared constant and the built-ins always get their share of the row. |
| B431 | **"Manage colors…" and `/colors manage` did nothing once you had switched tabs, or with Contacts open** | v0.19.8 | F115 bug check (self-found). Two pre-existing dialog faults that F115's new entry points made reachable. **(a)** `AutomationsPanel` reads `initialTab` into state on mount and re-syncs only when that PROP changes — so asking for the tab the dialog already opened on is a no-op in React, the effect never runs, and the dialog stays wherever you last navigated inside it while `/colors manage` still prints "Opened Automations → Colors." Older entry points were shielded by a second prop changing alongside (a prefill, a rule id); the color ones change nothing else, so they failed cleanly. Fixed by counting REQUESTS, not changes: `aimAutomations(tab)` bumps an `initialTabSeq` the panel's effect also watches, and every opener in GameWindow now routes through it (including the three ModeSwitcher "Manage" rows, which had the same bug for Groups). **(b)** `.cp-backdrop` and `.at-backdrop` are both z-index 200 and both portaled, so with Contacts opened after Automations, DOM order put Contacts on top and "Manage colors…" switched a dialog nobody could see — pitfall #118's signature exactly. Contacts now closes as it hands over, which is what the user asked for by clicking it. |
| B430 | **Two ways a palette could silently corrupt itself: a stolen id, and an unrelated color overwritten by an import** | v0.19.8 | F115 bug check (self-found, never shipped — every case below was reproduced against the real module before it was fixed, and each is now pinned in tmp-rules-harness). **(a) `coerceCustomColors` built ids in ONE pass**, so an earlier id-less entry could take a name slug (`buff-drop-2`) that a LATER entry already owned as its real id; the later entry was renamed to `buff-drop-2-2` and every rule linked to it resolved to nothing, freezing at its fallback hex — exactly what the id indirection exists to prevent. Now two passes: every explicit id is reserved first. **(b) `mergeCustomColors` matched by id BEFORE name.** An id-less bundle's ids are slugs minted from names, disambiguators and all, so importing one could collide with an unrelated local color and overwrite its value, repainting every rule linked to it in a stranger's color (reproduced: a local "Buff Drop 2" silently became `#222222`). The NAME is the identity ACROSS machines; an id is only an identity WITHIN one. Now name-first, with a fresh id + remap when an incoming id is already taken here. **(c)** `collectColorLinkIds`/`remapColorLinks` ran a regex over `JSON.stringify(everything)`, so a rewrite could corrupt a rule's PATTERN (`"the --lb-color-abc thing"` → `"…-zz thing"`), and the `JSON.parse` back dropped `undefined` properties and turned Dates into strings across the whole imported bundle. They now walk the values and ask `parseColorLink`. **(d)** Stored hex was never validated, so `colorLink` could mint `var(--lb-color-x, not a color)` — invalid CSS, so the whole declaration was dropped and the text painted unstyled; `normalizeHex` expands, lowercases, drops an 8-digit alpha and repairs an unusable value to grey, keeping the name and id so the Colors tab can fix it. **(e)** Names are tidied on load, so a stored `"  Ember  "` can be found by name and can't merge as a second color. **(f)** Taking a bundle's Named Colors left a same-named local color `retired`, so the import appeared to do nothing; it now un-retires, as `/colors add` already did. **(g)** `/colors rename` matched retired colors while its own error message lists only live ones, so it silently renamed something it had just said you don't have. |
| B429 | **The color popover opened smaller than its own content, cutting off Save/Cancel** | v0.19.8 | Sekmeht, Contacts → Templates: "when I add a custom color, the decision buttons are clipped off… the card that opens, the buttons aren't visible". `ColorField` placed its menu the way GroupPicker does — pick the side of the field with more room and cap the height to THAT room. For a field near the middle of a shorter window both sides are about half the viewport, so the menu opened shorter than its content; choosing "Save as a color…" then added the name + Save/Cancel row below the fold. Measured in an Electron repro at a 420px-tall window: a 194px box holding 273px of content. **Fix: place against the REAL measured height** — a `useLayoutEffect` reads the rendered menu's `scrollHeight`, puts it under the field, flips it above when that overflows, and otherwise SHIFTS it onto the screen; the height is capped only when the viewport itself is shorter than the menu, and then the newly opened row is scrolled into view. The menu also re-places on window resize and on a scroll anywhere outside it, so a scrolling dialog form can't strand it. Verified at 420/560/640/900px window heights and on several fields: the box is always its full content height and fully on screen. CLAUDE.md pitfall #109's new rider. |
| B428 | **A Line highlight's text effect never painted in the game, so a line effect and a word effect couldn't layer** | v0.19.8 | Sekmeht, 2026-09-16: a line with an effect plus a word in it with its own effect, and "the line's effect defaults to just the underlying colour". **The real defect was wider than the combination.** `getLineHighlightStyle` built the line container's style from background, colour and the legacy `glow` flag ONLY, so every other line effect, and line Bold, never reached the game window, the stream panels, the Overview cards or the Room panel, with or without a word rule. Confirmed by rendering the real `TextLineRow` to markup: a line-scope Rainbow produced `style="color:#ff0000"` and nothing else. **Why a line effect looked right on its own:** the Highlights editor's Preview was a hand-written copy of the composition that DID apply line effects and bold, while its header claimed it showed "what the game window will paint". That is the most likely thing that was seen; a line Glow was the only effect that genuinely worked in play. **Fix:** a line rule is now the widest layer of the per-property compositing. `resolveLineLayer` picks it once per line; its background, colour and bold go on the container; its effect paints every run that no match rule gives an effect of its own, riding an inner span so the segment keeps its preset styling and links; and it is the last fallback for match runs' colour, bold and effect. One entry point, `renderHighlightedLine`, now serves `TextLineRow`, `RoomPanel` and the Preview, so the Preview can no longer disagree. **Two follow-on fixes a real line effect needed:** Wave/Bounce letters (inline-blocks) let a line wrap mid-word, so `effectContent` groups each word in a `nowrap` span; and the stagger now counts from the line start taken modulo the cycle, or a long line's tail stood still for seconds. **Behaviour notes:** a word rule with a colour but no effect wears the line's effect in its own colour (the existing compositing model); contact names get no line effect (B116); a gradient restarts per run. Verified: both type-checks clean; a 21-case markup harness over the real modules; an Electron screenshot with the real stylesheet (rainbow + shimmer, gold + glow, glow + fire, frost over a speech preset with a background, neon + gradient, a narrow wave wrapping at spaces); the minified production CSS keeps `mod()`; rules harness 319/1 (known GemStone case), command harness 58/0. **Not yet run in the app.** CLAUDE.md pitfall #148. |
| B427 | **Wave and Bounce painted the wordmark white instead of taking the theme** | v0.19.7 | Sekmeht, 2026-09-15, on the new wordmark effect (F114). `paintBrandMark` rendered the word as ONE plain run for any per-letter OR colour-replacing effect, which is right for the colour-replacing ones (they paint the glyphs themselves) and wrong for wave/bounce, which are NOT in `FX_COLOR_REPLACING`. Dropping the `.toolbar-title-lich` / `.toolbar-title-borne` spans left them with no colour of their own, and `.app-bar` sets none either, so they inherited `--text-primary` — white on a dark theme. The only reason they were collapsed was that the per-letter stagger reads `--i`, which would restart at zero on a second span and break the wave mid-word. Fixed by giving the shared `effectContent` an optional `startIndex` (default 0, so every other caller is untouched) and keeping the two-tone spans for everything that is not colour-replacing. `resolveEffect`'s `colorReplacing: false` is a CONTRACT that the caller supplies the colour — see pitfall #147. |
| B426 | **Unconscious had no chip — DR signals it ONLY through the status prompt** | v0.19.7 | Sekmeht, 2026-09-15, from a capture: the prompt `SUP>` carries Stunned/Unconscious/Prone, and the `S` vanished exactly when `<indicator id='IconSTUNNED' visible='n'/>` arrived. Binu confirmed the `U` — it needs `set statusprompt`, and it cleared the moment he woke. There is no `IconUNCONSCIOUS` anywhere: Lich's ICONMAP lists 11 icons, Genie handles 13, Frostbite 11, and the word appears zero times across all three, none of which decodes the prompt letters. Fixed: the parser decodes ONLY the confirmed `U` (sampling the prompt-dedup flag before the emit, so `>` handling is unchanged) and emits a synthetic `unconscious` indicator on change; the icon bar's combat slot ranks bleeding > unconscious > stunned > dead; `$unconscious` joins the trigger and macro variables. It stays blank for a player without statusprompt, which is inherent to the source. Live confirmation is a tester step. |
| B424 | **The vitals bars could be pushed off a narrow window at large fonts** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). Regression from B386's px → em conversion: a 5em floor on five non-wrapping bars needs about 556px at font size 20, more than the app's 480px minimum width, so Spirit was clipped where the old 60px floor merely squeezed. Fixed: the floor is capped against the row (min(5em, 18%), and min(4em, 18%) compact), so it tracks the font until it cannot. |
| B423 | **The armed Kill confirm overflowed a narrow Lich Scripts panel** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). The question carried the script name, and the action cluster cannot shrink, so in a narrow docked panel the Kill and Cancel buttons were pushed off the right edge — and arming focuses Cancel, which scrolled the row sideways. Fixed: the question is just "Kill?" (the row names the script, and the full name is in the button tooltip), and the cluster may shrink and wrap while armed. |
| B422 | **The Injuries panel could say "Waiting for injury data" all session** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). Regression from B385. DR pushes its injuries dialog only when something CHANGES, so a healthy character may never send one — a full captured login session contains none — and the panel sat on "waiting" where it used to read "No active wounds.". Fixed: the affirmative line is back, with a tooltip saying wounds appear as the game reports them. The B224 wound-vs-scar rules are untouched. |
| B421 | **The Theme Editor froze palette-following colours into plain hex on a mere click** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). The colour field committed on blur even when nothing was typed. A theme var stored as var(--x) or color-mix(…) displays as its resolved hex, so tabbing through the rows wrote those hexes over the expressions, enabled Save and asked to discard on close — and a save stopped those colours following the palette. darkBase holds about 50 such values, the new --color-warning among them. Fixed: commit only what was typed, and only when it differs from the current value. Esc after typing a COMPLETE hex still falls through to the dialog (it is applied live, and the field knows only the resolved hex, so an undo there would itself freeze the expression). |
| B420 | **Clicking outside a dialog opened inside another dialog did nothing** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). Pre-existing, exposed by B405 making a nested backdrop transparent. backdropClose kept one module-level boolean saying "the press landed on my backdrop", but a dialog can be a React child of another dialog backdrop (Lich Setup inside Settings, the wizard prompts), and one mousedown runs both handlers: the inner set it true, the outer immediately set it false. Fixed by recording the press TARGET, which is identical for every handler the event passes through. CLAUDE.md pitfall #146. |
| B419 | **Deleting the Friends or Enemies contact template did not stick** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). Pre-existing, made visible by the save-time re-read added this session: the template loader re-adds a missing default on every read, so a deleted default came back and its colour kept painting names in game text. Fixed: the two built-in templates no longer offer a Delete. Making a default deletion persist would need a tombstone and is a product decision, not a bug fix. |
| B418 | **Debug Fires → Edit copied a character highlight into the All-characters list** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). Pre-existing. Fires → Edit opened a highlight by PREFILL, and in All-characters scope the panel could not tell an existing rule from a new one, so Save appended a copy — carrying the same id — into the global store. Fixed: highlights open by id like triggers already did (highlightOpenFireId), Automations switches to This character through the unsaved guard when the id is a character rule, and both open-by-id targets are cleared when Automations closes so a stale one cannot re-open later. |
| B417 | **Clicking into a colour box and tabbing out counted as an edit** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). The colour text fields normalized on blur and wrote the result unconditionally, so a rule whose stored colour was absent went from undefined to an empty string and read as dirty — a "Discard unsaved changes?" for a rule nobody edited. Fixed in Highlights, Triggers and Groups: blur writes only when normalizing actually changed the value. |
| B416 | **Asking to open the same rule again did nothing** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). Regression from this session. The rule panels remember the last openRuleId they applied so a later save cannot snap the editor back, but the memory was never cleared when the request went away — so a second Debug Fires → Edit on the same rule, after anything else had opened an editor, was ignored. Fixed in all five panels: the memory resets when the request clears. |
| B415 | **Focus landed in the command bar hidden under the Team Login progress overlay** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). The overlay had no Esc-stack entry, so when the picker unmounted the stack emptied and the last-dialog home focus put the caret in the covered command bar: typing was invisible and Enter reached the game (pitfall #131). Fixed with an EscToClose entry carrying a no-op close (pitfall #141d) — the run still cannot be dismissed, but the overlay now counts as a dialog. |
| B414 | **A second close request queued an orphaned "Discard unsaved changes?"** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). The app-bar and menu toggles bump a closeRequest counter, and each bump called the guard: choosing the same dialog twice from the native menu (the confirm scrim covers the app bar, so only the menu can) queued two prompts, and answering the first left the second asking about a dialog that had already closed. On the Theme Picker it also re-ran the editor cancel from a stale closure. Fixed in confirmDiscard: one discard question at a time. |
| B413 | **A confirm handed focus back to the button, undoing what the confirmed action focused** | v0.19.7 | Final bug check (2026-09-15). ConfirmHost restored focus to whatever held it when the confirm opened, one frame after settling — so after "Discard changes" on "+ New highlight" the caret left the new rule's Name field and went back to the + New button, where typing did nothing and Enter minted another blank rule. Fixed: the restore only runs when the action left focus nowhere, and never while another confirm is showing; a queued second confirm inherits the first one's return target, since the button it would return to is about to be removed. |
| B412 | **Spell Monitor: an ended card's hover text blinks every second** | v0.19.7 | Sekmeht (2026-09-15). The card's tooltip carried its live clear-out countdown ("…clears from the grid in 34s"). A native tooltip can't update while it's showing, so Chromium hid it each second. It looked tied to the RT/CT/aim chips because they refresh on the same one-second beat; Sekmeht's test isolated it to ended cards. Fixed: the tooltip now says the card clears when its bar runs out, and the card itself still shows the seconds. The Moons ⟳ tooltip had the same bug ("12s ago" ages, re-rendered every 2s) and now lists clock times. CLAUDE.md pitfall #145. |
| B411 | **Spell Monitor: one card with a note makes every card in its row taller** | v0.19.7 | Sekmeht (2026-09-15, screenshot). A spent "Cage of…" cell kept its last reading ("Fading") and printed it as a second row. A grid row takes the height of its tallest cell, so the whole row grew. Fixed: a cell is now one line plus a bar slot, always. `spellSlotText` puts the note in the time slot where it says more than the label (fading, percent and unknown readings). A timed effect keeps its minutes, with any charge level beside them (`spellSlotAside`). A spent cell keeps "ended" and its clear-out countdown, and its stale reading moves to the tooltip. The slot is capped with an ellipsis, and a barless cell reserves the bar's space (`.sm-bar--none`). 9 tmp-rules-harness cases. |
| B409 | **Transition, radius and shadow scales have drifted** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: no `transition: all` remains in the touched files; transitions use `--ui-transition`, control radii `--radius-sm` / `--radius-md` and popover shadows `--popover-shadow`. |
| B408 | **Dead code** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: LichScriptsPanel.tsx, LichProfileModal.tsx and the CSS only they used are deleted, the never-rendered standalone Highlights/Triggers/Macros modal branches and their CSS are gone, and login.css's unreferenced rules are removed. The rules used only by LoginScreen.tsx's unrendered JSX are kept and themed until that file is retired. |
| B407 | **Wording drifts between surfaces** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the label rules are written down in DESIGN §43.7 and CLAUDE.md UX #12 and applied across the touched dialogs: sentence case, "…" only when more input follows, Delete vs Remove vs Clear vs Reset vs Revert, Close vs Cancel with no Done, "+ New thing", and a disabled button's tooltip saying why. |
| B406 | **Code fonts are inconsistent, and one rule references an undefined variable** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: code and file content uses `--code-font-family`, and map-panel.css's undefined `--font-family` is `--game-font-family`. |
| B405 | **Stacked dialogs darken the screen twice** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: ImportWizard, LichSetupDialog and ThemeEditor take a `nested` prop that keeps their backdrop clear, passed where they open over another dialog (Automations, Settings, the Add Account wizard, the Theme Picker). |
| B404 | **Most dialogs aren't marked as dialogs for assistive tech** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: every dialog root carries `role="dialog"` (the confirm `alertdialog`), `aria-modal` and `aria-labelledby`, and tab rows are marked as tabs. |
| B403 | **z-index drift outside the tier map** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: `.gp-menu` and the Macros "$" menu 450, `.panel-add-menu` 500 with the other game-area popovers, the Maps overlay 250. The tier map in CLAUDE.md #118(d) now lists the Overview's 65, 250, 450, 500 and the confirm tier 2080. |
| B402 | **No warning colour token; about 6 reds, 3 greens and 6 ambers are hand-picked** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: `--color-warning` joined darkBase, and the listed files plus app-bar, panels, slash-palette, toasts and free-layout use the danger/success/warning tokens. Deliberately left: the Moons sky-band amber, the Overview's blue Direct-mode chip, the copper SimuCoin and the character-tab health orange. |
| B401 | **Six different "are you sure" patterns** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: exactly two shapes remain: `confirmAction` / `confirmDelete` (ConfirmHost, the confirm tier 2080) for anything not open in an editor, and `InlineConfirm` for an editor's own footer Delete and a script's Kill. The launcher's private confirm modal is gone; the Quit and account-conflict dialogs are deliberately separate. |
| B400 | **About 13 dialogs still wear the older chrome** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: every remaining dialog wears the About look: Settings, the Layout Manager, the Session Log, the Theme Picker and Editor, Transfer, Import, the Lich Dashboard, AI Consent, the Experiences shelf, Add Account and Lich Setup, the Maps overlay and Quick Send's header. The stale "canonical recipe" comments are gone. |
| B399 | **No shared control system: buttons, fields, tabs, labels and close buttons are hand-rolled per dialog** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: a shared control layer, ui.css (the `ui-btn` family, `ui-close`, `ui-field`, `ui-tabs`, `ui-section-label`, `ui-hint`, `ui-empty`, the `ui-modal-*` skeleton and tokens), imported first in main.tsx so components refine it (CLAUDE.md pitfall #144). Every dialog's buttons, fields, tabs and ✕ now come from it, delete buttons are red at rest, checkboxes take the accent, and the shared sidebar search uses `--modal-input-bg`. |
| B398 | **View-control tooltips differ between a floating Experience and the same Experience docked** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: one `VIEW_CONTROLS` set of labels and tooltips, exported from PanelFrame.tsx, serves both. |
| B397 | **Most large dialogs open with nothing focused** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: Settings, Automations, Contacts, the Theme Picker and Editor, the Layout Manager, the Lich Dashboard, the Session Log, Transfer, Import, Edit Profile and AI Consent put focus inside on open. |
| B396 | **Lists: Contacts search has no clear or count and isn't sorted, truncated names have no tooltip, and there's no arrow-key navigation** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: Contacts search has a clear button (Esc clears first), a "12 of 140" count and a case-insensitive sort; every rule and contact list label carries a tooltip with the full text; ↑/↓ move the selection through the guarded select. |
| B395 | **Strips that break the line-height / em standard, plus two leftovers** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the Moons strips, the icon-bar chips and the Debug toolbar use line-height 1 and em padding; `.moons-pill` reads `--game-font-size`, so it follows the window's A−/A+; the armed Kill button is a theme-safe danger chip. |
| B394 | **Duplicate time formatters disagree** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: Moons uses the shared `formatAgo`, and a new `utils/formatUptime.ts` (the Overview's old `shortDuration`) serves both the Lich Scripts panel and the Overview card. |
| B393 | **Overview: the summary strip hides informational chips behind "all calm", and card timers ignore Timer Style and animate width** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: "✓ all calm" shows alongside the informational chips, and card timers honour Timer Style and drain with `transform: scaleX()`. |
| B392 | **Panel headers name something different from their tabs** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the panel header reads "Lich Scripts" and the Spell Monitor header "Spell Monitor". |
| B391 | **Context menus disagree on Clear/Close placement and wording, and a floating Debug window has no Close** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: one builder (`buildTextMenu` in StreamPanel.tsx) gives the game-text, stream and tab menus the same shape: content actions, a divider, the timestamp toggle, a divider, then Clear and Close last. A floating Debug window has a Close, and launcher menus put Delete last after a divider. |
| B390 | **Transfer: Export has no busy state, so a double-click writes twice** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: Export has a busy state, so a second click is ignored until the first write finishes. |
| B389 | **Enter-to-submit is inconsistent across editors and dialogs** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: Enter saves in Triggers, Aliases and Macros (not in the key recorder or the Test field), the Contacts and template fields, Edit Profile, the team editor's name, Attach and the Add Account password, skipping textareas and IME composition. |
| B388 | **"+ New" works two ways, and template Cancel leaves junk entries** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: "+ New" opens a pending draft for contacts, templates, groups and modes; nothing is written until Save, and Cancel discards it. |
| B387 | **Theme Editor: Save sits before Cancel, Cancel hovers red, and preset colour rows apply half-typed hex live** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the footer reads [Cancel] [Save] with Save primary and a neutral Cancel. Every colour row shares one field that applies live only on a complete hex and otherwise commits on blur or Enter; Esc on a half-typed value reverts just that value. |
| B386 | **Fixed-px columns in scaled panels overflow at large fonts** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the Lich Scripts badge and uptime columns, the Debug columns (multiples of a `--debug-font` variable, so the header and rows stay aligned) and the vitals bar minimums are em. |
| B385 | **Empty states are blank, faint or premature** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: built-in stream panels say "Nothing in {panel} yet.", Debug's Events and Raw XML tabs show empty lines, Exp says it's waiting until the first update arrives (Injuries did too, until B422 put its affirmative line back — the game sends injuries only on a change), the Overview card names an empty stream by its label, the Lich Scripts footer tells the truth before Lich answers, and empty text uses `--text-muted`. |
| B384 | **The Add Account wizard and Lich Setup backdrop whitens the screen on light themes** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: `.wiz-backdrop` uses `--modal-scrim` with no blur. |
| B383 | **The floating-window grip tooltip offers a right-click Close on windows that have none** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the grip tooltip mentions right-click Close only when that menu exists. |
| B382 | **The Overview card shows two health colours** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: `healthBand()` in VitalsBar.tsx drives the card's header, bar and chips from the configurable thresholds. The game window's own bar keeps 30/50, because those thresholds are Overview options. |
| B381 | **Stream-panel right-click lacks Mute, Substitute and Show in Log** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: stream panels offer Modify Text ▸ (Highlight / Mute / Substitute) and Show in Log, through stable openers threaded via sharedFrameProps. lbAI gets no Show in Log, since AI output is never logged. |
| B380 | **The Macros "$" insert menu ignores Esc, so Esc closes all of Automations, and its items aren't keyboard-reachable** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: Esc closes only the menu and returns focus to the "$" button; the items are buttons, the first takes focus, and ↑/↓ move between them. |
| B379 | **Save validation is inconsistent, and Triggers can save a text trigger that never fires** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: Triggers requires a pattern and a valid regex for a text trigger, the variable for a variable trigger, and a non-empty action; Highlights, Mutes and Substitutes refuse an invalid regex; Contacts and templates disable Save on an empty name. Every disabled Save says why, and the save functions re-check so Enter can't bypass them. Behaviour change: a macro now needs a recorded key before it can be saved. |
| B378 | **Import Wizard: a backdrop click closes it mid-import and is ignored only on the finished screen** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: a backdrop click closes the wizard only on the first step and the done screen. |
| B377 | **Two native `window.confirm` boxes remain** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: both are `confirmAction` ("Remove duplicate copies?" / "Remove obsolete rules?"), and `window.confirm` is gone from the renderer. |
| B376 | **Closing a dialog doesn't return focus to the command bar** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: when the last dialog closes and focus was left nowhere or in the app bar, the stack calls the home focus App registers through ConfirmHost: the Overview's input bar in the Overview, otherwise the active command bar. A confirm returns focus to whatever held it when it opened. |
| B375 | **The command bar keeps focus under an open dialog, so Enter can reach the game** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the dialog stack (useEscapeClose) blurs a covered command input when the first live dialog opens, and AI Consent joined `anyModalOpenRef`. |
| B374 | **The Team Login progress overlay sets an inline z-index of 9000, above toasts, About and context menus** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the Team Login progress overlay drops its inline z-index 9000 and takes `.launcher-connecting`'s 1500 tier. The shutdown overlay uses `--modal-scrim` and keeps the Quit tier on purpose, since it must cover everything while the app closes. |
| B373 | **Lich Dashboard: the ✕ clips off-screen in narrower windows** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the title, pill and tab chips sat in a wrapping `.lp-header-main`, and the ✕ never shrinks. **Mechanism SUPERSEDED in v0.19.8 (F119)** — the tab chips moved to a row of their own, so the ✕ now shares the band with only the title and the session pill, and the wrapper was deleted. `ui-modal-head > .ui-close { margin-left: auto }` supplies the right-alignment the wrapper's `flex: 1 1 auto` used to (that was the trap: the alignment was never on the button). Symptom stays fixed with ~40px of headroom at the 480px window floor. A workaround whose only job is to stop a header overflowing is the tell that the header wants a second row — DESIGN §43.8. |
| B372 | **The Experience A−/A+/⚙ stack clips off the top of short windows** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the floating and docked A−/A+/⚙ controls are one horizontal row with a clamped lift (`--exp-ctl-lift`), so they stay visible in a 48px strip, and both ⚙ popovers open above the row so they can't cover A−/A+. Computed from the CSS; the short-window check is in the test plan. |
| B371 | **Contacts: new contact, new template and delete template skip the save hook** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: every Contacts write goes through `commitContacts` / `commitTemplates`, which save, update the list and call `onSaved`. Each commit re-reads storage first, so a save no longer rewinds other contacts' last-seen and encounter stats to when the panel opened. |
| B370 | **Many destructive actions run in one click** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: every row ✕ and list-level Delete, Delete team and profile, Remove account, custom theme Delete, Group and Mode Delete, Settings Reset to defaults (moved to the footer, away from the ✕), Reset panels and Rebuild, analytics Reset, the AI key (now Delete) and a Lich variable ✕ ask through `confirmAction` / `confirmDelete`; editor footer Deletes use `InlineConfirm`. A keyboard-focused row ✕ is visible (`.list-item-delete:focus-visible`). |
| B369 | **Lich Dashboard: an in-progress script or profile edit is lost on a tab switch, Esc or backdrop click** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the Scripts and Profiles editors report dirty, and the Dashboard guards tab switches, Esc, the ✕, the backdrop and the app-bar toggle (`closeRequest`). |
| B368 | **Unsaved edits are dropped with no warning in every editor** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: every editor keeps a baseline beside its draft and asks "Discard unsaved changes?" before a switch would lose it: Highlights, Triggers, Macros, Aliases, Mutes, Substitutes, Groups and Modes, contacts and templates, the Theme Editor, Edit Profile and the team editor. Container dialogs (Automations, Contacts, the Lich Dashboard, the Theme Picker) route the ✕, Esc, the backdrop, tab and scope switches and opening Import through `useUnsavedScope().guard()`, and GameWindow's app-bar and menu toggles now bump a `closeRequest` counter instead of unmounting them, so that path asks too. Contacts' own tab switch deliberately doesn't ask, since both drafts live in the panel. Found on the way and fixed: the openRuleId effects re-selected the opened rule after every save, and a Debug Fires → Edit save appended a duplicate highlight. |
| B367 | **The `.sl-` class prefix is shared by the Lich Scripts panel and the Session Log, so each restyles the other** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX consistency pass (2026-09-15). Fixed: the Session Log's prefix is now `.slog-` (SessionLogModal.tsx, session-log.css); the Lich Scripts panel keeps `.sl-`. Styles each had been borrowing through the collision were made explicit: the panel's disabled buttons, and the log's row separator, hover fade and italic empty text. |
| B366 | **Smaller macOS/Linux hardening items** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: (a) `LANG` defaults to `C.UTF-8` on Linux, still only when unset; (b) on macOS/Linux Lich is spawned with its own folder as the working directory, so rbenv's `.ruby-version` is honoured — the Windows spawn options are identical; (d) passwords.json, ai-keys.json and window-state.json (and its temp file) are written with mode 0600, plus a `chmod` off Windows. Not fixable here: (c) Electron doesn't emit minimize under Wayland — a comment now says so. (e) the better-sqlite3 glibc floor is a measurement, moved to the v0.19.7 test plan. |
| B365 | **CI only runs on Windows and skips the main-process type-check** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: the main type-check's one baseline error is gone. The cause was the reverse of the description above — the filter narrows fine; the scene events pushed onto the result didn't fit — fixed with an explicit `GameEvent[]` annotation, no runtime change. Both type-checks are now clean, and ci.yml runs both on a Windows + Ubuntu matrix (Ubuntu's case-sensitive filesystem catches wrong-case imports that Windows forgives). |
| B364 | **Linux: Check for Updates can stay on "Checking…" in an extracted AppImage** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: when a MANUAL check (the Help menu or the launcher's update button) finds no active updater, main sends a `[notice]` that this copy can't update itself, shown as a toast. The automatic startup check stays silent. |
| B363 | **Linux: packaged windows have no icon, and no desktop-entry link** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: `build/icon.png` ships as `resources/icon.png` (top-level `extraResources`) and packaged Linux windows pass it as their icon when present. `desktopName: "lichborne.desktop"` plus `linux.syncDesktopName: true` name the AppImage's desktop file and set Electron's Linux app id / WM_CLASS to match, so windows group under the launcher entry. Needs a Linux screenshot. |
| B362 | **macOS: quitting in fullscreen may save the wrong window size** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: window-state capture is skipped while `isFullScreen()` and runs again on `leave-full-screen`, so the last windowed rectangle is what's saved. What macOS reports mid-transition still needs a Mac tester. |
| B361 | **macOS menu and Dock rough edges** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: an explicit "Lichborne" app menu on macOS — About (the themed About), Services, Hide Lichborne, Hide Others, Show All, Quit Lichborne (⌘Q) — so no label depends on `app.name`; File → Quit dropped on macOS; the Window menu has `role: 'window'` so macOS lists open windows; the Next Character hint is Ctrl+Tab on macOS; and clicking the Dock icon with no visible window restores and focuses the main window. The labels still need a Mac screenshot. |
| B360 | **Linux (X11): the remembered window position may creep on every launch** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: Linux only, and a no-op when there's no creep: after a restored window is shown, `trackWindowState` compares the requested position with the reported one, and a small non-zero difference (within 64px on both axes) is stored as an offset and subtracted from every later save. A larger difference (a clamp, or Wayland's 0,0) is ignored; nothing changes on other platforms. X11 behaviour still needs a Linux tester. |
| B359 | **macOS: the trigger `notify` action probably never shows a notification** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: a safe fallback, since it's unverified whether ad-hoc signing suppresses notifications: on macOS a trigger notify action ALSO bounces the Dock icon through the existing flash path (`flashFrame`), and the Triggers editor says so. Harmless if notifications do show. A Mac tester should confirm which the user sees. |
| B357 | **Linux: every auto-update renames the AppImage, breaking shortcuts** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: the Linux artifact is version-less — `Lichborne.AppImage` (package.json `linux.artifactName`, with release-verify.mjs's `PLATFORM_ARTIFACTS` in lockstep; check-workflow.mjs 11/11). An existing versioned download is moved to `Lichborne.AppImage` by its next update — one last broken shortcut — and keeps that name from then on. README and the User Guide explain it, and CLAUDE.md records why the version must not come back. |
| B356 | **macOS: Help → Check for Updates gives no visible answer** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: main marks user-facing updater messages with a `[notice] ` prefix and App shows those as an "Updates" toast (everything else stays a console log). On macOS the menu's check now visibly says auto-update is unavailable and to download from GitHub Releases. |
| B355 | **First run on macOS/Linux can save a Ruby too old for Lich** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: the candidate list adds the asdf and mise shims and the Homebrew keg paths (`/opt/homebrew/opt/ruby/bin/ruby`, `/usr/local/opt/ruby/bin/ruby`) ahead of the plain Homebrew bins, and drops `/usr/bin/ruby` on macOS. Off Windows, discovery takes the first Ruby that reports 4 or newer (`firstModernRuby`, bounded to 8 seconds on top of the per-probe timeout); the silent startup pass saves nothing if there is none, and an explicit Auto Detect offers a Ruby 4 when the configured one is older. Windows is unchanged — still no `ruby -v` on the silent path. |
| B354 | **macOS restart or log-out may be interrupted by Lichborne** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: on Linux and macOS a `powerMonitor` `shutdown` handler marks the quit consented and runs the shutdown drain with no prompt, calling `preventDefault()` so the system waits for it; Windows doesn't register it. On macOS the app menu's Quit is now a click item calling `app.quit()` instead of the `quit` role, because that role goes out as the native `terminate:`, which may reach the shutdown handler and skip the multi-character confirm (read from Electron's source, unverified). The Dock's Quit may still arrive that way — flagged for a Mac tester. |
| B353 | **Quitting with a decoupled window open logs its character out even if you cancel the Quit prompt** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: `before-quit` sets a quit-in-progress flag, and a secondary window's close handler defers to the primary's app-scope confirm while it is set — that confirm lists every connected character, and the shutdown drain destroys every window. The flag clears when the confirm is cancelled and self-heals after 3 seconds, so it can never leave a window's ✕ doing nothing. An already-consented quit (installing an update) keeps the old path. Electron's close ordering on quit still needs a tester. |
| B352 | **The trigger `log` action writes into the install folder** — lost on a Linux AppImage, wiped by upgrades on Windows and macOS | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: trigger logs go to `{userData}/TriggerLogs` in dev and packaged builds — a top-level folder, so no clash with a character folder under Logs/. `safeTriggerLogName` takes the last path segment after EITHER separator (a backslash survives `path.basename` on macOS/Linux), replaces control characters and the characters Windows forbids with `_`, and drops empty or all-dots names — which also stops `:` writing an NTFS alternate stream on Windows. The Triggers editor now says where the files go. Logs already written next to the program are left where they are. |
| B351 | **The slash-command error hint names Ctrl+Shift+I on every platform** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: the hint now says "View → Toggle Developer Tools" — Electron's label for the menu's `toggleDevTools` role, right on every platform. |
| B350 | **No Mac chord for scrolling the story with Home/End while typing** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: Cmd+Home/End scroll the story on a Mac as well as Ctrl+Home/End. Whether Chromium on macOS moves the caret on plain Home/End still needs a Mac tester. |
| B349 | **Keys pressed during IME composition act on the command bar** — Esc wipes the line, arrows browse history | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: an `isComposing` / `keyCode === 229` guard at the top of `handleCommandKey`, the SlashPalette key handler, ScrollbackSearch and the Overview input's own onKeyDown. Enter still submits when nothing is being composed. |
| B348 | **A font picked on Windows falls back to generic monospace on macOS/Linux** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: `resolveFontFamily()` (settings.ts) gives a raw font name a real cross-platform tail — mono, serif or sans by name — instead of bare `monospace`; it drives the game text and the Settings preview (which had no fallback at all). The legacy `sansserif` → Segoe UI migration stays, and now renders sans on a Mac. The four static stacks gained Menlo / DejaVu Sans Mono. |
| B347 | **Option/Alt macros don't fire on macOS, and Mac-recorded ones store unreadable keys** | v0.19.7 | macOS/Linux bug check (2026-09-15). Fixed: `formatKeyCombo` / `matchKeyCombo` take a `{ mac }` option, so macros.ts stays harness-bundleable. On a Mac with Option held, the key is named from the physical key (`e.code`) — letters, digits and US punctuation, with the SHIFTED US character when Shift is held (Option+Shift+1 → `Alt+Shift+!`, the string Windows records) — so imported and transferred `Alt+` macros match. Matching on a Mac also accepts the old `e.key` form, so macros already recorded as `Alt+†` keep firing. Windows and Linux are unchanged. 23 new tmp-cmd-harness cases (58/58). What Chromium actually reports in `e.code` on macOS still needs a Mac tester. |
| B346 | **The Experiences button stayed highlighted all the time** | v0.19.7 | Sekmeht. Not stale state: `panelExperiences` was `showExpShelf` OR `expAnyOpen`, and `expAnyOpen` counts an Experience docked as a panel TAB (even a background one), which is permanent layout — so for anyone with a docked Moons / Spell Monitor / Tableau the button never went dark. The rule predates this release; B345 made it visible by turning the open state from a faint 10% tint into an accent border and ring. Verified against the real profiles: no floating Experience was open, no unknown ids, the CSS correct, the ⋯ button unaffected. Fixed by making the button follow the rule every other app-bar button follows — lit while its SHELF is open. Same change closes a latent hole found on the way: `expAnyOpen` counted an open instance whose id the registry no longer knows, which ExperienceLayer skips (kept on disk for a future build), so it would have held scene work on for a window nobody could see or close; it now counts only known instances. DESIGN §34.5. |
| B345 | **Smaller interaction inconsistencies** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). App-bar open buttons get accent text, border and an inset ring and keep them on hover, while hovering a closed button is neutral; open More-menu items get an accent bar; the duplicate state rules in app-bar.css are gone. The panel + menu closes on Esc; the Experience ⚙ closes on an outside click or Esc; Debug's Export CSV has a min-width that fits "Saved ✓"; Edit Profile's Save stacks both labels in one grid cell; the SimuCoin menu is a fixed 26em with a two-line summary reserve. |
| B344 | **Side-by-side buttons in the Automations and Debug headers render in different fonts and sizes** (pitfall #113) | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). One shared metrics rule per header row: the Automations tabs, scope, Analytics and Import buttons (that row was SPLIT in v0.19.8 — F119; the rule survives in narrower form on `.at-scope-btn`, which still shares a row with the tab chips and so still has to match their metrics — measured equal at 24.224px), and the Debug tabs with Copy All / Export CSV / Clear, all inherit the game font with matching size and padding. `.rule-scope-btn` and `.aa-reset` inherit the font too. |
| B343 | **Closing Quick Send leaves keyboard focus nowhere** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). Closing Quick Send returns focus to where it was when Quick Send opened, falling back to the command bar, or to the Overview's input bar in the Overview; it never focuses a command bar the Overview covers (pitfall #131). |
| B342 | **Some backdrops close when a drag ends outside the dialog** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). `backdropHandlers` on the + window, the Lich-launch conflict, the Keep/Switch chooser and the Team Login summary. The Overview grid deselects only when the press also started on empty space, recorded with a native mousedown listener because the cards are portaled in. |
| B341 | **Esc closes some dialogs and not others; About can open hidden** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). A shared `useEscapeClose` hook (hooks/useEscapeClose.ts): a mount-ordered stack served by ONE window-level bubble-phase listener, so Esc closes exactly the topmost dialog, and a search box, dropdown or the Quit confirm handles the key first. About 30 dialogs are registered, including every one that had no Esc and the two whose own listeners didn't stop propagation (About, Quick Send). About's backdrop 300 → 2010. CLAUDE.md pitfall #141. |
| B340 | **Hovering a selected or active item erases its selected look** (pitfall #107) | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). About 40 hover rules now exclude the selected/active state (`:not(.x--active):hover`): panel tabs, every rule list, the Automations/Contacts/Theme/Lich/Session Log/Transfer/Import/Debug tabs, Settings controls, the map buttons and chips, the Mode button, the Exp pickers, the wizard tiles and the app bar's More-menu items. Two Theme Picker/Settings font rules that re-declared the selected colour on hover were deleted. CLAUDE.md pitfall #107 rider. |
| B339 | **Experience P / S / T / G badges sit loose in a too-tall box** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). `.exp-badge` gets `line-height: 1`. |
| B338 | **Lich Map room details and search results ignore Font Size and A+** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). `.map-panel` is anchored to `var(--panel-font-size, var(--game-font-size))`, and the room details, search results, cross-zone rows and empty states are em (÷12). The toolbar strips stay px, now with a "deliberately fixed px (B201)" comment on the rules and in GenieMapView. |
| B337 | **Experience panel's Stored / Usable bars clip their numbers at larger fonts** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). `.exp-rxp-bar-track` is `height: 1.63em; min-width: 8.13em`. The sweep's 1.33em/6.67em was wrong: the track inherits `.exp-rxp`'s 0.82em, and `em` on a box is relative to its own font (pitfall #58b), so these are the values that equal 16px/80px at the default. The text already had `line-height: 1`. |
| B336 | **Labels and tooltips that don't explain** (UX #8) | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). The setting reads "Roundtime / Cast Time Timer Style". Lich Setup, + Add account and the team ⋯ explain what they do; the Genie map toolbar explains the folder, zone and level chips and why ◆/▤ are disabled; the Exp Badging/Focus pickers have hints like Sort; Overview Send reads "Send to <name>"; the command input has an accessible name. |
| B335 | **Controls that can't be reached by keyboard** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). Panel tabs are `role="tab"` with a tab stop and Enter/Space (drag intact), and their × is a real button. Character tabs get a tab stop. Settings switches are `<button role="switch">`, so clicking the label text now toggles them. Rule-list rows, the Automation-health bar, the panel + menu items, the empty panel slot and the Import Wizard source cards are keyboard-reachable through the new `pressable()` (utils/pressable.ts). A MOUSE click on a panel or character tab drops focus afterwards, so a following Space still reaches the command bar (F60). CLAUDE.md pitfall #142. |
| B334 | **Close and clear ✕ buttons with no label** (~23 sites) | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). Every close ✕ in the dialogs has `title`/`aria-label` "Close"; search clears say "Clear search". StreamPanel, GenieMapView, MapPanel and MapImageView, which the sweep listed, have no search-clear ✕ at all. |
| B333 | **Game-status chips and bars don't explain themselves** (UX #8) | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). Icon-bar chips carry explanatory titles: Joined states the follower-not-leader rule from its code comment, and Webbed/Bleeding/Stunned/Dead reuse the Overview's `ATTENTION_DEFS` wording. Compact vitals title each bar with its full name and value. The roundtime/cast/aim strips carry titles and aria-labels and now take the pointer so the tooltips show; `.cmd-input-wrap` forwards a press on a strip to the input. |
| B332 | **More unbounded or unclamped popovers** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). All four verified real. `.launcher-dialog` (the Keep/Switch chooser, the Team Login summary, the launcher confirms) is viewport-bounded with a scrolling body. The slash palette clamps horizontally and opens below when there is no room above. The contact popover clamps into the window with a max-height. The Exp pickers and the panel + menu cap their height to the room left in the panel. |
| B331 | **"+ Group" and Mode menus have no height limit** (pitfall #109) | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). `.gp-menu` and `.ms-menu` are bounded and scroll with `overscroll-behavior: contain`. GroupPicker opens toward whichever side has more room, caps its height to that space and keeps its left edge on screen; ModeSwitcher caps its height to the room in the direction it opens. |
| B330 | **Dialogs opened from the native menu while the + window is open appear BEHIND it** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). The native-menu subscriber closes the + window before any session action (Settings, Automations, Maps…, all z 100–400) or Transfer opens. Team Login, Quick Send and About already sit above it and are left alone. |
| B329 | **Toasts render under the dialog that raised them** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). `.toast-stack` z-index 200 → 2050: above every modal and Quick Send (2000), below `.ctx-menu` (2100). |
| B328 | **Add Character wizard and Lich Setup are fixed at 520px** — clipped in windows under 520px | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). `.wiz-modal` is `width: min(520px, calc(100vw - 16px))`, and LichSetupDialog's inline `width: 520` is gone, so it takes its width from the same rule. |
| B327 | **Settings is wider than the smallest window — its ✕ goes off-screen** | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). `min-width: min(560px, calc(100vw - 16px))`. Esc now closes Settings; with text in the search box, Esc clears it first. |
| B326 | **Hard-coded colours on UI surfaces** — invisible or off-theme on light themes | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). The Triggers match result, the Theme Picker swatch-dot border, the Highlights list-swatch border, the Maps walk button and close hovers, the map error overlay and the location-unknown states all come from theme vars via `color-mix`. `.btn-primary` is `--accent-dim` with hover `--accent`, keeping white text with a halo (the saturated-fill exception). Not yet eyeballed on a light theme. |
| B325 | **Text colours mixed toward `white` lose contrast on light themes** (11 rules) | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). Every text colour mixed toward `white` now mixes toward `var(--text-primary)`: global.css (the update banner and its buttons), game.css (app-bar button states and alert chips), map-panel.css and panel-manager.css. No `, white)` text mix remains. |
| B324 | **Undefined CSS variables: three surfaces always use their dark-theme fallback** — Quick Send's typed text is near-invisible on light themes | v0.19.7 | UI/UX sweep (2026-09-15). Quick Send's typed text is `var(--text-primary)`, the Attach error `var(--color-danger)`, and the Exp panel's G badge a fixed teal mixed 65% toward `--text-primary` (pitfall #63), matching its siblings. A scan of every `var(--x)` against every definition finds no undefined variable left in the renderer. |
| B323 | **The + window opened as a dark unframed box, then snapped into the logon panel** | v0.19.7 | Sekmeht. Two causes. (1) The Launcher's early returns — the loading state and the first-run empty state — rendered `launcher launcher--loading` / `launcher--empty` WITHOUT `launcher--compact`, so inside the + modal they missed the compact panel chrome (`.add-character-modal .launcher--compact`: panel background, border, radius, max width) and drew with the full-page logon background instead. (2) The modal mounts a fresh Launcher on every open, so it started in that loading state EVERY time. Fixed by giving both early returns the compact class, and by seeding the character list from the last one this window loaded (`lastLoadedCards`, module scope) so a reopen shows the list at once while the mount-time `refresh()` corrects anything that changed. Pre-existing; not introduced by this release's launcher work. |
| B322 | **Dialogs opened from the + window rendered BEHIND it** — Edit Profile from a character tile's ⋯ appeared to do nothing | v0.19.7 | Internal — found while building F110. All four `.cne-backdrop` dialogs (Edit Profile, Attach, Team Login, the team editor) are portaled to `document.body`, so they escape the + modal's stacking context, and `.cne-backdrop` sat at `z-index: 100` against the modal's 1000. Attach alone had been fixed with an `attach-backdrop` override, with Edit Profile explicitly left as a known latent bug; F110 putting Teams and Team Login into the + window would have added two more. Fixed at the one shared rule (`.cne-backdrop` → 1600: above the + modal and the connect overlay, below QuickSend and context menus), per-dialog override removed. CLAUDE.md pitfall #118(c). |
| B321 | **The + (add character) modal looked like it had no close button** — only clicking outside the panel closed it | v0.19.7 | Sekmeht. The ✕ existed, but it was absolutely positioned against `.add-character-modal`, the full-window backdrop, so it sat 16px from the corner of the whole WINDOW, out over the dark scrim and far from the centred launcher panel — nobody reads that as the panel's close. It also used `var(--text, #ccc)`, a variable no theme defines. Fixed by wrapping the launcher in a relatively-positioned `.add-character-panel` and pinning the ✕ to the panel's top-right, styled with the house modal close recipe (`.about-close`, `--modal-ctl-hover`; UX standard #10). F113 (same release) then made that panel the themed dialog and moved the ✕ into its header band. It can't go inside the Launcher itself: the launcher is the scroll container, so the ✕ would scroll away. Clicking outside still closes. Esc does not (it never did) — noted as a possible follow-up. |
| B320 | **Overview: one card looked selected while the input bar said "All characters"** — by default the active tab's card wore a "current" chip and an accent border, and clicking a card moved the input target but not the tab (clicking a tab moved both) | v0.19.7 | Sekmeht. Labelling, not logic: the chip marked the character Session view would show (`isActive`), the ring marked the input target — two correct facts, but beside a target picker "current" reads as "selected", and the bar opens on All on every visit, so the default state contradicted itself. Fixed by making ONE selection: a card click now also calls `setActive` ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) — the path a tab click from the Overview already takes, so the theme-owner freeze and the B270 refocus guard already cover it — and the card's `--active` border/tint and "current" chip are gone. The target ring — a border only, no background tint (Sekmeht) — is the only marker; the tab strip above answers "which tab". Accepted side effect: leaving the Overview lands on the last card clicked. |
| B319 | **The Spell Monitor's header could WRAP to two lines and double its own height**, breaking the one-text-row contract a chrome strip is built on. Found by audit; latent since v0.19.5 and made far more reachable by v0.19.6. | v0.19.6 | `.sm-head-title` carried no `flex-shrink: 0` and no `white-space: nowrap`, so it was a shrinkable flex item whose automatic minimum size is its min-content width — i.e. it could be squeezed until "ACTIVE SPELLS" wrapped. `.sm-head` pins `line-height: 1` for a single row (pitfall #77), so a second line silently doubles the strip and shifts the geometry the scene's flex column is built on. **Latent in v0.19.5** (at the 180px minimum width the title plus count fit at the default font, but at a large game font the title alone exceeds the window) and **made ordinary by v0.19.6**, which put the feed readout in the same row to compete for the space. Fixed with `flex: 0 0 auto; white-space: nowrap` on the title — it is the window's identity and must be the last thing to give way — and `flex: 0 0 auto` on the count, since a pill that compresses its own padding stops reading as a chip. The feed readout keeps `min-width: 0` + ellipsis and is now the only element that yields. **The instructive part is that the CSS comment beside it already CLAIMED this property** ("the title identifies the window, so it is the one thing that must never be squeezed") while nothing implemented it — an aspirational comment reads exactly like a guarantee, and only the flex rules decide. |
| B318 | **The Spell Monitor could not be resized down to the height of its own contents** — the window stopped ~25px above a one-row grid, reading as though it simply refused to get smaller. Reported by Sekmeht. | v0.19.6 | A floating Experience is `kind: 'panel'`, so it inherited `MIN_WIN_PX` = **180×110** ([freeLayout.ts](src/renderer/freeLayout.ts)) — a floor sized for a panel of TEXT. The Spell Monitor's natural one-row height is ~84px at the default game font (header ~20 + grid padding ~13 + one cell ~31 + feed strip ~18 + border 2), so the floor bound first and left dead space no drag could remove. **This exact failure already had a precedent in the same file:** the chrome bars (command/vitals/icon) got per-kind floors because *"the 110px panel floor would force them tall and 'unshrinkable vertically' the instant you resize"* — the Spell Monitor is the same shape of surface (a grid of short cells; "a strip across the top of the window" is how it was originally conceived) and needed the same escape hatch. Fixed by adding an optional **`ExperienceDef.minSize`** to the registry, passed by `ExperienceLayer` to `FloatingWindow` and preferred over `minSizeFor(win.kind)`; the Spell Monitor declares `{ w: 180, h: 48 }` — about one cell row plus the grid's padding, and low enough to hug the content with the header and feed strips switched off. Declared **per Experience rather than lowered globally**, because "how short can this usefully be?" is a property of the SCENE: a sky or a tableau genuinely needs height, and the default floor stays a fair guard for them. Width is unchanged — the grid's `minmax(11em, 1fr)` columns make a narrower window useless anyway. The existing `Math.min(floor, current)` clamp means a window already smaller than its floor is never snapped back up. |
| B317 | **Two comments in the Moons Experience still described Katamba's glow after it was removed** — *"its halo is a haze that DARKENS the sky"* and *"its faint glow reads 'no light'"*. Found by audit in the same version that removed it. | v0.19.6 | Pitfall #126's exact shape (a comment outliving the code that justified it), here by a single day. Cosmetic in effect but load-bearing in kind: the second comment was next to the pill-dot palette and would have had the next reader looking for a glow that no longer exists. Both rewritten. The same sweep found a REAL divergence beside them: `lakeReflect` still selected reflecting moons with a NAME check (`b.k !== 'katamba'`) while the glow gradient and the disc bloom had both been converted to key off the `glow` FIELD — with a comment promising the field is the switch *"so this and the disc layer below can never disagree"*. Three code paths answer "which moons give light" and only two shared a source (pitfall #127); the reflection now reads `!!b.s.glow` too. It selects the same moon today — the point is that it can no longer stop doing so. |
| B316 | **The Spell Monitor burst false "ended" cells after a reconnect-in-place** — a regression introduced by v0.19.6 itself, found by audit. | v0.19.6 | "Ended" is a DIFF against the previous `percWindow` block, and a tab Reconnect swaps the sessionId **without remounting** the GameWindow (pitfall #69), so `spellPrevRef` carried the dead session's spell list across. The first block of the new connection then read every effect that had lapsed while you were disconnected as having ended *at that instant* — a wall of greyed "recast me" cells stamped with the wrong time, which is precisely the false certainty the two-stage end model exists to avoid. Fixed by nulling `spellPrevRef` in the sessionId-change effect that already resets the sky-sync flags for the same reason, making the first new block a fresh baseline. `spellMaxRef` is deliberately KEPT: the learned bar ceilings are facts about the SPELL, not about the connection. |
