| B315 | **The Spell Monitor's 1 Hz clock could retire while a greyed "ended" cell was still on screen, stranding it well past its one-roisan grace.** Found by audit. | v0.19.6 | The clock is self-retiring by design (B292 — an interval that never clears is a session-long cost), and `soonest` bid only on LIVE effect expiries. So once the last countdown ran out the interval cleared and `now` froze — and a spent cell's grace is measured against `now` by `liveSpellEffects`, so the cell sat there until DR happened to repaint. On a repaint-on-change feed that can be many minutes, and in the common case (your last buff drops, nothing else is up) there is nothing left to *cause* a repaint. Fixed by having `soonest` bid on **the next moment the display changes** rather than the next expiry: the union of the live anchors and `endedAt + SPELL_ENDED_TTL_MS`, the latter only while the `expired` layer is on, since a hidden cell has nothing to expire. The `> now` future filter is unchanged and equally load-bearing — the interval self-clears at `soonest`, so a moment that stayed in the value would leave the dep unchanged and freeze every countdown behind it. |
| B314 | **Theme Editor rows for a var whose value is a cascading EXPRESSION showed a black swatch, misrepresenting the colour actually painted.** Found by audit after adding 15 Spell Monitor colour rows; affects pre-existing rows too. | v0.19.5 | `darkBase` deliberately stores expressions rather than literals — `var(--text-dim)` so a var follows its parent, `color-mix(… var(--text-primary))` so a fixed hue self-corrects per theme (pitfalls #34/#63). `createCustomThemeFrom` ([myThemes.ts](src/renderer/myThemes.ts)) copies the WHOLE of darkBase into a new custom theme, so those expressions reach `ColorRow` verbatim — and **`<input type="color">` silently falls back to `#000000` on anything that is not `#rrggbb`**. Every such row therefore rendered a black swatch and a raw `color-mix(...)` string in its text field. Pre-existing for the handful of `var()`-valued rows (`--hand-label-color` and friends); the Spell Monitor made it conspicuous by adding twelve badge colours and three countdown colours, all of them `color-mix`. Fixed with `resolveDisplayHex` in [ThemeEditor.tsx](src/renderer/components/ThemeEditor.tsx): a literal hex takes exactly the old path, and anything else is resolved by asking the BROWSER what it currently evaluates to (a hidden probe element + `getComputedStyle().color` → hex), which is the only correct answer since it depends on the live theme. The COMMIT path is untouched — an edit still writes a plain hex, so a theme never comes to depend on the expression form — and the probe uses the `style.color` property setter rather than `cssText`, so a stray `;` in a hand-edited theme cannot escape it. |
| B313 | **An Experience's ⚙ options popover was CLIPPED in a short window — the top options unreachable, with no scrollbar and nothing to indicate they existed.** Found by audit while adding options to the Spell Monitor, not reported. | v0.19.5 | `.exp-inst-options` ([experiences.css](src/renderer/styles/experiences.css)) had no `max-height` and no `overflow`, while BOTH of its hosts — `.fl-body` ([free-layout.css](src/renderer/styles/free-layout.css)) and `.panel-frame-body` ([panel-frame.css](src/renderer/styles/panel-frame.css)) — are `overflow: hidden`. The popover is `position: absolute; bottom: 28px` and grows upward, so once its height exceeded the window it was simply cut off at the top. Its height is the OPTION COUNT, which varies per Experience against a freely-resizable and often deliberately short window: **Moons declares 17 options** and has shipped since v0.15.1, making it the real casualty — the Spell Monitor's eight merely made it easy to notice. Fixed with `max-height: calc(100% - 40px)` + `overflow-y: auto` + `overscroll-behavior: contain` (both hosts are `position: relative`, so the percentage resolves against the host; 40px clears the 28px offset plus a margin). **The lesson generalises pitfall #109:** that pitfall says a popover sized by USER data needs a ceiling — but a popover sized by a REGISTRY count inside a resizable window fails identically. The source of the count is irrelevant; bound every one. |
| B312 | **Panel content "quivered" and highlighted text appeared to bold then revert — intermittently, across many versions, never reproducible** (Sekmeht). Seen alongside B311 but INDEPENDENT of it, and it long predates that fix. | v0.19.5 | The unread dot is a **conditionally-rendered 6px flex child** ([PanelFrame.tsx:584](src/renderer/components/PanelFrame.tsx#L584)), so a stream tab going unread/read changes that tab's width. `.panel-tab-list` is `overflow-x` with **content-driven height**, and `.panel-frame-body` is `flex: 1` — so crossing the overflow threshold made a horizontal scrollbar appear, ADDING its height to the strip and taking it straight out of the body: every line of game text reflowed, repeatedly, as unread states churned. Worst on HIGHLIGHTED runs, whose filled background makes the subpixel re-raster read as "bolding" (which is why it looked like a text-effect bug — it isn't; Sekmeht uses no text effects). Unreproducible because it needs the strip sitting right AT the threshold: panel width × tab count × label length × font size. **Compounded by a second, inert-rule bug:** `scrollbar-width: thin` was set, which per B178 DISABLES all `::-webkit-scrollbar` styling in modern Chromium — so the `height: 3px` rule never applied and the real bar was Chromium's native ~11px, making each toggle a large jump. Fixed by `overflow-x: scroll` (gutter always reserved → constant height) plus removing `scrollbar-width`/`scrollbar-color` so the themed 3px bar is restored, which is what makes the always-on gutter visually free (track = `var(--bg-sunken)` = the strip background). Pitfall #138. |
| B311 | **Moving a stream tab between windowed panels left the source panel's tabs jittering/blinking indefinitely, and collapsed the map's animation FPS** (Sekmeht) — *"that tab area looks like it's being repeatedly resized via loop"*. | v0.19.5 | Cross-frame adoption ([GameWindow.tsx:5061](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx#L5061)) REMOVES the tab from the source frame, unmounting the element that carries `onDragEnd` ([PanelFrame.tsx:557](src/renderer/components/PanelFrame.tsx#L557)) — the browser fires `dragend` on a now-detached node, which never reaches React, so the source frame's `dragTabId` stayed set forever. The F46 FLIP layoutEffect has NO dep array and PanelFrame re-renders on every game batch, and its only guard is `dragTabId === null` — so it re-measured (forced reflow) and re-transformed every tab per batch, reading positions MID-TRANSITION so the next render always saw a >1px delta and re-animated: self-sustaining while text flowed, and the per-batch reflow starved the main thread badly enough to wreck the map's FPS (pitfall #126's contention signature). Needs the source window to keep ≥2 tabs — an emptied one closes outright. Fixed by clearing `dragTabId` when the dragged tab is no longer in `tabs`. PRE-EXISTING since v0.18.2 (when cross-frame adoption shipped), NOT from PR #2. Pitfall #137. |
| B309 | **Attach-time vitals resync painted every bar at ZERO** (Kahlen, PR #2) — attaching to a detachable Lich showed health/mana/spirit/stamina all empty, which reads as "one hit from dead" rather than "no data yet". | v0.19.4 | Lich's `detachable_client_send_init` hardcodes `value='0'` and carries the numbers only in `text`, and in DR that text is `health 100/` — Lich derives max by scanning DR's own percentage bar text ([xmlparser.rb:743](file:///c:/temp/lich-dev/lich-5/lib/common/xmlparser.rb#L743)), finds ONE number, and max interpolates empty. GS sends `health 100/100`, so a pair regex parses GS and silently never matches DR. Parser now takes the first number and treats a missing/zero max as 100, **gated on `value === 0`** so live bars are untouched. Pitfall #135; protocol in Knowledge.md §19. |
| B308 | **A reconnected tab could stay greyed out while game text streamed into it** (Kahlen, PR #2) — toolbar kept offering Login on a live session. LATENT in released code, not attach-specific. | v0.19.4 | GameWindow cleared its `dropped` flag only on `s.connected && s.message === 'Connected'`, making the connected FLAG decorative and a human-readable STRING load-bearing — so any connect path with different wording (attach's `'Attached'`/`'Re-attached'`) left the tab greyed. Now keyed on `s.connected` alone, which is safe because main sends `connected: true` only from a genuine connect. The mirror `!s.connected && s.message === 'Disconnected'` check is deliberately LEFT — there the string separates a final drop from pre-connect progress lines. Pitfall #134. |
| B307 | **`/ai catchup` went to the lbAI stream even with the panel closed — nothing in the game window until the stream was opened** (Sekmeht, on v0.19.2: *"it's like the stream was being captured in the background invisibly even if it was closed"*) | v0.19.3 | The routing keyed on whether an `lbAI` TAB EXISTED anywhere (`watchedStreamsRef`), not on whether it was SHOWING — so a background lbAI tab (present in a zone/window but not its active tab) captured the whole recap with only an unread dot, which from the game window reads exactly as reported. **Mechanism caveat, stated honestly:** the set can only hold ids of tabs in rendered zones/windows, so an lbAI tab must have existed in the layout during the v0.19.2 test; it was not reproducible after B306, whose only source change (`shopWindow: 'main'`) has no path to lbAI routing — the state that made it visible on 0.19.2 was never captured. The fix removes the whole class rather than chasing that state: the target is now `watched && active` — the tab must exist in a rendered surface AND be its active tab — so a stray or background lbAI tab can never swallow a recap again. Same change gates `activeIdsRef`'s panels-mode set on the zones' `*Added` flags (a removed zone's stale activeId counted as "visible" for unread suppression too — pitfall #39). |
| B306 | **`shop` printed nothing in the game window at DR's surface-based shops** (JadedSoul, Fang Cove: *"I typed shop, nothing showed up … I literally had to log out and relog in to reset it to the main window"*) | v0.19.3 | DR sends the SHOP replies — the surface list, `shop window`, `shop <item>` — inside `<pushStream id="shopWindow"/>` (the 'Shopping' title on her discovered panel is DR's own). `STREAM_FALLBACK` had no `shopWindow` entry, and the routing at GameWindow's stream-text case shows an UNWATCHED stream in main only if it has one — otherwise it is pushed into a stream buffer nothing displays. So the listing was invisible unless a Shopping panel happened to be open, and went dark again the moment it closed; every symptom in the report, from one missing table row. Fixed by adding `shopWindow: 'main'`. Grounded in the sibling clients: Frostbite `writeTextLines()`s shopWindow straight into its main text and Profanity lists it among the streams rendered in main when no window exists — and the pitfall-#49 check passes by the report itself: had DR emitted an outside-the-block copy, she would have seen *something*. The stream stays discoverable, so a Shopping panel still captures it. **Residual:** her second screenshot shows `shop` printing in main after a relog with no code change, which this analysis does not explain — possibly DR-side session state in when it wraps the reply; the fix is correct in both cases, so it was not chased. |
| B285 | **Click "Overview", start typing → nothing** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | The clicked toggle `<button>` kept focus (Chromium focuses a button on click; App's blur-on-enter covers only `.session-shell`), and the input bar's type-anywhere allowlist correctly refuses keys focused outside `.ov-shell` — so the most common entry path defeated the exact papercut the handler exists to prevent, and Space/Enter re-fired the toggle itself. Fixed with `e.currentTarget.blur()` in ViewToggle's onClick: focus lands on `<body>`, which the allowlist accepts. |
| B286 | **"Spoken to" chip + app-bar badge never expired on a quiet character** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | The flag is a 60s time window computed inside a push-driven `useMemo` with no time dep — entry worked, expiry needed a re-run a parked character never got, so a whisper lit the badge for hours. Fixed with one bounded `setTimeout` per speech ([useSessionStats.ts](src/renderer/hooks/useSessionStats.ts) `spokeTick`) that re-runs the memo just past the window's end — chosen over consumer-side derivation (the `idle` model) because the app-bar badge's 1 Hz clock only runs while the Overview is OPEN, so a consumer-derived expiry would fail exactly where it matters. The hook's one deliberate piece of React state; fires at most once per window. |
| B287 | **Disconnected Overview card showed "up 0s"** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | The code's own comment promised uptime "stops (rather than resetting) on a drop"; the code hard-reset it. Now a `stoppedAt` stamp freezes the clock at the drop (`(stoppedAt \|\| now) - startedAt` — never 0, never growing past the drop); the `[i.sessionId]` effect still resets both for a fresh connection, and sessionId joined the uptime effect's deps for the reconnect-in-place edge (pitfall #69). Digest untouched — its `uptimeStartedAt` has no consumers today. |
| B288 | **After a tab Reconnect, other windows kept the stale character name** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | The one live pitfall-#86 instance: `setSessionName(session.sessionId, …)` in the mount-once event effect captured the first-render id; after a reconnect-in-place main's getSession silently dropped the dead id, so the roster (Quick Send / Overview labels in other windows) kept the old/typed-case name. One line: `sessionIdRef.current`. |
| B289 | **Contact popover ignored the Font Size setting entirely** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | All 8 rules were `rem` with no game-font anchor, while pitfall #45(b) explicitly names this portaled popover as one that must size off `var(--game-font-size)`. Root now carries the anchor, children are em at old-rem × 16/12 (default look byte-identical), and the v0.8.6 290px width became 24.17em so the two-column stats fit survives any font size. |
| B290 | **Six game-area controls ignored the Font Size setting** *(sweep-found, pitfall #45 class)* | v0.19.2 | Panel tab ✕ and + (frozen px inside the game-font-anchored strip), the add-stream popover's rows/input/OK, the `.exp-tab-missing` placeholder, the "N new lines" badge, and the empty-panel-slot labels — all `rem`. Converted to em off the anchored container / `var(--game-font-size)` / the F31 chain, factors chosen to preserve the default-12px look exactly. The + button's width also went em-of-own-font (pitfall #58b) so the click target grows with the glyph. |
| B291 | **Turning card feed lines 0→N committed the entire scrollback into every card for one frame** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | The shell publishes corrected capacity in an EFFECT, so the option flip rendered cards with stale capacity 0 — and `slice(-0)` is the WHOLE array (up to 2400 TextLineRows × every character), torn down a frame later. Guarded: `capacity <= 0` renders an empty feed for that one frame (invisible — the feed was off a moment ago). |
| B292 | **Every Overview card ticked at 10 Hz forever after the character's first roundtime** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | `useTimers`' interval stopped only at all-zero expiry stamps, which are set by events and never zeroed — one RT armed a permanent 100ms tick per card (and in the command bar, where the hook also lives). The interval now self-clears once every stamp is in the past; the final tick still renders the 0, and a new timer event re-arms a fresh interval. |
| B293 | **`/view set` offered 4 of the 6 card-section toggles** *(sweep-found, Principle #11)* | v0.19.2 | `conditions=` and `timers=` had Settings toggles and card gates but no slash keys. Added to the options list (hints included, order mirroring the card top-to-bottom) and the executor's key map. |
| B294 | **overview.css restated `flex` against the shared command-bar rule** *(sweep-found, pitfall #111)* | v0.19.2 | `flex: 1 1 auto` at equal specificity vs game.css's `flex: 1` — decided by bundle order, in a file whose own header forbids exactly this (and whose comment claimed "only the flex behaviour is ours" while the shared rule set flex). The restatement is deleted; game.css owns `flex`, the borrower keeps only the additive `min-width: 0`. |
| B295 | **Hovering an urgent/active card replaced its severity border with the generic hover border** *(sweep-found, pitfall #107)* | v0.19.2 | `.ov-card:hover` (0,2,0) outranked the state classes (0,1,0) — the red border vanished under the cursor at the moment of confirmation. Fixed by exclusion, never specificity duels: the border hover excludes `--urgent`/`--alert`/`--active`; the background tint stays as the hover affordance everywhere but `--active` (whose stronger tint it would have dimmed). |
| B296 | **The Overview grid never re-planned on a font-size change** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | The shell's fresh-per-render `getComputedStyle` was justified by a comment claiming "the shell renders on the 1 Hz clock" — false; its only changing subscription was options, so re-plans happened only via incidental App re-renders (the Settings modal closing masked it). The shell now subscribes to the clock (`useOverviewNow()`), making the comment true by construction; cards are portaled so a shell render doesn't touch them, and the clock only runs while the view is open. |
| B297 | **`--ov-feed-lines` was a dead CSS var with a comment crediting it for a mechanism that doesn't exist** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | Written on every card, zero consumers; the grid "quiver" it claimed to prevent is actually prevented by the row floor + overflow clipping. Deleted, with a tombstone comment so nobody hunts it. |
| B298 | **`DIGEST_KEYS` exhaustiveness was not actually compile-enforced** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | The `[...] as const` list only typed entries as a SUBSET of the digest's keys — a forgotten 12th field would compile clean and silently never re-notify, the exact staleness the key loop exists to kill. Now derived from a `Record<keyof CharacterDigest, true>`, so a missing (or stale extra) key is a compile error. Pitfall #130's wording corrected to describe the enforced shape. |
| B299 | **expParse held two same-question key sets with divergent case handling** *(sweep-found, pitfall #127 shape)* | v0.19.2 | `NON_SKILL_KEYS` (lowercased compare) vs `META_KEYS` (raw compare), same four keys — one rename from the card and the panel disagreeing on what a skill is. Merged to one set + one `isNonSkillKey()` predicate (lowercased — the superset filter). Harness re-run green. |
| B300 | **Nerve damage could label `worstParts` under a severity that deliberately excluded it** *(sweep-found)* | v0.19.2 | `nsys` is excluded from `worstWound` (it has its own field) but the `worstParts` sweep included the Other section, so an equal nerve rank listed "Nerves" under a wound label that left nerves out. The sweep now skips `nsys`; it still counts into `woundCount` on purpose (nerve damage IS active damage the chip should tally), stated in a comment. |
| B301 | **`makeCharacterId` existed as two must-match copies in main and renderer** *(sweep-found, pitfall #127 shape)* | v0.19.2 | The duplicate-constant shape that shipped the v0.19.0 IPC break, guarding the id every cross-window surface keys on. Now ONE definition in [shared/characterId.ts](src/shared/characterId.ts); main imports it, SessionsContext imports-and-re-exports it (existing importers untouched). |
| B303 | **Overview input-bar ↑ history was wiped on every Session⇄Overview flip** *(sweep-found; fix ordered by Sekmeht 2026-08-23)* | v0.19.2 | The bar unmounts with the view and the history lived in a component ref — per-VISIT lifetime against B278's documented per-WINDOW intent (pitfall #132 in reverse: lifetime narrower than the documented scope). The array now lives in overviewStore (module = window lifetime, capped at 200); the browse index and draft stay component-local on purpose — re-entering at the live line is correct. |
| B305 | **A−/A+ panel buttons + floating compass don't track the font setting — CONFIRMED DELIBERATE** (Sekmeht, 2026-08-23) | v0.19.2 | Not a fix — a decision recorded where it stops being re-flagged: the A−/A+ buttons are the controls that CHANGE the panel font (sizing them by the value they change would make them grow under your clicks), and the compass is one fixed-24px-cell pixel design whose fonts can't scale without the whole geometry. Both now carry "deliberately fixed" comments citing this entry. |
| B284 | **Every visit to the Overview stopped starting on "All characters"** *(audit-found, in the same session that caused it)* | v0.19.1 | Caused by F106: the input bar's target moved from component `useState` into `overviewStore` so cards could select it. The bar unmounts when you leave the Overview, so the reset to All had come **free from the state's lifetime** — module state persists, so re-entry quietly restored the last target, the opposite of F101's documented "opens on All" and a straight fail of test 2q. Reset moved into `setViewMode` rather than a component, so it holds however the view changes — toggle, menu action, or `/view`. **The transferable lesson: widening a piece of state's SCOPE silently removes whatever its narrower lifetime was doing for you.** Nothing documented that unmount reset; it was a property of where the state lived, and it disappeared without a single line changing at the place that depended on it. |
| F106 | **The Overview is sticky — cards select instead of navigating, and the theme stops moving** (Sekmeht: *"make overview more sticky, so you don't accidentally start bouncing around the views"*) | v0.19.1 | Three parts. **(i) Theme OWNERSHIP is frozen, not theme application.** Exactly one GameWindow writes theme + settings to the document and it was always the ACTIVE character, so clicking a tab from the Overview handed ownership over and re-themed the dashboard. Now whoever owns it on entry keeps it until you leave; the owner's effect still re-runs on theme/settings changes, so a theme picked FROM the Overview still lands — it just stops moving house on every tab click. Leaving releases ownership to whoever is active, so the character you land on applies its own theme and ordinary Session tab-switching is untouched. **(ii) A card click SELECTS** it as the input bar's target (accent ring) instead of navigating; clicking empty grid space widens back to All. That required moving the target into the store — three surfaces now read and write it — which collapsed F104's reset nonce into a plain `setOverviewTarget(null)` and caused B284. **(iii) Leaving is deliberate:** double-click a card, or **"Go to <name>'s game session"**, a new optional entry in the shared menu builder so the character TAB's menu does not grow one (a tab already navigates when clicked). Keyboard parity: Space selects, Enter opens. **Accepted trade (Sekmeht):** settings edited in the Overview belong to the ACTIVE character, which may not be the owner, so they appear when you leave — the same behaviour as editing a background character's settings in Session view, and each card already re-maps its own font inline, so only the summary strip and input bar are affected. |
| B283 | **Lich 5.20's opt-in room-id title placement broke the subtitle parser** *(found reviewing Lich 5.20.0)* | v0.19.1 | `;display uid` + `;display roomid title` produces `[Town Square - 1234 - (u230008)]` — the UID **inside** the brackets. Neither existing pattern matched it: the dash form needs digits at the very end (this ends with `)`) and the parens form needs them AFTER the `]`. So the id was lost **and the decorations stayed in the room title**, which would also have broken the Genie map's title matching. Opt-in only — Lich kept defaults unchanged — so no one hits it without turning it on. The in-bracket `(uNNNN)` is unambiguously the game uid, so it now wins over the ambiguous `- NNNN` slot. Verified against all **8** subtitle shapes Lich 5.20 can emit, old and new, with no regression on the five that already worked. |
| B282 | **The Lich Map's fast room lookup resolved ~3% of rooms** *(found reviewing Lich 5.20.0)* | v0.19.1 | The index was built `db.set(r.id, r)` — keyed by **Lich's own** room id — while the `roomId` fed to it comes from `<nav rm>` and the subtitle marker, which Lich's own code calls *"the authoritative room UID"*: the **game's** id. Different number spaces, measured on a real 18,779-room DR map: ids run 1–52,274, uids up to 9,798,920, and only **471 of 15,521** overlap. Every miss fell silently through to the fragile title+desc match — the "map lost me" symptom. Fixed by indexing the `uid` array too and trying uid → id → title; measured **3.0% → 100%** against that same map file. **Not a 5.20 regression** — 5.20 is what makes it fixable, because DR now emits `<nav rm>` on every arrival instead of only when the player had the game's ShowRoomID flag on. Two reasons it survived so long: `LichRoom` never modelled the `uid` field, so the data was invisible to the type, and the failure mode was a silent fallback rather than an error. Both id spaces are still tried, because the `- NNNN` dash slot is genuinely ambiguous — the game's room number natively, Lich's own id under `display_lichid`, indistinguishable from the text. Full reference: Knowledge.md §17. |
| F105 | **Text effects on the contact TAG, independent of the name** (Sekmeht: *"I could see someone just wanting text effects on the tag, but not the name perhaps"*) | v0.19.1 | `tagEffect` / `tagGlowColor` on `ContactTemplate`, separate from the name's pair, so a tag can shimmer while the name stays plain or the reverse. The tag had never carried an effect at all — the field's own comment said "effect on the contact's NAME" — so this is new capability rather than a fix. The editor's tag controls appear **only when there is tag text** (UX #1: a control for something that does not exist is noise). **`normalizeTemplate` was updated in the SAME edit as the type**, and harness cases added in the same change — `tagEffect` is precisely the optional-field shape that B279 destroyed one release earlier, and pitfall #121 now states that the rebuild and a round-trip test are part of adding the field. |
| B281 | **A contact template's text effect never appeared in the preview** (Sekmeht: *"the changes do not show in the preview text, it only shows in the actual game text"*) | v0.19.1 | Both previews — the contact editor's template chip and the template list's summary row — built a plain `style={{ color }}`, while `renderSegmentFull` resolved and applied the effect. So a rainbow template previewed as flat colour and you only discovered the effect by finding that contact in play, which is the opposite of what a preview is for. **Fixed by extracting `paintContactText`** ([utils/contactStyle.tsx](src/renderer/utils/contactStyle.tsx)) and having the game renderer AND both previews call it — the fix is the single definition, not a second correct copy, because two copies is how they drifted. A live **Preview** row was also added inside the template editor, so the effect is visible while you pick it rather than after the fact. Related: B279, where the effect was being destroyed on load — with that fixed, the preview gap was what remained. |
| B280 | **A tiny Overview tile set to Experience kept rendering the skill table** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.19.1 | Found by the post-F103 bug check, asking what the density tiers do to the new content. At the 15em container tier the card hides `.ov-card-feed` AND `.ov-card-streampick` — the tile is down to essentials and there is no room for text. `.ov-card-exp` occupies the feed's slot but was not in that rule, so a tile too small for a text feed still rendered a skill table, **and with the picker hidden there was no way to change it back**. One line, plus a comment on the rule saying that any future alternative content for that slot needs the same treatment — the gap exists because the slot gained a second occupant and only one of them was named. |
| F104 | **Clicking Overview while already in it aims the input bar back at All characters** (Sekmeht) | v0.19.1 | The counterpart to F101. Clicking a character tab NARROWS the bar's target to that character; clicking the view button you are already on WIDENS it back to a broadcast — without leaving the view or opening the dropdown. Clicking the active view was otherwise a dead no-op, so the gesture was free. Implemented as a NONCE in `overviewStore` rather than moving the target into the store, because the target is genuinely the bar's own state (it resets on remount, and exactly one bar exists) — this is a one-way poke. A number is a primitive, so the cached-snapshot rule (pitfall #129) needs no caching. The bar seeds its tracking ref at mount so a nonce incremented while it was unmounted cannot fire a spurious reset, the same shape as the follow effect. Clicking the active SESSION button still does nothing, exactly as before. The active Overview button's tooltip says what the click does (UX standard #8) — otherwise it is an invisible affordance. |
| F103 | **The compact experience view, on an Overview card** (Sekmeht: *"experience monitoring is really important... I just want the compact experience view being available in the overview's character panel"*) | v0.19.1 | Reached by picking **Experience** in a card's stream dropdown, which now renders the compact view instead of a text feed. **This supersedes the v0.19.0 decision to exclude `exp` from that dropdown** — that exclusion was right for the reason given (experience is assembled from `exp-component` EVENTS into a state map, so a FEED of it could only ever say "nothing yet"), and it stopped being right the moment the card could render the state. The dropdown is now "what this card shows" rather than "which stream". Reported first as *"Experience doesn't appear in the dropdown — is this a bug?"*, which it was not; the honest answer was that no stream selection could ever serve it. **The FILTER and ORDER are shared, the MARKUP is not.** `compactExpRows` in expParse.ts (filter to actively-training skills, then sort) is used by BOTH ExpPanel and the card, so the two can never disagree about which skills appear or how they sort; the panel's rows keep their pin buttons and RXP footer and a card stays read-only. Pins are honoured for ORDERING — the skills you pinned are the ones you want at the top of a small tile — with no button to toggle them. Memoized on the skills map, because `compactExpRows` parses every entry (the comparator parses again) and a card re-renders on every game line. TOP-anchored, unlike the text feed: a feed clips its oldest line because the newest matters most, a table's first row is its most important. Sort follows the character's saved preference, **read once at mount** — ExpPanel owns that state and emits no change event, so a sort changed in the panel reaches the card on its next mount; reading localStorage every render on a per-game-line component was not worth instant sync. **A quirk pinned rather than fixed:** `sortDesc` multiplies the comparator, so for `alpha` it is FALSE that gives Z→A and TRUE that gives A→Z — confusing, shipped, and depended on by the panel's stored default, so the harness now pins both directions instead of renaming it. Section K of tmp-rules-harness covers the shared ordering (124 → 134 cases); it caught the author's own inverted assumption about that flag before it shipped. |
| B279 | **Contact templates kept their colour but silently lost their text effect** (Sekmeht: *"contacts do not respect the text effects, like rainbow... it tends to just keep the colorized option with no effects"*) | v0.19.1 | Pitfall #121, textbook. `normalizeTemplate` ([contacts.ts](src/renderer/contacts.ts)) REBUILDS each template field by field and never copied `effect` / `glowColor`, so the fields were **destroyed on load rather than ignored**. The full chain: the editor genuinely offers the effect picker and writes it to the draft; `saveContactTemplates` persists it raw, so it really does reach localStorage; `loadContactTemplates` strips it; the renderer therefore receives a template with no effect and paints colour only — and the panel then saves what it was handed, so the **next save destroys it on disk**. Colour survived precisely because colour is one of the fields the rebuild does copy, which is what made the symptom read as "effects don't work" rather than "my setting vanished". `renderSegmentFull` was never at fault: it already resolves a contact's effect and has since the field was added. **`tsc` could not have caught this** — both fields are OPTIONAL, and optionality is exactly what makes them safe to add to the type and invisible to omit from a rebuild. Contacts themselves are unaffected: `loadContacts` returns the parsed array raw and has no normalizer. Fixed by copying both fields, and guarded by a **save/load round-trip case in tmp-rules-harness** (section J) so the next optional field cannot slip the same way — a comment on the rebuild says so too, since that is what the next person reads before adding one. |
| F102 | **RT / Cast / Aim strip on each Overview card** (Binu: *"is it possible to add a little RT/Cast/Aim time bar somewhere on each pane of overview?"*) | v0.19.1 | Two thin lanes above the vitals bar - roundtime on top, cast and aim sharing the one below - in the SAME colour vars as the game command bar (`--rt-end` / `--ct-end` / `--aim-end`) and with the same precedence, cast painting over aim because cast is the PvP-critical one. On a dashboard this answers *who can act right now*, which is precisely what tabbing around cannot tell you. **The implementation is a MEMO'd leaf, and that is the whole design.** `useTimers` runs a 100ms interval while anything counts, and the Overview card is deliberately NOT memoized (its `stats` object is fresh every render, so a shallow compare could never bail) - so calling `useTimers` at card level would have re-rendered each card, feed and all, TEN TIMES A SECOND per character in combat. Confined to `CardTimers`, whose props are three primitives, the tick repaints three divs and a card re-render caused by anything else does not reach it. `useTimers` returns early at all-zero, so a calm dashboard runs no interval at all - the §35.6 "free until used" rule the rest of the view follows. **The strip's height is always reserved**, even with nothing running: a card that grew when a roundtime started and shrank when it ended would twitch through every fight, which is the quiver this view has been bitten by once already. The empty lane renders as a faint groove rather than nothing, so a running timer reads as filling a track you already knew about. Hidden below the 21em container tier, where flags, vitals and the feed matter more, and behind a **Settings → Overview → "Show roundtime on cards"** toggle like every other card section. Aim is scaled against CAST's max while cast runs, so the two widths are comparable in absolute seconds rather than each as a share of its own max - mirroring `TimerDisplay`. |
| F101 | **The Overview's "to" target follows the active character** (Sekmeht: *"when the tab is cycled, the 'TO' character selector switches to that character... but by default All characters is perfect"*) | v0.19.1 | Switching tabs from the Overview - Ctrl+1..9, Ctrl+Tab, or clicking a tab in the app bar - now retargets the input bar at that character, so the card highlighted as **current** and the character you are about to type at are never two different people. All three routes go through the same `setActive`, so following `activeId` covers them with one mechanism rather than three. **Only on a CHANGE, never on mount:** the tracking ref is seeded with the value it already has, precisely so the first render is not read as a switch and the bar opens on **All characters** as asked. The bar is remounted each time the view opens, so every visit starts on All until a tab is actually picked. That deliberate seeding is the INVERSE of pitfall #12 (which warns that seeding a ref with a real value hides the first change) - here hiding it is the entire point. A null previous value is not treated as a switch either, since that is the roster resolving rather than a choice. **Two behaviours this leans on, both verified rather than assumed:** the app bar renders above the overlay and is not view-gated, so its tabs really are clickable while the dashboard is up; and `refocusActiveCommandBar` early-returns in Overview (added with B270), so a tab switch does NOT pull focus into the hidden game command bar - you can be mid-sentence in the input bar, press Ctrl+2, and keep typing. **Ctrl+0 was NOT added** despite the request's phrasing: it is an Electron-reserved zoom accelerator and the operational guardrails forbid rebinding it, so the range is 1-9 as before. Targeting a character that is offline is honest rather than silent - the bar says "target offline" and Send stays disabled, instead of quietly falling back to broadcasting. |
| B278 | **No command history in the Overview's input bar** (Binu) | v0.19.1 | The bar shipped without arrow-key recall, so anything you typed at a character from the dashboard had to be retyped. It now mirrors the game bar exactly: newest-first, ↑ walks back, ↓ forward, entering history from the live line STASHES the draft so ↓ returns it, Enter always resets to the live line whether or not the command was stored, and the push honours the same app-wide `commandHistory.minLength` gate plus a consecutive-repeat check. **↓ is clamped at -1** — B120 was Binu's own report against the game bar (presses past the bottom accumulated negative counts and you had to press ↑ that many times to climb back), and there was no reason to ship it a second time. **The history is the BAR's, not the target character's, and in-memory rather than persisted.** This one control can be aimed at any character or broadcast to all of them, so "recall the last thing sent" is the only question with an unambiguous answer — a per-character history would change what ↑ gives you depending on a dropdown, and mean nothing at all while targeting All. Each character's own persisted history is untouched: a command sent from here still lands in the TARGET's history, because it goes through that character's `dispatchUserText`. |
| B277 | **The end of a room description was cut off on an Overview card** (Binu: *"the end of the rooms, when there were more than 1 or 2 mobs, or too many things on the ground"*) | v0.19.1 | Two defects in one rule. The feed capped each line at `max-height: calc(3 * 1.3em)` with the overflow hidden, so a "You also see..." line that wrapped past three rows lost its tail — and the tail is the half you were reading. It also never cut on a row boundary: `.text-line` sets its own `line-height: var(--game-line-height)`, the user's PROSE setting of 1.4-1.6, so a 3.9em box against ~1.5em rows is **2.6 rows** — two full rows and a sliver of a third, which is the artefact visible in the screenshots. The visible row count also moved with a prose setting and could never land on a whole number. Same family as pitfalls #62/#116: arithmetic against a length that is not the one actually rendered. **Fixed by removing the per-line cap entirely** rather than correcting the constant. The feed is already bottom-anchored (`justify-content: flex-end`), so when content exceeds the tile the OLDEST lines clip off the top and the newest line is always whole — which is how the game window behaves, and this card's stated governing rule is that it should read like one. The child selector also moved to `.ov-card-feed > .text-line` (0,2,0) so it beats game.css's `.text-line` deterministically instead of tying at equal specificity and losing on bundle order (pitfall #111), and the `min-height` that keeps a blank line holding a row is now expressed in the row's OWN metric (`calc(1em * var(--game-line-height, 1.4))`) so it cannot drift from the line-height that renders it. |
| B276 | **The attention badge counted states that are NORMAL, and the health number disagreed with the health chip** (Sekmeht: *"I want them to actually be useful and of value"*) | v0.19.0 | Three findings from reviewing every chip. **(i) `mind-lock` drove the badge.** It is push-driven, so unlike `idle` it genuinely reached the count - and mind lock is the NORMAL state of any character grinding a skill, so the badge showed a permanent number. That is precisely the *badge you learn to ignore*, which costs you the one time it means something. Fixed with `ATTENTION_ALERT_FLOOR` (40 = `spoken-to`, the lowest flag genuinely about you) and a shared `needsAttention(score)` used by the badge, the summary strip's count AND the card's border tone - which had the same `score > 0` test, so a mind-locked character also wore a permanent amber border. Below the floor sit `mind-lock` and `idle`; both still CHIP on the card, they just no longer claim attention. **(ii) The header health percentage and the Critical/Hurt chips used different ladders.** The number was hardcoded 80/50/30 while the chips use the CONFIGURABLE `healthCritPct` (25) / `healthLowPct` (50) - so even at defaults a character at 27% showed a RED number beside a chip that only said "Hurt", the number screaming while the chip shrugged, and raising `healthCritPct` diverged them further. `healthClass` now takes the thresholds, so red means exactly Critical and orange exactly Hurt whatever the user configures. **(iii)** `free-to-act` removed - see B274. **The harness caught a real regression during this change** (110/1): three cases asserted the old free-to-act contract. They were INVERTED rather than deleted, since they are what would catch it being reintroduced by reflex, and six cases were added for the floor - 117 total. |
| B274 | **The Overview's "Free" attention chip was unreachable — REMOVED** | v0.19.0 | `computeAttention` set `free-to-act` only after `freeToActSeconds` (10s) of idle with no roundtime - but it runs inside a `useMemo` whose deps are all PUSH-driven, with `lastInboundAt` deliberately excluded, so it only evaluated when text had just arrived and the idle clock was ~0. `idle` has the identical shape and is solved by consumers re-deriving it against the shared 1 Hz clock; nothing re-derived this one. It could still fire coincidentally if some other dep changed 10s+ after the last line, which is worse than never - an inconsistent chip. **Resolved by removing it** rather than making it work: at a 10s threshold it would have lit for nearly every quiet character, which is close to what `idle` already says and is the noise UX standard #1 exists to prevent. `freeToActSeconds` is retained in the thresholds so no stored profile changes shape. **The transferable rule, now in DESIGN §47: an attention flag whose condition is ELAPSED TIME cannot be computed at publish time** - the digest is push-driven by construction and a character that has gone quiet has by definition stopped publishing. Such a flag must be derived by the consumer; `idle` is the pattern. |
| B275 | **A freshly-connected tab was blank until the game next spoke — no "Please wait for connection to game server."** (Sekmeht, on the SECOND character of a Team Login: *"completely blanks out... eventually I see the game respond, but its kind of a weird experience that you dont see anything at login"*) | v0.19.0 | **Not Team-Login specific — every connect lost its opening text**, including the first character; you only notice it on the second because that is the tab you are watching when it lands, while the first fills in before you look. The chain: `wireSession` runs inside `createSession`, so the parser -> queue -> flush pipeline is live the moment the session exists, but the `LOGIN` handler is an **invoke that resolves at the END of login**. The game's first data is that wait message, so it is flushed while the renderer still does not know the `sessionId` — and the whole renderer has exactly ONE `onGameEvent` subscriber, inside `GameWindow`, which cannot exist yet. Every listener filters by `sessionId`, so the batch went on the floor. `historyBuffer` recorded it, but `session:request-replay` explicitly returned early for a fresh connect. **Fixed with the machinery already there:** LOGIN now sets `replayTarget` + `holdingForReplay`, so main BUFFERS instead of firing into the void, and the GameWindow's existing mount-time replay request delivers it once and resumes live. It cannot double — the old justification for skipping the replay — because the hold means there was no live delivery to duplicate. **Two ordering details that decide whether this works:** the 5s `scheduleReplayHoldRelease` net is armed AFTER connect resolves, not where the hold is set, because it both releases AND delivers, and its window must measure "until the renderer mounts" rather than "how long login takes" — armed at the top it would fire mid-login into the same void on any Lich launch over 5s. And a tab **Reconnect** swaps the sessionId WITHOUT remounting (pitfall #69), so the mount-time request never fires again and the hold would have sat until that net expired; the sessionId-change effect now requests too, ordered AFTER the ref update the `onGameEvent` filter reads. Both comments in the replay handler that documented the old behaviour were corrected in the same change — one of them read "fresh sessions have an empty buffer, so this is a harmless no-op on a first connect", true of the buffer and wrong about the outcome. |
| B273 | **Overview card chips that explained nothing, and one unreadable label** (Sekmeht: *"let's review all of the chip and 'at a glance' items on each of the cards"*) | v0.19.0 | Two defects out of the review. **(i) Three tooltips were tautologies:** the Hidden / Invisible / Joined condition chips passed `title={label}`, so hovering "Joined" explained "Joined" - a tooltip that repeats the visible text is not an explanation (UX standard #8). Each now carries a real one, and the Joined wording is the genuinely non-obvious one: DR's `IconJOINED` marks the **follower**, so a group LEADER correctly shows no chip. That meaning was already tester-corrected (Cherisse/Agan) but lived only in an IconBar code comment where no user could reach it - surfacing it is the point. **(ii) `lpm` was the one initialism** among stat labels that are otherwise all words (`up`, `idle`, `ranks`, `rooms`, `deaths`, `locked`); renamed `lines`. Everything else audited clean - every chip and stat carries a real explanation, the flag row collapses to a single affirmative marker rather than a row of zeroes, and the condition line renders only what is not normal. One observation deliberately NOT changed: a disconnected character states it three ways (the Offline flag, the hollow dot, the dimmed card), which is defensible severity reinforcement rather than noise. The review also produced B274. |
| B272 | **The SimuCoin icon was hard to see, and sat badly on several themes** (Sekmeht, relaying feedback: *"it's pretty hard to see, and if it can be made a bronze, or copper color... currently it doesn't sit right with some themes"*) | v0.19.0 | Two independent causes, and measuring rather than eyeballing separated them. **(i) The idle treatment was far too aggressive:** `grayscale(.85) brightness(.78)` at `opacity: .55`, which computes to **2.36** contrast against a dark app bar and **1.34** against Classic Light. That is the state the button wears nearly all the time - it renders whenever an account is eligible, not only when coins are waiting - so the common case was a smudge. The loud/quiet distinction is already carried by the border tint, the glow, the sheen and the count badge, so the coin only ever needed to read as *dormant*; UX standard #1 is about removing noise, not about hiding a control. Now `grayscale(.4)` at `opacity: .9`, with brightness untouched, since lowering it is precisely what killed it on dark themes. **(ii) Gold was the wrong hue for a two-family theme set:** its value sits close to a light theme's background and its desaturated form close to a dark one's, so it had no reliable separation at either end. Recoloured to copper/bronze (`#bd6f38` core), a mid-tone that stays warm against both; the accent hue used by the ready border, tint, glow and badge moved with it so the button reads as one object. Result **3.58 / 3.04**, both clear of the 3:1 non-text floor. **A third change makes it theme-proof rather than merely better:** the coin gained an outer `.sc-coin-ring` mixed from `--text-primary`, the one part of the artwork that is NOT baked - so a depicted object with a fixed palette still keeps a visible edge on any background. Standard #9a's rider gained that clause. The dull state remains derived from the single artwork in CSS, so the two states still cannot drift, and the per-instance `useId` def namespacing (pitfall #95) is untouched. |
| B271 | **The Overview's settings could be silently reverted by a second window** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.19.0 | Found by asking the persistence question of the new feature rather than by a report. `buildSharedProfile` assembles `_shared.yaml` from ~20 sources and **every one of them reads FRESH from localStorage at save time** (`loadMyThemes()`, `loadAIConfig()`, `getItem(...)`) - localStorage is shared across windows, so a flush from any window is automatically current. That is the invariant the whole shared profile rests on, and nothing states it out loud. `overview: getOverviewPersisted()` was the **only entry reading MODULE MEMORY**, deliberately (so an unflushed working copy could not overwrite a live change) - sound within a window, and quietly breaking the cross-window invariant. Two consequences: a second window showed stale options until restart, and worse, any shared save it happened to make (a Lich setting, a SimuCoin check, a custom colour) wrote its stale overview block **over** the other window's change. Fixed with a `storage` listener beside the theme one; re-seeding memory keeps it equal to localStorage, which fixes the stale display and the revert together and preserves the reason for the memory read. Everything else on this axis came back clean and was verified rather than assumed - `coerceOptions` covers all 16 fields and is type-enforced, `optionsEqual` loops the defaults object (pitfall #130 done right), `overviewStream` correctly needs only its one `viewPrefs` registration, and the schema addition is an optional field needing no version bump. **A second, latent bug surfaced when changing the default card order:** `coerceOptions` RESTATED its enum fallbacks (`o.sort === 'tab' ? 'tab' : 'attention'`), so changing `DEFAULT_OVERVIEW_OPTIONS` would have had no effect on a missing or invalid value - the source saying one thing and the app doing another. Both branches now reference the defaults object. See pitfall #121. |
| B270 | **Typing while the Overview was open could send commands to the game invisibly** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.19.0 | The Overview is an overlay that deliberately leaves the session shell laid out beneath it, so the active character's virtualised scrollback keeps measuring and returning to Session view needs no re-snap. The cost: the command bar underneath is not `display:none`, so it remains a perfectly good focus target the user cannot see. Two guards existed - a blur on entering the view, and GameWindow's document `keydown` early-return - and **neither is sufficient**, because a document listener does not control where the BROWSER routes a key; whatever holds focus gets it. Three routes put focus back: a GameWindow **mounting** while the view was open (`autoFocus` plus two mount effects, none view-aware - reached by connecting a character or moving one into the window), the cursor-marker `@` path in `sendCommandSequence` (newly reachable because v0.19.0 routes the Overview bar and Quick Send through `dispatchUserText`), and no focus-restore on LEAVING, which left the caret nowhere so the first thing typed after returning went nowhere. Symptom in all three: you type, nothing appears, and Enter still reaches the game - the same invisible-input class as B268. Fixed with one guarded `focusCommandInput` that every programmatic focus routes through (so a new call site cannot forget), `autoFocus` gated on the same condition since an attribute cannot go through a helper, and a focus-on-leave counterpart. A fourth, separate hole: the Overview bar's type-anywhere listener guarded on a tag DENYLIST, which a modal holding focus on a plain `<button>` passes - so typing pulled the caret out of a modal into a field behind it. It now guards on the LOCATION of focus (body, or inside the Overview), which stays correct for whatever modal is added next. New **pitfall #131**. |
| B269 | **Quick Send and the Overview's input bar reached NOBODY - every send a silent no-op** (Sekmeht: *"none of the commands are making it to those character's sessions... launching a script in lich via ; doesn't go to any game windows or react"*) | v0.19.0 | A regression introduced by B268's fix, and the `;script` detail is what cracked it: an alias could plausibly fail a dozen ways, but a Lich script command failing on *every* character means nothing is arriving at all. The IPC channel map existed in **three** copies - the exported `IPC` in `shared/types.ts` plus private `const CH` objects in `main.ts` and `preload.ts` - and the new channel went into two of them. `CH.SEND_USER_TEXT` in main was therefore `undefined`, so `ipcMain.on(undefined, handler)` registered a listener on a channel literally named **"undefined"** while the renderer published on `send-user-text`. Note what did not catch it: both builds passed (valid code), the renderer `tsc` passed, and every individual site reads perfectly. `ipcMain.on`/`webContents.send` take a plain `string`, so an `undefined` channel is a silent no-op at BOTH ends. Located in one command - `grep -c send-user-text dist/main/main.js` returned **0** while preload and the renderer bundle both had it - after a long stretch spent theorising about alias resolution instead. **The diagnostic signature is the reusable part: a feature that fails COMPLETELY and SILENTLY across every target, with no error anywhere, is a WIRING break; grep the BUILT output for the route string before re-reading the logic.** Fixed structurally: both private copies deleted, main and preload now use the shared list (values were diffed first and agreed, so the swap was behaviour-preserving). **And the reason it could ship at all:** `tsconfig.json` includes only `["src/renderer", "src/shared"]`, so `npx tsc --noEmit` had **never looked at `src/main`** - the whole main process was outside Check zero for the project's life. `tsc -p tsconfig.main.json` reports it on the exact line and is now part of Check zero. See pitfall #127. |
| B268 | **A Quick Send arrived invisibly, and a `/command` sent that way was forwarded to DragonRealms as literal text** (Sekmeht: *"I noticed this by quicksending 'wave', but on the 2nd character I checked, that command was not present and seen as an input, all I saw was the response from the game server"*) | v0.19.0 | Quick Send wrote straight to the socket with a raw `window.api.sendCommand`, which skips the renderer-side echo entirely - B199's signature, reached from the other end. So the emote ran on every target while the receiving characters showed only the game's *reply*, with no `>wave` input line; it read as the game acting on its own. The same raw path never reached the slash intercept at the top of `dispatchUserText`, so `/highlight add ...` typed into Quick Send was sent to the game as if typed in-game - a client command must never leave the client. Fixed by giving both surfaces one bridge: a send addressed to a `sessionId` is delivered to the GameWindow that owns it and run through its normal input path, so it echoes, logs, resolves aliases and splits on `;` - indistinguishable from typing it there, which was the ask. **The routing detail is load-bearing:** it hops through MAIN rather than a renderer DOM event, because Quick Send deliberately targets the CROSS-WINDOW roster and a DOM event only reaches its own window - a decoupled character would have silently received nothing, quietly re-creating the bug in a narrower case. Consequence worth knowing: text arriving this way now also splits on `;` and resolves aliases, so the pre-v0.19.0 notes saying Quick Send does neither are superseded. |
| F100 | **Show the last-known SimuCoin balance** (Sekmeht: *"it would be nice for it to show the balance from the last check"*) | v0.18.6 | The balance was ALREADY scraped on every check and already on `SimuCoinStatus` — used for the claim toast, then thrown away, so the popover could only ever say "nothing to claim". Now surfaced in both places, with **no new network work** (same once-per-launch check, no polling, no extra sign-in). **Persistence** was the missing half: main's `lastStatus` is an in-memory Map, so a restart forgot the figure and Settings read "Not checked yet" until the launch check finished; `lastBalance`/`lastCheckedAt` now ride the per-account config into `_shared.yaml` (optional fields → old files load unchanged, no migration), recorded at the single `App.runSimucoin` choke point every check route converges on. **Honesty rules, per §42.4's posture:** `rememberBalance` no-ops on a failed check so an outage keeps the previous figure *and its real age* rather than blanking it or stamping a fresh timestamp on a stale reading; the age is never optional (a bare number reads as "now") and is RELATIVE, since the reader's question is "is this current?"; unknown renders nothing rather than a placeholder; revoking consent drops the cache. **Surfaces:** Settings owns per-account detail (a quieter second line under the state line — state is *what needs doing*, balance is *what you have*); the coin popover gets ONE aggregated line that never grows with the roster (pitfall #109 / B235), sums only KNOWN balances, says "across 2 of 3 accounts" when partial, and quotes the OLDEST reading in the sum as the honest bound on how current a total is. One formatter (`simucoinBalanceText`) serves both so they can't drift (B234). **Trap hit while building, worth noting:** `loadSimuCoinConfig`'s coerce rebuilds each entry (pitfall #121, handled) — but Settings' `setSc` ALSO restated the shape inline as `Partial<{consented, autoClaim}>`, silently excluding the new fields at the write path. `tsc` caught it; both now reference `SimuCoinAccountConfig`. **And a THIRD site, caught only by a pre-ship pass and the worst of them:** `importSharedProfile` ([profile.ts](src/renderer/profile.ts)) had its own inline copy of the coerce that rebuilt each entry as `{consented, autoClaim}` — and it runs at EVERY LAUNCH, so it would have stripped `lastBalance`/`lastCheckedAt` on startup and silently defeated the whole persistence half of this feature. It restated the type inline too, so `tsc` was blind to it. Fixed structurally rather than by patching the copy: one exported `coerceSimuCoinConfig` now serves both readers, so the two can no longer drift (pitfall #121 compounded by #127 — two code paths answering one question must share a definition, not a convention). Verified by bundling the real module and asserting a `_shared.yaml`-shaped record survives the round trip, that a hostile record still coerces safe, and that a failed check leaves the stored figure untouched. Design: DESIGN §42.9. |
| F99 | **Confirm before a close that logs 2+ characters out** (Sekmeht: *"if there's multiple characters, a prompt stating Are you sure you want to disconnect all characters and quit?"*) | v0.18.6 | Closing the primary window quits the app and drains every session — correct behaviour, but one reflexive click away from losing position, roundtime and whatever was in progress across several characters. Now confirms when **2 or more** are connected, naming them (a count is a warning; the names are what let you notice an alt you had forgotten). Counts **connected sessions, not tabs** — the reporting case had a disconnected tab, which costs nothing to close — and app scope spans **all windows**, since closing the primary kills decoupled windows' characters too. Threshold is 2 deliberately: prompting at one would prompt on essentially every quit, and a dialog people learn to click through protects nothing. Lives in `win.on('close')` so X / Cmd+Q / File → Quit / taskbar close all inherit it. Rendered as the canonical themed modal (UX #10, `--modal-*` tokens, z 10000) with a **native fallback** if the renderer doesn't ack, because main blocks on the answer and a dead renderer would otherwise make the app unquittable. Update installs bypass it (`quitAlreadyConfirmed`) — `quitAndInstall()` routes through `app.quit()`, so without that, clicking Install would raise a quit dialog you already answered and cancelling would silently abandon the install. **That bypass flag SELF-HEALS after 10s** (found in the cross-platform pre-ship check): it was latched forever, so if `quitAndInstall()` didn't actually quit — nothing staged, or the OS refused — the confirmation stayed silently disabled for the rest of the session. Restoring it is the safe direction; the worst case is one extra prompt on a quit the user meant anyway. **Cross-platform:** every close route reaches this, including `{ role: 'close' }` (Cmd/Ctrl+W) on the primary, which is correct since closing the primary IS the quit; "Close Character" is a separate menu item and does not. The native fallback's `response` is an INDEX into `buttons`, so `response === 1` is correct regardless of per-platform button ordering, and `noLink` is ignored off Windows. On macOS the confirm cancels an OS-initiated logout the same way any unsaved-work prompt does — deliberate for live game sessions, but a behaviour change worth knowing on that platform. | Full design: DESIGN §13.9; failure modes: pitfall #128. |
| B267 | **A one-shot trigger re-armed itself after a restart** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.18.6 | Found by sweeping for the same shape as B266. `triggerCallbacks.disableTrigger` (how a `oneShot` trigger disables itself after firing) called `saveTriggers`, which writes localStorage — but scheduled no profile save, so the YAML kept `enabled: true`. `importCharacterProfile` restores `state` from the YAML on the next connect, so the trigger came back **armed** and could fire a second time. Intermittent for the same reason as B266: any other change that scheduled a save would carry the disable along. Fixed by scheduling the save in the same handler. **Follow-up from the same release's bug check:** the first fix put `scheduleProfileSave` *inside* the `setTriggers(prev => …)` updater, following the pre-existing `saveTriggers` line — but StrictMode double-invokes updaters in dev, so both side effects ran twice (a doubled localStorage write, which is what bites with a big imported ruleset — pitfall #90). Reworked to compute from a `triggersRef` mirror with the side effects outside the updater, per this file's own house rule. Production was never affected (the double-invoke is dev-only); the same shape still exists in `setPinnedSkills` and the macro seeds, both idempotent and left alone. |
| B266 | **A theme picked from the Theme Picker reverted after closing and reopening** (Sekmeht) | v0.18.6 | Reported as a shutdown-persistence problem; it wasn't. The close path is sound — `__flushProfileSaves` exists and main calls it — but it flushes only *pending* saves, and **nothing was ever pending**. `ThemePicker`'s `onThemeChange` was wired as `id => setCurrentThemeId(id)` with no `scheduleProfileSave`. `applyTheme` had already written `lichborne.theme` to localStorage, so the theme *looked* persistent, while the character YAML kept the old id — and `importCharacterProfile` copies `YAML.theme` **back into localStorage** on the next connect (profile.ts), reverting the pick. Intermittent, which is why it survived: any OTHER change that scheduled a save would rebuild the profile from localStorage and carry the new theme with it, so only a session where the theme was the sole change lost it. Exactly the B56 shape (`handleFocusChange` missing the same call). **The rule this keeps re-teaching: writing localStorage is HALF a per-character change** — Principle #1 makes the YAML the truth, so the `scopedKey` write and the `scheduleProfileSave` belong in the same handler. A sweep of all 62 persistence writes in GameWindow found one further instance (B267); `detectedGuild` looked like a third but is a false positive (its save happens inside `handleFocusChange`). **Known remaining gap, deliberately left:** `saveCommandHistory` has the same shape, but it writes on every command, so scheduling a save there means a full profile serialize + disk write roughly every 2.5s during active play — the trade wants a decision, not a reflex. |
| B265 | **Trigger notifications worked only by ACCIDENT of a permissive handler — made deliberate** *(audit-found, not tester-reported)* | v0.18.5 | Flagged during the Electron 43.2 platform audit as "the `notify` trigger action can never fire", because `setPermissionRequestHandler` allowed only `local-fonts` and `midi`. **That diagnosis was wrong, and testing it before changing anything is what caught it.** `setPermissionCheckHandler` was `() => true`, which blanket-granted every permission CHECK — and `Notification.permission` is a check, not a request — so it read `granted`, the action's first branch fired, and notifications worked. Meanwhile `Notification.requestPermission()` resolved `denied` from the request handler: the two handlers actively contradicted each other. Verified all three states with a throwaway Electron harness: **(a)** handlers as shipped → `permission: granted`, notify works; **(b)** one shared allowlist including `notifications` → `granted`, and `requestPermission` finally agrees; **(c)** shared allowlist WITHOUT `notifications` → `denied`, notify silently does nothing. **(c) is why this was worth fixing** — the obvious security hardening (make the check handler mirror the request allowlist) would have silently killed a shipped feature, a landmine sitting under a change someone would eventually make for good reasons. Fix: ONE `ALLOWED_PERMISSIONS` set (`local-fonts`, `midi`, `notifications`) shared by both handlers, so the feature works by intent and the two can no longer disagree. Also a **tightening** — `() => true` was granting geolocation, media, serial, HID and USB to any check; verified nothing needs them (only `queryLocalFonts` and `requestMIDIAccess` route through a permission check; clipboard deliberately goes through main IPC per pitfall #28). Evidence recorded in the code comment. See pitfall #127. |
| B263 | **The map felt sluggish while walking — and turning Genie Map Animations off made the TEXT CONSOLE faster too** (Sekmeht: *"the Genie Animations also makes the console less sluggish and the text flow better... when its off I mean"*) | v0.18.5 | The cross-surface symptom was the diagnostic key: one setting on the map changing the responsiveness of an unrelated subsystem means **main-thread contention**, not a defect in either. Root cause: [map-panel.css](src/renderer/styles/map-panel.css) froze animations with `.genie-pan-dragging *` and `.genie-anim-off *`. Blink builds a style **invalidation set** per selector — `.a .b` invalidates only descendants carrying `.b`, but `.a *` invalidates the **entire subtree**. The pan group holds ~1057 room groups (each a `<g>` + rect + label) plus arcs and auras, so every toggle of the class forced a full-subtree style recalculation — and `genie-pan-dragging` is applied on `isDragging \|\| inMotion`, with `inMotion` flipping true on *every room change*, so walking toggled it continuously. Style recalc is main-thread work, and the renderer's main thread is shared with React, Virtuoso and the whole game-text pipeline — hence the console improving when the map's animations were off (that path applies `genie-anim-off` **permanently**, so it never toggles and never re-invalidates). Fix: both rules now enumerate the 18 animated classes explicitly. Verified behaviour-identical by script — 18 animated classes, `anim-off` covers all 18, the transient freeze covers 16 exempting exactly the ping and heartbeat — so only the invalidation cost changed. The two `animation-play-state: running !important` counter-rules were deleted; the exemptions are now expressed by omission. **Note the four `genie-mote-drift-*` classes declare their animation via LONGHAND** (`animation-name`/`animation-duration`), so a `grep "animation:"` sweep misses them — the list was built by parsing rule blocks, not by eye. Triage note: `mapAnimations` is a **per-character** setting, so turning it off on one character leaves every other tab paying full price ("I turned it off and it's still slow" usually means that, not a failed fix). Full write-up: CLAUDE.md pitfall #126. |
| B264 | **Mouse-wheel zoom on the Genie map was choppy, while dragging was smooth** (Sekmeht) | v0.18.5 | The drag/zoom asymmetry is the tell. Dragging changes only *translate*, so the CTM **scale** is constant and the rasterized subtree is reused. Zooming changes scale, which re-rasterizes ~1057 node groups **and** recomputes stroke geometry for every `vectorEffect="non-scaling-stroke"` element (the node rects, the arcs) — non-scaling stroke width is defined against the CTM, so a scale change invalidates all of it by definition. On top of that, the wheel handler called `setTransform` once per **event**, and a wheel fires well above 60Hz (free-spin wheels and Precision touchpads burst far higher) — so one flick queued more re-renders and more full-SVG re-rasterizations than there were frames to draw them in. Fix: wheel deltas now **coalesce into one rAF-batched transform commit** — factors multiply, the latest cursor position anchors the zoom so it still tracks under the pointer. The clamp is applied to the *result* rather than the factor (a burst that overshoots `MAX_SCALE` lands exactly on the limit instead of being rejected), and the updater returns `prevT` unchanged on a no-op so sitting at a zoom limit doesn't mint a new transform object. Landed alongside a same-release change that drops the camera glide during zoom. **`non-scaling-stroke` was deliberately KEPT** — removing it is the largest remaining zoom win but would make node borders scale with the map (chunky zoomed in, near-invisible zoomed out); that is a visual call, and zoom was judged good enough without it (Sekmeht). |
| F98 | **Stop a Team Login part-way** (Sekmeht: *"I dont see the cancel option when logging in my team characters, I only see it when logging in an individual character"*). A sequential run of five characters could take several minutes of Lich waits with no escape, while the single-character path had gained a working Cancel in B261. | v0.18.4 | **A REVERSED decision, not an oversight.** The code carried an explicit note — *no cancel button mid-sequence (would leave a partially-connected state)* — and the objection was real but pointed the wrong way: partial connection is unavoidable the moment the run starts, so the choice was never partial-versus-clean, it was *partial with an escape* versus *partial while you sit through every remaining wait*. Labelled **Stop**, not Cancel, because it deliberately promises less than the single-character Cancel: `window.api.login` cannot be aborted, so the character in flight lands and is KEPT (tearing it down would waste a wait of up to 30s and leave its account slot churning), and Stop means only *attempt no more*. Skipped characters are reported as **skipped, muted and not as failures** — nothing went wrong and they can simply be connected afterwards — and the summary title says the run was stopped, so it does not read as the team having come up short by accident. The flag is a REF, because the loop body holds a stale closure over any state value for the whole run; a small mirrored state exists purely so the button can show *Stopping…*. The button hides on the last character, where it could not do anything. |
| B262 | **A symbol glyph can arrive as a COLOUR emoji on macOS and stop obeying `color`.** Codepoints that are `Emoji=Yes, Emoji_Presentation=No` render monochrome on Windows out of Segoe UI Symbol, but macOS can resolve them through Apple Color Emoji instead — and a colour glyph ignores the `color` property, so it stops matching whatever band or text it sits in. Found by a macOS-focused pass over v0.18.4 rather than by a tester: the new Teams section header uses U+2694 (crossed swords), which is exactly this class, and a coloured version of it would have reproduced B260's symptom by a different route. | v0.18.4 | Appended **U+FE0E** (VARIATION SELECTOR-15, *text* presentation — the opposite of U+FE0F, whose emoji presentation is what the original comment correctly warned against). **Best-effort, not a guarantee:** VS15 still requires a text font that has the glyph, so if a Mac tester reports a coloured icon the fix is a non-emoji codepoint such as U+2691, not more selectors. **26 pre-existing bare glyphs of the same class remain** — `⚙` x14 (Lich Setup, panel gears), `⚠` x7, `⚡` x4, plus one in RoomPanel — and were deliberately NOT swept: nothing here can verify macOS rendering, no Mac tester has mentioned coloured icons, and changing 26 call sites on an unverified theory is the speculative edit Principle #10 warns against. One screenshot of the Lich Setup button settles it. `⚠️` in the Moons season icons keeps its U+FE0F deliberately — that one WANTS colour. New **pitfall #125**. |
| B253 | **The moons ran a minute or two behind the community site** (Sekmeht, checked against moonwatch.dr.elanthia.online). Two independent defects, both biased LATE, stacking. (a) `remainingMinutes` subtracted `Math.floor(elapsed / 60_000)`, so the value driving the arc position changed only once per whole minute: the moon froze for 60s while the real sky moved, then jumped — and because flooring elapsed can only ever HOLD a moon back, the error was one-sided (0 to 0.98 min). (b) The orbital constants were moonwatch's PRE-v4.2 rounded integers; the script moved to OLS-fit fractional periods in v4.2 and five of our six were SHORT by up to 0.78 min, which also renders behind. | v0.18.4 | Constants now DERIVE from `Moons::CONSTANTS` in seconds, so they cannot drift again. Interpolation is continuous, anchored on the moment a countdown steps down by exactly 1 — but at the TOP of the floor bucket (`+59/60`), not its floor: `floor(seconds/60)` becomes N when seconds hits `60N+59`. The first fix anchored at N and shipped a constant ~1 minute FAST (70m against the site's 71m), which is what `STEP_TOP_MIN` and its harness assertion now pin. |
| B254 | **The sun ran ~4 minutes fast** (Sekmeht: Lichborne "sets in 137m" against the site's 141m). Not drift, a wrong MODEL. The sun was derived from observed sunrise/sunset prose with an even **180/180 day assumed** until both had been witnessed. Elanthian daylight is neither even nor fixed: 120 rois at the winter solstice, 180 at the equinoxes, 240 at the summer solstice — and on the reported day it was 170, so the assumption ran 10 minutes long. Deriving it from one observed rise-to-set gap is no better: right for that day, then drifting, because the real values are per-day-of-year. | v0.18.4 | Ported moonwatch's model VERBATIM into the new [elanthianSun.ts](src/shared/elanthianSun.ts) — both 400-entry empirical tables — plus calendar math (`CALENDAR_EPOCH`, `elanthianDate`) in [elanthianTime.ts](src/shared/elanthianTime.ts). Rise and set are stored separately because the game is not symmetric about midday: they sum to 360 on only 332 of 400 days. **Verified by evaluating moonwatch's own Ruby `DRTime` module and diffing against it — 52 timestamps x 10 fields, 2104 assertions, zero mismatches** — then reproducing the report exactly (T-600s gives 141m). The sun is now a pure function of server time like lunar phase: no Lich, no feed, no witnessed transition, correct on the first frame direct-SGE. |
| B255 | **`INV HELP` rendered as a sheared table** (Sekmeht). Rows beginning with a `<d>` command link lost their indentation and jumped to column 0 while plain-text rows beside them kept theirs, splitting the table in half. | v0.18.4 | The parser drops whitespace-only text at line start — right for prose, wrong in MONO mode where the indent IS the layout. An indented `<d>`-led row arrives as a standalone whitespace token with nothing pending yet, so it was discarded. One-line mono carve-out, matching the one already present in the `preset` capture path. Verified by replaying the capture through the real parser: COMBAT at 4, RANGED/ARMOR at 14, non-mono still stripped. |
| B256 | **Remote-viewed text arrived shredded mid-sentence** (Q++, confirmed by Sekmeht and Elore) — shadewatch mirrors, arena view and distant gaze all broke speech across lines ("A crimson mist" / " briefly appears around" / " Sheearyn's arms."). DR splits ONE sentence across consecutive `pushStream`/`popStream` blocks on a SINGLE physical line, and the parser flushed on every push and pop — and in our architecture a flush emits a `stream-text` event, which the renderer draws as a discrete LINE. | v0.18.4 | Flushing deferred to `claimPending`, which fires only when the target stream genuinely changes: a same-stream pop-then-push merges, a real switch still splits, and the end-of-line flush closes the line either way. **The sibling clients settled the design** — Profanity flushes on both open and close too, but its flush WRITES CHARACTERS, so consecutive flushes append to one line; Frostbite caches across chunks and re-joins. None of them treats a stream boundary as a line boundary; ours did. New **pitfall #120**. 15 harness assertions built from the verbatim capture, with the negative cases carrying the weight. |
| B257 | **The Genie map stranded on "N zones loaded, waiting for game data"** with no way back (Sekmeht: right-click walk during combat, over to the Lich map, back again). Switching views UNMOUNTS GenieMapView, so a fresh mount lost the displayed zone AND the resolver's breadcrumb — and with no breadcrumb, tiers 5/5b/6 of the ladder have nothing to walk from, so an ambiguous same-titled room resolves to null. `currentLocation` then stays null, the auto-switch never fires, and the resolve effect only re-runs when pool/desc/exits change, none of which move between same-titled rooms. | v0.18.4 | MapPanel is per-character and survives the switch, so it now holds a `persist` ref carrying zone, level and breadcrumb. Deliberately NOT solved by keeping the view mounted: that would add ~1000 SVG nodes per CHARACTER for anyone parked on the Lich map (Sekmeht's multiples caution). Plus a self-heal — if the displayed zone is not in `zones` (fresh mount, or a stub click whose target XML was never loaded), adopt the player's own zone. It cannot fight manual browsing, because a zone picked from the dropdown is by definition present. |
