| B258 | **The Living Tableau overlapped its own text.** Two reports: the Bleeding/status chips sat on top of the BAL/POS/RNG gauges (Sekmeht), and a UI/UX pass found speech bubbles overlapping each other in a busy room. | v0.18.4 | The gauge clamp reserved `2 x gaugeH`, an estimate that knew nothing about the status chips hanging below the name — now measured. The bubble overlap was the placement pass CLAMPING to the header when it ran out of headroom, shoving a bubble back DOWN into the space the collision loop had just escaped, and recording the clamped rect while the loop had tested the unclamped one. It now DROPS instead — the oldest speech, since entries are newest-first — **except the newest, which always clamps and renders**, because dropping unconditionally regressed a short panel-tab stage into showing no speech at all. Also: bubbles reserve the bottom band the thought log and gauges occupy; spacing derives from the game font instead of raw px; wisp opacity floors were raised off muted-dimmed-twice; and the height estimate was corrected from an under-generous 2.3em to 2.8em. New **pitfall #123**. Confirmed by Sekmeht after the pass: *"the living tableau looks good this version."* |
| B259 | **A team saved in Team Login was invisible on the logon screen until a restart** (Sekmeht: save, then Cancel). The Launcher re-read teams only on a `refreshKey` bump, and Cancel does not bump it. | v0.18.4 | The trap CLAUDE.md documents repeatedly: a `storage` event never fires in the window that made the write. `saveBulkSets` now dispatches `BULK_SETS_CHANGED_EVENT` **from inside itself** rather than from each call site, so every writer is covered and a future one cannot forget; the Launcher listens for that plus `storage` filtered on the key — which also means a team saved in one window now appears in another window's launcher. |
| B260 | **The Teams section rendered with browser-default chrome** — a white bar with tiny centred caps and a pink glyph (Sekmeht). | v0.18.4 | Pitfall #113 for the third time this session, having been written FROM the first two. The whole accent-band treatment — including the button resets — lives on `.launcher-section--favorites > .launcher-section-header`; Teams matched only the generic label rule, which sets no background, border or text-align, so it rendered as a raw native `<button>`. Fixed by extending the SHARED rule, not by writing a parallel block. The glyph was borrowing `--header-heart`, hardcoded `--color-danger` (right for a heart, an alarm on a team icon) and now has its own class. A follow-up audit of every class rendered by the files touched this version caught one more of the same kind — `.tl-saveteam` had no rule — and that audit is recorded in the test plan as a repeatable check. |
| B261 | **Cancel on the connecting screen did not cancel** (Sekmeht: "there's no way to stop it until you login, then have to logout"). The button existed but was wired only to the 1.5s grace timer; past that it hid the overlay while the login ran to completion in main, so the character connected anyway. | v0.18.4 | `window.api.login` has no abort, so cancellation is honoured at the three points around it — and the check before `handleConnected` is the one that matters, because the tab is never added and a cancelled connect cannot flash a session in and out. If the login already landed it is closed with `disconnectAwait`. A cancelled FAILED connect is silent too. The limitation is stated rather than hidden: the account slot stays busy until the attempt finishes, so cancelling a live attempt raises a toast saying so. **The pre-merge check then caught the real hazard** — `pendingCancelledRef` was reset in `handleCardConnect` only, while three other paths reach `runConnect` (the tab menu's Reconnect, and both attempts of the conflict resolve), so after any cancel the next connect returned instantly and silently. Reset moved into `runConnect`. New **pitfall #122**. |
| F96 | **The Debug window is exempt from the window lock and always on top** (Sekmeht). Locked, it rendered no header at all, so there was nothing left to grab; and it could sink behind the game window and become unfindable. | v0.18.4 | One shared `isDebugWindow` predicate in freeLayout.ts drives the toggle, the lock exemption and the z-order, so they cannot disagree about which window Debug is. A DELIBERATE exception to the section 33.8 container/content line: Debug is not part of the layout you froze, it is a transient diagnostic surface. **The pre-merge check caught the z bug** — `DEBUG_Z` was an OFFSET of 10,000 while `focus()` only renormalizes above 100,000, so any window past 10,000 would outrank it and the original complaint would return after a long session. Now absolute and derived from the exported ceiling. New **pitfall #124**. |
| F97 | **Teams on the logon screen** (Sekmeht). The old Sets dropdown named a noun, explained nothing and hid its contents behind a click (polish standard #8). Replaced by a collapsible **Teams** section: name, members, Connect, and a kebab menu. Members carry state — already logged in is struck through (Connect skips them), archived is faint italic — and Connect counts only who it will actually log in. | v0.18.4 | Ordered Favorites, Teams, Accounts, with Favorites as the quick-select block holding **both** pinned teams and characters. Teams gained `favorite` and `notes` (both optional, so no profile-shape break) plus an **Edit Team** dialog with a rename-clash guard. Team Login restructured so **saving is an opt-in checkbox** rather than its own zone: unticked there is no team vocabulary in the modal at all and it is honestly a multi-character login. The load dropdown is gone, since the section is where you pick a team. One hoisted `TeamRow` serves both sections. **Two silent data-loss traps caught while building:** `coerce` rebuilds every entry and `saveBulkSets` runs it on the way OUT, so an uncopied field is destroyed rather than ignored (**pitfall #121**); and `upsertBulkSet` overwrote wholesale, so re-saving from Team Login — which knows only name and roster — would have wiped notes and pins every time. Both are now locked by the rules harness (34 to 42 cases). |
| B252 | **Linux AppImage: File → Open Installation Directory opened a throwaway temp folder.** An AppImage runs from a transient squashfs mount, so `app.getPath('exe')` resolves to something like `/tmp/.mount_LichbAbc/usr/bin` — a path that does not survive the app quitting and tells the user nothing about where their install lives. Found in a cross-platform supportability audit, not by a tester; it would have surfaced the first time anyone was asked to "open your install folder". | v0.18.3 | Electron's AppImage launcher exports `$APPIMAGE` with the real path to the `.AppImage` file, so the menu now opens the folder the user actually keeps it in. Windows, macOS and a non-AppImage Linux build fall through to the previous behaviour unchanged. New **pitfall #119**. |
| F95 | **Help → About reports the platform and architecture**, not just the version. A tester saying *"I'm on 0.18.3"* does not say whether that is Windows x64, macOS arm64 or a Linux AppImage — and the three genuinely differ (macOS ships unsigned with no auto-update, AppImage resolves its own paths, arm64 vs x64). Every support thread was opening with us asking. | v0.18.3 | Reads `v0.18.3 · macOS arm64` / `· Linux AppImage x64` / `· Windows x64`. `process.arch` and an `isAppImage` flag joined `platform` on the preload bridge. Rendered NEXT TO the version rather than tucked into a details pane, because the point is that it travels in a screenshot. Groundwork for **F88** (diagnostics bundle) in the §46 backlog. |
| B251 | **The ⋯ menu on a character card did nothing in the compact launcher** (Sekmeht): after connecting, clicking **+** to add another character opens the launcher inside a modal — and every card's more-options menu stopped responding, while connecting itself still worked. Pure z-ORDER: `ContextMenu` is **portaled to `document.body`**, so it escapes the modal's React subtree and its z-index has to win on its own. At `z-index: 500` it lost to every modal in the app (wizard 600, AI consent 620, add-character **1000**, connecting overlay 1500, Quick Send 2000). The menu opened correctly every time and painted UNDERNEATH the modal's dark scrim — which reads exactly as "the button is dead". | v0.18.3 | `.ctx-menu` raised to **2100**, clearing the highest modal. **Latent for every context menu opened from inside any modal, not just this one.** Checked the other two portaled popovers (`ContactPopover`, `ModeSwitcher`, both also at 500) and deliberately left them: both render only in the game area, so nothing can paint over them today, and raising them speculatively risks the inverse bug — a popover floating above a modal opened after it. New **pitfall #118**, which states the rule: a portaled popover must out-z the modal tier, and a new modal must stay below 2100. |
| F85 | **Team Login: leave accounts out, and save named sets** (Binu). Two gaps in one feature: *"option to uncheck some of the accounts on the screen so you don't have to log in 1 from every account you have saved"* and *"let's say I want to spend the day farming, I have a set named farm that logs in my best farming character from whichever accounts I want"*. Every saved account was previously always included, and there was no persistence at all. **Bulk Connect was also renamed Team Login** in the same pass (Sekmeht) — "bulk" described the mechanism, "team" describes the point, and it now matches what a set actually is. | v0.18.3 | Per-account include checkboxes (exclusion tracked SEPARATELY from the pick, so unticking and re-ticking restores your chosen character rather than resetting to the default), plus named sets in `SharedProfile.bulkSets` with all three Principle-#1 registrations. A set is a **template**: it stores character names, one per account by construction (DR's rule, and the only shape the picker can express); loading one replaces the whole selection; connect-time drops whoever is already on — proven against the real `planReconnect`, which also supplies the per-account keep/switch chooser when a DIFFERENT character holds an account. Launchable from the picker or straight from the launcher's **▦ Sets…** dropdown, which routes through `handleReconnectLast` and so inherits all of that vetting for free. Unknown names are DROPPED rather than erroring, because F79 archives characters rather than deleting them, so a set should survive one being archived and restored. **Bug caught in the pre-commit check:** saving a set first used the CONNECT list, which excludes already-connected accounts — so building your "farm" set while the farmer was logged in silently saved it without him, discoverable only the next day when the team came back one short. Roster and connect list are now separate functions. **Rename is display-only** — `bulkSets`, `bulkConnectSeparateWindows`, the `bulk-connect` menu action, the CSS modifier and the filename are all unchanged (ids are data, labels are display). No slash command: Team Login is a launcher action and there is no command bar before you connect. **Layout reworked in the same release** after a UX review (Sekmeht: *"does its layout make sense?"*): the body was a flat stack of identical rows opening with ~100 words of preamble, with the Save control sitting ABOVE the list its copy told you to fill in first. Now three labelled zones — **Set** (load) · **Accounts** (with a live "3 of 5 selected") · **Save as a set** (below the list, carrying the explanation of what a set IS) — plus the connect option moved inside the body (it had been between body and footer with a hardcoded 16px inset against the body's 14px, 2px out of line), a reserved-height save row so starting a save no longer shifts the list, a danger hover on Delete, and all ~15 inline `style={{}}` objects folded into `.tl-*` classes. No behaviour change. New **polish standard #11**. |
| B250 | **A Lich script that runs everywhere else dies in Lichborne on macOS** (Zithri): `;guild-h` → `--- Lich: error: invalid byte sequence in US-ASCII`, from `script.rb:956` — the regex walk over the lines of the `.lic`. Ruby derives `Encoding.default_external` from the LOCALE; we spawned Lich with no `env` at all, so it inherited Electron's, and a macOS app launched from Finder/the dock has **no `LANG`**. Ruby therefore read the script as US-ASCII, and a single non-ASCII byte anywhere in the file — a curly quote, an em dash, an accented name in a comment — killed it before it ran. Frostbite works because Qt calls `setlocale()` at startup, so the Ruby it spawns inherits a real locale. | v0.18.3 | The Lich child now gets `LANG=en_US.UTF-8`, but ONLY when it would otherwise have none: Windows inherits unchanged (code page, no `LANG` convention, already working), and an existing `LANG`/`LC_ALL` is respected as a deliberate choice. **Same root cause as `resolveRubyw`'s note** — a GUI-launched app inherits no shell environment; we knew it had no `PATH`, and it has no `LANG` either. Likely explains other "works elsewhere, fails here" script reports on macOS/Linux, since it hits ANY script containing a non-ASCII character. New **pitfall #117**. |
| B249 | **The Lich Dashboard's search highlight sat half a line off** (Sekmeht, reproduced at line 1083 of a 1105-line profile). Long-standing and never reproducible before, because it is INVISIBLE in short files: the two reports together — *fine in the Ruby editor, off in the YAML profile* — are what identified it as ACCUMULATION rather than a mismatch. All four metrics were correctly pinned to the same `1.3rem`; the value itself was the problem. `1.3rem` = 20.8px, and Chromium lays out on a **1/64px grid**, where 20.8 is not representable (20.8 × 64 = 1331.2) — so the USED height snaps to 20.796875px while `getComputedStyle` still reports `"20.8px"`. The overlay positions itself as `paddingTop + n × lineHeight`, so 0.003px/line accumulates to ~3.4px by line 1083, downward. | v0.18.3 | All four sites pinned to `21px`. A whole pixel is exactly representable, so computed == used and the arithmetic is exact at any line number; row height moves 0.2px and all four move together, so the gutter stays aligned (pitfall #62). No code change. The mechanism is INFERRED from the arithmetic — an Electron harness to measure it directly was abandoned after its entry-point resolution broke on the `--` in the scratchpad path and threw dialogs at the tester — but an integer line-height can only make `n × lineHeight` more accurate, so the fix is safe either way. New **pitfall #116**. |
| B248 | **The script list labelled the user's own scripts "core"** (Zithri): *"custom scripts I've written are all bundled under core right now. Not sure how it's delineating core vs. custom."* Not a macOS bug and not mis-filed — the handler uses `path.join` throughout and filters on `isFile()`, so the split is platform-correct and did exactly what it says: `scripts/custom/` → custom, everything directly in `scripts/` → core. His scripts were in `scripts/`. The LABEL was the defect: "core" reads as *shipped with Lich* when it only ever meant *in the scripts root*, and nothing can actually tell a Lich-authored script from a user's at that path — there is no manifest. | v0.18.3 | Filters and badges now name the FOLDER — `scripts/` and `custom/` — with tooltips explaining each. The edit warning was overclaiming the same way (*"Core Lich script"* would have appeared over his own file); it now leads with the location, which is true either way, and keeps the real advice: copy it into `scripts/custom/`, which Lich checks first and updates leave alone. The `'core' | 'custom'` VALUES are untouched — they pick the directory in `write-lich-script` and are the CSS modifier suffixes. Labels are display, ids are data. |
| F84 | **Moons: the sun is properly behind the moons, and a near-new moon silhouettes as it crosses it** (Sekmeht). Two parts, from one observation: *"I saw the sun setting then realized Xibar was there too, but Xibar was invisible… it's basically nearing its new moon stage."* | v0.18.3 | **(a) Occlusion.** Paint order was already correct (sun at L1520, moons L1541, weather L1860) — the real cause was a side-effect of F64a: masking each moon to its LIT part leaves the unlit limb fully transparent, so the moon stopped occluding anything and the sun shone through its dark side. Every up-moon is now punched out of the sun's layer at FULL disc radius. Subtractive on purpose — painting an opaque disc behind each moon would have to match the sky, and the sky is an HTML gradient layer BENEATH the SVG, so nothing there can sample it. **(b) Silhouette.** A shadowed disc + rim fades in as a moon nears the sun, scaled by proximity × how unlit it is — so it only ever appears in the situation that prompted it, and a gibbous moon (which already reads) is left alone. The rim is deliberately lighter than the fill: a setting sun puts this against a darkening sky, where a pure-black edge would vanish (the Katamba-glow lesson). |
| F83 | **Three-state connection dot** (Sekmeht). The dot beside the wordmark only ever described the ACTIVE tab, so a character that dropped in a background tab was invisible — the dot sat green while someone was logged out. | v0.18.3 | Green now means *everything is fine*, not merely *this tab is fine*: **red** = the tab you're looking at is disconnected (unchanged), **yellow** = this tab is fine but another open tab is not, **green** = all clear. The tooltip names who is down, so the warning points somewhere. A tab mid-RECONNECT is deliberately excluded (`reconnectingIds` was already being passed in) — it is already being handled, and a warning you cannot act on is noise. Scope is THIS WINDOW's tabs, i.e. exactly what the tab bar beside the dot shows, so the warning always has something you can click to; a decoupled window carries its own bar. Colour only, no pulse — motion is decoration, colour is signal. |
| F82 | **Choose the shortest command worth remembering for ↑/↓ recall** (Qij, via Sekmeht). A history full of `n` / `s` / `ne` buries the long command you actually wanted back. | v0.18.3 | Settings → Behavior, 0–5 characters, **default 0 = exactly the pre-F82 behaviour** (Sekmeht: *"the default setting should read what it is today"*), plus `/history` (status) and `/history min 3` (alias `/hist`). APP-WIDE in `_shared.yaml` via `SharedProfile.commandHistory` with all three Principle-#1 registrations — the history DATA stays per-character, only the preference is shared. Three calls beyond the ask: **slash commands are always remembered** whatever the threshold (`/ai`, `/mode` are short but are exactly what you want to recall, and are never the movement spam this filters); length is measured **after trimming**, so `"  s  "` can't slip past; and the setting is read FRESH at push time rather than held in a ref, so a Settings change applies instantly to every open character with no cross-window event (the `loadSessionLogSettings` precedent). Only affects NEW entries. The gate needed exactly one insertion point because `dispatchUserText` is the sole writer of history. |
| B247 | **Lich Setup showed no path validity until you clicked Auto Detect** (Sekmeht: *"is there an indicator saying the lich path is invalid when autodiscover happens?"*). The per-field ✓/✕ and the status banner both derived from `discoveryResult`, which is `null` until Auto Detect runs — so opening Lich Setup on a machine with an EMPTY Ruby path (the deliberate macOS/Linux default) showed nothing at all: no ✕, no warning. The one screen that could have explained our first Mac tester's failure stayed silent unless he happened to press a button. | v0.18.2 | Per-field icons now run their own existence check on open and (debounced) as you type, independent of Auto Detect. Uses ONLY `rubyAlreadyValid`/`lichAlreadyValid` — "does this exact path exist" — never the discovered candidates, so it can't imply your path is fine because a Ruby was found elsewhere; and it omits `probeDesktop`, because that raises a macOS privacy dialog that must only ever follow a click. The BANNER still reports the last Auto Detect, so its meaning is unchanged (separate `drRubyOk`/`drLichOk`). |
| B246 | **macOS: clicking Connect showed the "Add account" wizard, with no explanation** (ohbeanz). Reported as a Lich failure — *"Keeps getting me to try and add an acct... that's when he tries to connect using the lich chip"* — but Lich was never reached. `runConnect` does `if (password === null) { setShowWizard(true); return }`: a character whose password isn't saved sends you to the only screen that can capture one. Bare, that reads as the app forgetting your character and demanding it be re-added. Platform-independent in principle; it only surfaces where the credential store is unavailable or the password was never saved. | v0.18.2 | The wizard now states why it opened (accent banner, step 1): *"Lichborne needs the password for account X to connect Y. It is not saved on this machine."* Accent, not red — a missing prerequisite isn't an error the user caused. Also fixed alongside: the "password saving is unavailable" notice named **GNOME Keyring and KWallet on every platform**, which is nonsense on macOS/Windows; it now names Keychain / Credential Manager / the Linux keyrings per platform. **Still unverified:** whether `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` is genuinely false on his Mac (it should work through Keychain even ad-hoc signed) — ask whether "Remember password" was greyed out. |
| B245 | **macOS: the app could not be quit — Force Quit was the only way out** (ohbeanz: *"literally can't close the app 😂"*). The primary window's `close` handler calls `preventDefault()` to hold the app open for the session drain. **Preventing a window close CANCELS an in-progress `app.quit()`**, so after the drain nothing re-issued it. Windows and Linux only recovered by accident, via `window-all-closed → app.quit()` for non-darwin; macOS deliberately skips that by convention, leaving the app alive with zero windows. Same path for the red X, ⌘Q, and File → Quit — which is why none of them worked. | v0.18.2 | `runAppShutdown` now ends with an explicit `app.quit()` once the flush + drain settle. Fixes every platform at the source and demotes the `window-all-closed` branch to a redundant safety net rather than the mechanism. New **pitfall #114**. |
| B244 | **Add Character wizard: footer buttons were different sizes and their labels word-wrapped** (Sekmeht). Two independent causes. **(a)** `.wiz-btn-lich-setup` was never added to the shared metrics selector group, so it rendered with no border-radius, no padding and the BROWSER'S default UI font while its four neighbours had all three. **(b)** The finish label is count-dependent (`Add 8 characters`, `Pick at least one`) and the row needed ~466px inside a 460px modal — flex items shrink by default, so the buttons compressed until their text wrapped, which is also what made them look mismatched. | v0.18.2 | Lich Setup joined the metrics group; modal widened 460 → 520px (sized for the WORST-CASE label, verified at 477 of 488px); `white-space: nowrap` + `flex-shrink: 0` so a label can never wrap inside a button; `line-height: 1` so the deliberately-smaller Lich Setup button is still the same HEIGHT; and `flex-wrap: wrap` on the footer as a last-resort safety net. The conflict dialog's `Disconnect {character} and continue` — the longest label in the wizard, and width-variable by character name — became `Disconnect and continue` (the name is already stated twice in the body). Cause (a) is **pitfall #113**. |
| B243 | **"+ Add account" pre-filled an account you already had** (Sekmeht: *"I'd imagine when I click add account it should give me a blank account to add, not re-add it like it's new"*). `useState(() => prefillAccount ?? localStorage.getItem('lichborne.account') ?? '')` — `prefillAccount` is set ONLY by the per-account ↺ Refresh button, but the fallback fired on the blank path too and pre-filled the last-used account. It looked like adding a new account while actually re-submitting an existing one, re-saving its stored password on the way through. | v0.18.2 | Add starts empty (`useState(prefillAccount ?? '')`); Refresh still gets its prefill because it passes one explicitly. Verified the wizard fully unmounts between opens (both close paths clear `showWizard` AND the prefill), so the `useState` initialiser re-runs and a Refresh-open can't leave a stale name behind on the next Add. |
| B242 | **"cloudless" was parsed as cloudy.** `\bcloud` matched *cloudless*, and because `clear` is unset whenever `clouds` is set, `"The sky is cloudless and bright."` produced 35% cloud cover and drew clouds on a clear sky. Pre-existing; surfaced by the new weather-severity harness rather than by a tester. | v0.18.2 | `cloud(?!less)`. The harness (`tmp-moon-harness/weather.mjs`, 21 assertions) locks the whole degree ladder — it was written for F81 and immediately earned its keep by finding this. |
| F81 | **Graded weather severity** (Sekmeht: *"I'd like to add adjectives too, like completely, very… so Lichborne can add more clouds, take away clouds"*). Weather prose now yields a 0–1 `cover` and `precip` rather than plain booleans, via a most-specific-first DEGREES table plus condition floors (a thunderstorm can't sit under a mostly-clear sky). | v0.18.2 | Cover drives cloud COUNT and per-cloud SIZE, so a sky thickens by having more cloud in it. An **overcast deck** (a gradient solid overhead, thinning to nothing at the horizon — deliberately the inverse of the fog gradient) fades in only past 0.9 cover, i.e. wording that genuinely means a closed sky. **The fog effect was removed entirely** (Sekmeht: *"let's avoid the fog effect in general"*) — a translucent haze washes out the sky, moons and landscape at once and reads as a broken render; `WeatherFx.fog` is still parsed and still suppresses shooting stars and floors cover, it just has no layer. Also: `"very cloudy"` originally rendered the deck and got reported as looking foggy, which is what forced the 0.6 → 0.9 threshold. |
| F80 | **"Show password" checkbox in the Add Account wizard** (Zithri, via Sekmeht: *"can save a 'this isn't working' complaint because someone mistakenly typed their password wrong"*). | v0.18.2 | The only place in the live UI where a password is TYPED rather than reused from the OS credential store (`LoginScreen` still has a field but is never rendered — it survives as a type host). Local state only, never persisted, gone when the wizard closes. Both `input[type=text]` and `[type=password]` were already in one style rule, so the field doesn't shift when revealed. |
| F79 | **Remove an account from the logon screen** (Sekmeht). `✕ Remove` on each account header, beside `↺ Refresh`, behind a confirmation that NAMES the characters (the section may be collapsed, so a bare count wouldn't tell you what's going). | v0.18.2 | **Archives, never deletes** (Sekmeht: *"the characters' profiles and logs should remain, just in case they are added back"*): YAMLs move to `profiles/Archive/` and adding the account back restores them, so themes, layout, automations and contacts survive a break. Safe by construction — both directory readers filter on `.endsWith('.yaml')`, so a subdirectory is invisible to them; restore refuses to clobber a live profile of the same name; moves use `rename` with a copy+unlink fallback for the cross-filesystem `EXDEV` case; profiles are archived BEFORE the password is forgotten (the reverse order could leave listed characters that can't sign in). Logs are never touched. **Caught in the bug check before shipping:** the first cut collected characters from the visible sections, so with "Show hidden" off it would have archived only the visible ones while still forgetting the password — orphaning hidden profiles that could never sign in again. |
| F78 | **"Panels" → "Layout", "Panel Manager" → "Layout Manager", and a real mode chooser** (Sekmeht: *"there needs to be a better way to display/show this from a UI/UX perspective"*). The old UI was a single banner describing whichever mode you were already in, with one "Switch to…" button — so the choice, and the fact that one option is being retired, were both invisible until you clicked. | v0.18.2 | Two cards state it plainly: what each mode is, which you're in (`In use`), that Static is `Legacy`, and that switching converts your layout. Same shape whichever is active (UX standard #2). Legacy is muted rather than alarming, and regains its background when it IS your mode. Free-mode controls became described rows (Lock / Fit bars / Rebuild) with visible explanations instead of hover-only tooltips. **Rename is display-only** — every persisted identifier (`layoutMode`, `freeWindows`, `freeLayoutLocked`, `panelWidth`, `panelFontSizes`, the zone keys, `toggle-panels`) is untouched, so no migration. The one place it mattered: Transfer serialises categories by **id**, so `label: 'Panel Layout'` → `'Layout'` while `id: 'layout'` stayed — renaming it would have made every existing `.lb.yaml` silently skip the category. |
| F77 | **Drag a stream from one window to another**, and **tab reorder no longer follows the layout lock** (Sekmeht). | v0.18.2 | Same gesture as reordering, released over another window's tab strip. The move is ONE atomic state update (`adoptTabIntoWindow`) because two steps could duplicate or lose the stream, and a duplicate tab id in one window breaks React keys; it lives in GameWindow because a frame only knows its own tabs. Transport needed a custom MIME type — the browser hides `getData()` until drop, so a type's PRESENCE is the only thing a strip can inspect at hover time. The lock change settles the line the lock draws: **it freezes the CONTAINER (position, size — what you knock askew by accident), never the CONTENT.** |
| F76 | **Right-click → Close on windowed panels, streams and Experiences** (Sekmeht). The ✕ is a hover-only ~1em glyph beside the corner resize handle, absent entirely on a collapsed or locked window. | v0.18.2 | Bound on the window ROOT so it works in every state including locked, yielding to any inner surface that already claimed the event (`defaultPrevented`) — stream text, maps, Debug and the tab strip keep their own menus. **Close means the CONTENT, not the container:** a tabbed window closes its active stream (the window follows only when the last tab goes), an Experience closes itself. **Excluded entirely: chrome bars AND the game window** — equipment rather than content, recoverable only through Layout Manager → Add Window. Also added to the stream panel's own body menu, which preventDefaults first and so never saw the window-level one. Two corrections during the build: it was first gated on `!locked` (backwards — locking hides the header AND the ✕, so right-click is the only affordance left), and first closed the whole window. |
| B241 | **After "Fit bars to content", changing a window's WIDTH snapped its HEIGHT larger** (Sekmeht, on a compact-vitals window — small enough to land under the interactive resize minimum). Two defects in `beginResize`, one of them long-latent. **(a)** The minimum-size clamp ran on **both axes on every resize**, regardless of drag direction: an east-edge drag never touches `height`, so `height` reached the clamp still equal to `start.height`, was found below `min.h`, and got raised. **(b)** The floor was absolute, so a window legitimately sized under it could never keep that size. Sekmeht asked whether snapping was involved — it is not: the snap block only acts on the dragged axis and runs BEFORE the clamp, so on a width drag the height arrives untouched and the unconditional floor is the whole story. | v0.18.1 | The clamp now applies **only to the axis being dragged** (every `ResizeDir` includes at least one of e/w or n/s, and corners include both, so corner drags still clamp both). And the floor is `Math.min(perKindMinimum, currentSize)` — the minimum exists to stop a DRAG producing an unusable sliver, not to forbid a size the window already has. That matters because **`fitChromeWindows` deliberately does NOT clamp to the minimum**: a compact vitals bar is genuinely smaller than the 24px floor, and clamping the fit would have left exactly the slack the feature exists to remove. |
| B240 | **The Genie map ignored the theme until a zone rendered.** Sekmeht, on a light theme: the toolbar, dropdown, footer and tabs were all correctly light while the map canvas sat **dark brown**, showing "90 zones loaded · waiting for game data" — and it snapped to the right colour the moment a zone appeared. Cause: `.map-overlay` carried a baked `rgba(10,8,4,0.75)`. The SVG **underneath** already uses `var(--map-bg)`, which is exactly why it looked right once painted — the overlay was the only thing that never followed the theme, so every state that shows it (Genie loading, Genie "waiting for game data", the Lich map-image loader, and MapPanel's loader — four states from one rule) was dark-only. | v0.18.1 | Veil is now `color-mix(in srgb, var(--map-bg) 82%, transparent)`; `--map-bg` is force-cascaded from `--bg-app` (`MAP_STRUCTURAL_CASCADE`, pitfall #75/B161) so it tracks every theme. The waiting placeholder's inline styles were also replaced with the shared `.map-empty-*` classes so it is themed and sized by the same rules as its sibling empty states instead of a private copy, and the legend's path swatch was corrected from `#f0d060` to the `#FFD060` the map actually strokes — that one stays a LITERAL on purpose (a depicted cue that reads on any theme, Principle #4's exception). Same pass fixed the worst adjacent offenders on the same recipe: `.map-btn--stop`, the legend swatch border, the you-are-here tier sample, the found/missing room-id badges, and the location-unknown hairline. **KNOWN REMAINING: ~23 hardcoded colours are still in [map-panel.css](src/renderer/styles/map-panel.css)** — the error overlay, the detail panel and its close button, further status chips, two amber text colours. All the same dark-theme-only class and all mechanical to convert; they WILL read wrong on Parchment/Ivory/Mist/Classic Light. Left as an explicit follow-up rather than expanding the fix further. |
| B239 | **Lich Dashboard → Scripts → the Edit button was permanently disabled**, with the actively misleading tooltip *"Can't edit — this file couldn't be read"* on scripts that had read perfectly. Shipped in v0.18.0 and not reported by anyone — found by the v0.18.1 bug check. Cause: that release's pre-merge sweep added the `readOk` gate (pitfall #100 — stop a failed read's `(could not read file)` placeholder being savable over the user's real file) to BOTH tabs' Edit buttons, but only wired the **Profiles** loader to set it. `selectScript` never called `setReadOk`, so it stayed `false` for the life of the component and the Scripts editor was unreachable. | v0.18.1 | One line: `setReadOk(text !== null)` in `selectScript`, matching `selectProfile`. **How it was found is the transferable part:** `tsc --noEmit --noUnusedLocals` flagged `setReadOk` as *"declared but its value is never read"* — a state SETTER that is never called is a strong smell that whatever it gates is stuck at its initial value. `noUnusedLocals` is not on in tsconfig (7 other pre-existing dead imports project-wide, all harmless), so nothing routine catches this; running it as a one-off during a bug check is cheap and worth repeating. Note the shape of the original defect too: a guard added to two call sites but wired at only one is invisible to `tsc` under normal settings, invisible to the build, and looks CORRECT in review — the gate is right there in the JSX. |
| B238 | **macOS: the downloaded app wouldn't open — *"Lichborne is damaged and can't be opened. You should move it to the Trash."*** (ohbeanz, our first Mac tester — M5, macOS 26.5 (25F71), v0.18.0 dmg via Chrome). Not a corrupt download, and NOT the friendly "unidentified developer" prompt every doc here promised: that message is how macOS words a **missing or invalid code signature**, it has no "Open Anyway" button, and it tells the user to delete a perfectly good build. Two causes stacked. **(a)** Apple Silicon requires every executable to carry a signature and the kernel refuses an unsigned arm64 binary outright — but the release workflow sets `CSC_IDENTITY_AUTO_DISCOVERY=false` (correct: there is no certificate to find) and electron-builder then **skips signing entirely**. A comment in the workflow asserted it "ad-hoc signs automatically"; the tester's screenshot is the evidence it does not. **(b)** The download carries `com.apple.quarantine` regardless, so Gatekeeper evaluates it at launch. | v0.18.1 | **(a)** New [build/afterPack.cjs](build/afterPack.cjs) hook ad-hoc signs the packaged app (`codesign --force --deep --sign -`) before the dmg/zip are built, and **fails the build** if that errors — a silently-unsigned arm64 artifact uploads cleanly and passes the verify job's filename check, then dies in a tester's hands, which is worse than no artifact. Idempotent by `--force`; **delete the hook when a real Developer ID cert lands**, since re-signing ad-hoc over a real signature would destroy it. The workflow needs no change to USE the hook (`afterPack` is read from package.json's build block), but two things were added around it: the hook logs on every platform so its ABSENCE from a build log is detectable — verified by packaging Windows locally, where it resolved and ran (`afterPack hook: platform=win32`), which is the only way to prove the `.cjs` path loads under electron-builder 26 without a Mac — and a mac-gated **`codesign --verify --deep --strict`** step now prints the signature into the release log. That step runs AFTER the upload so it cannot block one; the real gate is the hook throwing before any artifact exists. **The hook file must be COMMITTED** — untracked, CI checks out a tree without it and the Mac build silently reverts to unsigned. **(b)** Every doc that promised "Open Anyway" now leads with `xattr -cr /Applications/Lichborne.app` and explains that "damaged" is Apple's phrasing for "not notarized" — README, User Guide (install + troubleshooting), DESIGN §41, CLAUDE.md, and both workflow comment blocks. **UNVERIFIED:** whether ad-hoc signing alone converts "damaged" into the recoverable Open Anyway prompt is not proven — there is no Mac here and CI can build but cannot exercise Gatekeeper — so the docs give `xattr` as the primary instruction and ohbeanz needs to confirm the rest on the next build. The real fix remains the $99/yr certificate + notarization (deferred by product decision, DESIGN §41). |
| F74 | **Locked Windowed Panels should drop their window chrome and read like docked panels.** TheTargonian, comparing a windowed character against a panels-mode one: *"there's gotta be a way, when 'lock windows' is enabled, to remove all of the extra space between the panels — there are 6 of those extra buffers on my setup, which is close to half a window."* Measured cause: every floating window pays ~12px at each seam — a 9px header (`.fl-titlebar` / `.fl-grip`, both on a `min-height: 9px` floor) + its 1px hairline + the two windows' 1px borders — where panels mode pays ~1px. Six stacked windows ≈ 70px. Sekmeht's call: *"when you lock the windows, have it return to the panel style borders."* | v0.18.1 | `FloatingWindow` no longer renders the title bar or grip AT ALL while `locked`, and `.fl-window--locked` drops the lift-shadow (between two flush-tiled windows a soft shadow reads as a seam) and takes `--border-subtle` — the same var `.panel-frame` uses for its own divider. Seams go ~12px → 2px and every window gains ~10px of content height. **This does not violate pitfall #74** (deterministic floating-window geometry): resize is DISABLED while locked, so every height was authored with the header PRESENT — locking can only ever GIVE the body space, never clip a chrome bar — and the stored rect is untouched either way, so the toggle is lossless in both directions. The superseded rules that merely softened the locked header (`cursor: default`, hiding the F44 grip dash) were removed, since neither element renders now. **Follow-up in the same release — the header became an OVERLAY, which is the real fix.** Sekmeht on the first cut: *"now there appears to be small gaps because they are locked where the snapping occurred… when the border/moving bar is removed, that gap persists. I think this needs to be fixed so that space is not accounted / reserved in the resizing and snapping. So maybe the grabbing/mover needs to happen inside of the window vs. being a part of the window."* Exactly right: snapping aligns OUTER edges, so hiding a header that had RESERVED ~10px only moved the space instead of removing it — panels closed up, but a fixed-height chrome window just re-centred its bar and the gap survived. `.fl-titlebar` / `.fl-grip` are now `position: absolute` over the content (quiet at 55% opacity, solid on hover/focus, z-order body → header 1 → resize handles 2 → title buttons 3 so the top and corner grips stay reachable through it), and `buildWindowsFromCurrentLayout`'s `TITLE_PX = 16` allowance became `BORDER_PX = 2` — just the window's own border, which `box-sizing: border-box` folds into the stored height. Net: the header reserves NOTHING, what you arrange unlocked is what you get locked, and lock/unlock is a pure visibility change that never touches a rect — pitfall #74 settled outright rather than argued around. Chrome windows saved by a pre-v0.18.1 build still carry the old title allowance and load a touch tall; one drag fixes them, and they are deliberately NOT auto-migrated (pitfall #93 — never rewrite a persisted rect from a measurement). **THIRD pass — the overlay silently did not apply to the GRIP.** Sekmeht reported the padding and the lock-time reshift unchanged, with screenshots of a collapsed (grip) chrome bar. Cause: `.fl-grip` is declared twice, and its own later rule ended with `position: relative` — EQUAL specificity, later in source order, so it beat the `position: absolute` in the shared `.fl-titlebar, .fl-grip` overlay block. The title bar took the override (its rule sets no `position`) and the grip did not, so the fix worked for exactly the windows that weren't the problem, while chrome bars — the ones users collapse to a grip — kept reserving ~10px. Removed the stray `position` (the `::after` dash doesn't need it: an absolutely-positioned element is itself a containing block); new **pitfall #111**. Same pass: the grip is now `opacity: 0` until window hover, because a resting band would cover half of a ~20px vitals bar now that it floats over content — the title bar keeps a resting presence since it carries the window NAME. Net effect: dragging a chrome window's bottom edge while unlocked is finally WYSIWYG for the locked result. **FOURTH pass — the bars' own DOCKED-mode separators.** Sekmeht, correctly: *"there are borders inside of the windows with borders… clearly this was artifacts from the paneling."* Each strip draws its own hairline and vertical gradient because in Static Panels it butts against the game area and must separate itself — `.icon-bar` (border-bottom + `--bg-app`), `.vitals-strip` (border-bottom + raised→sunken gradient), `.command-bar` (border-top + sunken→base gradient). Inside a floating window whose own border already does that, each became a double border plus a shaded band that reads as un-resizable padding. Now flattened under `.fl-window--chrome` (borders none, background transparent → the window's own `--bg-app`), extending the existing `.fl-body .panel-frame { border-left: none }` precedent to chrome. **Heights deliberately untouched** — `buildWindowsFromCurrentLayout` measures the DOCKED strip to size these windows, so trimming padding here would re-open the slack this release closed; dropping each bar's 1px border just leaves the window 1px taller than its content, which errs toward not clipping. **FIFTH pass — "Fit bars to content"** (Panel Manager, free + unlocked only): snaps every chrome window to its measured bar height + the 2px border, for layouts saved before v0.18.1 that still carry the old title allowance and are fiddly to drag to exactly the right height. **POSITIONS UNTOUCHED — heights only**, so a neighbour below may end up with a gap or an overlap to drag closed; auto re-tiling would move windows they deliberately placed (offered as the alternative, not chosen). It is a FIT, not a shrink: `getBoundingClientRect` reports layout height, so a window dragged smaller than its bar (clipping it under `.fl-body { overflow: hidden }`) is grown back — correct in both directions, and the pre-merge check caught this being mis-described as shrink-only in four places. This is the one measure→persist shape pitfall #93 permits — explicit user action, never automatic, never from a render — and it guards the two ways a measurement can lie: a hidden tab's 0-height layer (pitfall #24) and a 0-height bar both abort rather than persist. Also this pass: the grip is now hover-ONLY (focus no longer holds it up — the focused window is always exactly one window, so a focus-visible grip meant a permanent band over someone's content), with focus moved to a 1px inset accent line on the window itself, which costs no content and works for nameless windows too. |
| F75 | **SimuCoin setup moves to Settings; the coin becomes a fixed-size "collect" surface.** Sekmeht, on B235: *"What's your thoughts about maybe adding simucoins to the settings menu, then that coin button just lights up when it's available and when you click it, it goes and gathers them"* — and then, on the interim two-account popover: *"I'm just concerned that there could still be a ton of listed accounts and it cause the window to be inconsistent."* | v0.18.1 | **Settings → SimuCoins** now owns onboarding: per-account enable, auto-claim, Check now, and the §42.3 disclosure rendered ONCE directly above the toggles that act on it (the AI-section shape, so consent still sits on the surface that enables it). The section hides entirely when no account has a saved password, and says how many were skipped and why. **The coin popover is now roster-INDEPENDENT** — one summary line plus full-width actions (**Collect available coins**, **Check now**, and a "Set up in Settings…" link so discovery survives) — so two accounts and twenty render at the same size. It reports the soonest `nextAt` when the store supplied one and never invents a date (DESIGN §42). `simucoinStateText` + `SIMUCOIN_DISCLOSURE` are single-sourced in `simucoinConfig.ts` so the surfaces can't drift (the B234 lesson), and a new `SIMUCOIN_CHANGED_EVENT` keeps the coin in step with Settings in the SAME window — a `storage` event never fires in the window that wrote (the `lichborne:ai-key-changed` precedent). Principle #11: the three `/simucoin` error strings now point at Settings. No profile-shape change — still rides `SharedProfile.simucoin`. **Follow-up:** a multi-account **Collect available coins** used to stack one toast per account; it now runs the batch `quiet` (the flag App's `runSimucoin` already had, threaded through AppBar → SimuCoinButton) and reports once — *"12 claimed across 3 accounts"*, with info/error variants when some fail. A single ready account deliberately keeps the original per-account toast, which is strictly more informative than a summary of one. |
| B235 | **The SimuCoin popover grew past the screen and shoved the whole app off-view.** JadedSoul, with 7 accounts: *"I can't scroll down in the box to get the account I really want to set it up with… then the whole frontend shifts, and I have to click somewhere on the frontend to get it back to normal."* Two compounding causes. **(a)** `.sc-menu` had no `max-height` and no scroller, and rendered one block per account INCLUDING a full 6-line consent disclosure each — several screens tall. **(b)** Far worse: `html, body, #root` are `overflow: hidden` ([global.css](src/renderer/styles/global.css)), and **`overflow: hidden` is still PROGRAMMATICALLY scrollable** — so the browser scrolling a clicked row into view scrolled the ROOT, dragging the entire UI off-screen with no scrollbar to get back. Clicking something near the top made it scroll back, which is exactly the recovery she described. | v0.18.1 | Ceiling (`max-height: 70vh`) + `overflow-y: auto` + `overscroll-behavior: contain`, the last of which also stops scroll chaining out to that root. The surface was then reshaped so its length can't depend on the roster at all — see F75. New **pitfall #109**. |
| B236 | **Leaving Edit mode in the Lich Dashboard set the search highlight but never scrolled to it.** Found while investigating Sekmeht's report that a find-highlight sat *"about 1/2 of a line off"* after validating and saving a profile YAML. On a fresh mount `lineMetrics` is still `{0, 0}`, and the parent's restore effect (keyed on `isEditing`) fires in the SAME commit: React runs child effects before parent ones, so the child's measuring effect has only SCHEDULED its update — the `useImperativeHandle` object the parent calls still closes over the pre-measure zero, hit the `lineHeight <= 0` guard, and skipped the scroll silently. So Cancel, and every save, left the search hit anywhere off-screen. | v0.18.1 | `scrollViewToLine` now measures from computed style at call time instead of reading measurement STATE. The class is hardened too: the overlay's metrics re-measure through a `ResizeObserver` on the pre, so any post-mount change to its box self-corrects whatever the trigger, and `.ld-validation-bar` gained a pinned `line-height` (it had none, so it inherited and rendered taller than its own text — pitfall #77). Shared components, so the Scripts tab gets all of it. New **pitfall #110**. **NOT confirmed fixed — the reported half-line offset itself could not be reproduced from the code:** the overlay's `top` is `pre.paddingTop + n × lineHeight` inside `.ld-yaml-inner` and the `<pre>` starts at that same origin with those same values; the gutter matches (pitfall #62 holding); and the validation banner is a SIBLING above `.ld-code-wrap`, outside the overlay's positioning context, which rules out the intuitive explanation. If it recurs, MEASURE rather than read — with the banner up and the highlight visibly wrong, compare `hl.getBoundingClientRect().top − pre.getBoundingClientRect().top` against `paddingTop + n × lineHeight`; the difference names whether it's the metrics or the origin. **v0.18.1 — that measurement is now BUILT IN as a temporary `[diag]`** (`diagHighlightOffset` in YamlHighlight, the CLAUDE.md investigation pattern): every Lich Dashboard search prints `[diag B236] line=… lineHeight=… paddingTop=… actual=… expected=… delta=…` to the console, so the next occurrence yields a number rather than an impression. It fires only on a deliberate search, so it can't spam a play session. **REMOVE the helper and its two call sites once B236 is settled either way.** |
| B237 | **The Scripts tab's find box said "Search YAML…" on Ruby files.** Sekmeht. `YamlSearchField` is shared by the Profiles and Scripts tabs and hardcoded its placeholder, so the Scripts tab described the file in front of the user wrongly. | v0.18.1 | `placeholder` is a prop — Scripts passes "Search script…", Profiles keeps "Search YAML…". |
| B234 | **`/simucoin check` never reported what it found.** It printed "Checking the SimuCoin store…" and then nothing — the one command whose entire purpose is to answer "do I have coins waiting?" never answered it (Sekmeht). Cause is architectural: slash executors are SYNCHRONOUS while a store check is a network round-trip, so the executor could only report that a run STARTED; the outcome went to a toast and the coin popover and had no route back to the window it was typed in. **Fixed:** `runSimucoin` now RESOLVES WITH the account's `SimuCoinStatus`, and GameWindow appends a result line per account as each run settles, via a new `emitClientLines` helper (the `internal-system` styling + `[sys]` logging slash results already use). `claim` reports the same way. Two constraints: the status is taken from the RESOLVED VALUE and never re-read from `sc.statuses` (that `setState` has not committed when the promise resolves, so a read-back formats the PREVIOUS run's row); and results append per account as each finishes rather than batched at the end, because the runs are deliberately sequential store round-trips (pitfall #101) and a batch report would leave the user staring at nothing for seconds. The line text comes from a single extracted `simucoinRowText`, shared with the bare `/simucoin` status readout so the two can never describe the same state differently. | Resolved (v0.18.0) |
| B233 | **The connect progress said "Step 2 of 4" twice and never named the Lich connection.** Sekmeht: "I saw step 2 last for 2 steps, then there's only 4, but it never says connecting to Lich service." Two defects in one feature. **(a)** The Lich path emitted FIVE distinct phases numbered **1, 2, 2, 3, 4** — step 2 was reused for "Signing in to your account" and "Getting the login key", so a watching user saw step 2 sit there through two different operations and the count never reached what the work actually did. The step strings were hardcoded at ten separate emit sites, which is how the numbering drifted unnoticed (DESIGN §44.1 even described it as "4 steps" while listing five). **(b)** The final phase was labelled "Waiting for Lich on port N" for its whole duration, so the connection to the Lich service was never announced — it simply jumped from "waiting" to connected. That is because `connectWithRetry` does not poll-then-connect: it retries the REAL connection until the port accepts, and that first success IS the session socket, so there was no separate "now connecting" moment to report. **Fixed:** totals live in `LICH_STEPS`/`DIRECT_STEPS` with a `step()` helper so every site derives its "of N" from one place; Lich is now 5 correctly-numbered steps with step 5 NAMED as the connect it actually is ("Connecting to Lich on port N…", keeping the elapsed-seconds and ≥10s diagnostic variants); and the direct path was split from 3 steps to 4 so both paths share the same first three phases instead of describing the same work differently. | Resolved (v0.18.0) |
| B232 | **Two raw NUL bytes in [wrayth.ts](src/renderer/import/parsers/wrayth.ts) made the file INVISIBLE to every ripgrep-backed search.** Committed since v0.12.0. They were intentional separators in the highlight dedup key (`` `${pattern}\0${textColor}\0${bgColor}` ``, the pitfall-#61a full-visual-identity rule) written as literal 0x00 bytes instead of the `\u0000` escape — so the STRING VALUE was always correct and the parser always behaved. The harm was tooling: ripgrep treats a NUL as a binary marker, so `Grep` returned **"No matches found"** for text demonstrably in the file. That is a correctness hazard for future work, not a cosmetic one — the Iron rule says claims come from grep + read, and a file that silently answers "no matches" invites the conclusion that code does not exist. (`git grep` reads it fine, which is why it went unnoticed.) **Fixed** by replacing the two bytes with the 6-char `\u0000` escape at the BUFFER level (pitfall #73 — a latin1 read/write round-trip double-encodes multibyte UTF-8); the script refused to write unless the reverse substitution matched the original byte-for-byte, and line endings were confirmed unchanged (390 LF before and after). Behaviour re-proved by bundling the real parser: exact duplicate collapses, same-pattern-different-colour survives, bg-only difference stays distinct. Repo-wide sweep found no other NUL bytes in any source file. | Resolved (v0.18.0) |
| B231 | **Genie Map felt sluggish while dragging and during heavy movement.** Reported by Sekmeht, who suspected the locator sonar ping. **The ping was measured and EXONERATED** — a harness at real zone density (1057 nodes, the largest zone in the dev genie-cache) panned for 300 frames with ping/heartbeat on and off held a flat 60fps with ZERO dropped frames in all four variants. The real cause was two React costs in [GenieMapView.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/GenieMapView.tsx), both measured through the React Profiler at the same node count. **(a) Every room change rebuilt the entire node layer.** `nodeRects` is memoized, but its dep `onNodeContextMenu` (right-click-to-walk) closed over `currentLocation`, so the callback got a fresh identity on every room change, busting the memo and re-creating all ~1057 room-marker `<g>` groups on every single step the player took: **8.92ms of React commit per room** vs 0.34ms when the memo holds — over half a frame budget spent re-creating markers whose geometry had not changed, and worse the denser the zone. **(b) The arc paths re-joined on every render.** `arcSegs` was memoized but the `.join('')` that turns ~2,400 segments into path `d` strings was not, and ran twice per render (3 categories x 2 passes) — ~0.15ms and **78KB of string garbage per render**, on every mousemove of a drag, every room change, and every parent re-render, to produce byte-identical output. **Fixed:** the walk handler's inputs moved into a latest-closure ref (pitfall #31) so its identity is stable, and the arc path ELEMENTS are memoized on `[arcSegs]`. Measured after: room-change commit **8.92ms → 0.09ms**, arc join **0.18ms + 78KB → 0**. Harness: `tmp-map-perf/` (gitignored). New pitfall #106. | Resolved (v0.18.0) |
| B230 | **Highlights and triggers whose pattern contains an unescaped `.` silently never fired.** No error, rule shows as enabled, just nothing happens. Shipped since v0.13.4 (B172); found in the v0.18.0 performance audit, not by a report — but six of one tester's live highlights were dead. | v0.18.0 (perf audit) | `extractRegexLiteral` ([regexLiteral.ts](src/renderer/regexLiteral.ts)) builds the cheap `fastLower` substring every highlight AND trigger is gated on before its regex runs (pitfall #82a). Its walker branched on `\|`, `(`, `[`, `^ $ )`, `? * + {` and `\` — but had **no case for `.`**, so the any-character metachar fell through to the literal-atom branch and was appended to the run as a literal dot. A bare `.` continued the run; `.+` hit the "required once" path and appended one. The gate then demanded a real `.` in the line that the match never has to contain. **`joins the .+` → gate `"joins the ."` → never matches "Bob joins the adventure"** — and that is CLAUDE.md's own documented Analytics example. Real dead rules found on one machine: `^(.*(concentrates\|concentrating) intently on.+)$`, `^Your focused insight of .+ has been fully explored\.$`, `^(.*The inventory of .+ rotates.*)$`, `^(.*You sense.+)(...)$`, `^(\S+ is teaching a class on.+...)$`, `^Roundtime.+.$`. **Fix:** `.` now ENDS the literal run (like a character class), consuming any quantifier. Verified by bundling the real module: 4/4 representative patterns went from gated-out to matching, and an **agreement check across 21,191 real gated rules** from every profile on the machine reports zero unescaped-dot literals remaining, with escaped `\.` still gating correctly. The check (`tmp-perf/check-literals.mjs`) is the harness CLAUDE.md referenced but which had gone missing — it existed to catch exactly this. New pitfall #104. **Costs nothing in speed — a MISSING literal only slows the gate; a WRONG one breaks the rule**, which is the invariant the file's own header states. |
| B229 | **SimuCoin: "authentication failure" on the SECOND account** — claimed on one account fine, then the next account reported a sign-in failure; clicking *try again* worked and it claimed normally (Sekmeht, first live run). | v0.18.0 (Sekmeht) | **Leading diagnosis (not provable from here — see below): a CACHED sign-in page.** ASP.NET pairs the sign-in form's `__RequestVerificationToken` FIELD with a matching COOKIE. Between accounts we cleared cookies (`clearStorageData({storages:['cookies']})`) — but an Electron partition's **HTTP cache is separate storage and was NOT cleared**, and the partition was created without `{cache:false}`. So the second account's GET of the sign-in page could be served from cache: a **stale token posted against a fresh cookie ⇒ rejected**. It matches the symptom exactly, including why the manual retry worked (the failed POST revalidated the page, so the retry had a matching pair) and why the FIRST account never failed (empty cache at launch). **Fix (three layers):** the partition is now created with `{ cache: false }`; a new `resetJar()` clears cookies **and** `clearCache()` before every attempt and after every run; and every request sends `Cache-Control: no-cache` / `Pragma: no-cache`. **Plus a guaranteed-safe belt:** `runSimuCoin` now **retries once automatically on `auth-failed`** against a fully reset jar — literally the "click try again" that worked, done for the user — so the symptom is gone even if the cache theory is incomplete. Bounded to one extra attempt (a genuinely wrong password can't become a retry storm against Simutronics' login), and it **cannot double-claim**: `auth-failed` is returned *before* any claim POST is issued. A second rejection is reported as a real credential problem ("check the saved password") rather than a transient one. **Residual:** the exact cause is unconfirmed — verifying it needs a live multi-account run; the retry makes that verification optional rather than blocking. |
| B228 | **Lich Dashboard → Scripts: the line numbers don't line up with the code** ("this was an issue with the profiles until a certain fix was implemented aligning all lines perfectly — please do the same"; works great in the Profile (YAML) area). | v0.18.0 (Sekmeht) | The pitfall-#62 fix (an ABSOLUTE `line-height: 1.3rem` shared by `.ld-line-num` / `.ld-yaml-preview` / `.ld-yaml-editor`) was already in place and **both tabs render the SAME components** (`YamlHighlight` / `EditorWithGutter`, differing only by `language`), so the per-line heights were never the problem. The difference is the CONTENT SHAPE: Ruby scripts are **wide**, so the content pane grows a **horizontal scrollbar** — which the `overflow: hidden` gutter never reserved. That gives the two different viewport heights and therefore different scroll RANGES: the content's max `scrollTop` is larger than the gutter's by the scrollbar height, so `gutter.scrollTop = content.scrollTop` clamps early and the numbers stop tracking their lines toward the bottom. YAML profiles are narrow → no horizontal scrollbar → never reproduced there. **Fix (in the shared `useGutterSync`, so both tabs and both modes get it):** the gutter's `padding-bottom` now reserves the **measured** scrollbar height (`offsetHeight − clientHeight − borders`, so it's correct on any OS/zoom and a no-op when there's no horizontal scrollbar), re-measured on scroll, on content change, and while typing. Same family as pitfall #67 — a gutter/header and the rows it labels must reserve the same scrollbar space. |
| B227 | **The `assess` stream can't be opened as its own panel — it isn't detected in Available Streams** (Illiahanna: "No assess stream"). | v0.17.3 (Illiahanna) | DR emits `<pushStream id="assess"/>` for the ASSESS combat block, so it's a real, viewable stream — but it was added to `NEVER_DISCOVER` in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) when the combat Tableau shipped (v0.16.1, which consumes the stream), so it never surfaced in Available Streams and had no built-in panel either → no way to view it as its own panel. The hide bought nothing: the Tableau's `assessAccumRef` accumulation runs in the stream-text case INDEPENDENTLY of discovery/watching, and `STREAM_FALLBACK['assess']='main'` already shows the text in main when unwatched. **Fix:** removed `'assess'` from `NEVER_DISCOVER` (purely additive — an Assess panel now appears in Available Streams after the next ASSESS; with no panel it still falls back to main; the Tableau arena is unaffected). Verified `assess` was the ONLY real stream wrongly hidden — every other `NEVER_DISCOVER` entry is internal (`main`/`raw`/room sub-streams) or an alias to a built-in panel (`thought`→Thoughts, `death`→Deaths, `logons`→Arrivals, `talk`/`whispers`/`conversations`→Conversation, `percwindow`→Active Spells, `inventory`→Inventory, `experience`→Experience). |
| B226 | **Command echo split from its prompt** — typing `stand` at an `s>` prompt showed `s>` on one line and `>stand` on the next; should read `s>stand` (Sekmeht). | v0.17.3 (Sekmeht) | Lichborne echoed a typed command as its OWN `>cmd` line, separate from the bare prompt DR shows when idle — unlike Wrayth/Genie/Frostbite, which append the command inline after the current prompt. **Fix:** new `appendEcho(prev, cmd)` in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) merges the command into a trailing bare prompt (`s>` + `stand` → `s>stand`, reusing the prompt's own `>`, clearing its `prompt` flag, reusing the line id so it updates in place with no flicker); falls back to a standalone `>cmd` line mid-stream. All three echo sites (`sendCommandSequence`, `dispatchUserText` split, canonical `sendCommand`) route through it and null `lastMainLineRef` after (a typed command is a break — the prompt returning after a no-output command still shows, guarding the stale ref the merge leaves). Display only — the Session Log still records the canonical `>cmd`. New pitfall #98. |
| B225 | **Prompt `>` deduplication: multiple `>`s in a row, and an "awkward missing time where there is no `>` after a room description"** (Sekmeht). | v0.17.3 (Sekmeht) | The parser keyed dedup on `lastMainText` (last MAIN-stream text). A move/look/combat flurry pushes room title/exits/players/combat-state to SUB-streams (never main), so the pre- and post-activity `>` compared EQUAL → the post-activity `>` was suppressed (the gap), while other turns leaked doubles. **Fix (two layers):** the parser now routes every event through one `emit()` chokepoint tracking `lastEmitWasPrompt`; `case 'prompt'` emits unless the *immediately-preceding emitted event* was that exact prompt (`lastEmitWasPrompt && prompt === lastPromptText`) — truly back-to-back identical prompts dropped at source, ANY non-prompt emit clears the flag. The display-layer collapse (pitfall #88, GameWindow) then drops a `>` only when the previous DISPLAYED MAIN line was an identical prompt — sub-stream/room activity is NOT a visible break (never lands in main), so a room render / combat flurry that fired several `<prompt>`s collapses to ONE `>`; the B194 mute case still collapses. A first attempt (making sub-stream activity break the run) produced the doubles the user reported and was reverted. Statusprompt drift (`H>`→`R>`) preserved by text equality. New pitfall #98. **Pre-merge sweep also caught a `sed`-introduced bug:** the mass-rename `this.events.push(`→`this.emit(` rewrote `this.events.push(...sceneLineEvents)` into `this.emit(...sceneLineEvents)`, and `emit()` takes ONE event — the spread silently emitted only the first scene event and dropped the rest (build + tsc both passed it). Fixed to a `for…of` loop. |
| B224 | **Injuries panel shows wounds that never clear** — a tester's wounds "have been staying persistent since she died". Her screenshot: `HEAL` reports *"body feels at full strength (100%)"* and *"You have no significant injuries"*, while the panel still lists Chest Moderate, Back Light, Right/Left Hand Light, Left Leg Moderate. | v0.17.1 (Sekmeht, via tester) | **NOT a refresh/staleness bug — the panel was updating fine and mis-reading the data.** DR encodes each part's state in the `<image name>`, and a **wound** and a **scar** are different states. Per Lich's parser (`lib/common/xmlparser.rb` ~681-690, the authority): `Injury<n>` = active wound rank n; **`Scar<n>` = the wound HEALED (wound explicitly set to 0) leaving a scar of rank n**; `Nsys<n>` = nerve damage. Lichborne's panel treated **any** name `!== the part id` as a wound and took its trailing digit as severity — so a healed-to-scar chest (`Scar2`) rendered as a **permanent "Moderate" wound**. That is exactly the screenshot: HEAL correctly said "no significant injuries" (no WOUNDS) while every row shown was a **SCAR** — and scars persist through death/healing, which is why they'd been there "since she died". **Fix:** `parseInjury()` in [InjuriesPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/InjuriesPanel.tsx) now mirrors Lich verbatim (`/^(injury\|scar\|nsys)(\d)/i`); scars still display (real information) but as **muted, neutral rows labelled "Light/Moderate/Severe scar"** with no wound hue or fill, so they can't read as active damage, and "No active wounds." shows whenever no wound is present even if scars remain. Deriving healthy from the ABSENCE of Injury/Scar/Nsys (rather than `name === id`) also makes it robust to whichever sentinel DR uses for an unhurt part. DESIGN's protocol table — which had documented the name as part-id-plus-digit and never mentioned scars, the source of the misreading — corrected. New pitfall #97. **Two related gaps found and deliberately NOT changed without evidence** (both want a raw-XML capture around a heal): the parser only emits `injury-update` when the dialog has ≥1 `<image>`, so a genuinely EMPTY dialog can never clear the panel; and the renderer REPLACES the whole injury map whereas Lich MERGES per-part (replace is wrong if DR ever sends deltas). |
| B223 | **The main story window spontaneously un-pins — scrolling locks and the "New Lines" badge appears with no user interaction, and never recovers.** Intermittent, observed across many versions. Tester's clue: the scrollbar thumb **"quivers — tries to move then snaps back down."** | v0.17.1 (Sekmeht) | **Root cause** (traced into react-virtuoso's source): `appendTrimmed` cuts 400 lines in ONE slice (2400→2000, recurring every ~400 lines once the buffer is full). That collapse makes Virtuoso read `scrollDirection === 'up'` — the gate for its **upward-scroll compensation** — while its index-keyed size tree is stale by 400 rows (`firstItemIndex` is deliberately unused, pitfall #81). Its rAF-batched re-measures then fire `scrollBy(-k)` that **nothing suppresses**, and `stickToBottom`'s settle loop slams the bottom back each frame — **that fight IS the quiver**. A compensation landing after the loop exits (2 stable frames) + the 250ms suppress tail reached `handleVirtuosoScroll`, which **cannot distinguish a library scroll from a user one**, so `dist > 40` un-pinned. `onRefocus` was gated on `pinnedRef.current`, so it could never heal. **Fix (A):** the un-pin branch now requires the distance to **persist across 2 frames** — a compensation is undone in a frame or two (cancelled), a real user scroll persists (commits ~32ms later). Safe by construction: wheel-up + PageUp/Home un-pin directly and never reach this branch. Source-agnostic on purpose (holds for Virtuoso, the trim clamp, or a stalled-rAF window) and does NOT depend on `document.hidden`. **Fix (C):** `onRefocus` self-heals when the un-pin timestamp is AFTER the blur (it happened while away ⇒ not the user); a timestamp comparison, not input detection, since scrollbar-drag `pointerdown` isn't reliably detectable. **Not done:** the trigger itself (the quiver remains visible) and a user-intent gate (rests on an unverified assumption). New pitfall #96. **Confirmation is a negative result — ships unconfirmed by design.** |
| B222 | **Switching character tabs strips the Moons scene's visuals** — Sekmeht: char1 logged in + moonwatch, switched to char2 + moonwatch, switched back to char1 and the sky/moons/rays went flat/blank (screenshot). | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | The classic duplicate SVG gradient-ID bug. `url(#id)` resolves against the FIRST element with that id in the whole document — and a background character tab is `display:none` but STILL MOUNTED (pitfall #24), so its Moons `<svg>` and gradient defs sit in the DOM alongside the visible one. Every Moons instance declared the SAME literal gradient ids (`lb-ground`, `lb-shade`, `moon-xibar`, `sun`, `glow`, …), so once a second character's Moons mounted, half the first instance's fills resolved to the second (hidden) instance's gradients and painted nothing — flat scene. **Fix:** per-instance-unique ids via React `useId()` — `const uid='m'+useId().replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9]/g,'')`, a `gref(name)=\`url(#${uid}-${name})\`` helper, and every gradient `id="lb-x"`→`id={\`${uid}-x\`}` / `url(#lb-x)`→`gref('x')`. The static-identical `lb-fog` gradient is left shared deliberately (referenced from the module-level `MoonsPrecip` sub-component; same stops every instance, so cross-resolution is harmless). New pitfall #95 records the rule: any component that can mount >once AND uses SVG `url(#…)` must namespace its def ids per instance (gradients, filters, clipPaths, masks, `<use href>`). |
| B221 | **Living Tableau thoughts obscure the scene** — telepathic "thought wisps" rendered top-right, over the creatures/figures. | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | The wisps were pinned top-right (`top:3.6em; right:0.6em`, up to 45% wide), right over the upper scene. **Fix:** moved to a quiet **bottom-left "thought log"** — anchored `bottom/left`, `flex-direction:column` so the newest sits at a fixed low spot and older ones stack UP + recede (`nth-last-child` opacity fade + smooth `transition:opacity`). Bottom-left clears the centre avatar, the bottom-centre gauges, and the right-edge A−/A+/⚙ controls; `max-width:42%` keeps it short of centre. Keeps the telepathy read (dashed italic dimmed, no body in the room, §32.2). |
| B220 | **Harmless NPCs that wander into the room during combat are all shown with threat indicators** on the Tableau. | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | The threat marker was a BLANKET "combat is live → flare EVERY creature" flag (`showThreat = !hidden.threat && inCombat`, applied to every live cast creature) — it couldn't tell hostile from harmless, so any NPC present while `inCombat` (8s sticky after any RT/CT/aim timer or wound) lit up. **Fix:** retired the blanket marker. Threat/engaged styling now comes ONLY from the **ASSESS view**, which knows who's actually at melee range — harmless bystanders are never flagged; assessed creatures at melee flare as *engaged* and your target keeps its gold ring. Without assessing, creatures show as plain monsterbold figures (combat itself is still signalled by the avatar's readiness rings + gauges). The ⚙ "Threat markers" option now gates that accurate assess flare (desc updated). Unrelated to B219 (which clears the assess *snapshot*, a different mechanism). |
| B219 | **After retreating to a new room, the Tableau kept showing the OLD room's creatures** as if they were there with you. | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | The `assess` snapshot has NO room binding — it's an on-demand snapshot cleared only by a `clearStream 'assess'`, never by a move — and the arena's name-mismatch fallback (show the raw snapshot rather than blank it) surfaced the stale creatures when the new room had creatures of its own. **Fix:** an effect watches the room identity (title OR room-id — a nav-only teleport changes the id without a new title, pitfall #46) and drops the snapshot (`assessAccumRef` + `assessCast`) on any change, so the new room re-assesses fresh and an empty room falls back to the now-cleared live cast. Composes with the B213 reconciliation (which governs within a room); this just stops a snapshot crossing the doorway. |
| B218 | **Compact vitals bar text looks slightly offset (top-aligned)** in the ~1em compact strip. | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | All-caps/digit labels ("H: 100%") have no descenders, so with `line-height:1` flex-centring the glyphs sit optically high in the tight box (residual of the v0.13.x `line-height:1` fix). **Fix:** a small `transform: translateY(0.07em)` on `.vitals-strip--compact .vital-text` to optically centre the glyph block. |
| B217 | **Windowed-Panels title bar is clunky: header height JUMPS when you show/hide the name, and the title text is top-aligned.** | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | Two bugs from over-slimming the title bar. (a) `.fl-titlebar` (~6px) ended up SHORTER than `.fl-grip` (8px), so hiding the name (titlebar→grip) grew the header — the tester nailed it ("the handle loads and makes it thicker"). (b) The box was shrunk so tight (6px box, 6px text) `align-items:center` had no slack, so the text read top-aligned. **Fix:** matched titlebar==grip at a slim CENTRED ~8px (box > glyph height), trimmed the top resize handle 6→4px so the slim bar stays draggable. Then a UX pass: "T" (hide-name) → a **chevron**, collapse/close controls **hidden until hover/focus** (quiet by default), soft gradient + hairline + 1px accent focus line + letter-spaced muted title type (all `color-mix`, theme-safe). CLAUDE.md §33 records the titlebar==grip invariant. |
| B216 | **Command input bar reads oversized (and its caret overlapped the RT/CT/aim timer strips).** | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | Two parts. (a) `.command-input` had no `line-height`, so it inherited `--game-line-height` (the PROSE setting, 1.4–1.6) — the text CARET is as tall as the line box, so a tall prose line-height stretched it into the timer strips pinned at the input's edges. Pinned `line-height:1.2`. (b) The bar read oversized next to the newly-slim chrome — trimmed outer padding 7→3px, input padding 8→7px, and slimmed the timer strips (chips 8→6px, bars 6→5px) so the caret still clears them. |
| B215 | **Living Tableau renders tiny / figures overlap / combat gauges clip when hosted in a small panel tab** (fine at full size). | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | Figures are %-POSITIONED but em-SIZED, so a fixed font kept them full-size in a small box → the % gaps collapsed (overlap) and the bottom-hung gauge panel clipped. **Fixes:** (1) the stage font **fits the container** — WIDTH-dominant (an early height-weighted fit made a wide-short tab ~6px "way too tiny, have to A+ a lot"), gentle height floor; (2) the **gauge panel is pinned to the stage bottom** (decoupled from the self figure so it can't clip); (3) the **self figure clamps up clear of the gauge panel** in a short stage (measured gauge height); (4) the **self renders above seated figures** (z-index) so overlaps read as depth. Related: a chrome-window "auto-hug" tried in the same pass corrupted saved heights and was reverted (pitfall #93). |
| B214 | **Combat gauges (BAL/POS/RNG) persist forever after combat ends** — the cockpit lingers in a peaceful room. | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | Superseded the earlier "just leave these on" persistent-HUD call. The gauges now hold through the 8s `inCombat` sticky then **fade out** (asymmetric CSS transition — quick fade-in when combat resumes, slow fade-out when it ends). Rings/danger/threat already only showed while live, and the assess arena self-clears (B215/B213), so the whole cockpit now decays coherently. |
| B213 | **After creatures die, the ASSESS arena removes their icons, leaving only the survivors** — killed/decayed creatures vanish instead of remaining as corpses. | v0.17.0 (Sekmeht) | Two coupled behaviors. First (the reconciliation) a killed creature must not keep showing as a live "engaged" threat — the arena reconciles against the SceneParser's dead-aware live cast by **name-count** (`normAssessName` normalizes article/case both sides; a name-mismatch falls back to the raw snapshot rather than blank the arena). But the reconciliation then DROPPED the dead ones entirely — Sekmeht wanted them to **remain as corpses**. **Fix:** render the cast's dead creatures as **✕ corpse figures** in a row above the tactical row until they DECAY out of the room (same dead-detection the non-assess creature view already uses); killing one of four drakes now shows 3 assessed + 1 corpse. |
