| B212 | **ASSESS arena could flicker blank for a frame if an assess block split across a flush boundary** (proactive — bug check, never observed). | v0.16.1 (Claude bug check) | Caught reviewing the combat-facet + assess changes. DR emits `<clearStream id='assess'/>` then the per-creature lines; GameWindow resets `assessAccumRef` on the clear and snapshots it at batch end. At the 16ms `scheduleFlush` coalescing (B172) the clear + lines almost always land in ONE batch, but a block straddling a flush boundary would apply the just-cleared EMPTY accumulator and blank the arena for a frame before the next batch refilled it. **Fix:** guard the snapshot on `batchAssessTouched && assessAccumRef.current.length > 0` — a completed assess always carries ≥1 entity (the self line), so the guard never suppresses a real update; a genuinely-empty assess (you left combat) ages out via the 30s TTL instead of clearing instantly. Provably can't regress a real update. CLAUDE.md's assess note records the clear-then-lines batch-boundary shape for future stream-repaint accumulators. |
| B211 | **The Living Tableau's A−/A+ (text size / zoom) buttons do nothing** — pressing them doesn't scale the scene's figures or gauges. | v0.16.1 (Sekmeht, combat-facet build) | The per-Experience A−/A+ shadows `--game-font-size` on the window subtree (ExperienceLayer), and every Experience is meant to size its content off that var. `.tableau-scene` (the scene root) was **missing `font-size: var(--game-font-size)`**, so its em-based figures/gauges floated on the inherited font instead of the override — the buttons scaled nothing visible. `.moons-scene` has the anchor (which is why Moons scales); the Tableau never got it. **Fix:** add `font-size: var(--game-font-size, 12px)` to `.tableau-scene` — the whole em chain now tracks the zoom. **Rule (CLAUDE.md):** any scene root that hosts A−/A+ MUST anchor to `--game-font-size`, and that element-scaling IS the right "zoom" for a fit-to-window graphical Experience (crisp, no crop) — a transform camera-zoom is for large navigable spaces (the map), not a fixed scene. |
| B210 | **Lich Map loses tracking when Lich's `display_uid` setting is on** — the room marker falls back to fuzzy title+desc matching instead of the exact room-id lookup. | v0.16.1 (Lich 5.18 review) | Surfaced during the Lich 5.18 compatibility review (not a 5.18 regression — pre-existing). When `display_uid` is on, Lich rewrites the room subtitle's parens id from the native `(12345)` to a `u`-prefixed `(u12345)` (same game uid). The parser's parens regex `/\]\s*\((\d+)\)/` ([StormFrontParser.ts](src/main/parser/StormFrontParser.ts) streamWindow handler) expects bare digits, so the `u` broke the match → `roomId` `undefined` → the Lich Map's instant `lichDb.get(roomId)` never fired and it degraded to the fragile title+desc path (pitfall #65). **Fix:** widen to `/\]\s*\(u?(\d+)\)/` — the optional `u` parses `(u12345)` to `12345` (the same uid the native flag emits, so the lookup is identical) while bare digits still match; non-numeric parens (`(**)`, `(unknown)`) still miss, preserving the id-absence no-op. Verified against the parser; CLAUDE.md pitfall #65 updated. |
| B209 | **Catch Me Up: a mid-stream Anthropic error is silently swallowed** — a summary that overloads *after* it starts streaming ends as a partial/empty result with no error shown (and the tokens are still counted). | v0.16.0 (Claude review) | Found in the v0.16.0 AI bug-hunt. `claudeProvider.handleFrame` ([claudeProvider.ts](src/main/ai/claudeProvider.ts)) branched only on `content_block_delta`/`message_start`/`message_delta`; Anthropic's SSE `error` frame (e.g. `overloaded_error`/529 arriving AFTER `message_start`) hit no case, was ignored, the reader loop ended normally, and the caller's `onDone` fired with whatever had accumulated — a partial or empty summary, no error surfaced. A PRE-stream failure was already handled (`!res.ok`); only MID-stream was lost. **Fix:** handle the `error` frame by THROWING → it propagates out of `claudeChatStream` → main's try/catch → the renderer's `✕ Catch Me Up failed: …` line. **Invariant (CLAUDE.md):** any SSE consumer must handle the `error` frame, not just the content deltas. |
| B208 | **Catch Me Up summary renders as unreadable dim grey, bleeds into the next paragraph, and (with timestamps on) adds an empty timestamped row** — Sekmeht: "it's dark grey when I have white text." | v0.16.0 (Sekmeht) | Three rendering issues on the streamed summary. **(1) Dim text:** AI output used the `internal-system` preset (`--text-dim`, the dimmest text rung — right for a throwaway "Connection closed." notice, wrong for a summary you READ). **(2) Bleed:** the summary butted directly against the next game line. **(3) Timestamped spacer:** a first fix using a separate blank spacer ROW picked up its own `[HH:MM]` when main-window timestamps were on. **Fixes:** a new **`ai` preset** ([panels.css](src/renderer/styles/panels.css)) that follows the GAME text color, tinted 25% toward the accent via `color-mix` (theme-safe on light AND dark, Principle #4) — the summary body renders on `ai`, the header stays quiet on `internal-system`, errors stay on `internal-system` (a notice, not content); and a **trailing `\n`** on the final line for one blank line of separation WITHOUT a separate row (so no orphan timestamp — `.text-line` pre-wrap renders it). |
| B207 | **`/ai ` doesn't list or Tab-complete its verbs** (unlike `/trigger`) — Sekmeht: "the /ai commandlets aren't being scored, I can't use tab to complete the commands." | v0.16.0 (Sekmeht) | The slash palette's `resolveCommand` ([SlashPalette.tsx](src/renderer/components/SlashPalette.tsx)) committed to a noun's BARE (verb-`''`) entry the instant you typed `noun ` (space), dropping into argument-hint mode — so `/ai `'s verbs (on/off/catchup/key/stop) never listed. Verb-only nouns like `/trigger` have no bare entry, so they stay in list mode and work; `/ai` and `/colors` were the only bare+verbs nouns, so they alone broke. **Fix:** `resolveCommand` skips the bare form for any noun in a new `NOUNS_WITH_VERBS` set, so `/ai `/`/colors ` STAY in list mode and rank their verbs like `/trigger` — the parser's `findCommand` still runs the bare form on Enter, so `/ai`→status is unchanged. Also removed the now-redundant `/ai status` verb (bare `/ai` gives status; two identical rows otherwise). CLAUDE.md's slash section documents the constraint (a bare+verbs noun must not add a redundant `status`-style verb). |
| B206 | **Commands seem to "clump together for processing" when sent in rapid succession** — Sekmeht: "I thought it was coincidental but I'm thinking maybe not." | v0.16.0 (Sekmeht — investigated, NOT a bug) | **Measured, not real.** Temporary `[diag]` probes timed each command's renderer→`socket.write` latency and the synchronous parse burst. Result: **`queued=0–1ms` even when stacking commands**, and the parse-burst probe stayed silent. The send path is immediate (`socket.write(cmd+'\r\n')` per command — no queue/throttle/batch, verified end-to-end). Incoming XML is parsed AS IT STREAMS (per line), and TCP delivers small ~1.5KB chunks, so each parse is sub-millisecond and the loop yields between them — queued sends interleave naturally. The only real batching is the DISPLAY-side 16ms `scheduleFlush` coalescing (B172), deliberate and sub-frame; if output ever *looks* clumped, that's frame-aligned rendering, not command delay. **Instrument lesson recorded:** a first lag probe (`setInterval(50)`) reported a CONSTANT ~12ms "blocked" even at idle — a **Windows 15.625ms timer-granularity artifact** (the interval fires on the next 15.6ms boundary ≈ 62.5ms), NOT real blocking; a measurement that reads constant regardless of load is measuring the instrument. Replaced with a `performance.now()` probe around the actual work (silent at idle), which confirmed no contention. All probes removed after; no code change. |
| B205 | **`;go2` reduces typeahead ("reducing typeahead setting") on Lichborne but not on Wrayth** — Rhorgul (Discord): "5 worked on Wrayth and running ;go2 on Lichborne triggered the reducing typeahead message." | v0.16.0 (Rhorgul — investigated, NOT a Lichborne bug) | **Confirmed from BOTH Lichborne's and Lich's source (`C:\temp\lich-dev`, `C:\Ruby4Lich5`).** With Lich in the middle the game's client is LICH, not the front end: `;go2` runs entirely inside Lich (generates movement, reads the game socket), and the FE isn't in that loop. Lichborne identifies to Lich as **`FE:WRAYTH /VERSION:1.0.1.22 /P:WIN_UNKNOWN /XML`** — byte-identical to Wrayth — so Lich routes it through the identical stormfront branch and **cannot distinguish them**. go2's reduction fires ONLY when the GAME puts `"Sorry, you may only type ahead"` into `$_SERVERBUFFER_` (server→Lich data — the FE can't write it), and go2 self-limits to `typeahead` outstanding commands. Nothing Lichborne sends reaches the game differently: `_flag Display Inventory Boxes 1` is consumed by Lich's `inventory_boxes_toggle` upstream hook (returns `nil`, never reaches DR — B169); `;`-commands (`;listall`) are eaten by Lich's `do_client` before it forwards. No autonomous game command from either side (Lich's only keepalive is TCP-level). Backpressure can't cause it either — `$_SERVERBUFFER_.push` + the XML parse that advances room state run BEFORE any FE write, and a stall makes go2 wait/restart, never over-send; and the auto-reduce is a per-run local variable, not persisted. **Conclusion: game-side jitter** — go2 backs off on any transient server-buffer hiccup, independent of the FE (Rhorgul himself hedged "latency demon… not a big deal"). To confirm if ever doubted: close the Lich Scripts panel, run the same route several times back-to-back on each FE — one reduction is noise; reliably-Lichborne-only would be genuinely surprising given the identical client-id. No code change; the command-send + XML-batching architecture is documented in CLAUDE.md/DESIGN. |
| B204 | **Moons Experience: the horizon/ground doesn't stretch the full width of a maximized panel window** — sky fills edge-to-edge but the ridges/ground sit centered with gaps at both sides. | v0.15.2 (Sekmeht, screenshot) | Root cause read from [MoonsExperience.tsx](src/renderer/components/experiences/MoonsExperience.tsx): the aspect-derived viewBox width was clamped to a **1100 max** — meant as an "extreme aspects" guard, but with `H = 250` any drawing area wider than ~4.4× its height exceeds it (Sekmeht's screenshot was already ~4.5 at a mere ~725px wide; a maximized panel far more). Once clamped, `preserveAspectRatio="xMidYMax meet"` letterboxes the SVG horizontally — the SVG ground/ridges/horizon center with side gaps while the **HTML sky-gradient layers** (divs, not SVG) keep filling the container, producing exactly the reported split. **Fix:** ceiling raised 1100 → **5000** and re-documented as a *degenerate-measurement* guard only (a transient 2000×2px mid-drag sliver would otherwise mint a 250,000-unit path) — no real monitor hits 5000 through normal use, so the viewBox now genuinely tracks the aspect and the ground reaches both edges. `ridgePath` cost is linear in width (34-unit segments) — trivial at any realistic W; all body/star/chip geometry already derives from `W`/`CX`, so nothing else assumed the old ceiling. |
| B203 | **Stream panels in windows stop following the last line** — thoughts (and logs, likely all streams) fill and scroll off screen; scrolling to the bottom once restores follow from then on. | v0.15.2 (Sekmeht) | Validated: a render-time "recheck" in [StreamPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/StreamPanel.tsx) re-derived `pinned` from raw geometry (`scrollHeight − scrollTop − clientHeight < 40`) on EVERY render — so any geometry change WITHOUT a user scroll silently unpinned: a floating window's 0-height mount frame (dist = scrollHeight → unpin), a drag-resize shrinking the panel, a layout-mode switch. Once false, only a manual scroll-to-bottom re-pinned (the exact repro). The pitfall-#71/B191 class: layout-induced signals must not drive user-intent state. **Fix:** unpin now happens ONLY in the scroll handler (guarded on `document.hidden` + zero-height, per B191 — a hidden/unmeasurable panel can't receive user scrolls), the render recheck is deleted (it could only ever unpin, so removing it errs toward staying pinned — recoverable by one wheel-up, unlike the bug), and a **ResizeObserver re-snaps to the bottom when pinned** on any container resize (passive bare `scrollTop` write, the pitfall-#68c rule — a scrolled-up reader is never touched). The main window was already immune (its `stickToBottom` architecture solved this class in B155/B158); StreamPanel was the primitive cousin that never got the lesson. |
| B202 | **Weather & Moons: no sun visible + a freshly-set moon drawn at the rise point; root cause of the sun half is a Ruby-Marshal decoder bug that ALSO corrupted the Lich Dashboard's Variables tab** — Sekmeht: "Katamba looks ready to rise shortly [at 161m to go]… I also don't see the sun." | v0.15.1 (Sekmeht) | Two independent bugs. **(a) Under-arc inverted:** the sky arc rises RIGHT and sets LEFT, but `underPos` ran the underground return leg right→left — endpoints never matched, so a just-set moon sat at the rise point. Fixed (`theta = π(1−progress)`); the loop is now genuinely continuous (set-left → under → rise-right). **(b) The missing sun** traced 3 layers down: the sun seed reads moonwatch's `UserVars.sun` from lich.db3 → `sun['timer']` was `undefined` → seed bailed silently. Dumping ALL 22 real uservars blobs with the real parser showed why: the `timer` KEY decoded as a DIFFERENT var's name in every blob (`"hunting_nemesis":177`, `"almanac_last_use":93`, `"moons":90`) — a **Marshal object-link table bug** in [marshalParser.ts](src/main/lichbridge/marshalParser.ts). Ruby's `TYPE_IVAR` wrapper gets NO object-table slot (marshal.c registers only the INNER value), but our `'I'` handler pushed a placeholder AND let the inner string register — one extra entry per I-wrapped value, shifting every later `@` link to a plausible-but-wrong earlier object. Sibling ordering bug in `'U'` (registered after its payload; Ruby registers before) fixed same pass. **This had silently corrupted link-heavy blobs in the Lich Dashboard Variables tab since the v0.5.0 parser shipped** — first-occurrence literals decoded fine (why it went unnoticed); only back-references broke. Fix verified by re-dumping all 22 blobs: every `sun` now reads `{day, night, timer}` exactly. Also hardened the seed's 180/180 synth against real >180-minute halves (a 187m day was in the live data — unclamped it made a future-dated anchor → null phase). Pitfall #91. |
| B201 | **Per-panel A+/A− font buttons don't work on the Maps stream (Genie Maps)** — the global Settings font size works, but the panel-local override does nothing. | v0.14.7 (Sekmeht) | The map's only font-wired text (the SVG landmark labels, [GenieMapView.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/GenieMapView.tsx)) read `var(--game-font-size)` DIRECTLY — skipping the `--panel-font-size` chain the A+/A− buttons set on the panel-frame-body wrapper (pitfall #58). Exactly why global worked and per-panel didn't. Fixed to the full chain (`var(--panel-font-size, var(--game-font-size, 12px))`); the map's other READING surfaces (hover tooltip, legend) joined via a shared `PANEL_FONT` anchor with em children. SVG node glyphs (↗ cross-zone, tool markers) deliberately stay geometry-sized; map chrome strips stay fixed px. **The follow-up audit of every panel root found ONE more offender: the Debug panel** — `rem` throughout (pitfall #45: pinned to the 16px html root, so it ignored BOTH the global font and A+/A−); its root now anchors the chain with children converted rem→em (×16/12 preserves default sizes). All other panels (stream/room/injuries/exp/compact-exp/lichScripts) were already correctly chained; the Lich Map view is image tiles. |
| B200 | **Line-scope highlights don't override preset-colored text** (thoughts / speech / lnet / substituted lines) — Cherisse: a "Line — entire line is styled" highlight on her Gweth/Lnet lines never showed; it failed "whether substituted or not." | v0.14.4 (Cherisse) | The highlight WAS matching — `getLineHighlightStyle` ([renderSegmentFull.tsx](src/renderer/utils/renderSegmentFull.tsx)) set the color on the `.text-line` CONTAINER, but each segment renders its own color: a thought/speech/lnet line is a `<span data-preset="thought">` (colored via [panels.css](src/renderer/styles/panels.css) `[data-preset]`), and a child's explicit color beats the inherited container color, so the preset green rendered on top and the line highlight was invisible. So line-scope only ever worked on UNCOLORED text; on anything preset-colored — and on substituted lines (B195 collapses them to one preset-styled segment) — it lost. Not substitution-specific; substitution just guarantees a colored segment. **Match-scope already overrode** (its `hl-match` run carries an inline color that beats the no-`!important` `[data-preset]` CSS). **Fix (Sekmeht: "make any highlight override it"):** thread the line-highlight's text color as an `overrideColor` into `renderSegment`/`renderSegmentFull` — the non-match runs now render with that color inline (winning over preset/fg), keeping the preset's background/italic + bold/links, while match-scope runs still punch through with their own color. Applied at the 2 line-render sites (`TextLineRow` → covers main window + all stream panels; `RoomPanel`). Regression-safe: no line highlight → `overrideColor` undefined → unchanged; a bg-only line highlight leaves text colors alone (only `color` is threaded); contacts keep their template color (B116). |
| B199 | **Trigger "command" actions don't echo `>cmd`** — Sekmeht: a trigger that sends `smile` showed only the game's response, never the `>smile` line a typed command paints. Inconsistent with typed/macro/alias/map-walk commands, which all echo. | v0.14.4 (Sekmeht) | The trigger engine's `command` action calls `triggerCallbacks.sendCommand` ([useTriggerEngine.ts](src/renderer/hooks/useTriggerEngine.ts) `executeAction`). In GameWindow that callback was wired to a **raw** `window.api.sendCommand(sessionIdRef.current, cmd)` — it sent the command but skipped the `setLines` echo, so the game responded with no visible `>cmd`. (The renderer-side echo is SEPARATE from the send — pitfall #86 — so a raw send is a silent no-echo.) The canonical `sendCommand` useCallback (used by map walks / exit buttons / in-text command links) DOES echo `>${cmd}` (`command-echo` preset) then send — and the command-send pipeline note already documented triggers as routing "via `triggerCallbacks → sendCommand`," so the raw wiring was the bug, not the design. **Fix:** route `triggerCallbacks.sendCommand` through the canonical echoing `sendCommand`. Because that useCallback is defined LATER in the component than the `triggerCallbacks` useMemo (TDZ), it's reached via a latest-closure `sendCommandRef` (pitfall #31) synced in a `useEffect`. Now trigger commands echo `>cmd` like every other command path (immediate AND `delayMs` actions, since both go through `cbs.sendCommand`); `sessionIdRef.current` keeps the send on the live id across a reconnect (pitfall #86). Session-logging trigger commands (the canonical `sendCommand`, like map/exit/link, still doesn't log to the `cmd` stream) is a separate pre-existing choice, out of scope for this echo fix. |
| B198 | **Profile export filename uses the NEXT day's date** (and a same-day re-export overwrote the first) — JadedSoul: exported 6/17 11:29pm → named `…2026-06-18.lb.yaml`; a 2nd export of the same char clobbered the first. Makes "most recent by filename date" misleading and silently destroys a prior export. | v0.14.4 (JadedSoul) | Two parts, both in the Profile Transfer export path. **(1) Next-day date:** `defaultExportFilename` ([profileTransfer.ts](src/renderer/profileTransfer.ts)) stamped the date from `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)` — `toISOString()` is **UTC**, so for a user west of UTC in the evening the UTC calendar date is already tomorrow. Fixed to build `YYYY-MM-DD` from LOCAL parts (`getFullYear`/`getMonth`/`getDate`). **(2) Silent overwrite:** the `profile-transfer:export` handler ([main.ts](src/main/main.ts)) did a bare `fs.writeFileSync(target,…)`, so a second export of one character on one day (same date-stamped name) silently clobbered the first (Principle #3, no silent data loss). Fixed to be collision-safe — if the target exists, insert `-2`/`-3`/… before the `.lb.yaml` double-extension; the returned full path (shown to the user) reflects the actual name written. Generalizes to pitfall #87/#90 family: any server/UTC-vs-local date stamp is a latent timezone bug — stamp filenames/displays from LOCAL parts unless an absolute UTC instant is intended. |
| B197 | **Deleting a highlight in Automations appears to "recreate"/duplicate it** — JadedSoul (Sekmeht confirms): delete "bronze", close+reopen Automations, it's back; repeat → 10+ copies; export grew 1955→2016 KB; persists across restart. Trigger context: a huge imported ruleset (Wrayth+Genie → 3436 highlights, many duplicates). | v0.14.4 (JadedSoul/Sekmeht) | **Root cause: localStorage QUOTA, confirmed by elimination + a code-confirmed causal chain.** The PURE DATA PATH WAS PROVEN CLEAN first — a throwaway harness (`tmp-dedup-repro`, since removed) ran the REAL `loadHighlights`/`saveHighlights` + the full `localStorage → YAML → reload + restart` round-trip; delete went 2→1 / total 4→3 and STAYED through reopen+restart, YAML matching (IDs unique via `nanoid`, `exportCharacterProfile` REPLACES not appends `state`). That eliminated load/save/profile as the cause. The chain that produces the exact symptom: 3436 highlights × several characters in ONE origin's ~5MB localStorage exceeds quota; a bare `setItem` then **THREW**, the delete's write was silently lost, `setRules` had already painted it gone, and reopen reloaded the stale list → the rule "came back" (and a re-save of the now-larger list grew the export). **Fix:** (1) all 6 rule saves (`saveHighlights`/`Triggers`/`Macros`/`Aliases`/`Mutes`/`Substitutes`) now route through `safeSetItem` ([characterScope.ts](src/renderer/characterScope.ts)) — catches the quota error, no longer fails silently, and shows a one-shot alert pointing the user at the dedup tool; (2) a **"Remove duplicate copies"** bulk action in Automation Analytics (keep one of each set) — because replacing the huge key with a much smaller deduped value is a NET decrease, that save succeeds even when an individual delete would still be over quota, getting the user back under the cap so normal deletes persist again. The `[hl-diag]` console diagnostics added during the investigation were removed once the chain was confirmed (Iron-rule: harness + code-read, not a guess). See pitfall #90. |
| B196 | **Group roster spams the main window when no Group panel is open** (Cherisse/Agan), and the "Grouped" status chip only lit for one character in a pair. | v0.14.3 (Cherisse/Agan) | Two parts. **(1) Spam:** `group` was in `STREAM_FALLBACK` with `→ main`, but the `group` stream is a clears-and-rewrites ROSTER (`<clearStream id='group'/>` + 3-4 `<pushStream id='group'/>` lines on every join/leave/hand-hold/health-tick) — STATE, not a message log, like `percWindow`/`moonWindow` (deliberately not in the table). So every refresh re-dumped the whole roster to the story window. Fix: removed `group` from `STREAM_FALLBACK` ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) — it routes only to its own buffer now (viewable if a Group panel is added), never main. Safe because DR sends group content ONLY inside the pushStream block (no native outside-copy, unlike speech/B136). **(2) "Grouped" chip differs per character — NOT a bug, verified from the XML:** DR sends `IconJOINED visible="y"` (+ prompt `J>`) only to the FOLLOWER (joined/following via hand-hold), `visible="n"` (+ `>`) to the LEADER — the leader has members joined to *them* but isn't "joined" themselves. Lichborne mirrors `IconJOINED` faithfully, so the asymmetry is the game's protocol. Renamed the chip "Grouped" → **"Joined"** ([IconBar.tsx](src/renderer/components/IconBar.tsx) + the Living Tableau legend) so the label matches what the game actually signals, removing the "why isn't the leader grouped?" confusion. |
| B195 | **Substitutes (and capture groups) don't fire on the game's own Gweth/thoughts, only on lnet** — Cherisse: `^\[(.*)\] Your mind hears (.*) thinking,\s*"<to you>" (.*)` → `[Gweth][$1][$2] to you: $3` worked on lnet thoughts (single segment via Lich) but never on native DR thoughts. | v0.14.3 (Cherisse) | A DR thought arrives as TWO segments — `<preset id='thought'>[Personal] Your mind hears X thinking, </preset>` + `"<to you>" "msg"` — and `applySubstitutesToSegments` ([substitutes.ts](src/renderer/substitutes.ts)) ran the regex PER-SEGMENT, so a pattern spanning the preset boundary matched neither half. lnet comes through Lich as one unbroken segment (why it "worked"). Highlights were already fixed for the same fragmentation (B115, joined-line scan) but only style ranges; substitutes replace text + reorder capture groups, so they were left per-segment. Both siblings substitute on the whole plain line (Genie `ParseSubstitutions`, Frostbite `alter->substitute`). **Fix:** per-segment pass first (preserves styling for in-segment matches, no regression), then a JOINED-line pass — if it differs, the match crossed a boundary, so collapse to one segment carrying the joined-substituted text styled like the first non-empty segment (visual fields only, no link fields). Verified against the real captured segments. **WYSIWYG:** the parser TRIMS the preset's trailing space (`thinking, ` → `thinking,`) and `.text-line` is `pre-wrap`, so rendered text === the regex's match text — Cherisse's literal `thinking,"<to you>"` (no space) matches what she sees, no `\s*` needed; the fix only lets the match span the segment boundary. (Reasoning from raw XML mis-led me into a wrong `\s*` suggestion first — the PARSER OUTPUT, not the raw socket XML, is the source of truth for what a renderer-side regex sees.) See pitfall #89. |
| B194 | **Muted lines leave orphaned `>` prompts that pile up** — Cherisse muted her mana-regen messages and saw bare `>` / `>>` / `>>>` prompts stack up; "looked weird and different than Wrayth/Genie." | v0.14.3 (Cherisse) | LAYER MISMATCH. DR fires a `<prompt>` after every server turn; the parser already suppresses a prompt identical to the last main text (`lastMainText`), so consecutive identical prompts collapse upstream. But MUTES are a display-only renderer concern ([applyTextMods](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx), DESIGN §31) that runs a layer LATER — so a turn of `<regen msg>` + `>` parses as real-text-then-prompt (parser correctly emits the `>`), the mute then removes the regen line, and the `>` is orphaned; several muted regens in a row pile up because the parser saw distinct real text between each prompt. Both siblings avoid this by gagging at the DISPLAY layer before the prompt commits — Genie's `PrintTextToWindow` (`Game.cs` ~2938) `return`s on a gag and prompt logic is a separate path; Frostbite's `MainLogger::onProcess` runs `if(alter->ignore(...)) return;` before its `prevType==PROMPT` bookkeeping and models the prompt as a lazy marker. **Fix (keep mutes in the renderer per Principle #2): replicate the parser's identical-prompt dedup at the display layer, AFTER mutes.** The parser tags each prompt line `prompt:true` ([types.ts](src/shared/types.ts)); GameWindow carries it onto the `TextLine` and a collapse pass after `applyTextMods` drops a prompt whose text equals the previous DISPLAYED main line when both are prompts (tracked across batches via `lastMainLineRef`). Gates on the `prompt` flag (repeated real content never collapses), matches on text equality (statusprompt `H>`→`R>` drift preserved), and is a no-op without mutes (the parser already deduped). Raw XML still streams and the Session Log still captures the pre-mute `lineText` — nothing lost. See pitfall #88. |
| B193 | **Debug → Fires tab "Edit" button is greyed out in Windowed Panels mode** (works in Static Panels). | v0.14.3 (Sekmeht) | The Edit button is `disabled={!entry.ruleId || !onGotoFireRule}`. DebugPanel is rendered TWO ways: the docked panels-mode strip ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) passed `onGotoFireRule={gotoFireRule}`, but the **`debug` tab hosted in a PanelFrame** ([PanelFrame.tsx](src/renderer/components/PanelFrame.tsx) `renderPanel`) — how it's shown in a windowed-mode floating window (and a zone-hosted debug tab in panels mode) — rendered DebugPanel WITHOUT `onGotoFireRule`, so the gate disabled the button. Fix: thread `onGotoFireRule: gotoFireRule` through `sharedFrameProps` → PanelFrame props → `renderPanel` → DebugPanel (passed RAW, not `?? NOOP`, so the per-entry "no rule id" disable still holds). `gotoFireRule` is host-independent (opens the highlight/trigger editor overlay, works in both modes). Same dual-hosting prop-threading trap as B166 — **any prop/callback the docked-strip DebugPanel gets must also ride `sharedFrameProps`, or the PanelFrame-hosted copy silently degrades** (pitfall #79 family). |
| B192 | **RT/CT bar jumps to full and counts down from a "really big number"** — Aubrey: the roundtime/cast-time bar overlaying the command input maxed out the moment RT/spell started, then drained far too slowly. | v0.14.2 (Aubrey) | NOT a Lichborne parse bug — the raw XML proved the server value was correct (`<roundTime value='1781629079'/>` with the next `<prompt time='1781629075'>` = a clean 4-sec RT). Root cause was **client/server CLOCK SKEW**: the bar computed `rt = (value*1000 − Date.now())/1000`, comparing the server's absolute end-time to the LOCAL clock — and Aubrey's PC clock was ~1–2 min behind the game server (stale NTP sync), so `rt = realRT + skew` (bar full, both `rt` and `rtMax` inflated equally → `rtPct≈100`, counting down `skew` seconds too late). Matched every symptom incl. "worked last night / stopped temporarily / update didn't fix it" (clock drift, not code) and "you can't reproduce it" (correct clock). Confirmed by Aubrey: clock off by a minute+, last synced the day before; "Sync now" fixed her immediately. **Robust fix (so no user's clock can break it):** anchor RT/CT to the SERVER clock via the `<prompt time>` tag — `<roundTime>/<castTime>` defer to the next prompt, then `expires = Date.now() + clamp(end − promptTime, 0, 300s)*1000`. Duration from the server, local clock only as the countdown anchor (compared against the same local clock), so skew cancels. **Both Frostbite (`xmlparserthread.cpp`: `t_to = N − promptTime`, 300000ms cap) and Genie (`Game.cs`: `rt = m_iRoundTime − m_iGameTime`) do exactly this** — neither uses the local clock; matching them is the quality bar (pitfall #87). |
| B191 | **Spurious "New Lines" badge on the ACTIVE character after tabbing to another Windows app** (NOT minimizing) — background-tab characters never showed it. | v0.14.1 (Sekmeht) | Same class as pitfall #71 (rAF throttles when the window is hidden/OCCLUDED — another app covering Lichborne counts, not just minimize — even with `backgroundThrottling:false`). The story-window pin is kept by `stickToBottom`'s rAF **settle loop**, which re-arms `suppressUntilRef` each frame and chases Virtuoso's ASYNC last-row measurement. When occluded the loop stalls, the initial +300ms suppress expires before that async measurement grows `scrollHeight`, and the late `scroll` event hits `handleVirtuosoScroll` with `dist > 40` → un-pins with **no user involvement**. `onRefocus` re-snaps only `if (pinnedRef.current)`, so once un-pinned-while-away it bails and the badge persists. Background chars are `display:none` (0×0, `dist≈0`) so they never tripped it — matching the report exactly. **Fix:** `handleVirtuosoScroll` returns early when `document.hidden` — a user can't scroll a hidden/occluded window, so any scroll signal there is layout, not intent; `pinnedRef` stays true and `onRefocus` re-snaps on return. The scroll-pin counterpart to the room pump's existing `document.hidden` synchronous-drain (the scroll machinery had no hidden fallback). |
| B190 | **Compass keeps the previous room's exits when a room has none** — Binu: the game sends `<compass></compass>` (empty) for an exitless room, but the FloatingCompass kept showing the old exits. | v0.14.1 (Binu) | The parser DELIBERATELY suppressed emitting `exits: []` for an empty `<compass>` (to dodge spurious empty compasses from intermediate stream updates / Lich refreshes), accepting "exitless room shows stale exits until next look" as a rare trade-off. But the only other thing clearing exits is the `clear-stream 'room-exits'` on a room/nav TRANSITION — so an exit change without a transition (or a no-exit room reached without re-firing the title gate) never cleared. Fix ([StormFrontParser.ts](src/main/parser/StormFrontParser.ts) compass close): the compass is authoritative, so an empty compass now emits `exits: []` — but **only on the MAIN stream** (`activeStream === 'main' && !streamStack.length`, the same gate the `Name:` detection uses). Empty compasses inside a `pushStream` block (the original spurious concern) are still ignored; the genuine game compass on main clears immediately. |
| B189 | **Macros echo their command but it doesn't run** — Morress: F4→`;t2` showed `>;t2` but the script never started; a NumPad+→`LOOK` macro echoed but didn't look either. | v0.14.1 (Morress) | NOT `;`-specific (the LOOK case proved it). Root cause is a STALE sessionId in held closures (pitfall #69): the macro keydown handler is mounted ONCE (`[]` deps) and calls `sendCommandSequence` directly; `sendCommand` is a `[]`-deps `useCallback`; `triggerCallbacks` is a `useMemo` — all capture `session.sessionId` from first render. A **tab Reconnect swaps in a new sessionId WITHOUT remounting the GameWindow**, so those closures send to the DEAD session — the echo still paints (stable `setLines`) but `window.api.sendCommand(deadId, …)` is dropped. Typed commands worked because the command-bar handler is re-created every render with the live id. Fix: every command-send closure now routes through `sessionIdRef.current` (synced every render, line ~288) instead of the captured `session.sessionId` — reconnect-safe by construction. Also fixed map-walk / room-exit / trigger-fired sends, which had the same latent staleness. **Same root cause, separately reported by Sekmeth via the automapper:** right-click-to-walk on the Genie map (`west`, `go stable door`) and the Lich map (`;go2 <id>`) echoed but the game never moved / never sent a `>` prompt back — both route through `sendCommand`, so the `sessionIdRef.current` fix covers them too. The signature ("commands echo but the game doesn't respond and no prompt returns") is the tell for this whole bug class. |
| B188 | **Theme Editor → Room & Exp → Experience: the "Rate" colour (and others) do nothing** — Morress set "Rate" to magenta but the "(30/34)" mindstate fraction stayed its old colour. | v0.14.1 (Morress) | Audit found SEVEN orphaned controls in the editor (exposed swatches with no CSS consumer). **Wired:** "Rate" (`--exp-rate-color`) → `.exp-mindstate-frac` (was hardcoded `--text-dim`; defaults to it, so no regression until set); "Mind-locked rate" (`--exp-locked-rate`) → new `.exp-row--locked .exp-mindstate-frac` rule; "Bar background" (`--exp-bar-bg`) → `.exp-bar-track` (was hardcoded `--border-faint`; each theme already ships a designed value). **Removed (no feature behind them):** the "Indicators (inactive)" group (`--ind-inactive-*` — the icon bar only renders ACTIVE indicators) and the compass "Center marker" (`--compass-center-text` — no center element). The underlying var definitions were left in the theme objects as harmless dead data; only the editor controls changed. General lesson: every Theme-Editor `c('--x', …)` entry must have a CSS `var(--x)` consumer — a grep audit catches the drift (watch comment-only false negatives). |
| B187 | **A phantom player named "leaves" in the Tableau.** | v0.14.1 (Sekmeht) | The cast extraction took the chunk's bare LAST WORD as the name (Lich's `PLAYER_NAME /\w+$/`), but flavor tails without a `who is/has` anchor survive the status strip — "Alytte covered in autumn leaves" minted a player named "leaves". DR names are always capitalized, so the name is now the last CAPITALIZED word in the cleaned chunk, and a chunk with no capitalized word is skipped rather than guessed at. |
| B186 | **The Living Tableau's default background didn't match the floating panel windows** — it read as a visibly darker surface next to them. | v0.14.1 (Sekmeht) | `--experience-scene-bg` cascaded from `--bg-base`, but `.fl-window` bodies sit on `--bg-app`. One-line cascade fix in `darkBase` ([themes.ts](src/renderer/themes.ts)): `--experience-scene-bg` → `var(--bg-app)`, so the Tableau follows the same surface as its sibling windows on every theme (pitfall #34 family). |
| B185 | **The Experience window doesn't snap against Windowed-Panels windows** (and vice versa). | v0.14.1 (Sekmeht) | Each layer's `getSnapTargets` queried only its OWN `.fl-window` elements, so the §34.4 "same snap targets" promise didn't hold across the ExperienceLayer/WindowLayer boundary. Both layers are absolute inset-0 over the same `.game-layout` root, so the fix is symmetric and tiny: measure `root.closest('.game-layout').querySelectorAll('.fl-window')` and offset against the measuring layer's own rect — Experiences snap flush against panel windows AND panel windows snap against Experiences. |
| B184 | **The exp panel's Badging dropdown doesn't follow the character's guild** — every character starts at "None" and must be set by hand. | v0.14.0 (Sekmeht) | Wired the guild in from the same source Lich's `DRStats.guild` uses: the `info` command's output line (`Name: … Race: … Guild: …` — drparser.rb `NameRaceGuild`, mirrored VERBATIM including the greedy quantifiers + trailing-whitespace anchor; a lazy version captures "Moon" instead of "Moon Mage"). The parser emits a `character-guild` event (main-stream-gated, cheap `startsWith` pre-check; NOT behind the §35.6 Experience gate — badging works without Experiences); GameWindow remembers it per-character (`detectedGuild` scopedKey → YAML; deliberately NOT in `TRANSFER_CATEGORIES` — it's character identity) and auto-selects the matching badge. The launcher profile's manually-set guild field seeds it as a secondary source (read-only — pitfall #26). **Precedence: an explicit user choice (the stored `focus` key) ALWAYS wins; an unmatched guild string changes nothing** (Sekmeht's fallback rule — `guildToFocusOption` returns null and the dropdown keeps its value). Lich's own drinfomon startup `info` is issued `quiet:` (squelched from the FE), so detection fires on any VISIBLE `info`; the profile seed covers sessions without one. **Two field bugs caught by Sekmeht within the hour, both design holes:** (1) his CLERIC showed Moon Mage badging — root cause was the PRECEDENCE model, not detection: **Profile Transfer's viewPrefs category carries the `focus` key**, so importing the Moon Mage's setup planted `focus: "Moon Mage"` on the cleric as an "explicit choice," and the first cut's "explicit wins forever" rule then deliberately ignored a corrective `info`. Fixed by adopting Sekmeht's original model: **an own-sheet detection is AUTHORITATIVE** (it calls `handleFocusChange`, overriding any stored pick); a manual pick holds until the next own-sheet `info` (deliberate + visible = rare); no-match still changes nothing. (2) The own-character gate first compared the sheet Name's FIRST TOKEN — but real sheets carry the FULL TITLED name (`Name: Soul Reaver Cordio Hawt-Seord, Divine Hammer of Elanthia   Race: Dwarf   Guild: Cleric` — titles BEFORE the name, hyphenated surname + comma honorific after), which would have rejected the player's own sheet. The gate is now "session character appears as a whole word anywhere in the Name field" (escaped, case-insensitive). The verbatim titled line is a harness case (+ multi-word guild, prose negative). |
| B183 | **Five lava drakes in the room showed as ONE creature chip** in the Living Tableau. | v0.14.0 (Sekmeht, live corpus) | The cast extraction deduped identical creature names — correct for players, data loss for creatures (DR rooms commonly hold N identical critters; Lich ordinals them). Now identical creatures TALLY (`SceneCreature.count`) and the chip wears a ×N badge that tracks kills live; merge across the two creature sources takes the larger tally, and "any of them dead" greys the chip. Same live session also corrected the chip's default tint from danger-red to NEUTRAL — room creatures are usually pets (gerbils, lynxes), and the room list can't distinguish hostiles; real threat coloring belongs to the future CombatParser's engagement data (which also recorded the ≤4-creatures-can-advance mechanic for G1's threat pips — Sekmeht's capture demonstrates it verbatim: 5 present, 4 engaged, the 5th `slinks southwest`). |
| B182 | **Speech bubbles too small to read** — "difficult to read without being really zoomed in," and worse past 12 players (the amphitheater's smaller figures shrank them further). | v0.14.0 (Sekmeht, screenshots) | Two passes. The interim fix (game-font `font-size` + an inline counter-scale against the figure's depth transform) still left bubbles inheriting layout context from the scaled figure tree. The real fix is the **bubble LAYER**: bubbles render OUTSIDE the figures entirely, in scene-pixel space (the component measures the scene via a 0×0-guarded ResizeObserver, pitfall #83/#84) — constant game-font size for every speaker BY CONSTRUCTION, the speaker's name inside the bubble (tinted with their avatar color), and **collision-aware placement**: the newest bubble (cap 6 visible) claims the spot nearest its speaker, earlier ones get pushed upward, so bubbles never overlap; the tail's `--tail-dx` keeps aiming at the speaker when a bubble had to shift. See pitfall #84 — the general lesson is "constant-size UI must not live inside a transform-scaled subtree." |
| B181 | **Speech bubbles "stopped working" in a crowded room** — worked at first, then most speakers' bubbles never appeared (25-player Empaths' courtyard). | v0.14.0 (Sekmeht, live) | Not a capture failure: bubbles only rendered on SEATED figures, and the 12-seat cap left half the room in the "+N others" chip — overflow speakers' bubbles were silently dropped (Umptiskrunk, 3rd in the list, always worked; Kirelys, 13th, never did — the diagnostic split). Fix is the spec's missing **promote-on-speak**: anyone who's spoken within 120s seats ahead of the silent crowd (stable sort, so quiet folks keep their order). The same session prompted the **adaptive arrangement** (>12 players auto-switches the arc to a 26-seat two-row amphitheater) and **conversation gravity** (§35.7 — chattiness pulls talkers into an inner circle, directed speech pulls pairs together, self included). |
| B180 | **Accent/adverb speech not captured** — `Aenigma says in a melodic accent, "…"`, `Linanna softly says, "…"`, `Harsh quietly says, "…"` flowed to the conversation stream but produced no Tableau bubble. | v0.14.0 (Sekmeht, live corpus) | The first corpus-verified say regex required the verb IMMEDIATELY after the name and the quote immediately after the verb — the documented gap waiting for real shapes. Sekmeht's live capture provided them: the pattern now allows one lowercase adverb before the verb and a quote-free manner clause after it (`[^,"]*` — which also covers directed says, `You say to Agan,`). The lowercase-adverb rule can't eat a capitalized multi-word NPC name. All shapes are permanent harness cases in `tmp-scene-harness/run.mjs`; the capture is banked as `corpus/2026-06-12-says-accents-crowd.xml` (first real user-corpus file — gitignored, contains player names). |
| B179 | **The app-bar's More ⋯ menu opens as a "tiny stub" / renders behind the game content** (first build after the B178 narrow-window work). | v0.13.5 (Sekmeht) | A B178 regression with a precise mechanism: `container-type: inline-size` (added to `.app-bar` for the degradation tiers) applies **layout containment, which forces the element to become its own stacking context** — the More dropdown's `z-index: 9999` became INTERNAL to the bar's context, and the bar itself sat at `z: auto`, so the game area below painted over the menu (only a sliver survived). **Fix:** lift the bar's whole stacking context above the content plane — `.app-bar { position: relative; z-index: 70 }` ([app-bar.css](src/renderer/styles/app-bar.css)): game content + the Windowed-Panels `WindowLayer` sit at z ≤ 60, overlays/modals start at 100, so 70 sits cleanly between (verified against the full z-index inventory of every stylesheet). **Portaling the menu to `document.body` (the usual popover escape) was considered and REJECTED:** the B178 `--overflow` menu items are shown/hidden by the app-bar's own `@container` query, and a portaled menu is no longer a descendant of the bar — the gating would silently die. The tab right-click ContextMenu was verified portaled (unaffected). General trap for the pitfall-#82/#83 family: **adding `container-type` to an element traps every absolutely-positioned dropdown inside it in a new stacking context — audit the element's popovers when making anything a query container** (the exp panel's picker menus were audited too: they're clipped inside the panel by its own `overflow: hidden` either way, unchanged). No profile-shape change. |
| B178 | **Window can't be resized below ~half screen** (the drag just stops; Morress wants 4 windows tiled on one monitor), **the tab strip's overflow scrollbar ignores the themed scrollbar CSS**, the action buttons crowd into the character tabs at narrow widths, and **the icon bar's six status slots stay full-size instead of flexing**. | v0.13.4 (Morress, Binu, Sekmeht) | Four coordinated fixes. **(1) The resize stop was simply `minWidth: 900`** in `createWindow` ([main.ts](src/main/main.ts)) — no clashing layout, just a conservative constant; now **480** (= 4 columns on a 1920 monitor, Morress's exact use case). **(2) App-bar degradation ladder** so 480 stays usable ([app-bar.css](src/renderer/styles/app-bar.css) + [AppBar.tsx](src/renderer/components/AppBar.tsx)): at ≤ 860px the wordmark hides (status dot + window title still identify the app) and buttons compact; at ≤ 700px the five inline action buttons (Panels/Maps/Automations/Lich/Settings) collapse into the ⋯ More menu — both the inline buttons and their menu twins are ALWAYS rendered with CSS deciding visibility (`app-bar-collapsible` / `app-bar-more-item--overflow`), no width-measurement JS (the v0.10.0 More-menu stance). Disconnect/Login deliberately never collapses (destructive/critical actions don't hide in menus). Media queries, not container queries — the app-bar always spans the window. **(3) The unstyled scrollbar:** `.character-tabs` set `scrollbar-width: thin` — modern Chromium implements the standard property, and per spec it DISABLES all `::-webkit-scrollbar` custom styling, so the strip got the native thin bar instead of the themed track/thumb. Removed; slimness now via `.character-tabs::-webkit-scrollbar { height: 5px }`, colors inherit the global themed rules. **(4) Icon-bar status slots** ([iconbar.css](src/renderer/styles/iconbar.css)): `width: 7.14em; flex-shrink: 0` → `flex: 0 1 7.14em; min-width: 0` + ellipsis — all six chips share identical flex factors so they remain equal-width at any container width (state toggles still never shift layout, the original fixed-slot goal) but shrink equally in narrow windows instead of overflowing. **Follow-up (same pass, Sekmeht):** the first cut let the chips squeeze the HANDS to zero ("L/R hands completely disappearing") and the Mode button wrapped its label to two lines. The bar's information priority is hands/spell (real game state) > active status chips > empty placeholder slots — so the icon bar is now a size CONTAINER (a container, not media, query — in Windowed Panels it lives in a floating window, so its OWN width matters): hands/spell carry a `min-width: 5.5em` floor (ellipsized, never vanish), and at ≤ 46em the **empty** status slots collapse entirely, handing their space to the hands — active states (Standing/Hidden/Bleeding/…) always stay visible. Accepted consequence, documented in the CSS: at narrow widths a state toggling on/off shifts the row — content beats slot stability there. The Mode button gets `white-space: nowrap; flex-shrink: 0` (it keeps its compact intrinsic size; the chips are the flexible parts). No profile-shape change. |
| B177 | **Exp panel doesn't scale in a narrow panel** — the skill NAME clipped to nothing (only ranks/%/mindstate showing), a horizontal scrollbar appeared, and the header pickers painted over each other ("BadgingFocus None"). | v0.13.4 (TheTargonian/Rakkor + Sekmeht) | The row grid (`16px 1fr 20px 5ch 4ch 14ch 7ch`) had ONE flexible column — the name — so a narrow panel collapsed it to zero while the ~250px fixed tail survived, then overflowed into a horizontal scrollbar; the ctrl-bar's Badging picker (`flex:1 min-width:0`) shrank while its nowrap label overflowed visibly over the shrink-0 Focus/Sort pickers. **Fix — responsive degradation via CSS container queries** ([panels.css](src/renderer/styles/panels.css), `.exp-panel` is now `container-type: inline-size`; Electron 31's Chromium fully supports CQ): the name column is `minmax(8ch, 1fr)` (never collapses); at ≤ 26em the `(n/34)` fraction column drops, the mindstate column narrows to 9ch (ellipsized), picker captions hide and the right pickers shrink-with-clip; at ≤ 19em the mindstate word drops too — name/ranks/% + the progress bar (which always carries the mindstate visually) stay readable down to very thin strips. Breakpoints are in **em**, which a container query resolves against the CONTAINER's font-size — so the tiers scale with the per-panel font override (F31) for free. `overflow-x: hidden` on the body kills the scrollbar; picker buttons clip/ellipsize instead of overpainting. Information-priority order (name > ranks > % > mindstate word > fraction) chosen because the bar + % always convey learning state. No profile-shape change. |
| B176 | **Ctrl+- zooms out, but Ctrl++ does not zoom in.** | v0.13.4 (Binu) | Electron's `zoomIn` menu role binds `CommandOrControl+Plus` — but on most keyboard layouts `+` is SHIFT+`=`, so the chord users actually press (Ctrl with the `=`/`+` key, or Ctrl with numpad `+`) sends `=`/`numadd` and never matches; `Ctrl+-` worked because `-` is unshifted. Chrome accepts `Ctrl+=` and `Ctrl+numpad+` for exactly this reason. **Fix:** hidden alias menu items in `setupMenu` ([main.ts](src/main/main.ts)) registering `CommandOrControl+=` and `CommandOrControl+numadd` for `zoomIn` (+ `CommandOrControl+numsub` for `zoomOut` symmetry) — an invisible item's accelerator still registers, and the visible role items keep their stock labels/shortcuts. NOT a guardrail violation: the "never override an Electron-reserved chord" rule is about rebinding built-ins to Lichborne actions; these aliases point at the same built-in zoom, matching standard browser behavior. No profile-shape change. |
| B175 | **Decoupling a character to a new window showed a clear+rewrite status stream (moonwatch) with N stale lines accumulated** — the clearStream wasn't honored until the next live refresh snapped it back to one line. | v0.13.4 (Sekmeht) | The pitfall-#60 replay delivers the session's whole history buffer as **ONE batch**, and GameWindow's batch processing aggregates `clear-stream` events into a Set and stream lines into per-stream arrays — **losing the interleaving**: a buffer holding `clear,line,clear,line,clear,line` coalesced to "clear once, append every cycle's line." Live cycles arrive one-per-batch so normal play never showed it (and B173's same-batch refresh detection still held — no unread dots, no growth). **Fix:** in the `clear-stream` case, a clear also EMPTIES the lines already accumulated in this batch for that stream (`newStream[id] = []`), so only lines after the LAST clear survive — interleaved semantics preserved for any multi-cycle batch (replays; in principle also a B172-coalesced 16ms batch carrying two refresh cycles). Downstream consumers verified: the `setStreamLines` merge and the B173 unread skip both handle the emptied arrays correctly. No profile-shape change. |
| B174 | **Windowed Panels: switching character tabs tore down and re-initialized the hidden character's ENTIRE floating-window set** — text/streams/map "blip out for a second then come back," the Genie map showed its full "Loading Genie maps…" reload, "everything basically redraws and reinitializes" on every switch (plus, earlier: "map panel empty at connect"). | v0.13.4 (Sekmeht) | **Root cause (pinned by temporary `[diag]` mount/unmount instrumentation after static analysis ruled out every key/provider/replay mechanism): [WindowLayer.tsx](src/renderer/components/WindowLayer.tsx) gated its windows on the layer's measured size — `{size.w > 0 && size.h > 0 && windows.map(...)}` — and an inactive character tab is `display:none`, which measures 0×0 (pitfall #24).** So the ResizeObserver pushed `{0,0}` the moment a tab went hidden and React UNMOUNTED every FloatingWindow (map, stream panels, main text, command/vitals/icon bars); switching back remounted them all from scratch — MapPanel re-ran `loadLichDb` (multi-second JSON parse) + `loadGenie`, Virtuoso rebuilt the text, stream panels re-rendered. Day-one v0.13.0 Windowed Panels bug (NOT today's B171/B172 work — first noticed because heavy tab-switching in windowed mode while watching for jank). **Fix:** the measure callback ignores 0×0 (keeps the last real size), so the render gate now means "never measured yet" (first-mount garbage-geometry guard) and windows are NEVER unmounted by a hidden tab — stale px geometry while hidden is harmless (the ancestor is display:none; the observer re-fires with the real size on show). **Companion fix, exposed by the same diag log (`loadLichDb start` ×2 per mount):** MapPanel's `lichMapVersion` reload effect guarded only `=== 0`, so once Lich had printed `--- Map loaded` (version ≥ 1) every panel REMOUNT ran the mount-effect load AND the version effect — the whole Lich map JSON parsed twice back-to-back. Now a `lastMapVersionRef` initialized to the mount-time version fires the effect only on post-mount version CHANGES. CLAUDE.md pitfall #83. No profile-shape change. |
| B173 | **Self-refreshing "status window" streams (moonwatch.lic-style) kept their unread dot permanently lit** — the clear+rewrite refresh re-marked the tab unread on every cycle, making the indicator meaningless for those streams. | v0.13.4 (Sekmeht) | The standard Lich status-window idiom is `<clearStream/>` immediately followed by `<pushStream/>` with the rebuilt content ([moonwatch.lic:121-122](c:/Ruby4Lich5/Lich5/scripts/moonwatch.lic) does exactly this on every moon change). Both land in ONE event batch (single socket write; the B172 16ms coalescing makes this even more reliable), so the unread-marking loop in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) now treats **content arriving in the same batch as that stream's clear as a REFRESH, not new messages** — no dot. Genuine message streams (thoughts, deaths, conversation, …) never clear themselves, so their dots are unaffected. A clear also DROPS an already-lit dot (the content it pointed at is gone) — which both matches user intuition and self-heals the rare split-batch refresh on its next cycle — and the user-initiated right-click → Clear does the same. No profile-shape change. |
| B172 | **Fast travel looks "chunky, not smooth" — the client visibly lags behind Lich's XML flow during room-to-room floods** (reported right after B171 fixed the at-cap hop; large imported rulesets, e.g. 1,544 highlights with 905 regex-mode). | v0.13.4 (Sekmeht) | **Four compounding costs, four fixes — all behavior-preserving.** **(1) No flood coalescing:** main's `scheduleFlush` was a bare `setImmediate`, so every small socket chunk from Lich became its own IPC batch and the renderer ran a full pipeline pass + React render PER CHUNK (several per frame). Now leading-edge coalescing (`FLUSH_COALESCE_MS = 16` in [main.ts](src/main/main.ts)): idle → immediate flush (zero added latency), during a burst → one batch per ~frame. **(2) Regex-mode rules skipped the literal fast path:** `fastLower` (the cheap `includes()` pre-gate every evaluation site checks) was `null` for regex-mode rules — and imported rulesets are regex-heavy (Genie triggers import as regex, Frostbite alternations become regex). New [regexLiteral.ts](src/renderer/regexLiteral.ts): `extractRegexLiteral` conservatively derives a literal substring GUARANTEED present in any match (bails on top-level alternation; recurses into required groups; optional atoms excluded; `\xHH`/`\uHHHH` decoded), `literalGate` is the single mode→gate decider used by BOTH compile sites ([HighlightsContext](src/renderer/HighlightsContext.tsx), [useTriggerEngine](src/renderer/hooks/useTriggerEngine.ts)). Validated against the real profile: 629/906 regex rules gated, **0 correctness violations, identical match counts**, full-ruleset pass 0.244→0.152 ms/line. Bonus latent-bug fix: multi-word TEXT-mode rules were over-gated (tokens compile joined by `\s+` but the gate checked the single-space string — a double space silently skipped the rule); text mode now gates on its longest token. **(3) Per-segment line scans:** `renderSegmentFull` re-ran every match rule + the contact regex against the full lineText once PER SEGMENT (B115 side effect; DR fragments lines into 3-5 segments) — new `computeLineMatchRanges` runs the scan ONCE per line ([TextLineRow](src/renderer/components/TextLineRow.tsx), RoomPanel sections) and segments intersect the shared ranges. **(4) Whole-tree re-renders:** only TextLineRow/TimerDisplay were memoized — every batch re-rendered every open panel. Now `memo()` on StreamPanel/RoomPanel/ExpPanel/MapPanel with stabilized props: PanelFrame's renderPanel passes module-level `EMPTY_ARRAY`/`EMPTY_SET`/`NOOP` fallbacks and id-taking callbacks (StreamPanel `onClear(streamId)` instead of per-render closures); GameWindow useCallback's `clearStream`/`toggleStreamTimestamp`/`openHighlightEditor`/`openTriggerEditor` and — the deep leak — **useMemo's both context provider values** (the inline `value={{...}}` literals re-rendered every useHighlights/useContacts consumer on every render regardless of memo). CLAUDE.md pitfall #82. No profile-shape change. |
| B171 | **Main-window text "hops"/flip-flops at the bottom after a long session, the pinned-to-bottom hold occasionally lets go, and scrolling gets less smooth** — the long-untraceable residue of the B122/B153/B155/B158 scroll saga. | v0.13.4 (Sekmeht) | **Root cause: the MAX_LINES (2000) scrollback cap — which is exactly why it only appeared "after a while" and survived every scroll-machinery rewrite.** Once full, every batch trimmed the head of `lines` to hold length exactly at the cap (`prev.slice(-(MAX_LINES - newMain.length))`). Two coupled mechanisms, both **verified by a simulation harness** (`tmp-scroll-repro/`, disposable) that replicates GameWindow's scroll machinery verbatim and measures POST-PAINT: **(1)** react-virtuoso keys its row-size/offset cache by INDEX (`computeItemKey` only stabilizes React DOM reuse, NOT the measurement cache — confirmed in the library source); an uncompensated head-trim shifts every row's index each batch, the cache goes stale against variable-height rows, and painted content visibly jumps BACKWARD — measured **26 backward content-jumps up to 147px (~8 lines) in 12s of at-cap flood, vs ZERO pre-cap**. **(2)** With length pinned exactly AT the cap, trim-N+append-N leaves total height ~unchanged, so `totalListHeightChanged` — the ONLY per-batch `stickToBottom` trigger — frequently never fires (measured ~2.0 calls/batch pre-cap collapsing to 0.73 at-cap): no correction, no suppress window armed, and a late Virtuoso re-measure could then trip the 40px deadband → un-pin ("stuck-to-bottom doesn't last"). The index churn across all ~300 mounted rows (B152's `increaseViewportBy` buffer) every batch is also the smoothness degradation. **Virtuoso's own remedy (`firstItemIndex`) was tested and REJECTED**: it triggers the library's compensating `scrollBy`, which races the homegrown pinning (more off-bottom painted frames in both tested variants — see pitfall #81). **Fix: hysteresis (chunked) trimming** — `appendTrimmed()` in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) lets `lines` grow to `MAX_LINES + TRIM_CHUNK` (2400), then cuts back to 2000 in ONE slice. Between trims the count grows every batch (the height callback keeps firing); the rare big cut changes total height so much the callback ALWAYS fires and the existing sync-write+settle loop absorbs it in the same frame — **0 hops, 0 backward jumps, max 1px off-bottom in the harness**. All six `setLines` trim sites route through the one helper (per-batch, unpinned hard-cap, End/badge re-pin, 3 command-echo sites — echoes used to micro-shift on every keypress at cap too). Selection-copy (B152/B18) unaffected and slightly improved: un-pinned (scrolled-up, selecting) never trimmed and still doesn't; pinned head-trims only remove the oldest lines, now ~400 lines later and 130× less often. DOM bound rises 2000 → ~2400 rows peak. No profile-shape change. CLAUDE.md pitfall #81. |
| B170 | **Macro-within-macro composition doesn't work**: fire a template macro (`get @ from my dergatine backpack` → bar shows `get ⎵ from…`, cursor in the gap), then fire a second cursor macro (`second ` / `second @`) — instead of inserting at the cursor (Wrayth behavior), it wipes the bar and shows just `second`. | v0.13.3 (JadedSoul) | **Not a regression — a never-built mode.** Git archaeology: the cursor-macro fire path was replace-only and byte-identical from v0.8.10 (B137, when `@` macros shipped) through v0.13.2; the v0.11.9/v0.12.0 macro-import work (the suspected culprit) only changed IMPORT-time parsing. The "insert at cursor" mode was the documented deliberate simplification (pitfall #51: "if a tester ever genuinely needs it, we'd add it") — JadedSoul needs it. **Fix: composition-aware insert** ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) cursor-marker branch): input FOCUSED + NON-EMPTY → insert the macro text at the caret (replacing any selection; caret lands at the inserted `@` offset) — Wrayth's type-into-the-entry model; empty/unfocused bar → the old replace (template fire starts fresh). No macro data change needed — her imported Wrayth macros compose as-is. Pitfall #51 updated. No profile-shape change. |
| B169 | **TRUE root cause of B165's "random" hand-bar desyncs: Lich's default `inventory_boxes_off` downstream hook eats `<right>`/`<left>` tags.** | v0.13.3 (JadedSoul / found via her screenshots) | Lich installs `inventory_boxes_off` BY DEFAULT for stormfront-style front-ends ([main.rb:546](C:/temp/lich-dev/lich-5/lib/main/main.rb)); its strip regex is **greedy** — `<inv.+\/inv>` on a server line that starts with `<container`/`<clearContainer`/`<exposeContainer` swallows everything from the first `<inv` to the LAST `/inv>`, **including a hand tag sandwiched between two inv blocks** (the shape DR emits on a GET from a container when the game-side "display all inventory and container windows" account flag is on). Wrayth escapes because it sends `_flag Display Inventory Boxes 1` at bootstrap — Lich's upstream hook consumes it, removes the eater, and persists the player_id ([xmlparser.rb:604](C:/temp/lich-dev/lich-5/lib/common/xmlparser.rb) re-removes at every login). Lichborne never sent it → hook armed every session → only JadedSoul (account flag on, a Wrayth convert) ever saw it. Explains EVERYTHING: random items (whichever get/stow shares a line with container updates — her screenshot literally shows `get fol from my tr pack`), the right-hand bias (container gets land in the free hand), persistence through restarts (with the flag on, the LOGIN state dump also rides container lines), and why no other client reproduces (direct clients have no hook; Wrayth disarms it; wizard/avalon/frostbite FE branches never install it). **Fix:** main's line handler sends `_flag Display Inventory Boxes 1` ONCE per Lich session on the first `player-info` event (the `<app>` tag — after `<playerID>`, so Lich's persistence write lands) — Lich consumes it (never reaches DR, real account flag untouched) and the eater is gone, this session and (via the DB) all future ones. Direct-SGE sessions deliberately do NOT send it (no hook to disarm, and sent raw to DR it WOULD flip the real account flag). The newly-flowing container XML is fully absorbed (`container`/`clearContainer`/`exposeContainer` are SILENT_TAGS; `<inv>` is capture-discarded; pushStream `inv` has no main fallback) — zero display change. Verified against Frostbite's real captured DR session (`support/mock.xml`): our parser handles the entire 1225-line capture incl. login hand tags perfectly, proving the loss was upstream of the parser. CLAUDE.md pitfall #80. |
| B165 | Held item shows as "Empty" in the icon bar (hand top bars) — right hand more often; e.g. Illia's cookbook / a bamboo folder genuinely in hand while the bar shows `R Empty`. | v0.13.2 + v0.13.3 (JadedSoul) | **TWO distinct holes found; both closed.** **(1) Protocol-level gap (v0.13.2):** DR does NOT push `<right>`/`<left>` XML for every action that puts an item in a hand (custom-verb event items — her cookbook and labeled folder are both event items; Profanity hard-codes the same workaround). Fixed with the Profanity-style glance fallback (`inferHandsFromGlance`, pitfall #78) — `glance` re-syncs the bars regardless of cause. **(2) Import-remount hole (v0.13.3):** a Profile Transfer import to an ACTIVE character remounted the GameWindow (reload nonce, pitfall #56) with **no state replay** — hands/vitals/room reset to defaults; vitals self-heal on the next change but a long-parked held item never re-sends a hand tag, so it stuck on `Empty` for the rest of the session. Fixed: main's `session:reload` arms the same replay the move-window path uses (`replayTarget`/`holdingForReplay` + safety release, pitfall #60) before the remount, restoring scrollback + every sticky state; an import remount also no longer clears scrollback (the old pitfall-#56 tradeoff). **Attribution nuance (JadedSoul, 2026-06-11):** she first tied the recurrence to an import ("definitely after I imported Illia's settings"), but then clarified she'd **closed and restarted LB several times between the import and the symptom** — a remount desync can't survive a restart, so her actual sightings were most likely hole (1); hole (2) is code-verified real regardless and was fixed on the same evidence. **Follow-up detail (JadedSoul, 2026-06-11): it hits RANDOM items** (cookbook, weirdly-labeled scroll case, her almanac, the **Stellar Collector apparatus** — game said "hands full" while the bar showed `R Empty`; both sightings right hand) — so it's not *which* item but **how it entered the hand**: hand state is EVENT-driven (DR only sends `<right>`/`<left>` on a change), so a spell-SUMMONED item (no GET verb) and an item already in hand at LOGIN (no change event in the login burst) never populate the bar — which is also why it "persists" through LB/Lich restarts: nothing persists, each session is freshly wrong until the next real hand change (supporting evidence: Lich's own late-attach init synthesizes hand state only for GS, never DR — main.rb:704). Pending raw-XML confirmation; if DR prints a "you are holding…"-style TEXT line at login, extend `inferHandsFromGlance` to match it so logins self-heal. Note her `;listall` shows she runs **`almanac` + `sanowret-crystal`** (dr-scripts that auto-get/stow items on a schedule — her hands churn far more than a typical player's, multiplying exposure to any gap) **and `textsubs`** (rewrites the downstream INSIDE Lich — a user substitution matching text in/around a `<right>` tag line would mangle the tag, and a malformed hand tag is silently dropped; that loss shape was verified in the parser testing). **TRUE root cause found and fixed in v0.13.3 — see B169** (Lich's default `inventory_boxes_off` hook greedily eating hand tags on container-interaction lines; disarmed by sending Wrayth's `_flag Display Inventory Boxes 1` once per Lich session). The glance fallback and import-replay fixes above are retained as defense-in-depth — DR genuinely doesn't push hand XML for some custom-verb/spell-summon shapes (Profanity's fallback exists for a reason), and `glance` remains the universal re-sync. Investigation history: parser verified clean against the real bundled `StormFrontParser` (exact item name, quotes, entities, 1000-char names, split lines); renderer/IconBar/replay-snapshot/interceptLine all verified. DESIGN §5.7. |
| B167 | In Windowed Panels, a stream tab's **unread dot stays lit permanently** — viewing the tab (locked or unlocked) doesn't clear it (noticed on Arrivals). | v0.13.2 (Sekmeht) | Two free-mode blind spots in the unread tracking, one bonus find. **(a)** `activeIdsRef` (suppresses marking a VISIBLE tab unread) was built from the zone active ids only — in free mode the zones aren't rendered, so every new arrival marked the tab unread even while it was on screen; now per-mode: free mode uses every floating window's `activeId` (all windows are visible at once). **(b)** `clearUnread` was wired only to the four zone `onActive` handlers — clicking a tab in a floating window never cleared; `renderFreeContent`'s `onActiveChange` now clears too. **Bonus:** the same audit found `mainTopActiveId` had been missing from `activeIdsRef` since the Main-Top zone shipped — in Static Panels, the *active* Main-Top tab re-marked itself unread on every new line. CLAUDE.md pitfall #79. No profile-shape change. |
| B168 | In Windowed Panels, **STREAM_FALLBACK routing reads the stale zone arrays** — a stream watched only by a floating window ALSO falls back to main (double display), and a stream still listed in a zone array but closed in windows has its fallback **blocked** (content silently buffered, invisible). | v0.13.2 (found by the pitfall-#79 audit, unreported) | Same family as B167: `watchedStreamsRef` (the B106 fallback gate, pitfall #39) aggregated zone tabs only, but in free mode the zone arrays are stale state (deliberately preserved so switching back to Static Panels restores the old layout) and the floating windows' tabs are the real watching surfaces. Now per-mode: free mode derives the watched set from every `kind:'panel'` window's tabs. The silent-buffer direction was the dangerous one — e.g. close the Thoughts window in free mode and thoughts stopped falling back to main entirely. CLAUDE.md pitfall #79. No profile-shape change. |
| B166 | In Windowed Panels (free layout), opening **Debug Mode renders the docked strip BEHIND the floating windows** — the strip is a flex child at the shell's bottom while the `WindowLayer` is `position: absolute; inset: 0; z-index: 60` over the whole shell, so bottom-area windows cover it. | v0.13.2 (Sekmeht) | Design decision (Sekmeht): rather than reserving a bottom inset for the strip, **in windowed mode Debug opens as a floating window** — a normal panel window seeded with the existing `debug` tab (`newFloatWindow('panel', …, { tabs: [makeTab('debug')] })`), so the player drags/resizes/closes it like everything else; the docked strip is now **panels-mode only**. The toggle (`toggleFreeDebugWindow` in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) removes the debug TAB wherever it lives (closing a window left empty) or adds a fresh "Debug" window. **Collection is now presence-based**: a `debugOpen` memo (same pattern as `lichScriptsOpen`, pitfall #76) — strip OR zone-tab in panels mode, window-tab in free mode — drives `showDebugRef` + the `debugPanelToggle` raw-XML IPC gate. That also fixes two latent gaps: (a) a zone/window-hosted Debug **tab** used to collect nothing unless the strip was also open; (b) the collection effect now re-fires on `session.sessionId` change, so a tab-menu **reconnect** (pitfall #69 — same GameWindow, new session) re-arms main's `debugPanelOpen` flag — previously raw XML silently stopped flowing after reconnect while the panel looked open. An interim "shrink the WindowLayer above the strip" approach (bottomInset) was built then replaced by this; don't resurrect it — the strip simply doesn't exist in free mode. No profile-shape change (`debug` was already a valid `TabDef`; `freeWindows` already persisted). |
| B164 | The Lich Scripts panel's **"ACTIVE SCRIPTS" header bar AND bottom status strip are oversized** — both ~2-3x their text height instead of tight strips, and don't match the other panes. | v0.13.1 (Sekmeht) | Same root cause on both strips — a tight bar that inherited the game line-height (~1.4-1.5) and ballooned to 2-3x its text (**pitfall #75 again**) — plus oversized text. **Header:** `.sl-header-title` `1.04em → 0.84em` (cf. `.room-panel-section-label` `0.88em`) + `line-height: 1`, letter-spacing `0.8px → 0.06em` (px→em); bar padding `6px → 2px` (vertical); refresh icon `1.33em → 0.9em` with `line-height: 1` + no vertical padding so it doesn't drive the strip. **Footer:** `.sl-footer` `0.96em → 0.8em` (metadata scale) + `line-height: 1`, padding `5px → 2px` — this strip had NO `line-height` and the largest font, so it was the taller of the two; now ~halved ([lich-panels.css](src/renderer/styles/lich-panels.css)). Both still anchored to `var(--panel-font-size, var(--game-font-size))` (pitfall #58) and themed. CLAUDE.md pitfall #77. No profile-shape change. |
| B163 | The Lich Scripts panel silently injects `;listall` into every connected character's Lich **every 5 seconds, even with the panel closed**. Binu hit this while mapping: the poll pulsed exactly as he entered a movement command, and Lich's automap captured `;listall` as the room's connecting command ("`;listall → 0.2` and `#### → ;listall` in the room's info"). | v0.13.1 (Binu) | The poll lives in `useLichBridge` ([useLichBridge.ts](src/renderer/hooks/useLichBridge.ts)), mounted per-session in GameWindow and gated only on `connected` — so it fired for **every** connected character regardless of whether any Lich Scripts surface was visible. And it can't be hidden from other Lich scripts: Lichborne and Lich share one front-end socket, and Lich runs `UpstreamHook.run` on every client line **before** handling `;` commands ([global_defs.rb:2225](C:/temp/lich-dev/lich-5/lib/global_defs.rb#L2225)), so automap's upstream hook sees our `;listall` byte-for-byte as if typed (no synthetic-input flag, no side channel — verified in the Lich source). **Fix (Idea A):** gate the poll on a `lichScripts` panel actually being open. A `lichScriptsOpen` memo in GameWindow (checks the zone tabs in Static-Panels mode / floating-window tabs in Windowed mode) drives a `lichPollRef` read inside the interval; it injects **nothing** while closed, and opening the panel fires one immediate seed poll (so the list isn't empty and pre-existing scripts show). With the panel closed, **zero `;listall`** is injected and automap is never disturbed. Player-typed `;list` still updates the panel (the response-side intercept, pitfall #22, is untouched); pause/resume/kill unaffected. CLAUDE.md pitfall #76. No profile-shape change. |
| B162 | Compact vital bars — the text ("M: 100%") sits slightly toward the **top** instead of true vertical center. | v0.13.1 (Sekmeht) | `.vital-text` had no explicit `line-height`, so it inherited the game-area line-height (~1.4). The roomy non-compact bar (1.83em) absorbs that extra leading, but the compact bar is only `1em` tall — the taller line-box overflowed it and the baseline pushed the caps/digits optically high. Fixed by pinning `.vital-text` to `line-height: 1` ([vitalsbar.css](src/renderer/styles/vitalsbar.css)) so flex centers the glyph box with no inherited leading. Affects both modes but only *visibly* changes compact (non-compact had slack). No profile-shape change. |
| B161 | The Genie/Lich **map background and labels don't follow the theme**: switching built-in themes changes the map, but editing a theme (Slate, Classic White, …) and changing **App Background** updates every panel *except* the map. The same root cause makes an imported 2-colour Frostbite theme (storm grey) leave the map darkBase-brown. | v0.13.1 (Binu, Sekmeht) | The Theme Editor only exposes the GENERAL vars; the map has its own `--map-*` vars. `darkBase` left `--map-bg` cascading (`var(--bg-app)`) but every OTHER built-in theme **pinned the map vars to literals**, so editing App Background couldn't reach them — only the stock **Dark** theme's map followed (it alone left them cascading; that was the tell Binu spotted). **Fixed at the root**: pulled the structural map colours (bg / chrome / text / borders / node fills / dots) into one `MAP_STRUCTURAL_CASCADE` wired to the general palette (`--map-bg`→`--bg-app`, `--map-text`→`--text-secondary`, …) and re-applied it **last** in `applyTheme`/`applyCustomTheme` ([themes.ts](src/renderer/themes.ts)) so a theme's pinned literal can't win. Only the **semantic** cues (directional arcs, the green "you-are-here" marker, selection accent) stay per-theme. Now the map canvas follows App Background and labels follow Game Text on EVERY theme, live in the editor, and custom/imported themes get a coherent map for free. The Lich Map's baked **PNG tiles** are unaffected (separate concern). Same family as pitfall #34. No profile-shape change. |
| B160 | Genie import pass (uniformity + comprehensive bug-check vs the Genie4-Dev source, real Genie-Remix config). Macros weren't uniform with Frostbite/Wrayth (own `splitAction`, stale "@ → move to a Lich alias" note); the "belongs in Lich" notices silently never showed; and two highlight/trigger pattern-matching bugs were found. | v0.11.9 (developer) | **Macro uniformity:** Genie migrated onto the shared `parseImportedMacroAction` ([macroAction.ts](src/renderer/import/macroAction.ts)) via a new **separator mode** (Genie `;`-splits and every command auto-sends, so nothing is appended — vs the send-token mode where a trailing no-token command gets `@`). `@` is now the native cursor (incl. mid-string: `first @`/`assess @`), NOT flagged. A `\x`-only "clear input" macro now correctly drops (no LB equivalent) instead of a mislabeled "Genie-internal" row. **Count-prefix bug:** Genie writes the SHORT forms, so `parseGenieFiles` matched only `#substitute`/`#variable` and reported **0** on real configs (suppressing the textsubs.lic/Lich-vars notices) — fixed to `#sub`/`#var`/`#gag`|`#ignore` (real config: 1 sub, 34 vars now surface). **Pattern bugs (found in the bug-check, fixed against Globals.cs:1007-1019):** (1) `/pattern/i` slash-wrapped highlights/triggers imported with the slashes+flag as literal text → **never matched** (`/The taipan…/i`); now `stripGenieSlashWrap` strips them and forces regex mode (4 highlights fixed; triggers hardened). (2) `beginswith` highlights (257) were mapped to a substring `phrase` that **over-matched** (fired anywhere in the line, not just the start); now imported as `^`-anchored regex per Genie's `HighlightBeginsWithList`.
