Verified end-to-end on the real config: 1295 highlights compile (0 fails), slash-forms now MATCH, beginswith anchors to line-start (no mid-line over-match), 67 macros with working `@` cursors, all JSON round-trip. **UI/UX:** confirmed the highlights preview is uniform across all 3 sources (shared table/columns, colored WYSIWYG chip + fg/bg swatches); added a scope hint ("· whole line") to the Mode column so whole-line highlights are distinguishable across every client. **Accepted minor gaps:** Genie `string` (6) → word-boundary `text` vs raw substring; hardcoded `;` separator; classes→groups still an unbuilt opportunity. **Capture-group triggers verified working:** Genie triggers like `^(\w+) adds you to (his\|her) group\.$` → `#echo >log $1 adds you to $2 group.` import as regex + an echo action carrying `$1`/`$2`; the engine populates numbered groups from the match ([useTriggerEngine.ts](src/renderer/hooks/useTriggerEngine.ts):353-356) so the echo resolves at fire time ("Bob adds you to his group."). **Sound-action fixes (Sekmeht):** (1) a `#play` sound no longer forces `partial` — a sound IS a supported action (uniform with Frostbite highlight sounds importing `ready`); it stays `ready` with an informational tooltip when a library name falls back to a preset. (2) The `sound` `TriggerAction` supports a real `soundFile` (WAV/audio path, played by `playWavFile`, priority over preset), but `mapTrigger` was discarding it and preset-mapping every sound — now `isAudioFile` paths/extensions are preserved verbatim as `soundFile` ("some users had WAV files") and only built-in NAMES fall back to a preset. **Import window width standardized:** `.iw-modal` now uses `--modal-w-standard`/`--modal-h-standard` (was a fixed 780px that squished the Genie highlights table) — matches Settings/Automations/etc. (pitfall #55 list). **No profile-shape change.** CLAUDE.md Import-wizard section. |
| B159 | Frostbite highlight import was broadly broken on a real export (Rakkor's `highlights.ini`): `\|`-alternation highlights (spell-up lists, character-name lists, globe/dome states — ~15 of his entries) matched nothing; the option bits (scope / word-vs-phrase) were read from the wrong bit; `[GeneralHighlight]` and `[AlertHighlight]` sections produced nothing; escaped values (`Redeemer\\'s Pride`) imported with stray backslashes; and **most rows showed "partial" in the preview**. | v0.11.9 (Rakkor) | **Root cause of the partial flood + wrong colours was a latent decoder bug** in [colorUtils.ts](src/renderer/import/colorUtils.ts) `parseQtEscapes`: Qt writes **minimal** hex escapes (`\x1`, `\x5`), not `\x01`, but the decoder always read **two** chars after `\x` and advanced by three — so on every single-digit escape it swallowed the following `\`, desyncing the whole byte stream after the spec byte (`\x1`). That corrupted the `options` bitarray byte (frequently flipping on bit 3 = "match groups" → my parser flagged `partial`) **and had been silently producing wrong colours since the Frostbite importer shipped**. Fixed to read 1–2 hex digits greedily and advance by what was consumed. **Parser rework** ([frostbite.ts](src/renderer/import/parsers/frostbite.ts)): `\|` → regex alternation (each alt regex-escaped, word-bounded when not partial-match); correct option-bit decode against Frostbite's `highlighter.cpp` (bit0=entire-row→line scope, bit1=partial→phrase/text, bit3=match-groups→partial note, bit4=case ignored — forced case-insensitive); Qt INI value unescaping; visual-identity dedup (pitfall #61a); fixed `[AlertHighlight]` count (keyed by name, no `size=`) and `[GeneralHighlight]` theme map (keyed by role id, no `size=`; both previously returned nothing) + added `i_script`→`--preset-cmd`; timer entries flagged `partial` not dropped. **Preview** ([ImportWizard.tsx](src/renderer/components/ImportWizard.tsx) + [import-wizard.css](src/renderer/styles/import-wizard.css)): each pattern now renders in its real fg/bg on a fixed dark game-like chip (WYSIWYG) + text/bg swatches — shared across Wrayth/Genie too. Verified against Rakkor's file via the real bundled parser: 63 highlights → 61 ready / 2 partial (timers) / 0 unsupported, 13 alternations converted, all colours correct. **Same-version follow-up — full-profile coverage** (a complete Frostbite `profiles/<name>/` folder, all four INIs): **macros.ini** now parses Frostbite's real `$n`/`$s`/`@` semantics (from `macrothread.cpp`) — both `$n`/`$s` send, a trailing no-send segment is type-and-wait with `@` as the cursor, so `take @ from my backpack` imports as a working cursor macro (was wrongly flagged "move to a Lich alias") and `"advance "` → `advance @`; dropped the bogus `;` split and the trailing-space trim. **general.ini** now also imports `[GameWindow] fontColor` → `--text-primary` (was only `background`→`--bg-app`) and surfaces `[QuickButton]` as a confirm-screen count ("recreate as macros/aliases"). **substitutes.ini** stays count-only → "use textsubs.lic" (text rewriting delegated to Lich by design — Sekmeht's call). **Comprehensive bug-check (clean):** bundled the REAL parser (esbuild) and ran two real profiles end-to-end through the full save/render path — **storm gray** (16/16 highlights compile via `buildHighlightRegex`, 29/29 macros → valid `MacroRule`s, all 3 `@` cursor macros produce the exact Frostbite `macrothread.cpp` cursor positions via the runtime `parseCursorMarker`) and **Rakkor** (63/63 highlights compile); everything JSON round-trips. Pre-merge four-questions all N/A or pass (no cross-feature state writes beyond the verified rule consumers; pure parser, no lifecycle; reads external files so no older-state concern; no profile-shape change). Three theoretical edge cases were considered and accepted as non-issues for real DR data (an escaped literal `\|` would be read as alternation; an `@` in a non-final SENT macro segment would be treated as a cursor; alternation patterns display their compiled regex in the preview chip). **Macro-import uniformity refactor (same version):** the duplicated per-client macro-action logic (Wrayth's `parseWraythAction`, Frostbite's `parseFrostbiteAction`, each with its own `hasUnescapedAt`) was consolidated into ONE shared `parseImportedMacroAction` ([macroAction.ts](src/renderer/import/macroAction.ts)) so both clients import macros identically (cursor `@` incl. mid-string, type-and-wait `@`-append, built-in filtering, `allBuiltin`→`unsupported`); each parser just supplies its send-token + cleaners. **Retroactive Wrayth check** (real Illiahanna.xml, 537 macros, before/after diff): byte-identical EXCEPT one improvement — `'}faunamae /quietly ` no longer trims its trailing space (`…quietly@`→`…quietly @`), so the cursor lands after the space (typing now yields `…quietly hello`, was `…quietlyhello`), matching Frostbite. Genie to be migrated to the shared helper next (it still carries the stale "move to a Lich alias" note). **No profile-shape change.** CLAUDE.md pitfall #72 + Import-wizard section. |
| B158 | Story window still **rests one line short of the bottom intermittently** — most new text pins flush, but every so often the last line sits one row above the vitals bar and a single mouse-wheel notch or one click of the scroll-down button snaps it to the true bottom. Also: **large XML bursts look "grouped"/batched** rather than flowing smoothly. "I need scrolling to always stay stuck to the bottom line no matter what, until someone manually scrolls up." | v0.11.8 (Sekmeht) | **Root cause: react-virtuoso measures the just-appended last row ASYNCHRONOUSLY** (its ResizeObserver fires a frame or two after the row mounts), so the scroller's `scrollHeight` keeps growing for a few frames after content renders. The B155 per-batch correction (synchronous write + a **single** rAF backup) could run before that measurement landed and converge on the short pre-measurement bottom — the intermittent one-line-short. Compounded by **three separate correctors racing the same target** (the per-batch inline write, the `resnapToBottom` settle loop for discrete events, and the inline ResizeObserver write). **Fix: consolidate all three into ONE primitive, `stickToBottom(reindex?)`** ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) — a self-cancelling rAF settle loop that re-asserts `scrollTop = scrollHeight − clientHeight` every frame until `scrollHeight` is **stable for two consecutive frames** (or a 20-frame cap), which always captures the async last-row grow. Per-batch (`totalListHeightChanged`) keeps the synchronous-first-write-before-paint then runs the loop for the tail; discrete relayouts (badge/End, font change, tab-show, focus-regain, replay) pass `reindex=true` to `scrollToIndex({index:'LAST'})` first for off-screen targets; the height-gated scroller ResizeObserver calls the same loop (writes only `scrollTop`, no re-render → no observer feedback loop). Keeping the loop alive across a flood (cancel+restart per batch) holds the bottom flush every frame, so **bursts read as continuous scroll instead of chunky jumps** (the second symptom). Trade-off (intended): during an active flood, suppression stays armed so only wheel-up un-pins; scrollbar drag/arrow un-pins once the flood pauses — i.e. "stay stuck until the user manually scrolls up." **No profile-shape change.** CLAUDE.md "Main text rendering" + pitfall #68 (d). |
| B157 | Map location indicator goes **stale whenever the window is left idle or minimized** — scripted movement runs but the "you are here" marker stays stuck on the room the player was in when the window went idle; restoring the window and typing `LOOK` snaps it to the correct room. "Why does just letting my computer go idle cause the location to be lost? Why doesn't scripted movement move the location while the window's been idle?" | v0.11.7 (developer) | **Not a matcher bug.** `roomState.title/desc/exits` (everything the matcher reads) is applied through the rAF room pump (`roomQueueRef` + `requestAnimationFrame`, pitfall #20), and Electron's `webPreferences.backgroundThrottling` **defaults to `true`**, which throttles/pauses rAF + timers once the window is minimized/occluded/backgrounded. Game events keep arriving over IPC and queue up, but the pump that drains them into `setRoomState` is frozen → room state sticks on the last room → the matcher correctly shows the last data it has → `LOOK` after restoring re-runs the pump and snaps. The "idle/minimized" tell from the reporter is what localized it. **Two-layer fix:** (a) `backgroundThrottling: false` on every `BrowserWindow` ([main.ts](src/main/main.ts)) so the renderer stays fully live when minimized (correct for an app designed to keep processing the game stream in the background; multi-session background characters need it too, pitfall #24); (b) the room pump checks `document.hidden` and drains the queue **synchronously** (coalesced to latest) when hidden, so room-state correctness never depends on platform throttling behavior (some OS/Electron versions still pause rAF for a minimized window even with throttling off). Visible-window path keeps the one-room-per-frame rAF pump (pitfall #20). **No profile-shape change.** CLAUDE.md pitfall #71 (+ #20 cross-ref). |
| B156 | Genie Map "you are here" indicator frequently lands on the **wrong room**, sometimes wildly off. Repro (developer): standing in `[Whistling Wood, Barrows]` (obvious paths east/northwest, "Several stumps line the pathway…" desc), the indicator showed Map68 "Shard South Gate" node **#138** — a southeast/northwest room with the "A mound of earth…" description. The data was fine: node **#172** is an exact description AND exit match. "Very inconsistent / feature-breaking." | v0.11.7 (developer) | **Two root causes.** (1) DR streams the room description **inline** in `main` as `<preset id='roomDesc'>…</preset>`, NOT as a `<component id='room desc'>`. The only code writing `roomState.desc` ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx), `stream==='room'`) was fed by the sparse component, so the desc was empty/stale during normal play and the Genie matcher's description tiebreaker fell through to **file order** across 40+ identically-titled "Whistling Wood, Barrows" nodes → #138. (2) The real GenieMaps client (`Node.Compare`/`CardinalCount`, [NodeList.cs](C:/temp/Genie4-Dev/Genie4/Mapper/NodeList.cs)) matches by **name + EXIT-SET + description** together; Lichborne ignored exits entirely. **Fix.** (a) GameWindow captures the inline `preset:'roomdesc'` main-segment into a batch-local `batchRoomDesc` and applies it to `roomUpdates.desc` **after** the event loop, so it overrides the B121 streamWindow `clear-stream 'room'` that lands later in the same batch (the inline desc is emitted before the streamWindow, so an in-loop write would be wiped). Also improves the Lich Map's findRoom title+desc fallback. (b) `roomState.exits` threaded GameWindow/[PanelFrame.tsx](src/renderer/components/PanelFrame.tsx)→[MapPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/MapPanel.tsx)→[GenieMapView.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/GenieMapView.tsx). (c) `currentLocation` rewritten as a ladder over the title pool: **desc-exact unique → exit-set-equality unique → desc-substring unique → graph-adjacency over the FULL pool → exit-aware conservative-cross-zone-fallback.** Exits are used ADDITIVELY (best-guess + cross-zone corroboration), never as a pre-filter before adjacency — an early cut that narrowed the pool by exits excluded the correct neighbour when Genie exit data was imperfect and stranded the marker, so adjacency runs over the full pool and the hold commits to a new-zone guess when exits corroborate it or when `prev`'s exits no longer match the live exits. Exit set excludes only `go`/`climb` (up/down/out count, mirroring CardinalCount); Genie full-words + DR abbreviations canonicalized to one token form. The fresh desc + exit set back each other up for near-100% accuracy and degrade gracefully on stale Genie data. `--stormfront` vs `--genie` is irrelevant (same title/desc/exits). **Developer-audit hardening (same version):** (1) a first cut PRE-FILTERED the pool by exits before adjacency, which excluded the correct neighbour when Genie's exit data for a room was imperfect and stranded the marker ("room text doesn't match anymore" / "stuck at the Shard healer") — exits are now additive only and the cross-zone hold is exit-aware; (2) the matching core was rewritten from an impure `useMemo` (mutated refs as it computed → double-counted under React StrictMode in dev) into a module-level PURE `resolveGenieRoom(...)` driven by a single idempotency-gated effect with explicit breadcrumb/stale-count refs. **No profile-shape change.** CLAUDE.md pitfall #70, DESIGN.md §19. |
| B155 | Story window bottom-pin broke several ways: the last line (e.g. the `>` prompt) **clipped under the vitals bar**; **switching character tabs** popped the "N new lines" badge and left the view a line short, and clicking the badge then stayed clipped; Binu hit it after **alt-tabbing to Discord and back**; it appeared on a character **split into its own window**; toggling **compact↔regular vitals** triggered it; and once layout was fixed, new lines showed a **"scrolled up a notch, then jumps to bottom"** flicker, plus a brief idle **jitter/"scaling fighting itself."** Decisive tester clue: moving the vitals bar top↔bottom **shifted the main window by a line.** | v0.11.6 (Sekmeht, Binu) | Turned out to be **three distinct bugs**, not the single "measurement timing" issue B153 (#43) assumed — fixed in three places. **(a) LAYOUT (the real clip root cause).** `.game-main` and `.text-window` ([game.css](src/renderer/styles/game.css)) were `flex: 1` children of flex **columns** with no `min-height: 0`, so (default `min-height: auto`) they refused to shrink below content and the column overflowed the window by ~a line, clipping the bottom — which is why moving the vitals bar flipped which end clipped. Added `min-height: 0` to both (textbook flexbox scrollable-child fix). B153/B122 chased this as scroll math and never found it. **(b) AUTO-FOLLOW two-step.** Virtuoso's `followOutput` aligns the last row to the bottom but under-measures it at fractional row/viewport heights (vitals/command bars scale by `em`), landing ~a notch short; the correction then snapped it down a frame later (visible on every line once (a) made the viewport cleanly fractional). Fix: **`followOutput={false}`** — when pinned, `totalListHeightChanged` sets `scrollTop = scrollHeight − clientHeight` **synchronously before paint** (rAF backup only for still-settling rows), so it's flush in the same frame, nothing to correct, no jump. `scrollHeight − clientHeight` is DOM-truth (immune to followOutput under-measurement). **(c) DISCRETE/CONTINUOUS RELAYOUTS.** A shared `resnapToBottom()` **settle loop** ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx); re-corrects each frame until `scrollHeight` stabilizes, 12-frame cap, suppresses synchronously, gated on `pinnedRef`) covers discrete transitions — badge/End, font change, `isActive` tab-show (inactive tab `display:none`→0×0, pitfall #24), `window 'focus'`+`visibilitychange` (alt-tab regain), and after a replay batch (split-window/decouple, pitfall #60 — its own required trigger since the `isActive` mount runs with `lines` empty and the new window's initial focus fires before the listener mounts, B149 race). A **passive height-gated `ResizeObserver`** on the scroller covers continuous viewport-height changes (vitals appearing at login, compact toggle, window resize) with a SINGLE bare `scrollTop` write — calling `resnapToBottom`/`scrollToIndex` from it re-renders→nudges the scroller size→re-fires the observer→visible idle "jitter" (a ResizeObserver→layout→ResizeObserver loop), which is why it stays bare. Verified by Sekmeht: stays pinned on new text, scroll-up badge + click-to-return still work, flush at font ≥ 13 / compact / vitals top↔bottom. No `CharacterProfile`/`SharedProfile` shape change. CLAUDE.md pitfall #68. |
| B154 | **Debug panel look & feel** — the Fires / Events / Raw XML diagnostic strip had transparent-looking rows, badly misaligned columns, a Goto control that was hard to read/find, and a cramped fixed 220px height with no way to review longer output. | v0.11.5 (developer) | Rework in [DebugPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/DebugPanel.tsx) + [debug.css](src/renderer/styles/debug.css). **Alignment:** the sticky header and rows are separate children of one `overflow-y:auto` scroller, so once the list overflowed and a scrollbar appeared they drifted apart by the scrollbar width → `scrollbar-gutter: stable` on `.debug-scroll` reserves the gutter so both share one content width. The fire log used `align-items: start` + unbounded wrap, so a long matched string made a 3–4-line-tall row with short cells floating at the top (ragged "misaligned" grid) → switched to `align-items: center` + single-line ellipsis per cell (`white-space:nowrap; overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis; min-width:0`) with full value in a `title` tooltip, so every row is one line and columns line up. Events converted flex→grid (header lines up with rows); Raw XML got a sticky header. **"Transparent" surfaces:** added zebra striping (`:nth-child(even)` via `color-mix(... var(--text-primary) 5%, transparent)`) on all three tabs + explicit theme-var backgrounds on chips/buttons; audited on light themes (pitfall #34/#55). **Goto:** the bare transparent `→` glyph became a filled, vertically-centred **"Edit →"** button in a fixed last column so it's always visible/clickable; `onGotoFireRule` wiring unchanged. **Scrollability:** drag-resizable height (top handle), default 300px (was fixed 220px), clamped 150 → 70vh, persisted per-character via `scopedKey(character,'debugPanelHeight')` (round-trips into YAML `state.*` automatically — **no profile-shape change**). Resize is opt-in via a `resizable` prop so the in-zone render (PanelFrame) still fills its zone (`.debug-panel--fill`) rather than imposing a height. CLAUDE.md pitfall #67. |
| B153 | Main story window rests **one line short of the bottom** at font size ≥ 13 — the last line (e.g. the `>` prompt) sits clipped at / under the vitals bar; you can always wheel down exactly one notch to reveal it. Fonts < 13 are fine. Recurrence of the B122 clip Rakkor reported earlier. Also: **changing the font size un-pins the view** — the "N new lines" badge appears as if you'd scrolled up, and the view strands ~1 line above the bottom. Happens with normal AND compact vitals (so it's not the vitals strip height). | v0.11.4 (Rakkor) | **Root cause (the clip):** Virtuoso's `followOutput` auto-scroll lands at "last item at viewport bottom" but **under-measures the last row at fractional heights** (a row is `min-height: 1.4em` + `0.15em` wrap padding ≈ 1.55em, non-integer at font ≥ 13), so the last line lands clipped — and, critically, `followOutput` runs **after** the synchronous bottom-correction in `totalListHeightChanged`, overriding it. The decisive tester clue: the view could always be wheeled down exactly one line, i.e. the true DOM bottom was reachable, the auto-follow just wasn't getting there. **The dead ends (recorded so they're not retried):** *(1)* integer per-row pixel heights — didn't help (the short-landing is followOutput under-measurement + override, not row-height rounding); *(2)* a **font-scaled footer** (v0.8.8 shipped a fixed 14px slack; scaling it to one row) — once the correction below started reaching the true bottom, any footer just became a one-line **gap** (last line a row ABOVE the vitals bar instead of flush), and it under/over-shot non-monotonically around font 13–14. Both reverted. **Fix:** (a) **defer the raw, DOM-truth bottom scroll into a `requestAnimationFrame`** in `totalListHeightChanged` ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) — it then runs after `followOutput` and wins, landing at `scrollHeight − clientHeight` (the genuine bottom, immune to Virtuoso's under-measurement), so the last line sits flush against the vitals bar at every font with **no clip and no gap**. **No footer.** (b) `scrollToBottom` (End key) and the font re-snap do the same rAF raw correction after their `scrollToIndex({align:'end'})`. (c) The re-snap effect arms `suppressUntilRef` **synchronously before** its rAF, so the relayout on a font change (rows AND the `--game-font-size`-scaled command bar both grow — pitfall #45) can't cross the un-pin deadband and strand the view / pop the "new lines" badge (a genuine wheel-up still cancels via the un-gated `onWheel`). No profile-shape change. Supersedes the B122 Footer approach (footer removed); CLAUDE.md pitfall #43 updated. |
| B152 | Selecting text to copy across a scroll boundary loses most of it — if you're scrolled up and select more than a screenful (so you have to scroll down while dragging), copy keeps "only a portion from the end" (~1 line past the final viewport). Regressed several releases ago; used to work. | v0.11.2 (Binu) | The story window is virtualized (react-virtuoso) — it only mounts the visible rows (+ a tiny default overscan). The browser drops **unmounted** DOM from a text selection, so when you start selecting up top and scroll down to extend, the start rows unmount and the mouseup auto-copy (`getSelection().toString()` → `window.api.writeClipboard`, [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) captures only the rows still mounted at release — i.e. the last viewport + 1. (The "used to work" regression is consistent with a virtuoso version bump tightening the default mounted-row window.) **Fix:** set `increaseViewportBy={{ top: 3000, bottom: 3000 }}` on the Virtuoso so ~150 rows each side of the viewport stay mounted, keeping several screenfuls of selection intact through the auto-scroll. Rows are memoized/cheap and `lines` is capped, so the extra DOM is negligible; scroll math (followOutput / totalListHeightChanged / pinning) is untouched. **Known bound:** a selection that scrolls more than ~3000px (≈150 rows) past its start still can't be fully captured by a DOM-selection copy — for the entire scrollback use **Session Log → Export**. A full data-model reconstruction (map selection anchor/focus to line ids and slice `lines`) would remove the bound but is deferred (complex, and the scroll path is delicate). |
| B151 | The parser never extracted the room id from DR's **`[Room Title] (56107)`** subtitle format — the **Simutronics room-id flag** display (room number in parens). So players who'd enabled that flag still got `roomId: undefined`, the Lich Map couldn't do its instant id lookup (forced into the fragile title+desc path → "location lost during movement"), and `$roomid` was empty. Found via a tester's telescope XML. | v0.11.2 (tester) | [StormFrontParser](src/main/parser/StormFrontParser.ts) only parsed the **other** id format — `[Whistling Wood, Barrows - 9479]` (id inside the brackets after `-`). DR (StormFront protocol) actually uses `[Title] (uid)` when the room-id flag is on, with `(**)` meaning unmapped (e.g. `[The Heavens] (**)` during a Moon Mage telescope flip). **Fix:** when the dash form isn't present, also match `\]\s*\((\d+)\)` after the closing bracket; `(**)` correctly yields no id (`\d+` doesn't match). roomId now flows from the subtitle on every transition for flag-on players → the Lich Map matches by id (robust, no description needed) and `$roomid` populates. **Purely additive / non-breaking when the flag is OFF:** no parens ⇒ `roomId` stays `undefined` exactly as before, and the whole pipeline already handles undefined (MapPanel falls back to title+desc; `$roomid` renders empty). The flag was never required and still isn't. The Genie map still matches by title+desc (its data has no game room ids) — see B148/B150. CLAUDE.md pitfall #65. No schema change (room-title `roomId` was already optional). |
| B150 | Same stuck-location symptom as B148 but on the **Lich Map** (image-tile view) — occasionally the marker doesn't update in a supported room | v0.11.2 (tester) | The Lich Map resolves the current room id-first (`lichDb.get(roomId)` — the game/Lich room id is the *primary, exact* signal, robust) and only falls back to title+description via [`findRoom`](src/renderer/components/panels/mapTypes.ts) when the id is absent or not in the loaded db. That fallback had the **same exact-equality-only description check** as the Genie map (B148): `normalizeDesc(d) === normDesc`, which misses when the stored description is a truncation of the live look, so an ambiguous title returned `undefined` (no location). **Fix:** applied the identical substring-containment fallback to `findRoom` — single-candidate now early-returns; on ambiguity, after exact equality fails, accept a candidate whose description contains/is-contained-by the live one, but only when exactly one matches and both are ≥24 chars. Same shape as the Genie `currentLocation` fix so the two map views behave consistently. **Note (active/inactive):** map location matching is per-session and state-driven (`MapPanel` gets each GameWindow's own `roomState.{title,desc,roomId}`, gated on the per-character `showMapOverlay`, NOT on `isActive`), so it tracks for every logged-in character whether the tab is focused or backgrounded — only the camera/layout is active-gated (pitfall #24, already handled). |
| B149 | In a decoupled (separate) window, the **Ctrl+Shift+Enter Quick Send** hotkey does nothing — the overlay never appears. ("All hotkeys should work based on the focused Electron window.") | v0.11.2 (tester) | The hotkey *was* firing — AppShell's keydown handler mounts in every window and called `setShowQuickSend`. But [QuickSend](src/renderer/components/QuickSend.tsx) `return null`s when the cross-window **roster** is empty, and a freshly-opened secondary window's roster *was* empty. Root cause: [RosterContext](src/renderer/RosterContext.tsx) was **push-only** — it subscribed to `onSessionRoster` but never pulled the current roster on mount. Main broadcasts the roster on `did-finish-load` ([main.ts](src/main/main.ts)), which a new window's renderer loses (it subscribes after React mounts), and after that there's often no further broadcast (a stable connected session emits none; `session:set-name` only broadcasts on a name *change*), so the empty roster never self-heals. `SessionsContext` avoided this by pulling owned sessions on mount (`get-owned-sessions`); the roster was left push-only — an oversight. **Fix:** added a `get-roster` IPC handler (returns `buildRoster()`), exposed `window.api.getRoster()`, and RosterContext now **pulls on mount** alongside the subscription (push still wins later). Quick Send (and any future roster-derived UI) now works immediately in a decoupled window. No schema change. |
| B148 | Genie map location stops updating while in a supported room — player is in `[Whistling Wood, Barrows]` (room 9479) looking repeatedly, but the green location marker stays stranded in another zone (Shard) | v0.11.2 (tester) | Genie maps match by **room title + description** (Genie builds its own internal id from those — its ids are NOT the same as Lich/game room ids, so id-matching isn't possible). The disambiguation in [GenieMapView.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/GenieMapView.tsx) `currentLocation`: when a title is ambiguous (many Barrows rooms share a title) and same/cross-zone adjacency doesn't resolve it, it falls back to the room **description**. The code's own comment promised "a normalized **substring**/equality match," but the code only did **exact** equality (`normalizeDesc(d) === nd`). Genie's stored descriptions are routinely a *truncation* (first sentence) of the live look, so exact equality missed on a real supported room — and the marker then hit the conservative cross-zone fallback (B110) that refuses to jump zones on a low-confidence `pool[0]`, stranding it in the previous zone. Standing still and `look`-ing didn't help because the memo is keyed on `[roomTitle, roomDesc, …]` — identical looks don't re-run it. **Fix:** added the promised substring-containment fallback after exact equality fails — accept a candidate whose description contains (or is contained by) the live description, but **only when exactly one** candidate matches and both strings are ≥24 chars (so a generic shared description can't mis-disambiguate). Additive: it can only resolve previously-unresolved ambiguity, never override an existing exact/adjacency match. No data/schema change. Could not be reproduced locally (the user's Genie map data isn't in the repo) — fix is by code inspection + the user's XML capture; flagged for the tester to confirm against their data. |
| B147 | Lich Dashboard → Profiles YAML editor: line numbers don't line up with the content, and search jumps to the wrong place on screen ("line spacing bleeding through") | v0.11.2 (tester) | The line-number gutter (`.ld-line-num`, `font-size: 0.75rem`) and the content (`.ld-yaml-preview` view / `.ld-yaml-editor` textarea, `font-size: 0.79rem`) both used a **unitless** `line-height: 1.65` — which multiplies each element's *own* font-size, so the gutter's per-line height was 19.8px vs the content's 20.86px. ~1px/line of drift that accumulates: by line ~50 the numbers are ~2.5 lines off the content they label. The search/overlay math reads `getComputedStyle(pre).lineHeight` (the content's value), so it scrolled to the right *content* line but the gutter and the user's mental mapping were off (the tester's "line spacing bleeding through" was exactly the line-height mismatch). **Fix** ([lich-panels.css](src/renderer/styles/lich-panels.css)): pin the gutter, the preview pre, and the editor textarea to one **absolute** `line-height: 1.3rem` so per-line height is independent of font-size; the computed value the search/overlay reads now matches the gutter too, so numbers, content, search scroll, and the highlight overlay all share one grid. Comments added on all three rules to keep them in lockstep. |
| B146 | Wrayth import drops every text highlight (and under-serves names/presets/macros) — a tester's real `Wrayth.xml` imported 0 highlights | v0.11.1 (Thanator) | Thanator brought a real Wrayth export, which surfaced that the parser ([wrayth.ts](src/renderer/import/parsers/wrayth.ts)) looked for a `<highlights>` block that Wrayth **never** emits — its text highlights live in `<strings>`, which the parser merely *counted* (mislabeled as substitutions). So every highlight was silently dropped. **Fixes (v0.11.1):** new `parseStrings` imports `<strings>` as match-scope highlights colored via `<palette>` (`@NN`→hex); `<names>` now also generates per-color **contact templates** (`colorNN`, by palette index) and assigns each contact to one; new `parsePresets` maps Wrayth `<presets>` → an "Imported from Wrayth" theme (same path as Genie's preset.cfg); macros now import from **all 10 sets** (was set 0 only) with cross-set key collisions flagged + an apply-time first-wins key dedup; `<ignores>`(gags)/`<vars>` surfaced as count-only notes. **Two sub-bugs found in the post-feature bug check, both fixed same release:** (a) the preview's **Color column scrolled off-screen** — `.iw-pattern`'s `max-width`/ellipsis is a no-op on an inline `<span>`, and Wrayth's full-sentence patterns expanded the cell ([import-wizard.css](src/renderer/styles/import-wizard.css): `display: inline-block`); (b) `parseStrings` initially deduped by **pattern alone** (copied from `parseNames`), silently dropping color-variant highlights like `shirt` @54 / `shirt` @26 — fixed to dedup on full visual identity (pattern+color+bg). No `CharacterProfile`/`SharedProfile` shape change. Entity decoding (`&apos;`→`'`, `&quot;`→`"`) verified working via `getAttr`→`decodeXmlEntities`. |
| B145 | Active Scripts panel uses a `C` badge for custom scripts but a bare `▶` arrow for normal ones — no clear visual for "this is a (normal) script" | v0.10.1 (Rakkor, Sekmeht, Binu) | Rakkor's suggestion (Sekmeht + Binu agreed): make it a matched pair so the column reads at a glance. Changed the normal-script badge from `▶` to **`S`** (Script) in [ScriptListPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/ScriptListPanel.tsx) — **S** = Script (anywhere in Lich's `scripts/` tree), **C** = Custom (`scripts/custom/` folder), mirroring Lich's own folder layout. The `custom` flag is already derived from the `scripts/custom/` listing ([useLichBridge.ts](src/renderer/hooks/useLichBridge.ts)), so the S/C split matches the folder structure exactly. Added a `title` tooltip per badge; `.sl-badge--core` already shared the bordered-box style with `.sl-badge--custom`, so the two now render as a consistent pair. The `▶` on the pause/resume control is unrelated (Resume action icon) and unchanged. |
| B144 | No visual separation between the "⋯ More" button and the Disconnect button in the top app-bar — easy to mis-click Disconnect when reaching for More | v0.10.1 (Binu) | Binu's suggestion (Sekmeht agreed: avoids accidental disconnects). Added a 1px themed separator `.app-bar-divider` ([app-bar.css](src/renderer/styles/app-bar.css) + [AppBar.tsx](src/renderer/components/AppBar.tsx)) between the ⋯ More button and Disconnect/Login, with `margin: 2px 3px` extra breathing room. `align-self: stretch` to span the row height; `var(--border)` so it follows the theme (light-theme safe, no hardcoded hex). Renders for both the Disconnect (connected) and Login (disconnected) states since it sits before the conditional. |
| B143 | Fires tab (Debug panel) — a long matched pattern overflows the row and pushes the → "GOTO" button off the right edge, where it can't be clicked | v0.10.1 (Sekmeht) | CSS-grid items default to `min-width: auto` (their content's intrinsic size), so the `1fr` matched-text column refused to shrink below the pattern's width and the row exceeded the panel, shoving the trailing 28px GOTO column off-screen. Fix in [debug.css](src/renderer/styles/debug.css): the flexible columns now use `minmax(0, …)` and the name/matched/detail cells get `min-width: 0` + `overflow-wrap: anywhere` so long content WRAPS within its column instead of overflowing; detail capped at `minmax(0,180px)` so it can't crowd matched; `align-items: start` for tidy multi-line rows; header + row grid templates kept identical so labels stay aligned. (User confirmed wrapping is preferred over horizontal overflow.) Shipped alongside a Debug-panel **✕ close button** (developer request — DebugPanel `onClose` → `setShowDebug(false)`, same teardown as the Debug toolbar toggle). |
| B142 | Settings → Font Size doesn't resize several game-area surfaces — icon bar (hands/spell/stance), the Mode button, the vitals bar (regular + compact), and the built-in **Lich Scripts** stream panel | v0.10.0 (self-found in testing) | Those surfaces used `rem` font-sizes — anchored to the 16px html root, so they ignore `--game-font-size` (the per-character Settings font). The **Mode button** specifically *regressed* in v0.10.0: the app-bar (2c) moved `<ModeSwitcher>` out of `.game-toolbar` (which sets `font-size: var(--game-font-size)`) into the unanchored `.icon-bar`. The vitals/icon-bar/Lich-Scripts cases were pre-existing (rem at v0.9.1 too). **Fix:** anchor each container to `var(--game-font-size)` (panels → `var(--panel-font-size, var(--game-font-size))`) and convert text + gating dimensions to `em` (×16/12 to preserve the default look). Caught the gotcha that `em` on width/height is relative to the element's OWN font-size — `.status-bar` width `6em` → `7.14em`. Exp/stream panels already scaled (their roots anchor the var). **Deliberately NOT fixed:** the Lich **Dashboard modal** (`.ld-*`) stays fixed `rem` (modals are the chrome-sizing exception, Sekmeht's call); and per-panel `A−/A+` overrides (F31) still pin a panel over the global font with no reset-to-global yet (by design — override wins). CLAUDE.md pitfall #58 + Principle #9. |
| B141 | Rest-xp Stored/Usable bar value text looks blurred / washed-out on light themes (Classic Light) | v0.10.0 (TheTargonian) | Found while stress-testing Classic Light during the top-chrome redesign. `.exp-rxp-bar-text` ([panels.css](src/renderer/styles/panels.css)) used `color: var(--text)` — an **undefined** variable (the codebase defines `--text-primary` etc., never bare `--text`), so it fell through to inherited dark body text, then wrapped that dark text in a triple `text-shadow` halo of `var(--exp-bar-bg)` which is near-white (`#f0f0f0`) on Classic Light → heavy white glow around dark text over the saturated accent/success fills = the "blur." Fix: the proven vitals-bar pattern — `color: #fff` + a dark `rgba(0,0,0,…)` halo, crisp over the saturated fills on every theme; the `--empty` variant (text over the bare track, no fill) drops the halo so it doesn't over-darken on light themes. |
| B140 | Panel-tab hover renders near-black on light themes (Classic Light) — hovering a stream tab turned its background dark | v0.10.0 (TheTargonian) | `.panel-tab:hover` and `.panel-tab-add:hover` ([panel-frame.css](src/renderer/styles/panel-frame.css)) hardcoded `background: #161616` instead of a theme var, so on light themes a hovered tab went black. Fix: `var(--bg-hover)` (the canonical hover-surface var — `#222` on dark, `#e8e8e8` on Classic Light). Same class as B108/B112/pitfall #34 (hardcoded dark hex breaking light themes). Surfaced via the multi-character/Classic-Light test that also surfaced B141; not a multi-session bug — reproduced with a single character. |
| B112 | Theme Editor — setting "Game Text" to black on a light theme doesn't catch all the "normal" text; some text stays greyish | v0.8.4 (Rakkor) | Reported by Rakkor (TheTargonian) while building a light theme — he wanted all body text black but `Game Text` alone didn't cover it, and `Dim Text` didn't either. **Two-part fix (verified at the code level in v0.9.1's review).** **(a) Cascade by default** — 8 panel-specific body-text vars in `darkBase` ([themes.ts:174-196](src/renderer/themes.ts#L174-L196)) now reference the general `--text-*` scale via `var(--text-…)` instead of literal hex: `--hand-label-color`/`--room-section-color`/`--exp-mindstate-color`/`--exp-rate-color` → `var(--text-dim)`; `--hand-empty-color`/`--spell-empty-color`/`--room-content-color` → `var(--text-muted)`; `--exp-skill-color` → `var(--text-secondary)`. So setting "Game text" / "Labels" / "Muted text" / "Dim text" in the Theme Editor cascades through every structured panel body automatically. Built-in themes (Parchment, Mist, etc.) that override these specialty vars still win via the `applyTheme` merge (the literal-hex values further down themes.ts are those per-theme overrides, not a regression). Held / active accents (`--hand-held-color`, `--spell-active-color`) deliberately kept as literal accent colors — they ARE the visual cue that a hand is full / a spell is prepared, not body text. **(b) Discoverability** — every affected row in [ThemeEditor.tsx](src/renderer/components/ThemeEditor.tsx) carries a `desc` rendered as a `title=` tooltip + a small `ⓘ` glyph; cascaded vars note "Falls back to `--text-*` if unset" so a tester can decide whether to override per-panel or ride the cascade. **One checked gap, covered:** `--room-title-color` (#e8e8e8) and `--room-desc-color` (#b8b8b8) stayed literal hex (they're a heading + prose, distinct from the cascading body vars) — on a custom light theme those would be near-white-on-white, but they aren't hidden: both are explicit, swatch-editable, tooltip-documented rows in the Room & Exp → Room Text group ([ThemeEditor.tsx:152-153](src/renderer/components/ThemeEditor.tsx#L152-L153)). So there's no remaining class of invisible / uncontrollable body text to chase. Complete by design; the only thing it had been waiting on was empirical sign-off, with no code-level gap remaining. |
| B139 | Lichborne→Lichborne (F29) import preview always listed imported triggers with no commands — the trigger preview rows showed an empty command set even when the trigger had command actions | v0.9.0 (self-found) | Surfaced not by a tester but by running `tsc --noEmit` during the v0.9.0 GTK-warning work — a long-standing type error that had been shipping silently because esbuild (main) and vite (renderer) both transpile without full type-checking. At [lichborne.ts:110](src/renderer/import/parsers/lichborne.ts#L110) the `ImportTrigger.commands` preview field was populated from `t.commands ?? []`, but `t` is a `Partial<TriggerRule>` and `TriggerRule` has no top-level `commands` field — its commands live in `actions[]` as command-type `TriggerAction`s. So `t.commands` was always `undefined` → `[]`, and the import wizard's preview showed every imported Lichborne trigger as having no commands. **Cosmetic only — not data loss**: the `ImportTrigger` intermediate is preview-only; the actual F29 import applies through `nativeRules` (the full native rule shape, pitfall #42), which carries `actions` correctly, so imported triggers always *worked* — only the preview list was wrong. Fix: derive `commands` from the command-type actions — `(t.actions ?? []).filter(a => a.type === 'command').map(a => a.command ?? '').filter(c => c.length > 0)`. The project is now fully `tsc`-clean (0 errors). General note: esbuild/vite don't gate the build on types, so running `tsc --noEmit` at version boundaries is worthwhile to catch this class of latent type error. |
| B138 | `;vars setup` opens its variable-editor window correctly, but interacting with it (clicking the "new var name" textbox to add a variable) disconnects Lich. Tester confirmed it works in Genie but disconnects in Lichborne. | v0.9.0 (Sekmeht) | **Root cause is a Lich-script GTK thread-safety bug, not a Lichborne bug.** Read the live `vars.lic` (`C:\Ruby4Lich5\Lich5\scripts\vars.lic` — it's a core Lich script, not a dr-script). The window paints fine; the crash is at [`vars.lic:136-141`](C:\Ruby4Lich5\Lich5\scripts\vars.lic#L136-L141), where the new-var textbox's `focus-in-event` handler spawns `Thread.new { ... sw.vadjustment.value = ... }` — mutating a GTK widget (the scrolled-window adjustment) from a non-main thread. That directly violates the warning at [`gtk.rb:4`](C:\temp\lich-dev\lich-5\lib\common\gtk.rb#L4) ("Calling Gtk API in a thread other than the main thread may cause random segfaults"). Under Lichborne's `ruby lich.rbw --stormfront --dragonrealms` launch the race segfaults Lich reliably; under Genie's launch the GTK runtime timing apparently avoids it. When Lich's process exits, [LichConnection](src/main/connection/LichConnection.ts) sees the socket close and emits `disconnect`. **Can't be fixed from Lichborne** without forking Lich's `vars.lic` (out of scope — not a Lich-replacement). **Resolution: built an in-app replacement** — the Lich Dashboard → Variables tab is now a full editor (add / inline-edit / delete) that does everything `;vars setup` does without the GTK window (see v0.9.0 release notes). Until/unless a tester needs it, the script's non-GTK subcommands (`;vars set NAME=VALUE`, `;vars delete NAME`, `;vars list`) still work fine. **Side correction**: this investigation disproved the older CLAUDE.md claim that `--stormfront` "skips the GTK main loop" — [`lich.rbw:135`](C:\temp\lich-dev\lich-5\lich.rbw#L135) runs `Gtk.main` unconditionally when GTK is defined. The loop runs; the failure is the off-thread widget mutation, not a missing pump. CLAUDE.md pitfall #52 (the `;vars setup` analysis + diagnostic protocol for future GTK-disconnect reports) and pitfall #53 (the editor's write-through-Lich pattern) document the outcome. |
| B137 | Macros always auto-send — no way to make a macro deposit partial text into the command bar and wait for the user to finish typing. Wrayth/Genie/Stormfront support this via the `@` cursor-position marker (and Wrayth via no-trailing-`\r`); Lichborne ignored both, so any imported macro using these conventions auto-fired with incomplete text and DR rejected it (Jaded's `<k action="&apos;}"/>` macro tried to send `'}` literally and got "To whom are you speaking?" every time). | v0.8.10 (Jaded → Sekmeht) | Jaded's testing surfaced this — `'}` is valid DR speech-target syntax (per Elanthipedia, `}<person>` and `@<person>` are interchangeable target operators in DR's `SAY` parser), so her macro was correctly designed to type `'}` into the box and wait for her to add ` sekmeht hey!`. Lichborne auto-sent it. Verified the convention model by reading Genie's [`FormMain.cs:1140-1175`](C:\temp\Genie4-Dev\Genie4\Forms\FormMain.cs#L1140) (`ParseInputBox`) and [`FormMain.cs:4079`](C:\temp\Genie4-Dev\Genie4\Forms\FormMain.cs#L4079) (`ClassCommand_SendText`): `@` anywhere in a macro command is the cursor-position marker; presence of `@` or `\x` switches the macro to "type into input box, no auto-send" mode; `\@` escapes to a literal `@`. Wrayth uses the same `@` convention plus `\r` as the explicit "send / Enter" marker — absence of `\r` is wait-mode. **Fix in four pieces**: (a) [`parseCursorMarker(command)`](src/renderer/macros.ts) in macros.ts — walks the string, detects `\@` escapes (preserved as literal `@` in output), finds the first unescaped `@` for cursor position, strips all `@` chars; returns `{ text, cursorPos }` or `null`. (b) [Macro fire path](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) — for each command in a fired macro, check cursor marker first; if found, flush pending send-commands, `setCommand(stripped text)`, wait one rAF for React to commit, then `inputRef.focus()` + `setSelectionRange(cursorPos, cursorPos)`. Stop iterating remaining commands. (c) [Wrayth parser](src/renderer/import/parsers/wrayth.ts) `parseWraythAction` — track whether raw action ends with `\r`; if not AND the last command doesn't already contain an unescaped `@`, append `@` so Lichborne's runtime triggers type-and-wait. Jaded's `&apos;}` decodes to `'}`, no `\r`, no `@` already → imports as `'}@` → on macro fire, types `'}` into bar with cursor at end. (d) [Macro editor tooltip](src/renderer/components/MacrosPanel.tsx) — hovering the command input explains the `@` convention so users can craft type-and-wait macros manually. **Genie imports** get the same treatment via a follow-on extension: (e) [`parseArgs`](src/renderer/import/parsers/genie.ts) rewrite — walks character-by-character to handle Genie's Level-1 escape sequences inside `{...}` arguments (`\\` → `\`, `\}` → `}`, `\{` → `{`). Pre-v0.8.10 the parser used `line.indexOf('}')` and terminated at the first `}` even when escaped, so any Genie macro using `\}` (literal close-brace inside an argument — common in compound macros like `{\\x;'\}sekmeht @}`) was parsed wrong and the rest of the action got dropped. (f) [`splitAction`](src/renderer/import/parsers/genie.ts) preserves `@` cursor markers through `;`-splitting and strips Genie's `\x` "clear input" command. **`\x` is purely import-time noise — Lichborne always replaces command-bar contents when typing into it, so the explicit clear is redundant.** Deliberate simplification from Genie's two-mode behavior (per Sekmeht 2026-06-01): only an explicit unescaped `@` triggers wait-mode, NOT `\x` alone. So `\\x;hide` imports as `hide` (auto-sends), and `\\x;whisper group @` imports as `whisper group @` (waits because of `@`, not because of `\x`). Users who want "insert at cursor without clearing first" can edit their imported macros after import — by default Lichborne's behavior is "clear and type" when a macro has `@`. So `\\x;'\}sekmeht @` (Jaded's exact Genie example shape) imports correctly: parseArgs resolves to `\x;'}sekmeht @`; splitAction strips the `\x` part and keeps `'}sekmeht @`; runtime parseCursorMarker types `'}sekmeht ` into the bar with cursor at end. **Backward compat for manually-created Lichborne macros**: any tester with a hand-created macro that has a literal `@` (e.g. for DR's `SAY @person message` syntax — though unusual since users typically type that directly rather than macro it) would now have the `@` interpreted as a cursor marker. The recovery path: edit the macro and change `@` to `\@`. Documented under "Upgrading note" in v0.8.10 release notes. CLAUDE.md pitfall #51 documents the cursor-marker model + the Wrayth `\r` translation. |
| B136 | Speech double-echoes in the main scroll when no Conversation panel is open — "You say, Hello." appears twice in main per `say`; opening the Conversation panel makes it correct (once in panel, once in main) | v0.8.10 (Sekmeht / Jaded testing) | Found by Sekmeht during B135 (rename) testing. Root cause: STREAM_FALLBACK in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) had `conversation: 'main'`, but DR's protocol already natively duplicates speech to main — every `<pushStream id="talk"/>"You say, Hi."<popStream/>` is followed by a second `"You say, Hi."` OUTSIDE the stream block that routes to main directly. With no Conversation panel watching, the inside-pushStream copy ALSO fell back to main via STREAM_FALLBACK, producing two copies in the main scroll. Verified by Sekmeht's raw XML capture and the screenshot pattern. Fix: removed the `conversation: 'main'` entry from STREAM_FALLBACK. With no panel watching, speech naturally appears once in main (DR's native duplicate); the inside-pushStream copy is dropped. With a Conversation panel open, speech appears once in conversation + once in main (each via its own DR-emit path) — correct. Other streams (thoughts/arrivals/deaths) keep their fallbacks because DR doesn't natively duplicate those — speech (and whispers, same XML pattern) is the only stream class that DR sends twice. CLAUDE.md pitfall #49 documents the STREAM_FALLBACK vs DR-native-duplicate interaction so a future stream addition doesn't reintroduce double-counting. |
| B135 | Lichborne used the plural `conversations` as its panel id while every other Stormfront-family client (Genie, Frostbite, Wrayth) uses the singular `conversation` — causing duplicate "Conversation" rows in Available Streams whenever Lich (or any script) emitted `<streamWindow id='conversation'/>` (the universal convention), since Lichborne treated it as a separate, unrelated stream | v0.8.10 (Sekmeht) | Discovered while investigating Jaded's ghost-stream report. Verified `conversation` (singular) is the convention everywhere: Genie's [`FormMain.cs:2786`](C:\temp\Genie4-Dev\Genie4\Forms\FormMain.cs#L2786) (`SafeCreateOutputForm("conversation", "Conversation", "log", ...)`), Frostbite's [`WindowFacade staticWindows`](C:\temp\Frostbite-Dev\frostbite\gui\windowfacade.cpp#L42) (`"conversation" << "whispers" << "talk"`), and Stormfront/Wrayth XML conventions. Lichborne was the outlier with the plural. Fix shipped as a coordinated rename across ~10 source files + a localStorage migration: **(a) Source**: `PanelType` `'conversations'` → `'conversation'`; `PANEL_LABELS.conversation = 'Conversation'`; `ALL_PANEL_TYPES` entry renamed; PanelFrame switch case renamed; default `topTabs` / `topActiveId` / `resetLayout` defaults all updated; `STREAM_FALLBACK` key renamed (later removed entirely in B136); trigger watchStream dropdown option renamed; SessionLogModal "Social" keep-list updated; NEVER_DISCOVER expanded with `'conversations'` (legacy plural alias) and `'whispers'` so neither surfaces as discoverable. **(b) Shared aliases module**: new [`src/shared/streamAliases.ts`](src/shared/streamAliases.ts) with `STREAM_ID_ALIASES` and `normalizeStreamId(id)` — used by parser (replaces inline `STREAM_MAP`), renderer's `echoToStream` (so imported triggers with `#echo >talk` etc. land in the right panel), Genie `parseEchoAction` (so import-time `>stream` tokens get normalized), and F29 `lichborne.ts` parser (so legacy `'conversations'` watchStream / echoStream values on imported triggers get rewritten). Aliases include: `talk` / `conversations` (legacy plural) / `whispers` all → `conversation`; `death` → `deaths`; `logons` → `arrivals`; `percWindow` / `percwindow` → `spells`; `experience` → `exp`; `inventory` → `inv`; `thought` → `thoughts`. **(c) localStorage migration** in new [`src/renderer/localStorageMigrations.ts`](src/renderer/localStorageMigrations.ts) called from `main.tsx` before React renders. Renames `'conversations'` → `'conversation'` in saved per-character tabs (id/type/label fields — case-insensitive matching catches `'Conversations'`, `'CONVERSATIONS'` custom-tab variants), active-id strings, `settings.panelFontSizes` keys, and trigger `watchStream` values. **Dedupe pass after rename**: if a user had both a builtin plural tab AND a custom plural-variant tab, both collapse to `'conversation'` and the second is dropped (no duplicate panel). Migration flag bumped to `conversationRename2` so users who installed an earlier draft of v0.8.10 with the buggy migration get the improved version on next launch. **Flag bumped again to `conversationRename3` after the v0.8.10 internal retroactive sweep found the migration only renamed the top-level `watchStream` field — missing `actions[].echoStream` on trigger echo actions. A pre-v0.8.10 user who created a trigger that echoed to the (then-plural) "Conversations" panel would have `echoStream: 'conversations'` persisted in their saved triggers; the runtime alias normalization at [GameWindow.tsx:543](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx#L543) kept echo routing functional, but the trigger editor's stream dropdown would show the legacy value outside its option set (which now lists only `conversation`). The conversationRename3 migration extends the trigger handler to walk `actions[].echoStream` in addition to `watchStream`, so the legacy value gets rewritten and the editor dropdown matches the stored value. Same case-insensitive matching as the watchStream rename.** **(d) Whispers**: Genie + Frostbite both route whispers into the same combined Conversation feed (Genie's `SafeCreateOutputForm("whispers", "Whispers", "conversation", ...)`, Frostbite's `if(id == "whispers") emit updateConversationsWindow(...)`). `whispers: 'conversation'` in STREAM_ID_ALIASES matches that convention — defensive against any script ever pushing to `whispers` (DR rarely does directly in modern play; whispers usually arrive on `talk`). CLAUDE.md pitfall #50 documents the shared streamAliases module and when to add new aliases. |
| B134 | Lich-emitted `<streamWindow id='conversation'/>` declaration (declared but never pushed to) appeared as a ghost "Conversation" panel in Available Streams — affecting every user since Lich's startup includes a Stormfront-compatibility window declaration | v0.8.10 (Sekmeht + Jaded) | Initial defensive fix shipped as `conversation: 'conversations'` in STREAM_MAP — routed the singular declaration to the existing plural panel id so the discovery filter would hide it as a builtin. **Subsumed by B135**: the full rename made this defensive entry unnecessary since `conversation` IS the canonical id now. The "ghost panel" symptom is resolved by B135 making `conversation` the actual builtin panel type. B134's entry is kept in the change history because it was the first step in the investigation that led to the broader rename. |
| B133 | Genie command coverage was incomplete and unrecognized `#commands` silently dropped without a warning, so user triggers imported with empty actions thinking they worked | v0.8.9 (Sekmeht) | Sekmeht-driven cleanup after Jaded's B130 case. Enumerated the full Genie `#command` list from the [Genie4 source](C:\temp\Genie4-Dev\Genie4\Core\Command.cs#L270) (`switchExpr = oArgs[0].Substring(1).ToLower()` then ~100 case branches). Pre-v0.8.9 we handled 7 commands and had 7 in the explicit dropped-with-note list; the other ~85 fell through to "silently skipped" — Jaded-style "trigger imported, fires nothing" with no user-facing signal. Two-part fix in [genie.ts:305-410](src/renderer/import/parsers/genie.ts#L305-L410): **(a)** new aliases recognized — `#q`/`#que`/`#queue` for game commands; `#variable`/`#setvar`/`#setvariable`/`#tvar`/`#tempvar`/`#tempvariable`/`#svar` for variable actions (Lichborne doesn't distinguish var scope at the action layer — temp/saved/string/server vars all collapse to the same `variable` action); `#playsound`/`#playwave` for sound actions; `#bell` for beep. **(b)** `##escape` correctly handled — `##5` strips the leading `#` and sends `#5` to the game (Genie's escape syntax for literal `#` text). **(c)** silent-drop hole closed — every unrecognized `#command` lands in the `dropped` array which surfaces in the import preview as "Unsupported actions skipped: #X, #Y" (using the existing partial-status note mechanism). `#nop` and `#comment` are explicit no-ops, silently dropped without "Unsupported" warning since the user never intended them to do anything. CLAUDE.md pitfall #48 documents the Genie command landscape — what maps, what gets dropped with a note (rule manipulation, UI control, connection control, scripting flow, math/random/eval, mapping/movement), and what's silently dropped as no-op. |
| B132 | Lich Map indicator stuck / off-camera on inactive character tabs after the character travels in the background | v0.8.9 (Sekmeht) | Spotted by Sekmeht while testing multi-character — GenieMap was working better but the Lich Map on an inactive tabbed character was stuck in a different location/zone. Root cause: direct port of B88 (v0.7.0 Genie Maps inactive-tab fix) was never applied to MapImageView. The auto-centering effects at [MapImageView.tsx:135-146](src/renderer/components/panels/MapImageView.tsx#L135-L146) used `svg.clientWidth / 2 - cx * scale` for the transform. When the tab is inactive (display:none per [CLAUDE.md pitfall #24](CLAUDE.md)), `clientWidth/clientHeight` return 0; the math becomes `x = 0/2 - cx*scale = -cx*scale` — strands the camera transform at negative coordinates. When the user tabbed back, the room state was correct but the camera was off-screen. Fix: same shape as the proven GenieMapView fix — added `if (!svg.clientWidth || !svg.clientHeight) return` guards to all three centering writes (image-onload effect, room-change effect, `recenter` callback) so inactive-tab updates don't write garbage; new ResizeObserver on the SVG (via `useEffect([])` with `recenterRef` for closure-staleness) catches the 0→non-zero transition when the tab is shown again and calls `recenter()` so the camera snaps to the player's actual room. Per pitfall #24, the underlying room-state updates ALREADY work for inactive tabs (no isActive gate on event processing); only the camera positioning needed visibility-aware handling. |
| B131 | Lichborne→Lichborne (F29) imports offered Replace mode, which on a Lichborne self-import is "merge my setup from another character but wipe my existing rules per category the import has data for" — never the intended workflow | v0.8.9 (Jaded → Sekmeht) | Jaded's testing produced multiple replace-induced wipes; Sekmeht-driven follow-up to identify Lichborne self-imports as a special case where Replace mode shouldn't be available. Fix in [ImportWizard.tsx](src/renderer/components/ImportWizard.tsx)'s merge UI: the "Replace all" radio is conditionally rendered `source !== 'lichborne'`; switching source TO Lichborne (`onClick` for the source card) auto-resets merge to `'append'` so a Replace state from a prior legacy-client wizard session doesn't carry over. Append's description for Lichborne sources is also rewritten to be explicit: "duplicates (same pattern + scope + case for highlights/triggers, same key for macros, same input for aliases) are skipped automatically" — so users know what dedup means. Replace's description for legacy clients also rewritten to reflect B127's per-type behavior: "For each category the import has data for, delete your existing rules of that type and replace with the import. Categories the import doesn't touch are left alone" — so Wrayth-Replace no longer reads as "delete everything." Users who genuinely want to start fresh on Lichborne can delete their rules manually first, then import via Append. |
| B130 | Genie triggers with `#send #flash` / `#put #X` (someone wrapped a Genie internal in `#send`/`#put` thinking they needed to "execute" it) imported with `#flash` as a literal DR command — DR rejected with "Please rephrase that command" on every trigger fire, invisible spam | v0.8.9 (Jaded) | Jaded confirmed her actual case was `#send #flash`. Pre-v0.8.9 the parser stripped `#send ` and pushed the inner `#flash` as a literal DR command at [genie.ts:316](src/renderer/import/parsers/genie.ts#L316). Fix: when the inner content of `#send`/`#put`/`#q`/`#que`/`#queue` itself starts with `#`, recursively process it via `parseActionParts` instead of treating as a DR command. So `#send #flash` → flash action correctly; `#send #beep` → beep; etc. The recursive call collects all of the inner part's contributions (commands, echoActions, varActions, logActions, soundFiles, hasFlash/Beep/LibrarySound, dropped) so any combination works. Companion fix B133 covers the silent-drop hole that meant other Genie internals went unflagged. **Existing broken data**: same caveat as B129 — triggers imported before v0.8.9 with `#send #flash` (or similar) already have `command: '#flash'` saved as their action. Recovery path is re-importing the source Genie `triggers.cfg` (or manually editing the trigger to delete the broken action and use the Lichborne Flash action type instead). v0.8.9 release notes call this out under the "Upgrading note." |
| B129 | Wrayth macro XML entity decode missing — Jaded's speech macro `'}` was stored in Wrayth's settings.xml as `&apos;}` and imported literally as the 4-character string, causing DR to respond "Please rephrase that command" every time the macro fired | v0.8.9 (Jaded) | Jaded couldn't speak after importing Wrayth macros — every speech attempt got "Please rephrase that command" because the macro was sending `&apos;}` (the XML-escaped form) instead of `'}` (the actual speech command). Root cause in [wrayth.ts:8-12](src/renderer/import/parsers/wrayth.ts#L8-L12): `getAttr` extracted the raw regex match without decoding XML entities. Wrayth's attribute values use `'...'` quotes, so any `'` character inside the action MUST be escaped to `&apos;` in the XML — and our parser returned that literal entity to the runtime. Fix: new `decodeXmlEntities` helper that decodes the five standard XML 1.0 entities (`&apos;` `&quot;` `&amp;` `&lt;` `&gt;`); `getAttr` runs every attribute value through it before returning. **`&amp;` is decoded LAST** so we don't double-decode entities introduced by earlier replacements (e.g. `&amp;apos;` → first replacement makes `&apos;` → second would wrongly become `'` instead of staying as `&apos;`). Numeric entities like `&#39;` aren't currently used by Wrayth's exporter but could be added if a tester hits one. **Existing broken data**: this fix only applies to FRESH imports — macros imported before v0.8.9 already have the literal `&apos;` (etc.) saved in their action text. Lichborne deliberately does not auto-modify previously-saved user data; the recovery path is "re-import the source Wrayth `settings.xml`" (or manually replace the entity text in the macro editor). v0.8.9 release notes call this out under the "Upgrading note." |
| B128 | Imported triggers default to `watchStream: 'any'` — speech triggers double-fired because DR routes "Bob says X" into both `main` and `conversations` streams | v0.8.9 (Jaded) | Jaded's double-echo on every speech line. Root cause: [mapper.ts:121](src/renderer/import/mapper.ts#L121) hardcoded `watchStream: 'any'` for every imported trigger. Imported triggers from legacy clients (Wrayth/Genie/Frostbite) all originally watched the main game text stream, so `'main'` matches the source semantics; the `'any'` default was over-permissive and produced the double-fire. Fix: changed default to `'main'` in mapper.ts (imports) AND in [triggers.ts:151](src/renderer/triggers.ts#L151) (manually-created triggers via "+ New Trigger" in the UI) so the fix applies uniformly. Users who want a stream-specific trigger can still change `watchStream` after import / creation; the default just no longer silently double-fires on speech. |
| B127 | Imports could wipe categories they didn't touch — Genie all-categories import followed by Wrayth Replace (which has zero triggers / zero aliases) wiped the user's existing Genie triggers and aliases because `saveX(character, [])` ran with empty arrays | v0.8.9 (Jaded) | The catastrophic bug Jaded's session uncovered. Root cause in [ImportWizard.tsx](src/renderer/components/ImportWizard.tsx)'s `doImport`: both Append and Replace branches called `saveX(...)` for ALL four rule types regardless of whether the current import had data for them. Replace path: `saveHighlights(character, mapped.highlights)` ran even if `mapped.highlights = []` (Wrayth Replace with zero highlights wipes existing). Append path: `[...existing, ...keepX]` is mathematically a no-op when `keepX = []`, but explicit `length > 0` gating removes any chance of a defensive bug in save/load round-tripping causing a wipe. Fix: **per-type save guards everywhere** — every `saveHighlights` / `saveMacros` / `saveAliases` / `saveTriggers` call is now gated on `mapped[type].length > 0`. Same guard added to the F29 native-data blocks (`nativeGroups`, `nativeModes`, `nativeContacts`, `nativeContactTemplates`) — `result.nativeX && Array.isArray(...)` passes for empty arrays too (`[]` is truthy and passes `Array.isArray`), so a Lichborne F29 export from a character with no groups would have wiped the recipient's groups on Replace. Now: every import only touches categories where it has data. Wrayth never touches triggers/aliases (its parser returns `[]` for them); Frostbite never touches triggers/aliases/contacts; Genie touches everything but only with selected data; Lichborne F29 touches whatever's in the export. Every cross-source combination is safe. The Append branch's existing dedup logic (`hlContentKey`/`trContentKey`/key/input) still runs for non-empty categories. CLAUDE.md pitfall #47 documents the "save guards on multi-category sources" principle so a future rule type doesn't reintroduce the wipe. |
| B126 | Lich Map sometimes "hangs" tracking the prior room until LOOK forces a refresh — Rakkor in room 14011, map indicator stuck on 7301 (Foothills, Oak Tree), Room panel showing the prior room's data; manual LOOK snaps everything to the right room | v0.8.8 (Rakkor) | Reported by Rakkor (TheTargonian) — intermittent, "doesn't happen all the time." Same shape as B110 (Genie Map stale-tracking) but on the Lich Map side this time. Root cause: when the player transitions to a new room, DR usually emits BOTH `<nav rm='X'/>` AND a fresh `<streamWindow id='main' subtitle='[Title - rmId]'/>`. Our parser handled `<streamWindow>` (extracts title + roomId from the subtitle bracket) but the `<nav>` handler at [StormFrontParser.ts:523-528](src/main/parser/StormFrontParser.ts#L523-L528) only emitted clear-streams — it **silently dropped `attrs.rm`**, the new room id DR carries on every nav tag. For most transitions this didn't matter because `<streamWindow>` arrived right after and updated roomId via the streamwindow handler. But for some transition shapes (teleports, NPC-induced moves, certain scripted moves) DR is silent on `<streamWindow>` while still sending `<nav rm>` — for those, the front-end never got the new roomId and the map stayed stuck. **Lich's `$room` variable was correct the whole time** because Lich's own scripting reads `attrs.rm` from `<nav>`; that's how Rakkor knew he was in 14011 even though the map showed 7301. **Fix**: added a new `RoomIdEvent` type in [shared/types.ts](src/shared/types.ts) (`{ type: 'room-id', roomId: number }`); the parser's `<nav>` case now parses `attrs.rm` (when present and numeric) and emits the event; [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)'s event handler routes `room-id` into `roomUpdates.roomId` only (leaves title / desc / sub-streams alone — we don't have fresh values for those, and forging an empty title would wipe the only data we have). The Lich Map's `useEffect` at [MapPanel.tsx:160](src/renderer/components/panels/MapPanel.tsx#L160) tries `lichDb.get(roomId)` BEFORE the title-based `findRoom` fallback, so a fresh roomId is sufficient for the indicator to track correctly. **Scope of the fix**: Lich Map tracking. **NOT fixed by this**: (a) Room panel title (still shows stale title until a real `<streamWindow>` arrives — only `<streamWindow>` carries the title string), (b) Genie Map (its `currentLocation` memo at [GenieMapView.tsx:467](src/renderer/components/panels/GenieMapView.tsx#L467) is title-based, not roomId-based; Genie zones use room titles not Lich's numeric ids). The B110 `staleZoneCountRef` escape valve still handles Genie Map's similar symptom via a different mechanism. **Caveat**: this fix only helps the case where DR sends `<nav rm>` but not `<streamWindow>`. If DR is silent on BOTH for some transition shape, neither this fix nor B110's escape valve help — only LOOK does. CLAUDE.md pitfall #46 documents the `<nav rm>` extraction pattern. |
| F36 | Apply the same highlight styling in the Room panel's structured sections (Objects / Players / Creatures / Extra) that already paints in the main scroll's "You also see ..." / "Also here: ..." lines | v0.8.8 (Rakkor) | Requested by Rakkor (TheTargonian) — his treasure highlights (e.g. blue color for various item names) painted the main scroll's `look` output but the Room panel's Objects section showed the same items plain. Initial diagnosis chased a parser-side hypothesis (maybe DR's `<a noun=...>` styling was being dropped in `captureSegments`), but the actual root cause was that [RoomPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/RoomPanel.tsx) deliberately destructured only `matchRules` from `useHighlights()` and ignored `lineRules` — a B111 design call dating to v0.8.4 with the rationale "a 'lobster' lineRule shouldn't paint the entire Objects section red just because one item matches." That rationale turned out to be over-cautious: in the main scroll the same content (the "You also see ..." line) gets line-mode treatment when the user authored a line-mode rule, so showing the same content un-painted in the Room panel was the inconsistency, not painting it. Fix in [RoomPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/RoomPanel.tsx): destructure both `matchRules` and `lineRules`; in `renderSegments`, compute `getLineHighlightStyle(segments, lineRules)` against each section's joined text and return both the rendered nodes AND the line style (as `{ nodes, style }`); each section's container div applies its own per-section style so a player-matching rule paints only the Players section, not the others. `desc` deliberately stays out of line-style scope — multi-sentence prose where a single match would over-paint a large block. Same architectural pattern as B117 (creature monsterbold) — Room panel mirrors main-scroll styling for the same content. CLAUDE.md pitfall #44 documents the "RoomPanel mirrors main scroll" principle so future styling additions don't have to re-litigate. |
| B122 follow-up | v0.8.7's threshold-lowering fix didn't actually solve the half-row prompt clip at font 13+ — Rakkor confirmed it still clipped, and his diagnostic "I can manually scroll down ~half a row to reveal the prompt" proved both raw DOM scroll math AND Virtuoso's `scrollToIndex({ align: 'end' })` API converged on the same wrong target (CSS layer issue, not scroll math) | v0.8.8 (Rakkor) | Three failed hypotheses across v0.8.7 and v0.8.8 before landing on the right fix shape: (a) v0.8.7 lowered the `totalListHeightChanged` re-pin threshold 2→0.5px on the theory of "1-2px fractional shortfall" — didn't help; (b) v0.8.8 Option A replaced raw `el.scrollTop = el.scrollHeight - el.clientHeight` with `virtuosoRef.current?.scrollToIndex({ index: 'LAST', align: 'end' })` on the theory of "Virtuoso has internal layout accounting differing from raw DOM math" — also didn't help, Rakkor's screenshots showed identical clip. (c) The actual fix: **Option B (defensive layout slack)** — added a `components.Footer` to Virtuoso rendering an invisible `<div style={{ height: 14 }} />`, gated on `settings.fontSize >= 13` (no slack at font 12 where row heights land on integer pixels and no clip occurs, avoiding the visible gap Sekmeht flagged). Constant 14px (was originally `1em` = scales with font, but Sekmeht reported "each font step adds 1px to the gap" → swapped to fixed 14px for visual consistency across fonts 13-19; 14px covers ~half-row clip at those sizes). **Pair fix in same release**: a new `useEffect([settings.fontSize, settings.lineHeight, settings.largePrint])` re-snaps via `scrollToIndex({ align: 'end' })` if the user was already pinned to the bottom — font / line-height / large-print changes reshape row heights, growing scrollHeight, leaving the old scrollTop short of the new bottom and the last line clipping into the footer slack. Bails on `!pinnedRef.current` so scrolled-up readers stay where they are. rAF gives Virtuoso a frame to re-measure rows at the new dimensions before the snap. CLAUDE.md pitfall #43 documents the general principle: when manual scroll reveals content past where two independent scroll APIs both think the bottom is, the bug is layout, not math — add slack instead of chasing scroll-math hypotheses. The original B122 entry below (v0.8.7) is preserved for history; this row supersedes its fix attempt. |
| (UX) | Mode toolbar button + popover items didn't scale with the user's Settings → Font Size — Mode button stayed at a fixed ~12px while every other toolbar button grew with the user's preference | v0.8.8 (Sekmeht) | Sekmeht-spotted while reviewing the toolbar visual consistency. Root cause: `.btn-mode-switcher` in [mode-switcher.css](src/renderer/styles/mode-switcher.css) used `font-size: 0.75rem`. `rem` is anchored to the browser root (~16px) and ignores `settings.fontSize`, so `0.75rem` = always 12px. The other toolbar buttons at [game.css:45-64](src/renderer/styles/game.css#L45-L64) (`.btn-debug` / `.btn-theme` / `.btn-settings` / etc.) use `font-size: 1.0em` and inherit from `.game-toolbar` which scales with the game font. Same em-vs-rem footgun B113 was about. Fix: changed `.btn-mode-switcher` to `font-size: 1em`. **Also fixed the popover items** in the same file — `.ms-item` / `.ms-item--manage` / `.ms-dot` / `.ms-hotkey` / `.ms-modified` / `.ms-empty` all used `rem` values (0.68rem to 0.82rem); switched to `var(--game-font-size)` with appropriate multipliers (e.g. `calc(var(--game-font-size) * 0.85)` for sub-text) — the popover is portaled to `document.body` so it can't inherit from `.game-toolbar` like the trigger button can, hence the direct var reference. Multipliers preserve the original visual hierarchy (manage/dot/hotkey/modified slightly smaller than main items). CLAUDE.md pitfall #45 documents the rule so new game-area UI doesn't reintroduce `rem` for sizing that should scale. |
| B124 | F29 Lichborne→Lichborne import silently strips most rule fields — exporting a bold + glowing highlight and re-importing on another character drops the bold and glow; trigger gates / oneShot / watchStream / action ordering all lost; group memberships wiped across every rule type so "share my hunting setup" puts rules into no group at all | v0.8.7 (Sekmeht, retroactive sweep) | Found by Sekmeht's proactive bug-check pass on v0.8.7 — not yet reported by a tester. Root cause: the parser at [lichborne.ts](src/renderer/import/parsers/lichborne.ts) routed every rule through the `ImportCandidate` neutral intermediate at [types.ts](src/renderer/import/types.ts), which was designed for Wrayth/Genie/Frostbite shapes and **doesn't model most native Lichborne fields**. Then the mapper at [mapper.ts](src/renderer/import/mapper.ts) hardcoded defaults for every missing field (`bold: false`, `glow: false`, `groupIds: []`, `allGroups: true`, `name: ''`, `enabled: true`, `gates: []`, `oneShot: false`, `cooldownSeconds: 0`, `watchStream: 'any'`, fresh action ordering). Verified field-by-field losses: **highlights** lost bold/glow/glowColor/groupIds/allGroups/name; **triggers** lost gates/oneShot/cooldownSeconds/watchStream/echo color/WAV soundFile path (converted to soundPreset which is the wrong type)/action ordering (reconstructed as commands→echos→vars→logs→sounds→flash→beep regardless of user's authored order)/groupIds/allGroups/name/enabled; **macros** lost groupIds/allGroups/name/enabled; **aliases** lost caseSensitive/delayMs/passThrough/groupIds/allGroups/name/enabled. The most damaging loss is `groupIds`/`allGroups` across every type — F29 is marketed as "share my hunting setup," but a user's rules-in-hunting-group become rules-in-no-group on import while the "hunting" group itself comes through correctly (via the `nativeGroups` bypass that skips ImportCandidate). Shipping silently since v0.8.4 — F29 sees low traffic in same-mode/allGroups configurations where group-membership loss is invisible. **Fix (Option B — Lichborne source bypass)**: added `nativeRules?: { highlights, triggers, macros, aliases }` to `ImportResult` and built it in [lichborne.ts](src/renderer/import/parsers/lichborne.ts) with fresh ids alongside the existing ImportCandidate arrays (index-aligned so the wizard's selection state applies unchanged); spreads the full source rule object then overrides `id: nanoid()` (and for triggers, each nested `TriggerAction.id` regenerates too). [ImportWizard.tsx](src/renderer/components/ImportWizard.tsx)'s `doImport` step now prefers `nativeRules` over `mapImportResult` output when present — falls through to the legacy mapper path for non-Lichborne sources (Wrayth/Genie/Frostbite still go through ImportCandidate since their source shapes need translation).
