The existing content-based dedup (`hlContentKey`/`trContentKey`/key/input) works unchanged on native rules since the keys read fields that exist on both ImportCandidate-output and native-rule shapes. Verified end-to-end with `npm run build` clean. CLAUDE.md pitfall #42 documents the bypass pattern so a future contributor adding a new rule type doesn't reintroduce the ImportCandidate translation loss. |
| B123 | A single Lich script that emits `<streamWindow id='X'/>` followed by `<pushStream id='X'/>` produces TWO identical rows in Panel Manager — adding one to a panel hides both | v0.8.7 | Reported by Sekmeht with Rakkor's `newkill-counter.lic` as repro. The script emits both XML tags for the same stream id `KillCounter` in a single batch — the parser correctly translates them to a `stream-declare` event (from streamWindow) and a `stream-push` event (from pushStream), and [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)'s event loop pushes the id into `newDiscovered` for BOTH. The dedup filter at the `setDiscoveredStreams` site only checked against `existing.has(id)` (the pre-batch state) — it didn't dedupe within `newDiscovered` itself. So two identical `'KillCounter'` entries both passed the filter and the array ended up `['KillCounter', 'KillCounter']`. PanelManager renders one row per array entry, and adding either entry to a panel made `openCustomIds.has('KillCounter')` true for ALL entries → both rows filtered out together (the symptom that pinpointed it as a single duplicate-id rather than two different ids with same display name). Fix: added a `seenInBatch: Set<string>` local to the filter; first entry in a batch passes and gets recorded, duplicates in the same batch are rejected. `discoveredStreams` is in-memory only (no persistence), so any existing dup clears on next launch — no migration. CLAUDE.md pitfall #40 documents the dedupe-within-batch requirement so anyone adding a third stream-discovery event type (or another script-driven path that emits two tags with the same id) doesn't reintroduce the bug. |
| B122 | Bottom prompt / display halfway cut off at font size 13 or higher even when scrolled to the bottom; scrollbar visibly nudges up for a moment before the next command snaps it back | v0.8.7 | Reported by Rakkor — the cutoff was only visible at font 13+, not at 12 or below, and a faint scrollbar twitch was visible right before the next command's arrival re-pinned the bottom. Root cause in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)'s Virtuoso `totalListHeightChanged` handler: the re-pin distance threshold was `dist > 2`, set conservatively in the v0.6.x scroll-pin performance pass to ignore sub-pixel rounding artifacts. At font 13+ each row is ~20px tall vs ~17px at font 12; the fractional shift Virtuoso introduces on a height recompute can land at 1.5–1.9px — under the 2px threshold, so the corrective re-pin didn't fire and the prompt sat below the visible window until the next command's render triggered a fresh pin. Fix: lowered the threshold to `dist > 0.5`. Still immune to genuine fractional-pixel rounding (browsers don't manufacture 0.3–0.5px scroll shifts spuriously), catches real cutoffs at any font size. The scrollbar twitch was the diagnostic — it showed the moment the new content rendered above the prompt and the pin-correction missed it. With the lower threshold the correction fires and the prompt sticks to the bottom at all font sizes. |
| B121 | Players section (and other Room sub-streams) shows stale data after room transitions — ghost players from a previous room until a forced `LOOK` | v0.8.7 | Reported by Rakkor — the Players section listed people who weren't in the room and only cleared after typing `LOOK`. Root cause: the components that populate the Room panel's sections (`<component id='room players'/>`, `room objs`, `room creatures`, `room extra`, `room exits`) carry over from the previous room when DR doesn't re-emit them on a transition. Without an explicit clear, the Room panel keeps surfacing the prior room's player/creature list. **First attempt was wrong**: I tried tracking per-batch `subStreamSeen` flags in GameWindow's event loop and clearing un-seen sub-streams on a title change. User caught the obvious failure mode: IPC batches can split the title-change from the component updates, so batch-local flags would falsely "see no players this batch" and wrongly clear data the next batch was about to populate. **Architectural shift**: moved detection to the parser layer ([StormFrontParser.ts](src/main/parser/StormFrontParser.ts)) where DR's XML protocol ordering is deterministic — `<streamWindow>` always arrives BEFORE any `<component id='room ...'/>` for the new room. New `lastRoomTitle: string` field on the parser; in the streamWindow handler, when `cleanTitle !== lastRoomTitle` emit `clear-stream` for `room`, `room-objects`, `room-players`, `room-creatures`, `room-extra`, `room-exits` BEFORE the typed `room-title` event. Components that DO arrive for the new room then re-populate via their own clear+stream-text flow; sections that DON'T arrive stay correctly empty. The `lastRoomTitle` gate ensures a `<streamWindow>` re-emit with the same subtitle (DR sends these for non-transition repaints) doesn't clobber freshly-populated sub-stream data. **B117 compatibility verified** — the parser's `captureSegments` work for monsterbold happens inside the `<component>` flow which fires AFTER our pre-emit clear-streams, so the new clear doesn't interfere with bold capture. CLAUDE.md pitfall #41 documents the parser-vs-batch ordering rationale and the lastRoomTitle invariant. **B121 may also marginally help the Genie Maps "forgets location" symptom** (Rakkor's follow-up wonder): clearing `roomDesc` to empty string on subtitle change ensures the GenieMapView `currentLocation` adjacency-disambiguation memo never sees a ghost description from the prior room, so it falls cleanly through to graph-adjacency or pool[0] fallback instead of locking onto a wrong same-titled candidate. The primary fix for "Genie forgets location entirely" remains B110's `staleZoneCountRef` escape valve (v0.8.4). |
| B120 | Pressing Down arrow past empty command box accumulates negative count; requires extra Up presses to recover; entering a fresh command after over-pressing Down clears the "blank entries" pseudo-state in a way that doesn't match user expectation | v0.8.7 | Reported by Binu with a clean reproduction: from a clear command box with any command history, press Down 3 times → now have to press Up 4 times (not 1) to get the last command back. Root cause in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) command-bar keydown handler: the Down-arrow path did unbounded `historyIdxRef.current - 1` with no floor, so each press past `-1` (the "no history selected" sentinel) drove the ref deeper negative. Up-arrow's `historyIdxRef.current + 1` then had to crawl back through that negative depth before any history entry surfaced. Fix: clamp Down at `-1` via `Math.max(-1, historyIdxRef.current - 1)`. Up was already correctly bounded by `Math.min(h.length - 1, historyIdxRef.current + 1)`. One-line change. |
| F35 | New built-in theme: light-mode "Classic" — a paper-white companion to the existing dark Classic, originally Rakkor's "My Ivory" custom build | Rakkor | Requested by Rakkor (TheTargonian) after building it as a custom theme. Promoted to a built-in so it ships with Lichborne and survives profile resets / fresh installs. Theme constant `classicLight` defined in [themes.ts](src/renderer/themes.ts) with ~150 vars: indigo accent (`#3d4580`) on a true `#ffffff` canvas, near-black body text (`#1a1a1a`), hand-tuned room/exp/map colors for strong contrast. Catalog entry `{ id: 'classic-light', name: 'Classic', category: 'general', vars: classicLight, swatches: ['#ffffff', '#3d4580', '#1a1a1a'] }`. **Both built-in Classic themes use the display name "Classic"** — the visual differentiation is the swatch dot (black for dark Classic, white for Classic Light). Internal ids stay unique (`classic` vs `classic-light`) so persistence + theme lookup work correctly; ThemePicker React keys use `item.id` so no double-key warning. `initTheme` reads the saved id from localStorage and resolves via `THEMES.find(t => t.id === savedId)` — display-name collision is never load-bearing. |
| F34 | Per-contact social stats — "Encounters" and "Time Encountered" counters with a per-contact Reset button | Sekmeht | Sekmeht's idea, extending the existing last-seen tracking. New optional fields on the `Contact` interface in [contacts.ts](src/renderer/contacts.ts): `encounterCount?: number` (times the contact has appeared in `room.players` after an absence), `timeSpentMs?: number` (cumulative time observed in the same room), `lastEncounterAt?: number` (internal timestamp for the cooldown gate). All optional so existing contacts load with no migration. New `formatDuration(ms)` helper renders `42m`, `2h 13m`, `3d 5h`, or `—` for sub-minute. **Tracking architecture** ([GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)): existing last-seen effect extended to bump `encounterCount` on detection when BOTH `(now - lastSeen) > ABSENCE_THRESHOLD_MS` (90s) AND `(now - lastEncounterAt) > ENCOUNTER_COOLDOWN_MS` (10 min) — single-gate cooldown alone wasn't enough because a contact who stays in your room for >10 min would re-tick the counter every time `room.players` changed. New 60s polling tick adds 60s to `timeSpentMs` for each contact currently in `room.players`; uses `pendingContactsRef.current ?? contactsRef.current` as its base so it doesn't clobber in-flight last-seen buffer updates. **UI** ([ContactsPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/ContactsPanel.tsx)): two stat rows under "Last seen" labeled **Encounters** and **Time Encountered**, with a small per-contact **Reset** button on the right that zeros both counters + clears `lastEncounterAt`. Mirror display in [ContactPopover.tsx](src/renderer/components/ContactPopover.tsx) under "Last seen" — **always rendered**, even at zero, so the user can answer "have I encountered this person yet?" at a glance for fresh contacts (Rakkor's follow-up — first cut hid the row when both counters were zero, which made the feature invisible until the first encounter). `.cpop-notes` `max-height` bumped from 100px → 200px in [contact-popover.css](src/renderer/styles/contact-popover.css) to give multi-paragraph notes more breathing room before the scrollbar appears. `.cpop` width bumped 240px → 290px in the same file so the two-column stats row ("Encounters: N" + "Time Encountered: 2h 13m") fits on one line without the value wrapping below its label. **Persistence**: rides through the existing `saveContacts` → localStorage → `buildCharacterProfile` YAML pipeline automatically (Contact[] serialized as JSON; new optional fields just round-trip). **F29 import/export**: same automatic carry-through — Contact cast in `doImport` accepts the new shape. CLAUDE.md pitfalls **#36** (pendingContactsRef invariant) and **#37** (encounter detection needs both absence + cooldown gates) document the architectural traps. |
| F33 | Click the `>` prompt marker next to the command bar to open Quick Send (mirroring Ctrl+Shift+Enter) | Rakkor | Requested by Rakkor (TheTargonian) — wants the prompt glyph to be discoverable as a QuickSend launcher, not just a visual cue. The marker in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) is now a `<button>` that dispatches a `lichborne:open-quick-send` custom DOM event on click; AppShell's listener (added in the same change) reads the visible session-shell's `.command-input` value and opens QuickSend with that prefilled — identical behavior to the Ctrl+Shift+Enter chord. **Why a DOM event, not a callback prop**: AppShell owns the QuickSend modal state and already queries the DOM for the source input value in the keyboard path. Custom event keeps the trigger logic in one place without prop-drilling through every GameWindow instance. Button styling in [game.css](src/renderer/styles/game.css) strips browser button chrome (transparent bg, no border, inherited line-height) so the glyph still reads as a plain `>`; hover brightens from `--accent-dim` to `--accent` to advertise it's clickable; `:focus-visible` outline for keyboard users. Tooltip is "Quick Send (Ctrl+Shift+Enter)" so the keyboard alternative is discoverable. |
| B118 | F31's floating font controls overlap the panel's vertical scrollbar when content scrolls, making them hard to hit | Self-found | Self-found while bug-checking F31. `.panel-font-controls` was `right: 4px`; combined with the global 12px scrollbar (`::-webkit-scrollbar { width: 12px }` in [global.css](src/renderer/styles/global.css)) the buttons sat directly over the scrollbar rail when stream panel content scrolled. Bumped to `right: 18px` in [panel-frame.css](src/renderer/styles/panel-frame.css) — 12 scrollbar + 4 gap + 2 padding = buttons sit just to the left of the scrollbar rail at all times. |
| F32 | Clicking a character tab should auto-focus the active GameWindow's command bar (mirror the Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+# keyboard paths that already do this) | Rakkor | Requested by Rakkor (TheTargonian) — clicking a tab left focus wherever it was, so testers had to click into the command bar before they could type. Fix in [App.tsx](src/renderer/App.tsx): new `useEffect` watching `activeId` from `useSessions()` runs `refocusActiveCommandBar()` on every change. The existing keyboard handlers' explicit `refocusActiveCommandBar()` calls remain (harmless redundancy; both paths converge on the same `document.querySelector('.session-shell:not(.session-shell--hidden) .command-input')` selector). `requestAnimationFrame` waits a frame for the session-shell hidden-class flip before the input becomes focusable. Net result: every path that changes `activeId` — tab click, Ctrl+Tab, Ctrl+1..9, future triggers — auto-focuses the command bar. Same B91 pattern from v0.7.1, just generalized to react to state changes instead of being called explicitly from each handler. |
| F31 | Per-panel font size — let users tune any one panel's text size independently of the global game font size (e.g. tiny Conversations / huge Thoughts) | Rakkor | Requested by Rakkor (TheTargonian) with a Frostbite side-by-side. New `AppSettings.panelFontSizes: Record<string, number>` keyed by tab id (stable across zone moves). UI: floating **A−** / **A+** buttons in the panel-body's **bottom-right** corner ([PanelFrame.tsx](src/renderer/components/PanelFrame.tsx)) — top-right was initially placed but conflicted with Exp's Sort/Focus pickers and other panel headers; bottom-right is freer of chrome. **Vertical (stacked)** layout halves the horizontal footprint so the buttons cover less content. Opacity 0.15 at rest → 0.6 on panel-frame-body hover → 1.0 on button hover; `pointer-events: none` until hover so the buttons don't intercept clicks beneath them at rest. Each press adjusts ±1 px, clamped 8–24. PanelFrame applies the override as a `--panel-font-size` CSS var inline on `.panel-frame-body`; [panels.css](src/renderer/styles/panels.css) rules for `.stream-panel`, `.room-panel`, `.exp-panel`, `.injuries-panel` use `var(--panel-font-size, var(--game-font-size))`. **Critical fix during testing — `.text-line` CSS coverage**: stream panels (Active Spells, Thoughts, Conversations, Arrivals, Deaths, Familiar, Inv, custom) route through TextLineRow which renders each row as `.text-line`. That class had its own `font-size: var(--game-font-size)` rule in [game.css](src/renderer/styles/game.css) which won via CSS specificity over the `.stream-panel` parent's font-size, so adjusting the panel size visibly changed nothing for stream panels until `.text-line` was switched to `var(--panel-font-size, var(--game-font-size))` too. Main scroll's `.text-line` is outside any `.panel-frame-body`, so `--panel-font-size` is undefined there and the fallback to `--game-font-size` kicks in — main scroll unaffected. **Persistence fix during testing — `scheduleProfileSave`**: the first cut of `onAdjustPanelFontSize` wrote to localStorage but didn't trigger the debounced YAML save, so panel sizes only made it to disk when some other setting change triggered a save. Now matches the Settings panel's onChange pattern: `setSettings(next); saveSettings(character, next); scheduleProfileSave(account, character, game, useLich)`. Round-trip verified end-to-end: A− click → localStorage → debounced YAML write → next character mount → loadSettings reads it back → PanelFrame applies it. **Scope for v0.8.5: text-heavy content panels** (stream / room / exp / injuries — every panel that uses `.text-line`) respect the override; Map / Debug / Lich Scripts panels have their own CSS rules without the var-with-fallback so the buttons fire but those panels don't visibly scale. Mostly chrome / structured content; extension is a one-line CSS change per file when a tester asks for it. |
| F29 (layout) | Extension to F29 (v0.8.4 Lichborne import/export) — also export and import the panel layout (zone enabled flags, tab arrangements, zone heights, panel widths, per-panel font sizes) so a full setup carries between characters | Rakkor | Requested by Rakkor right after F29 landed. Bumped Lichborne export format from v1 → v2 in [AutomationsPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/AutomationsPanel.tsx). New `buildLayoutSnapshot(character)` helper reads every layout-related localStorage key via scopedKey (all four `*Added` booleans + `*Tabs` arrays + `*ActiveId` strings + `*Height` numbers + `panelWidth`) and bundles them under a `layout` block on the YAML payload; `panelFontSizes` is pulled from settings (where it lives at rest) into the same block. [parsers/lichborne.ts](src/renderer/import/parsers/lichborne.ts) accepts v1 and v2; rejects > v2 with the "newer than this Lichborne supports" error. The layout rides on a new `nativeLayout?: unknown` field on `ImportResult` (typed at the apply site). ImportWizard surfaces an opt-in **"Apply imported panel layout"** checkbox on the confirm step ONLY when the file actually carries a layout block (default checked); user can untick to keep their current arrangement. doImport writes the keys back via direct localStorage.setItem (boolean / numeric / string / JSON helpers, each type-checked); panelFontSizes merges into the existing settings object so a partial import is non-destructive of unrelated fontSize entries. **Layout reads happen at GameWindow mount**, so the user needs to reconnect (or disconnect/reconnect that character) for the new layout to take effect. Done-stats message notes this with "(reconnect to apply)". Round-trip: A exports → B imports → B has A's layout + rules after reconnect. Older v0.8.4 importers handling a v2 file fall through to the version-rejection path cleanly. |
| B117 | Creatures in the Room panel's structured sections render as plain text instead of with DR's monsterbold styling that the main scroll shows | Rakkor | Requested by Rakkor (TheTargonian) with a side-by-side screenshot — `an ice archon` rendered as bold/colored in the main scroll's `look` output but as plain text in the Room panel's Objects section. Root cause: DR wraps creature names in `<pushBold/>...<popBold/>` inside `<component id='room objs'/>` (and `room creatures` / `room players` / `room extra`), but the parser's `captureBuf` was a single string — it accumulated all the text inside the component without per-piece formatting, then emitted a `stream-text` event with a single bold-less segment. Bold info lost at the parser layer. Fix: parser ([StormFrontParser.ts](src/main/parser/StormFrontParser.ts)) gained `captureSegments: TextSegment[]` alongside `captureBuf`; `text()` pushes each non-empty text node into the array tagged with current `boldDepth` state; component-emit prefers `captureSegments` over the single-segment fallback when any were captured. captureSegments resets at all 7 sites that init captureBuf (4 init + close + reset() + preset-init). `RoomState.objects` / `players` / `creatures` / `extra` switched from `string` → `TextSegment[]` in [shared/types.ts](src/shared/types.ts); `desc` kept as `string` (MapPanel takes it as string, room descriptions rarely have bold spans). GameWindow stores `evt.segments` directly into roomState rather than joining to `lineText`; contact-tracking effect joins to text for the regex sweep; clear-stream sets to `[]`. RoomPanel's `renderSegments(segments, keyBase)` builds joined `lineText` + per-segment offset and calls `renderSegmentFull` with both, so cross-segment regex (B115) + priority overlay (B116) + monsterbold (B117) all compose correctly. Bold-segment slices through renderSegmentFull preserve `bold: true` via `{ ...seg, text: ... }` spread, so a creature name split across a highlight match still renders bold on its non-matched portions. |
| B116 | Match-scope regex highlights silently dropped from rendering when a contact name match starts at the SAME position as the highlight — e.g. contact `You` matching the first 3 chars of a `Your mind hears .*? thinking,` highlight wiped the entire highlight from view | Rakkor | Reported by Rakkor (TheTargonian) — v0.8.4's B115 fix made the regex MATCH cross-segment lines (confirmed via Debug → Fires), but the actual rendering was still blank. Diagnosis via Rakkor's screenshot: contact `You` and highlight both started at index 10. The old algorithm in [renderSegmentFull.tsx](src/renderer/utils/renderSegmentFull.tsx) was "first wins, drop anything that overlaps": sort ranges by start (contacts beat highlights on ties), iterate, push non-overlapping. So contact `You` at [10,13] won the tie, advanced the cursor to 13, and the highlight [10,46] was completely dropped because its start (10) was less than the cursor — even though [13,46] didn't overlap with anything. Fix: replaced overlap-removal with a **boundary-based priority overlay**. Collect every range start/end + 0 + text.length into a sorted boundary set. For each adjacent boundary pair, find the highest-priority range fully covering that gap (contact > highlight > base). Merge adjacent same-range pieces into runs so the rendered DOM stays compact. Result: for Rakkor's line, runs are `[General] ` (plain) + `You` (contact blue) + `r mind hears ` (highlight orange) + `Balistrade` (contact blue) + ` thinking,` (highlight orange) + `"..."` (plain). Algorithm is backward-compatible — when ranges don't overlap, it produces identical output to the old "first wins" version. Side benefits: nested contacts (e.g. one inside another) now resolve consistently (first-pushed wins), and multi-highlight overlaps no longer drop overlapping rules silently. The B115 fix (cross-segment regex matching via `lineText` + `segOffset`) is preserved unchanged — that fix was correct, B116 is the rendering-layer follow-up. |
| F30 | RT / CT timer chips are too small to read at a glance — eyes have to focus to count them, defeating their "see remaining time without looking away" purpose | Rakkor | Requested by Rakkor (TheTargonian) with side-by-side screenshots showing Lichborne's tiny dashes vs Frostbite's seconds-bar. Each chip represents one second of remaining timer; the old 8×6px dashes with a 6px gap looked like static decoration rather than countable seconds. Sekmeht's plan: "double the chip size, then reduce the gap by like 1/3" — landed exactly that in [game.css](src/renderer/styles/game.css): width 8→16px, height 6→12px, gap 6→4px, border-radius 2→3px (proportional softening at the new size). Tested live with Rakkor — five chips at the new size read as "5" without him having to count ("I don't even have to count to know that's 5"). Strip still sits at top:0 (RT) / bottom:0 (CT) of the cmd-bar via absolute positioning, `pointer-events: none` so it doesn't interfere with typing. Animation (chip-pulse 1.1s) and epilepsy-safe override unchanged. |
| B115 | Match-scope regex highlights don't apply in the Thoughts stream (and other panels) even though the same regex matches in the live editor preview | Rakkor | Reported by Rakkor (TheTargonian) — his "Your mind hears .*? thinking," highlight worked in the editor preview but never painted on actual thoughts. Root cause in [renderSegmentFull.tsx](src/renderer/utils/renderSegmentFull.tsx): regex matching ran against ONE segment's text at a time, but DR wraps player names (e.g. "Yarwend") in XML attributes that split a single line into 3+ segments. The pattern could never match because the text was fragmented across segments — every regex test ran against a tiny slice. Line-scope highlights already worked correctly because `getLineHighlightStyle` joins `fullText` before testing; match-scope highlights needed the same treatment. Fix: `renderSegmentFull` got two new optional params `lineText` and `segOffset`. When provided, regex matching runs against the joined line text, then each match is intersected with the current segment's range and translated to segment-local coordinates. [TextLineRow.tsx](src/renderer/components/TextLineRow.tsx) now builds `line.segments.map(s => s.text).join('')` once per render and threads the running cursor offset into each `renderSegmentFull` call. Backward compatible — callers that omit the new args keep per-segment behavior. Side benefit: contact-name lookups (`nameRegex`) also work across segments now, so a contact whose name happened to span a segment boundary (rare but possible) would also match. Applies to main scroll AND every stream panel (Thoughts, Conversations, Arrivals, Deaths, etc.) since they all funnel through `TextLineRow`. |
| F29 | Lichborne-to-Lichborne import / export for sharing rules + contacts between characters or backing up just the automations side | Rakkor | Requested by Rakkor (TheTargonian). The per-character YAML profile already contains everything (rules + theme + layout + connection settings + …), but doesn't give a clean "share just my hunting setup" workflow. **Export**: new button in the Automations panel header (next to Import) bundles the active character's `highlights`, `triggers`, `macros`, `aliases`, `groups`, `modes`, `contacts`, and `contactTemplates` into a YAML payload with `formatVersion: 1`, exporter metadata, and timestamp. Triggers a browser download named `{character-slug}-automations-{YYYY-MM-DD}.yaml` via Blob + temporary `<a download>` — no IPC needed since the file save is browser-native. **Import**: new "Lichborne" tile as the 4th option in the Import Wizard step 1, single file slot for the `.yaml`. New parser at [parsers/lichborne.ts](src/renderer/import/parsers/lichborne.ts) does a near pass-through (data is already in native shape) into ImportCandidate records so the existing preview UI works; groups / modes / contacts / contact templates ride along in new `nativeGroups` / `nativeModes` / `nativeContacts` / `nativeContactTemplates` fields on `ImportResult`. Format-version check rejects files newer than the current importer supports; parse errors surface as a single "unsupported" pseudo-highlight in the preview. File picker's `accept` attribute extended from `.cfg,.ini,.xml` to also include `.yaml,.yml`. **Dedup**: append-merge dedup uses **content-based keys** (mapper.ts assigns fresh nanoids on every import, so id-based dedup never matched anything for highlights/triggers — silent bug in the first draft, fixed). Helpers `hlContentKey()` (pattern + scope + caseSensitive) and `trContentKey()` (pattern + caseSensitive) are file-scope so both preview computation and apply-time dedup share the same logic. Macros dedup by `key`, aliases by `input`. Native data dedup: groups/modes/contact templates by `id`; contacts by `name`. Replace-merge bypasses dedup. **Preview visibility** (Rakkor's "check what already exists vs. the ones being imported and have the ability or option to only import new items"): `goToPreview` computes per-index duplicate-flag sets `dupH` / `dupT` / `dupM` / `dupA` against existing rules; duplicate rows render with a muted `EXISTS` badge in the Status column and `opacity: 0.55` so they recede. A "Hide items already in this profile" toggle in the select-bar hides duplicate rows entirely for a clean view of what's new. **Replace-mode safety**: an earlier draft auto-unchecked duplicates in the initial selection, which broke Replace merge — if user switched to Replace, the unchecked duplicates would be wiped from their profile along with everything else, leaving them with neither the imported version nor their original. Reverted to ship everything checked by default; duplicates are visually flagged but stay selected, so Replace works correctly (overwrites including duplicates) and Append silently skips them at apply time with the count reported on the done screen. The done-stats summary reports both imported counts and skipped duplicates. **Round-trip**: character A exports → character B imports → B has A's automations applied. Same character re-importing onto itself with `append` is idempotent (everything skipped); with `replace` it's exactly idempotent (every checked item overwrites its existing twin). |
| (UX) | Automations header **Export** / **Import** buttons looked off-theme on hover — used full `--accent` border + `--accent` text with no transition, which on themes where `--accent` is a high-contrast brand color produced a jarring pop and what Sekmeht described as "losing the colors from the theming" | Sekmeht | Fix in [automations.css](src/renderer/styles/automations.css): aligned the `.at-import-btn` style with the main game-toolbar button pattern (`.btn-debug`, `.btn-settings`, etc. in [game.css](src/renderer/styles/game.css)) — softer `--text-muted` resting text instead of `--text-dim`, `--accent-dim` border on hover instead of full `--accent`, added `background: var(--bg-hover)` for a visible hover surface, and added a 0.2s ease transition for smoothness. Buttons now match the visual language of the rest of the UI across every theme. |
| B114 | Color-blind (and high-contrast) accessibility overlays are silently erased by theme writes — most visible when live-editing a custom theme | v0.8.4 | Self-found while auditing the accessibility setting per the developer's request. `applyTheme` / `applyCustomTheme` in [themes.ts](src/renderer/themes.ts) write every theme var to `:root.style`, which overlaps the same vars the accessibility overlays set (e.g. deuteranopia's `--vital-health-ok-start`, high contrast's `--bg-app`). Without a re-apply, the overlay was effectively dead after any theme write. Three paths hit this: **(a) ThemeEditor live editing** — every color change calls `applyCustomTheme(next)` and the GameWindow effect doesn't re-fire because `currentThemeId` doesn't change during editing; the colorblind overlay stayed dead until save. **(b) ThemePicker preview paths** (`handleCustomizeBase`, `handleEditCustom`, `handleEditorCancel`) — apply themes without updating `currentThemeId`. **(c) Theme switching** — single-frame flash where the overlay was gone before the GameWindow effect re-fired. Fix: themes.ts gained a module-level `postApplyHook` and `registerThemeAppliedHook(fn)` export. `applyTheme` / `applyCustomTheme` call the hook after writing vars. GameWindow's theme-apply effect registers a callback that closes over the active session's `settings` and re-runs `applySettingsToDOM(settings)` — which re-writes both the high-contrast and color-blind overlay vars. Cleanup on effect teardown clears the hook so an unmounting GameWindow doesn't apply the wrong character's overlays after the next mount. Settings.ts can't be imported from themes.ts (would create a circular dep), so the registration pattern is the right shape — themes.ts knows it has a hook to call, doesn't know what's in it. New pitfall in CLAUDE.md (#33) documents the pattern for future overlay additions. |
| B113 | Game text in Lichborne reads "not as black" as Frostbite even when "Game Text" is set to #000000 on a light theme | Rakkor | Reported by Rakkor (TheTargonian) with side-by-side screenshots comparing Lichborne (text reads grey on white) vs Frostbite (text reads true black on white). Already on Consolas (his first swap), so font choice wasn't the gap. Root cause: **Chromium's DirectWrite vs Frostbite's GDI ClearType**. Both render `color: #000`, but DirectWrite at small sizes paints more grey-fringe pixels around each stroke (its antialiasing is more "soft"), while GDI ClearType uses LCD subpixels more aggressively to keep strokes crisp. Same color value, different antialiasing, visibly different darkness. Not something CSS can flip — the renderer is Chromium-fixed. Fix: added a per-character **Text weight** setting on `AppSettings` ([settings.ts](src/renderer/settings.ts)) that tunes text rendering on a symmetric 7-option scale (-0.6 / -0.4 / -0.2 / 0 / +0.2 / +0.4 / +0.6). Positive values add `-webkit-text-stroke` (faux-bold via stroke widening, capped at 0.6 because above ~0.5 the text reads blurry rather than bold). Negative values lower CSS `font-weight` below the default 400 (-0.2 → 300, -0.4 → 200, -0.6 → 100). The negative path only renders thinner on fonts that ship light weights — Cascadia Code (default) has 200/300/350; Consolas / Lucida Console fall back to 400 silently. **Cascade scope: body, not just .text-line.** Initial implementation was scoped to `.text-line` (main game scroll) — Rakkor pointed out the room title and other panels stayed at fixed weights when he picked Thinnest. Moved `font-weight` and `-webkit-text-stroke` declarations to `body` in [global.css](src/renderer/styles/global.css) so every text surface inherits. Bold-styled elements (room title, roomname/bold presets, exp group headers, footer labels, RXP labels, prompt marker, etc.) now use `--ui-bold-weight: calc(var(--game-text-weight) + 200)` in [theme.css](src/renderer/styles/theme.css) so the bold contrast survives at any user setting — Thinnest → body 100 / bold 300, Default → body 400 / bold 600, etc. HTML `<strong>` / `<b>` defaults to browser UA `font-weight: bold` (700) which would bypass the cascade — added `strong, b { font-weight: var(--ui-bold-weight); }` to [global.css](src/renderer/styles/global.css) so HTML bold tags scale too. Chrome elements (toolbar, login, settings UI, modals) deliberately left at fixed weights — they're application chrome, not game content. UI is a dropdown in Settings → Display with all 7 options; the live Settings preview mirrors both the stroke and font-weight so users can compare options before applying. Persists per-character via the dynamic `state:` pipeline (textWeight rides into `lichborne.{character}.settings` localStorage entry, which `buildCharacterProfile` already iterates into the YAML state map — no profile schema change). Verification: Rakkor (or any tester on a light theme + DirectWrite) should try Slightly bolder (+0.2) → Bolder (+0.4) → Boldest (+0.6) and confirm text reads as black as Frostbite at one of those steps. |
| B111 | Highlights (contacts + match-scope) don't apply to the Room panel's Players / Objects / Creatures / Extra sections — names that turn blue in main text stay default-colored in the structured Room display | v0.8.4 | Reported by Rakkor (TheTargonian). He had Dawan and Moza tagged as blue contacts; when he typed `look`, the main scroll showed both names blue correctly, but the Room panel's Players section rendered them in the default text color. Other panels (Thoughts) DO apply highlights, which made the gap visible. Root cause in [RoomPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/RoomPanel.tsx): it rendered each section (`room.players`, `room.objects`, `room.creatures`, `room.extra`, `room.desc`) as raw strings inside `<div>{room.players}</div>` — never went through `renderSegmentFull` so it bypassed the contact name regex and match-scope highlight rules. Sekmeht's instinct on diagnosis was right ("room is a different type of stream — code constructing the xml vs streaming text") — RoomState is built by the parser from `<component>` elements; the Room panel reads it as plain strings. Fix: import `useContacts` + `useHighlights`, wrap each text section's content in a `TextSegment` (`{ text }`) and route through `renderSegmentFull(seg, key, contacts, templates, nameRegex, matchRules, onContactClick, onSendCommand, false, false)`. URL auto-linking is disabled — room text isn't a URL surface. The room title is left as-is (it's a heading, separately styled, rarely contains contact names). lineRules (whole-line highlights) are NOT applied — a "lobster" lineRule shouldn't paint the entire Objects section red just because one item matches; only match-scope highlights and contacts make sense here. Side benefit: existing match-scope rules like creature-name highlights for hunting now work in the structured Creatures section too. |
| B110 | Genie Map loses the player's location and stops tracking — marker stays at the previous spot even after walking far away; `look` doesn't help; reloading the Genie maps snaps it back | v0.8.4 | Reported by the developer — walked outside Shard, marker stayed inside Shard's "Forging" area; `look` did nothing; ↺ Refresh Genie maps fixed it. Root cause is the "conservative cross-zone fallback" added at [GenieMapView.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/GenieMapView.tsx) `currentLocation` memo to prevent file-order accidents from briefly snapping the marker into the wrong zone: `if (prev && pool[0].zone !== prev.zone) return prev`. The fallback was meant for one-off transients but had no escape — once the player legitimately moved to a different zone *and* there was no description / same-zone-adjacency / cross-zone-stub link to confirm the move (teleport, `;go2`, recall, death/depart, or a missing stub in Genie's hand-curated XML), every subsequent room title also triggered `!== prev.zone` and the fallback kept returning `prev` indefinitely. `look` only helps when a description match resolves it — if the destination zone is loaded but the description doesn't normalize-match any of its nodes' descriptions exactly, `look` is ineffective. ↺ Refresh remounts the component, resetting `prevLocRef` to `null`, so the next memo run skips the fallback condition (`prev` is falsy) and `pool[0]` wins. Fix: added `staleZoneCountRef` counter that increments each time the conservative fallback returns `prev`, resets on any confident-match path (single match / description match / same-zone adjacency / cross-zone stub adjacency / first non-stale fallback). After `STALE_ZONE_ESCAPE = 3` consecutive blocked steps, the fallback gives up on `prev` and commits to `pool[0]` — three different room titles in a row all wanting the same different zone is strong evidence the player actually moved, not a parser glitch. One-off file-order accidents (a single bad title between real matches) still get blocked exactly as before because the counter resets. The ref mutation inside the memo is a deliberate exception — the result still depends only on the memo's declared deps, and the counter behavior is independent of render timing. |
| B109 | Lich Map flashes "NEEDS MAPPING" warning for one frame between every walk step before the locator snaps to the new room | v0.8.4 | Reported by the developer. The Lich Map's currentRoom match effect in [MapPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/MapPanel.tsx) re-runs whenever `roomTitle`, `roomDesc`, or `roomId` props change — and during fast walking those three update across separate render commits (the game streams them in slightly different XML elements, and the room-state rAF pump in GameWindow can land them in adjacent frames). For the in-between frame, `findRoom` was being called with the new title but stale/missing description and roomId; it returned `undefined`; `setCurrentRoom(undefined)` committed; the banner condition `roomTitle && !currentRoom` evaluated true → "NEEDS MAPPING" painted for one frame. Next render the remaining props arrived, findRoom resolved, banner cleared. Visually: a single-frame flash on every step. Fix: keep the immediate `setCurrentRoom(found)` path when a match resolves, but defer the `setCurrentRoom(undefined)` clear by 400ms via a `setTimeout` whose cleanup cancels the timer if a new effect fires first. If a real match arrives within 400ms (the normal walk case), the timer is cancelled and the banner never paints. If the player genuinely is in an unmapped room and stays there, the 400ms delay before the diagnostic appears is imperceptible. Side effect: the old `currentRoom` stays on screen during the transient gap rather than blinking off, so the locator doesn't briefly disappear between rooms either. Only affects the Lich Map view; Genie Map uses a separate match path (`currentLocation` memo in GenieMapView). |
| B108 | Genie Map background stays brown-black no matter what App Background a user picks in the Theme Editor | v0.8.4 | Reported by Binu. The map's `--map-bg` CSS var is a separate theme variable from `--bg-app` (the App Background that drives the other game windows). The Theme Editor's `TABS` schema exposes `--bg-app` but **not** `--map-bg`, so custom themes never set `--map-bg` — meaning every custom theme falls back to `darkBase`'s default `#0a0807` regardless of what the user picked for App Background. The map ended up visually disconnected from the rest of the themed UI. Fix in [themes.ts](src/renderer/themes.ts): `darkBase['--map-bg']` changed from the literal `#0a0807` to `'var(--bg-app)'`. CSS variables can reference other CSS variables, and the resolution happens at use time — so `background: var(--map-bg)` now resolves through `var(--bg-app)` to whatever the active theme set. **Built-in themes that intentionally set `--map-bg` to something different from `--bg-app` (Parchment, Mist, etc.) keep their explicit value** because the theme merge overrides darkBase. Only custom themes (which never set `--map-bg`) and the bare `dark` theme are affected. Out-of-the-box, the dark theme's map now matches its panel chrome (both `#0f0f0f`) — Binu's specific request. If a future power user wants independent map-bg control, the right move is to add `--map-bg` to the Theme Editor's TABS schema rather than re-introduce a literal default. |
| F07 | Stormfront/Wrayth repeat-command bindings — Ctrl+Enter sends the last typed command, Alt+Enter sends the second-to-last; one-key send-or-repeat from the numpad | v0.8.3 | Requested by Binu — "the only thing that keeps me going back to Frostbite." Implemented Frostbite-style as **special macro tokens** rather than hardcoded keybindings, so users can rebind to whatever key they want. Three tokens added in [macros.ts](src/renderer/macros.ts): `{RepeatLast}` (sends history[0]), `{RepeatSecondToLast}` (sends history[1]), `{ReturnOrRepeatLast}` (if command bar has text, sends it + pushes to history + clears the bar — i.e. acts as Enter; if empty, falls through to RepeatLast — matches Wrayth's NumpadEnter behavior). `getMacroToken(cmd)` recognizes them when the entire trimmed command equals the token; mixed text passes through literally. **Dispatch refactor**: extracted `dispatchUserText(text, opts)` from `handleCommand` in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) so the macro fire site can replay a historical command through the *same* alias-resolution + echo + log machinery as a fresh user-typed command — so repeating "hunt" re-fires the `hunt` alias instead of sending the literal word raw. `commandRef` mirror added so the once-mounted global keydown handler can peek at the command bar (for ReturnOrRepeatLast); `dispatchUserTextRef` mirror so the handler can call the latest function instance despite empty deps. Token-aware macro fire site batches plain commands into `sendCommandSequence` (preserving `delayMs`) and dispatches tokens individually with the right history/clear semantics. **History push policy**: only the bar's normal Enter and ReturnOrRepeatLast-with-typed-text push to history — RepeatLast/SecondToLast do NOT push when replaying, otherwise pressing RepeatLast twice would re-pin the previous command and break the next RepeatSecondToLast. **Seeding**: fresh characters get `Ctrl+Enter → {RepeatLast}`, `Alt+Enter → {RepeatSecondToLast}`, `NumEnter → {ReturnOrRepeatLast}` pre-seeded; gated by a per-character `lichborne.{character}.seededRepeatMacros` flag so a user who deletes the defaults doesn't see them resurrected. Skips any seed whose key is already bound (never silently overrides user customization). **Editor UX**: `MaVarPicker` gained an optional `tokens` prop — Macros tab passes `MACRO_TOKENS`, Aliases tab does not (tokens don't make sense in alias bodies); picker renders a "Special tokens" sub-section below the `$vars`, clicking inserts `{TokenName}` syntax. |
| B107 | A Lich script's declared stream (e.g. moonwatch's "Moons") never appears under Available Streams in the Panel Manager — script works fine for other testers | v0.8.3 | Reported by Legiro via Binu. Same shape as B106 but in PanelManager. `allTabs` in [PanelManager.tsx](src/renderer/components/PanelManager.tsx) was built from `[...mainTopTabs, ...topTabs, ...midTabs, ...bottomTabs]` regardless of the per-zone `*Added` flag — so a custom tab sitting in an un-added zone (either from a removed zone whose tabs weren't cleared in an older code path, or from a default-populated tabs list for a zone the user never added) counted as "open custom", which the `availableCustom` filter uses to hide already-placed streams from Available. Net effect: discovered stream id is in `discoveredStreams`, but its phantom invisible tab blocks it from rendering under Available Streams, so the user has no path to add it to a visible slot. Fix: gate `allTabs` on the `*Added` flag, mirroring the watchedStreamsRef gate from B106. Same logic in two places now — a stream id can't be considered "open" if its hosting zone isn't actually rendered. If a future report still shows a discovered stream missing from Available after this fix, the next suspects are: (a) the Lich script never emits `stream-declare` on that install (parser / network capture needed), or (b) the id matches a NEVER_DISCOVER / ALL_PANEL_TYPES entry (the discovery filter would reject it before it ever enters `discoveredStreams`). Both diagnosable in DebugPanel → Events. |
| B106 | Combat lines stop reaching the main window when no panel hosts them; adding the Main-Top panel later auto-populates Combat + Room as if pre-configured | v0.8.3 | Reported by the developer. Two compounding root causes: **(1)** `watchedStreamsRef` in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) was computed from all four `*Tabs` arrays regardless of the `*Added` flag — so a zone with un-added=true but tabs=[room, combat] still "watched" combat, blocking the `STREAM_FALLBACK` route to main. The combat lines went into `streamLines.combat` and visually disappeared. **(2)** Main-Top's `mainTopTabs` default was `[room, combat]`, populated even though Main-Top defaults to `mainTopAdded=false`. So a fresh user who never touched Main-Top still had phantom tabs blocking fallback (cause #1), AND the first click on Add Main-Top in the Panel Manager surfaced those phantom tabs as if pre-configured (the user expected an empty slot to fill manually, matching the after-remove → re-add flow). Two-part fix: **(a)** `watchedStreamsRef` now gates each zone's tabs on its `*Added` flag — phantom tabs in an un-added zone no longer block stream fallback. **(b)** `mainTopTabs` default switched to `[]` so Add Main-Top always produces an empty placeholder for both fresh installs and the after-remove path. Existing users with saved tabs keep them — only fresh-install / never-touched Main-Top is affected. The deeper pattern (`tabs.length > 0` ≠ "zone is in use") is documented in CLAUDE.md pitfall #31 (extended). |
| B105 | External URL clicks fail through the play.net bounce — destination page shows "site doesn't exist" with a URL like `https%3A%2F%2Fimgur.com%2Fa%2FcYoVxuW` | v0.8.3 | Reported by the developer. The F23 web-link safety wrapper in [renderSegment.tsx](src/renderer/utils/renderSegment.tsx) constructed the bounce URL as `https://www.play.net/bounce/redirect.asp?URL=` + `encodeURIComponent(href)`. The encoding was the bug: play.net's `redirect.asp` takes the value after `URL=` literally (Genie's `FormMain.cs` LinkClicked path does raw string concatenation for exactly this reason), so encoding `://` to `%3A%2F%2F` hands the bounce page a malformed destination — it renders the encoded string verbatim instead of redirecting, and the user sees "site doesn't exist." Confirmed by the developer that the unencoded form (`?URL=https://imgur.com/a/cYoVxuW`) redirects correctly. Fix: drop the `encodeURIComponent` call — concatenate the href raw, matching Genie's behavior. The F23 comment in the wrapper was updated to call out why encoding must NOT be added back (so a future contributor doesn't "fix" the unencoded URL on principle and break the bounce again). |
| F27 | Lich Map right-click should run `;go2 <room-id>` instead of the local step-by-step walker | v0.8.2 | Requested by Binu. The Lich Map's right-click and "Walk here" button previously ran a local BFS walker that stepped through cardinal-direction commands every 600ms — brittle in practice, since any blocking event (creature attack, locked door, hidden exit, roundtime stall) killed the walk and left the player stranded mid-route. Lich ships a stock `go2` script that's specifically designed to move to a target room and handles all of that. Fix in [MapImageView.tsx](src/renderer/components/panels/MapImageView.tsx): right-click and the "Walk here" button both now `onSendCommand(\`;go2 \${id}\`)`. Local walker infrastructure deleted — `walking` state, `walkTimers` ref, the unmount-cleanup `useEffect`, the `cancelWalk` function, the top-toolbar Stop button. To cancel a `;go2` in flight, users type `;k go2` in the command bar (the standard way to kill any Lich script). The local BFS in `bfsPath` is kept for the left-click visual path pin (static preview, not movement) and the `(N steps)` distance cue on the "Walk here" button label. The "Walk here" button now has an explicit tooltip `Sends ;go2 <id> to Lich` for discoverability. **Genie Maps untouched** — Genie XML edges are direction commands (north, climb tree, etc.) not Lich room IDs, so `;go2` doesn't apply; Genie's `sendWalkPath` stays. |
| F26 | Lich Map "you are here" indicator is hard to spot — easy to lose the player's position on white tiles or green map regions | v0.8.2 | Requested by Binu. The prior indicator was a green-tinted rect with a stroke pulse — the `#40e080` stroke disappeared into Lich's green map regions and the opacity pulse was too subtle to find on white/cream PNG tiles. Rewritten to mirror the Genie Maps sonar pattern adapted to per-room rectangles: two `genie-here-ping` expanding rings centred on the room, plus a dual-contrast solid ring (dark backdrop `rgba(0,0,0,0.55)` 5px + bright lime accent 3px) using `vectorEffect="non-scaling-stroke"` so it stays crisp at any zoom. The dark backdrop guarantees visibility on white/cream Lich tiles; the bright accent guarantees visibility on dark themes / dark map regions. Saturated lime `#00ff80` chosen over the Genie indicator's muted `#4caf50` because Lich tiles run cream/white where the muted Genie green washes out. Added a **bullseye centre dot** (dark backdrop dot + bright accent dot on top) to both Lich AND Genie indicators so dense same-cluster rooms can still be distinguished at a glance — Genie's existing indicator gained the same dot for consistency. **Themable**: three new CSS vars in [themes.ts](src/renderer/themes.ts) darkBase — `--lich-here-color`, `--lich-here-backdrop`, `--lich-here-fill` — so themes can re-pick the Lich palette without touching Genie's `--map-current-color` (the two were kept independent because Lich Map's white/cream aesthetic and Genie Map's themed-bg aesthetic want different colour choices). Ring radius is `max(w, h) / 2 + 3` in image-coord units so it sits just outside the room rect for rooms of any size; dot radii floor at 1.5/0.9 so they stay visible on tiny rooms. CSS keyframe (`genie-here-ping`) reused as-is — `transform-box: fill-box; transform-origin: center` makes the scale animation work around the element's bbox centre regardless of where cx/cy sit in image space. |
| B104 | Floating compass centre dot draws off-cell at common font sizes | v0.8.2 | Reported by the developer with a screenshot showing the `·` glyph sitting up-and-left of where the cell centre actually was. Root cause: the centre cell rendered a U+00B7 middle-dot character at `font-size: 1.2rem`; per-font baseline metrics put the glyph high-and-left of the visual cell centre on the default fonts (Cascadia Code especially). The CSS grid placement of the cell was correct — the *glyph drawing inside the cell* was the issue. Fix: removed the `.fc-cell--center` `<div>` from [FloatingCompass.tsx](src/renderer/components/FloatingCompass.tsx) and the matching CSS rule from [floatingcompass.css](src/renderer/styles/floatingcompass.css). The 8 directional arrows around the cell already imply the centre by negative space — the dot was a redundant cue. `--compass-center-text` theme var left in `darkBase` so themes don't error on missing vars; sweep next theme pass. |
| B103 | Right-click → "Trigger for this line" sometimes opens the wrong trigger in the editor | v0.8.2 | Self-found while bug-checking the new Fires GOTO work. The v0.8.2 Fires → button introduced a new `triggerOpenId` state on GameWindow that drives an "open existing trigger for edit" path in TriggersPanel. The other paths that open the Automations panel into the Triggers tab (`openTriggerEditor(pattern)` from right-click → "Trigger for X", and `openHighlightEditor` which switches tabs) didn't clear this new state — so after a Fires GOTO + close + right-click trigger-for-line, TriggersPanel ran both useEffects on initial render: the `prefillPattern` effect created a new trigger from X, then the still-set `triggerOpenId` effect fired AFTER and overwrote the draft with the old GOTO target. User saw the wrong rule. Fix: `openTriggerEditor` and `openHighlightEditor` both clear `triggerOpenId` on entry, so the Fires GOTO path is the only one that leaves it set. Found before any user could hit it. |
| B102 | The "Copy All" button in Debug silently does nothing | v0.8.2 | Reported by the developer. Same root cause as B18 (the v0.1.9 auto-copy bug): `navigator.clipboard.writeText` is refused by the renderer's permission handler — Electron's internal permission name doesn't match a whitelist, the call returns a swallowed promise rejection, nothing reaches the system clipboard. Lichborne already routes its auto-copy through a native Electron IPC channel (`write-clipboard`) for exactly this reason — DebugPanel was just the one site that never got migrated. One-line fix: `navigator.clipboard.writeText(text)` → `window.api.writeClipboard(text)` in [DebugPanel.tsx](src/renderer/components/DebugPanel.tsx). The per-tab routing logic (Fires / Events / Raw XML → respective text serialization) was already correct and is unchanged. While here: also bumped Debug buffer limits 500 → 2000 (`MAX_DEBUG_EVENTS` and `MAX_RAW_XML_LINES` in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx)) since the developer asked for more history — collection is gated on `showDebugRef.current` so closed-Debug overhead is unchanged. |
| B101 | The Debug → Fires tab shows the same highlight firing dozens of times per line on text that doesn't visually highlight anything | v0.8.2 | Reported by the developer with a live capture: clicking through the world produced 5+ "Necromancers? \| Necromancy \| …" fire entries per line on text containing zero necromancy words. Diagnosis: the user's highlight pattern was `\b([Nn]ecromancers?\|...\|zombified\|)\b` — note the trailing `\|` right before the closing `)`. That's an *empty alternative* in the alternation, so the regex matches the zero-length string at every `\b` word boundary in the line. The visual rendering layer ([renderSegmentFull.tsx:47](src/renderer/utils/renderSegmentFull.tsx#L47)) already had a `m[0].length === 0` guard and correctly skipped the zero-width match — so no highlight was painted. But `logHighlightFiresRef` in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx) used `regex.test(text)` to decide whether to log a fire; `test()` returns `true` for an empty match, so the Fires log accumulated phantom entries on every line with any word boundary (which is every line). The user was misled into thinking their rule was firing on text it wasn't touching. Two-part fix: **(1) The user's data** — removed the stray `\|`. **(2) The engine** — `logHighlightFiresRef` now uses `regex.exec()` and walks past zero-width matches (`lastIndex++` prevents an infinite loop on the global-flag regex), only logging when it finds a match of length > 0. The Fires log now mirrors what the renderer actually highlights, so this footgun can't mislead anyone again. The visual rendering path was already correct; only the diagnostic log was lying. |
| B100 | Combat stream appears twice in Available Streams — adding it from the second row creates duplicate tabs with the same id | v0.8.1 | Self-found while exercising Panel Manager V2. The `combat` PanelType was a builtin entry in `ALL_PANEL_TYPES` but the `combat` stream id was missing from the hand-curated `NEVER_DISCOVER` set in [GameWindow.tsx](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx). So when the game emitted a `combat` stream (which it does as soon as combat starts), the renderer added `'combat'` to `discoveredStreams`. PanelManager then rendered Combat **twice** in Available Streams: once as the builtin "Combat" row (correctly hidden after add because the builtin filter checks `openTypes`), and once as a "discovered custom" row labeled Combat (which the old `availableCustom` filter only checked against `openCustomIds` — open builtin tabs have `type: 'combat'`, not `type: 'custom'`, so the discovered row never disappeared). Clicking the second row pushed a duplicate tab with the same `id: 'combat'`, repeatedly. Two-layer fix: **(1) at discovery** — the filter now also rejects any id matching an entry in `ALL_PANEL_TYPES`, so a builtin panel id can never enter `discoveredStreams` regardless of whether someone remembers to add it to `NEVER_DISCOVER`. Adding a new builtin PanelType in the future is automatically covered. **(2) at display** — PanelManager's `availableCustom` filter defensively also excludes ids matching a builtin type or an open builtin tab, so a stale discovered-streams state from an older session can't reintroduce the duplicate. While here, the hand-curated `NEVER_DISCOVER` list was trimmed of entries (room/thoughts/deaths/conversations/spells/familiar/inv/exp/debug/log) that the broader `ALL_PANEL_TYPES` check now catches; only the aliases (`experience` → `exp`, `thought` → `thoughts`, etc.) and structural ids (`main`, `raw`, `room-objects`…) remain. `discoveredStreams` is in-memory only (no persistence), so the fix takes effect on next session with no migration. |
| F25 | Add a search field to the Lich profile YAML editor — find a key in view mode, jump to it when entering Edit mode | v0.8.1 | Requested by the developer. New `Search YAML…` input in the profile-edit-bar of the YAML editor, visible in both view mode and edit mode. Enter or click Find scrolls to the first matching line (case-insensitive substring); repeated clicks cycle through matches, wrapping to the top after the last one. Implementation: converted `YamlHighlight` and `EditorWithGutter` ([LichDashboard.tsx](src/renderer/components/LichDashboard.tsx)) to `forwardRef` with a shared `YamlViewHandle` imperative interface — `find(term)` returns the matched line index (or -1) and advances an internal cursor for cycling; `scrollToLine(idx)` jumps to a specific line; `resetSearch()` clears the cycling cursor. Parent owns `yamlSearch`, `lastFoundLine`, and a single `yamlViewRef` that React swaps between the two components as the user toggles Edit/Cancel — so the search input is one DOM element regardless of mode. **Click Edit while a match is highlighted in view mode → editor scrolls to the same line** via a `useEffect` on `isEditing` that re-issues `scrollToLine(lastFoundLine)` post-commit (the ref points at the just-mounted instance). In edit mode the match is also selected in the textarea (browser shows the selection highlight). Search cursor resets when the file changes (`selectProfile`) so the next Find starts at line 0 of the new file; the typed term is preserved across file changes so the user can re-find the same key without retyping. Scrolling math is `el.scrollTop = lineIdx * (scrollHeight / lineCount) - clientHeight/2 + lineHeight/2` — centers the matched line in view; works for both the `<pre>` (view) and `<textarea>` (edit) since both are monospace with equal-height lines. |
| F24 | Add a new resizable panel zone above the main text area (Room + Combat by default) | v0.8.1 | Requested by the developer. New panel zone sits in the top portion of the left side of the game window (above the main scrolling text + command bar; right panel column unaffected). Implementation: **(1)** New `'combat'` PanelType added in [PanelFrame.tsx](src/renderer/components/PanelFrame.tsx) — label "Combat", routes the existing `combat` stream (already emitted by [StormFrontParser.ts:38](src/main/parser/StormFrontParser.ts#L38)) to its own panel via `sp('combat', streamLines.combat ?? [])`. When no panel subscribes to `combat`, the existing `STREAM_FALLBACK` map ([GameWindow.tsx:103](src/renderer/components/GameWindow.tsx#L103)) re-routes the lines into `main` so closing the Combat tab is non-destructive. **(2)** New `mainTopTabs` / `mainTopActiveId` / `mainTopHeight` state in GameWindow, persisted to per-character localStorage like the right-column zones. Defaults: `[Room, Combat]`, active `room`, height 250px (≈top 1/3 of a typical game window). `watchedStreamsRef` aggregation widened to include `mainTopTabs` so combat is recognised as watched when the tab is present. **(3)** Layout: wrapped the existing `.text-window-wrap` in a new `.game-main-area` flex-column container that holds the optional main-top zone + a `.panel-h-divider` (reuses the right-column divider styling) + the unchanged text-window-wrap. Right column is a sibling of the wrapper, untouched. **(4)** New `'main-top'` case in the row-divider mousemove handler; bounded against `mainAreaRef.current.offsetHeight` minus a 120px floor for the text below so the main text can never be squeezed into invisibility. Min height 100px, max 600px. **(5)** Right-column `topTabs` default trimmed from `[Room, Conversations]` to `[Conversations]` since Room moved up into the new main-top zone (resetLayout updated to match; existing users with saved topTabs keep whatever's there). |
| F23 | Add Genie-style web-link safety — route external URL clicks through Simu's bounce page so users get a warning before leaving Play.net | v0.8.1 | Requested by the developer. Mirrors Genie's `bWebLinkSafety` config flag (in Genie source at [FormMain.cs:8321](C:\temp\Genie4-Dev\Genie4\Forms\FormMain.cs#L8321) `FormSkin_LinkClicked` — wraps any clicked http/https link as `https://www.play.net/bounce/redirect.asp?URL=<link>` when the safety flag is on). Lichborne does the same, gated on a new `webLinkSafety: boolean` field on `AppSettings` (default `true`). `wrapExternalLink()` helper in [renderSegment.tsx](src/renderer/utils/renderSegment.tsx) prepends the bounce URL when the flag is on and the link is http or https (file:// / mailto:// etc. pass through). The flag is threaded through `renderSegmentFull` → `TextLineRow` → `StreamPanel` → `PanelFrame` → GameWindow's `sharedFrameProps` so every URL-click site (main text window + every stream panel + custom panels) gets the same treatment. New SettingsPanel toggle below "Auto-link URLs" — same on/off model as Genie. URL is `encodeURIComponent`'d before being inserted as the query value — Genie's source doesn't encode, just string-concatenates, but encoding is the safer move for URLs containing `&` or other reserved chars. |
| F20 | Per-character notes section with Guild + Circle + free-text notes, shown on the tile and edited via a modal; collapsible account sections (default collapsed) | v0.8.0 | Requested by the developer. **Three additions:** **(1)** `CharacterProfile` gains three optional fields: `guild?: string` (canonical lowercase key matching themes.ts entries — `empath`, `moonmage`, etc.), `circle?: number`, `notes?: string`. All optional; no migration needed. New `CharacterNotesEditor.tsx` modal opens from each tile's ⋯ menu → "Edit Profile…" — guild dropdown (the 12 DR playable guilds with "— None —"), circle number input, multi-line notes textarea, Save / Cancel. A new generic `patchCharacterProfile(name, patch)` helper persists all three fields in a single read-modify-write round-trip (intentionally separate from the per-field toggles to avoid renaming hands-on call sites). **(2)** Tile meta line now appends `· Empath 50` (guild + circle) when set; a small inline `✎` indicator with a `--accent-dim` color renders at the end of the meta line when notes are non-empty. Indicator is purely visual — clicking it doesn't do anything special, the edit path is via the ⋯ menu. **(3)** Each account section is now collapsible (default collapsed). Header is a button that toggles expand/collapse, with a ▶/▼ chevron prefix and a "N characters" count suffix. Expanded-account names persist in `localStorage` under `lichborne.launcher.expandedAccounts` so a tester who expanded FortissABrok yesterday still sees it expanded today. **Favorites section is exempt** — always rendered open at the top of the launcher (testers wanted it to be the standard at-a-glance view); collapse only applies to the per-account groupings. ⋯ menu item ordering: Edit Profile… → Hide/Unhide Profile → Delete Profile…. |
| F19 | Add a Favorites section at the top of the launcher (heart toggle on each tile) + show Lich/Direct as a paired pill (active colored, inactive grey, click the grey to switch) | v0.8.0 | Requested by the developer. **(1)** New `favorite?: boolean` on `CharacterProfile` (optional, no migration). Tiles get a heart button in the top-left of the header (♡ when off, ♥ when on, colored with `--color-danger` for the universal "favorited" red). Toggling persists via new `setCharacterFavorite()` helper. A new "Favorites" section renders at the top of the launcher when at least one tile is favorited — section header has a heart icon prefix to echo the toggle. Favorited tiles **mirror** into Favorites (they ALSO still appear in their account / game section below) — Favorites is a quick-access shortcut, not a re-categorization. Hidden overrides favorite — hidden tiles don't appear in Favorites unless Show Hidden is on. **(2)** Replaced the single click-to-toggle LICH/DIRECT badge with a **paired pill** (`.launcher-card-modes` container with both `.launcher-card-mode--lich` and `.launcher-card-mode--direct` rendered side-by-side). Active pill keeps its themed color (lich = green, direct = blue); inactive pill is greyed out (`--text-faint` background + border + text), with a hover brightness bump. Click the inactive pill to switch — the active pill is `disabled` (clicking it is a no-op). Replaces the v0.8.0 F18 single-button toggle. Moved the pill row out of the header onto its own line below the meta so the heart + name + ⋯ menu can fit comfortably in the header at the 260px minimum tile width. |
| F18 | Add an account-discovery flow that builds tiles for every character on the account in one pass; make per-tile Lich/Direct a click-to-toggle badge; allow hiding tiles | v0.8.0 | Requested by the developer to "improve the logon process." Three coupled changes: **(1)** Rewrote `AddCharacterWizard` from per-character (account + password + mode + game + character name → connect) to per-account (account + password + game → EAccess discovery → checkbox list → bulk-create stub YAMLs). The wizard no longer connects — picking a character to actually log in is a separate click on the tile afterwards. EAccess returns the same character list for both Lich and Direct paths (the SimuCo auth service is mode-agnostic), so discovery never needs Lich running. Stub YAML per character: `{ profileVersion: 2, account, character, game, useLich: true, theme, state: {} }`. Skips characters that already have a profile (no overwrite of saved automations/themes). Renamed launcher entry-point and `+` tile from "Add character" to "Add account" to reflect the new shape. **(2)** LICH/DIRECT badge on each tile is now a clickable button — click flips `useLich` between true/false and persists via `setCharacterUseLich` (mirrors `setCharacterGame`). Tooltip explains both states. Same single-click persist pattern as the DRT checkbox. **(3)** Added a `hidden?: boolean` field on `CharacterProfile` (optional — existing profiles parse fine; undefined === visible). Tiles get a visible ⋯ kebab-menu button (right-click still works) with two options: "Hide tile" / "Unhide" (toggles `profile.hidden`) and "Delete…" (existing flow). A "Show N hidden tiles" toggle row appears at the bottom of the launcher when at least one tile is hidden. Plus a "↺ Refresh" button on each account header that opens the wizard pre-filled with that account so the user can pull in new characters they've made in-game without retyping. |
| F17 | Same-account conflict on connect was a flat refusal — should ask the user before refusing | v0.8.0 | Reported by the developer. DR only allows one character per SimuCo account at a time, so connecting Sekmeht when Sekmeht is already connected on the same account returned "Sekmeht is already connected on account FOO. Disconnect them first" and stopped — forcing the user to go disconnect the other character manually. Now the launcher (and wizard step 1) raises a confirmation modal: *"{conflict} is currently connected on account {acct} ({game}). DragonRealms only allows one character per account at a time. Continue and {conflict} will be disconnected automatically before {new} ({game}) connects."* Buttons: Cancel / Disconnect-and-continue. On Continue: the conflicting session is gracefully disconnected via a new awaitable IPC (`disconnectAwait` — wraps `gracefulDisconnect()` and resolves when the server has actually dropped the slot, or 5s timeout); then `runConnect(new)` fires. A single 2s retry on the new connect catches the common race where DR's server-side account-slot release lags the local disconnect by a beat. The conflicting tab is intentionally NOT removed — it stays in the bar in disconnected state (same as if the user had pressed the in-tab Disconnect button), so the user can close it or re-login to it later. |
| F16 | Per-shard tabs — Sekmeht-DR and Sekmeht-DRT (same character on different shards) should be separate tabs, not the same tab renamed | v0.8.0 | Requested by the developer. `CharacterId` was `account::character` (lowercased); a connect to the same character on a different shard reused the existing tab. Changed to `account::character::game` ([SessionsContext.tsx](src/renderer/SessionsContext.tsx) `makeCharacterId`); the tab bar already rendered `session.game` next to the name (`.character-tab-game`) so visual distinction was there — only the keying needed widening. DR's one-character-per-account rule still applies (the F17 conflict modal handles same-account conflicts), so you still can't have Sekmeht-DR and Sekmeht-DRT *connected at once* — but you can have one connected + one in disconnected state for re-login. The character profile YAML stays keyed by character name only — automations / theme / layout are shared across shards, which is the right call (same character, different server). |
| F21 | Bulk Connect — login one character per account in a single click sequence | v0.8.0 | Requested by the developer. New `⚡ Bulk Connect` top-bar button + `BulkConnectPicker.tsx` modal. Click → modal lists each account with a dropdown of its connectable characters (defaults to a favorited character if any; alphabetical-first fallback). Already-connected accounts are disabled with a hint. Confirm → sequential connect (one character at a time — DR's account-slot rule forbids parallel on the same account; sequential is simpler for error isolation across different accounts too). Progress overlay during the sequence; per-character errors don't abort the run; final summary modal reports per-character success/failure. Implementation reuses the existing per-character connect plumbing in `App.tsx` (`runBulkConnect` walks picks, calls the same login IPC + profile import/export + session.add chain as a single-tile click). |
| F22 | Launcher UX overhaul — smart auto-expand, top-bar Add Account / Bulk Connect, tile compression, Favorites hint, character-tab reorder | v0.8.0 | Requested by the developer (UX phases 1+2). Five coupled changes: **(1)** Smart auto-expand — single-account users always see their account expanded (collapse toggle hidden), and freshly-added accounts auto-expand once after the wizard creates them (wizard writes the account name to `lichborne.launcher.expandedAccounts` before bumping refreshKey). Solves the "stared at empty collapsed launcher" first-impression problem. **(2)** Persistent `+ Add account` and `⚡ Bulk Connect` buttons in the top bar — Add Account no longer requires scrolling past collapsed accounts; Bulk Connect surfaces conditionally based on `bulkConnectIsEnabled()` (≥2 accounts with connectable characters). Welcome card copy / CTA updated from "Add character" to "Add account" to match the actual flow. **(3)** Tile structure compressed from 5 stacked rows to 3 (header / meta / single action row). TEST SERVER → TEST, merged into the action row alongside LICH/DIRECT. New `showAccount` prop on CharacterCard drops the redundant account name from tiles rendered inside an account section (it's already in the section header); Favorites stays account-stamped (account-mixed). **(4)** Favorites discoverability hint — dismissable single-line tip above account sections when user has tiles but no favorites; dismiss state persists in localStorage. **(5)** Character tab reorder — Name → L/D pill → game code → indicators (was Name → game → L/D); pill margin tightened so the cluster reads together. |
| B99 | Closing Lichborne while connected takes several seconds (up to 10s with multiple characters) and the window appears frozen | v0.8.0 | Reported by the developer — counted ~10s with two characters connected. The shutdown path in [main.ts](src/main/main.ts) drains active sessions via `gracefulDisconnect` in [ConnectionManager.ts](src/main/connection/ConnectionManager.ts), which sent QUIT and **waited up to 5s for the server-side disconnect ack**. Behavior since v0.7.0 (B85 era). Two-part fix: **(1)** New `quickClose: true` option on `gracefulDisconnect` skips the ack-wait entirely — sends QUIT, gives the local Lich TCP send queue ~300ms to flush, then force-closes. The character is still logged out either via clean QUIT or via Simu's socket-drop timeout. Wired into the app shutdown path (`mainWindow.on('close')`); in-tab Disconnect and the conflict-modal auto-disconnect keep the full 5s wait since they need the account-slot release confirmed. Total shutdown drops from up-to-5s-per-session to ~300ms regardless of session count. **(2)** Added a "Closing — disconnecting N character(s)…" overlay (new `shutdown-starting` IPC + `onShutdownStarting` preload bridge) so even the brief sub-second wait has feedback rather than a frozen-window feel. **(3)** Timing instrumentation (`console.log('[shutdown] ... +Xms')`) added to each shutdown phase so if testers report it still feels slow we can pinpoint where the time actually went (renderer flush vs disk backup vs network drain). |
| B98 | The Add Character modal (clicking + while logged in) doesn't scroll — content past the viewport edge is cut off and unreachable | v0.8.0 | Reported by the developer. The modal's container (`.add-character-modal`) uses flex centering (`display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center`). The launcher inside it has `flex: 1; min-height: 0; overflow-y: auto` (its standard rules), but those only kick in when the parent gives the launcher a *bounded* height. Flex centering doesn't — the launcher's height is content-driven, it grows past the viewport, and `overflow-y: auto` never activates because the element isn't constrained. Outside the modal (full-page launcher) this works because the launcher is the child of a `flex-direction: column; min-height: 0` container chain rooted at the AppShell. Fix: scoped CSS rule `.add-character-modal .launcher--compact { max-height: calc(100vh - 48px); width: 100%; max-width: 760px; ... }` — caps the height to the viewport minus the modal's 24px-top+24px-bottom padding, so `overflow-y: auto` from `.launcher` finally has something to overflow. Also pinned width and added a subtle background/border so the modal-launcher reads as a distinct card rather than the page bleeding through. |
| B97 | Toggling Lich/Direct/Test Server (or any other tile pill) on a currently-connected character un-favorites them and strips hidden / notes / guild / circle from the YAML | v0.8.0 | Reported by the developer. Two-layer chain: **(1)** The GameWindow's debounced `scheduleProfileSave` (fires 2.5s after any per-character settings change) calls `exportCharacterProfile`, which used to call `buildCharacterProfile` and write the result verbatim. `buildCharacterProfile` only knows about `{ account, character, game, useLich, theme, state }` — the launcher-managed fields (`favorite`, `hidden`, `notes`, `guild`, `circle`) aren't in that list. So every save from an active GameWindow silently *stripped* them. **(2)** The same save also overwrote `game` and `useLich` with the GameWindow's *stale* `session.game` / `session.useLich`, reverting any launcher-side pill change the user had just made. Fix: `exportCharacterProfile` now does a read-merge-write — reads the existing YAML, builds the GameWindow's contribution (`buildCharacterProfile`), then spreads them with the existing YAML winning on launcher-owned fields (`game`, `useLich`, `hidden`, `favorite`, `guild`, `circle`, `notes`) and `built` winning on GameWindow-owned fields (`theme`, `state`). The race window between launcher-write and GameWindow-save is narrow (launcher writes in <50ms; GameWindow's debounce is 2.5s) so the launcher's writes are almost always durable by the time the merge reads them. |
| B96 | After disconnecting a character then reconnecting (same character, possibly different shard), the tab stays greyed out in the disconnected state even though main is fully connected | v0.8.0 | Self-found while exercising the B95 fix — log in DRT Sekmeht, click Disconnect, click DR Sekmeht in the launcher, the new session connected fine but the tab rendered as if still disconnected (dim + italic, "Login" button in the toolbar). Root cause in [SessionsContext.tsx](src/renderer/SessionsContext.tsx) `addSession`: when a session for an existing `characterId` (same `account::character` slug) was being replaced — the reconnect path — the new `SessionRecord` was constructed with `status: prev[existing].status`. That preserved the prior session's status object, including the `connected: false` written by `updateStatus` during the user-initiated Disconnect. The new session then carried the stale flag, and the tab bar's connection-state styling (which keys off `status.connected`) rendered it as disconnected. The same preservation also leaked stale `bleeding` / `dead` / `rtExpires` / `healthPct` into the new session — the next game events would refresh them but for a moment the tab showed wrong character state.
